# 1785 Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra Expanded Services to the Venerable Fathers of the Kyiv Caves (Служби преподобним Отцям Печерським, Київ: Лавра, 1785) <a id="1785-kyiv-pechersk-lavra-expanded-services-to-the-venerable-fathers-of-the-kyiv-caves"></a>

*(Front Binding — Authentic eighteenth-century leather binding with blind-tooled borders. Text vacant.)*

*(Left page — Front pastedown inside the upper board. Nineteenth-century manuscript provenance inscriptions, archival shelfmarks MKAU-30552, SD-443, and cursive notation: "Matveev... the year 1843".)*

*(Right page — Front flyleaf recto. Manuscript ownership inscription dated 1836:)*
This book belongs to the recipient[^1] Yevstafiy Savych as his personal property. A parental blessing.
In the month of February, the year 1836.
In the month of February, the 17th day. Of the holy favorites of God and wonderworkers of the Caves, A. V.
O Lord God, Great King without beginning, send, O Lord, Thy Archangel Michael. Recipient.
Relative of Christ, may she intercede for us *FRONT MATTER: TITLE PAGE AND PREFATORY APPARATUS:*

*(Left page — Front flyleaf verso. Manuscript provenance notations of the nineteenth century:)*
This book... of Minin Peter Petrov.
The humble elder, Hieromonk Viktor, performing the duties of Treasurer of the Lubny Mhar Monastery, on the 27th day of the month of December, the year 1851.
Signed by the head of the second-class Kyiv-Pechersk Printing House, Cathedral Elder I. Pavlov...

*(Right page — Master title page framed within an ornate Baroque woodcut border:)*
To the glory of the Holy, Consubstantial, Life-Creating, and Undivided Trinity: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit:
By command of the Most Pious, Most Autocratic, Great Sovereign, our Empress Catherine Alekseyevna of all Russia[^2]; during the reign of her heir, the Right-Believing Sovereign Tsesarevich and Grand Duke Paul Petrovich, and his consort, the Right-Believing Sovereign Grand Duchess Maria Feodorovna, and the Right-Believing Sovereigns and Grand Dukes Alexander Pavlovich and Constantine Pavlovich; and the Right-Believing Sovereigns and Grand Duchesses Alexandra Pavlovna and Elena Pavlovna;
And with the blessing of the Most Holy Governing Synod, this book:
SERVICES TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES, whose incorrupt relics rest in the Near and Far Caves,
Was printed in the Holy Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra, in the Stavropegion of the same Most Holy Synod, under Archimandrite Zosima[^3]:
In the year from the creation of the world 7293, and from the Nativity in the flesh of God the Word 1785, Indiction 3, in the month of August.

Christ in glory receives the most pure soul of the Mother of God, surrounded by the bodiless angelic hosts. The holy Apostles stand around the bier holding tapers and a censer. Lower left engraver monogram: SFNM.[^4])*

Above in clouds: the Pechersk icon of the Mother of God with the Pre-Eternal Infant; flanked by Saints Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves with opened scrolls, amidst the hills and caves displaying choirs of the venerable fathers; below stands the Great Lavra Church of the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos. Inscription: "Synaxis of our Venerable Fathers Anthony, Theodosius, and the rest.")*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As a fiery pillar and radiant sun that shone forth upon the hill of the Caves, we bring before Thee, O Christ, the great Anthony unto prayer together with the whole synaxis of the God-bearing fathers; for their sake we pray: grant grace unto our monastery, and great mercy unto our souls.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Beholding with noetic eyes the choir of ascetics gathered in mind, come, O people, let us honor them with praises, and bowing before their relics, offer gifts of love; for they are intercessors before God and pleaders for our souls.

*(Right page — Folio 1 recto, ornate woodcut headpiece of the Dormition of the Mother of God:)*

# MONTH OF SEPTEMBER

*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Kyiv Caves Who Rest in the Near Caves:*
Which is chanted after the Apodosis of the Feast of the Elevation of the Precious Cross of the Lord, on the first Saturday.[^5]

*On Friday evening, the bell-ringer strikes the klepalo.[^6] The 20th Kathisma antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I Call," 6 Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Despaired" (Всю̀ ѿложи́вше):*

Dancing in choir with the singing of spiritual songs, we summon all the assemblies of feast-lovers to praise the union of labor-loving, God-bearing fathers, and here we lay as foundation the great and marvelous Anthony, and Theodosius the divinely wise; for their sake, and through the prayers of all the fathers of the Caves, we entreat Thy love for mankind, O Christ, King of all: grant grace unto Thy people, and save from perils the souls of those who faithfully praise Thee at their memorial.

Let us all reverently sing with hymns Anthony of the Caves, the supreme among the fathers and beginner of the whole synaxis, and together let us honor with praises the bright ray and unsluggish emulator of his virtues, the God-bearing Barlaam, and let us cry out with a voice of joy, saying: O our most blessed venerable fathers, entreat Christ God that mercy be obtained for those who perform your memorial with love!

Rejoice, O fruitful Ruthenian land[^7], that hast nurtured citizens of the Jerusalem on high, *(Left page — Folio 1 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
Sing the celebration with a sound of gladness; and thou, O marvelous Cave hill, that hast assembled warriors unto the Heavenly King, and hast shown forth steadfast men of spiritual desires, unto whom let us now cry out, saying: O God-bearing fathers, noetic luminaries, enlighten with the light of your grace the souls of those who honor your synaxis with love!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Who shall tell the mighty acts of the Lord, or what tongue of earthborn men shall utter your marvelous life, O God-bearing fathers of ours of the Caves! How ye endured sufferings, and what ascetic labors ye bore, wearing down your flesh with vigilance, fasting, and tears, and through prayers and humility crushing the pride of the enemy; and having shone forth in a mortal body like angels, ye were translated unto heavenly mansions; therefore visit on this present day those who praise in song your honorable, fasting assembly in God, and ask of Christ God that we may obtain great mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion according to the tone. Seek at the end of this book (see fol. 151).[^8] Then, the Entrance: Prokeimenon of the day. And 3 Readings for the Venerable:[^9]*

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom 3:1–9):*
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was accounted to be misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction, but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly rewarded, for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace hath He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign in them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy is in His venerable ones, and visitation among His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom 5:15–6:3):*
The righteous live unto the ages, and their reward is in the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive a kingdom of glory, and a diadem of beauty from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take to Him His jealousy for complete armor, and make the creation His weapon for the vengeance of His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall put on unfeigned judgment as a helmet.

*(Right page — Folio 2 recto, running header: OF THE NEAR CAVES:)*
He shall take invulnerable holiness for a shield. His severe wrath shall He sharpen for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the unwise. Then shall the right-aiming thunderbolts go abroad, and from the clouds, as from a well-drawn bow, shall they fly to the mark. And hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as out of a stone bow, and the water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall harshly drown them. Yea, a mighty wind shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall it blow them away; thus wickedness shall lay waste the whole earth, and ill dealing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye that be judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule the people, and glory in the multitude of nations; for power is given you from the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom 4:7–15):*
Though the righteous be overtaken with death, yet shall he be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor that is measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is ripe old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him; so that living among sinners he was translated. Yea, speedily was he taken away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of wickedness obscures things that are good, and the wandering of concupiscence undermines the simple mind. He, being made perfect in a short time, fulfilled a long time; for his soul pleased the Lord, therefore hasted He to take him away from among the wicked. This the people saw, and understood it not, neither laid they up this in their minds: that His grace and mercy is with His venerable ones, and that He hath respect unto His chosen.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "As One Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Come, O people, let us anticipate with hymns the foremost choir of God-bearing fathers who shone forth in the cave by fasting as good armor-bearers of Christ; for behold, from noetic Zion they swiftly come unto us, taking thought for their fulfillment and dispensing grace abundantly unto those who honor their honorable synaxis!

*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord gave ear to them (Ps. 33:18 [LXX 33:18]).*
Having assembled today in this holy temple, O ye people, bearing torches meet the monastic assembly that, like noetic stars, hath illuminated the honorable firmament upon the sensible sphere, and cry out with supplicatory voices, saying: O God-bearing and most blessed ones, pray for those who remember your fellowship with love!

*(Left page — Folio 2 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
*Verse: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous (Ps. 31:11 [LXX 31:11]).*
Rejoice, O choirs of Christ-loving people, assemble with those celebrating today with us, and weaving wreaths of praise, greet the synaxis of God-bearing fathers, invoking all by name together, who shone forth in the cave by fasting, and illuminate with the rays of their grace the souls of those who magnify them with love and faithfully perform their memorial!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Enriched from the glory of the Lord, arming themselves with His Cross as with a spear, the assembly of ascetics came together, and the host of God-marked venerable fathers stood upon the hill of the Caves, bearing an invincible weapon against the enemy, whereby they completely cast down the demonic battle-arrays; and having attained unto the heavenly mansion, they received crowns of victory from Christ God and our Savior, and pray unceasingly for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true vine that didst bud forth for us the Fruit of life: we entreat thee, intercede, O Lady, together with the venerable fathers, that He may have mercy on our souls.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As a fiery pillar and radiant sun that shone forth upon the hill of the Caves, we bring before Thee, O Christ, the great Anthony unto prayer together with the whole synaxis of the God-bearing fathers; for their sake we pray: grant grace unto our monastery, and great mercy unto our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The Mystery hidden from all ages and unknown unto the angels was made manifest to those on earth through thee, O Theotokos: God incarnate in an unconfused union, willingly taking upon Himself the Cross for our sake, whereby He raised up the first-fashioned and saved our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Thou Art the Trinity" (Трⷪ҇ца є҆сѝ):[^10]*
Through many afflictions ye traversed the path of this life and attained the goal of noetic desire, taking up your abode in the chamber of divine glory; and now rejoicing with the King of all, God, pray that we also may become partakers of your gladness, we who honor your synaxis, O most blessed fathers!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who art full of grace, through thy mediations and prayers entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of transgressions, for all who sing of thee, the honorable Mother and Virgin!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Having heard of the marriage of the Lamb in the heavenly chamber, and the invitation of the King on high having gone forth unto all, ye left behind all worldly attachments, O God-bearing fathers; and having cleansed your garments with streams of tears, ye honorably entered into the banquet of Christ God: but before the doors of that entrance are shut, pray that we also, having entered through repentance, may become partakers with you!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Beholding the chamber adorned with everlasting glory, we have the desire of the Most High to enter therein, but having no wedding garment, we are ashamed lest we be bound by the angels and cast out; O Virgin Mary, who alone art the garment of boldness for the naked, adorn us and lead us into the kingdom of Christ!

*(Right page — Folio 3 recto, running header: OF THE NEAR CAVES:)*
*Then, "Praise ye the name of the Lord," and the Megalynarion:*
We magnify you, O venerable fathers of ours of the Caves, and we honor your holy memorial, for ye pray for us to Christ our God.

*Verse 1: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous; praise is becoming to the upright (Ps. 32:1 [LXX 32:1]).*
*Verse 2: They that sow with tears shall reap with joy (Ps. 125:5 [LXX 125:5]).*
*Verse 3: The venerable shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds (Ps. 149:5 [LXX 149:5]).*
*Verse 4: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous; and boast, all ye that are upright in heart (Ps. 31:11 [LXX 31:11]).*
*Glory... Both now... Alleluia, thrice.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Following the calling of your Lord, taking the Cross as a staff into your hands, ye feared not the terror that stood before you in the darkness, but with hope ye passed over unto the harbor free of perils, and found the reward of your ascetic labors, rejoicing in unapproachable light; therefore do we honor you, O most blessed fathers, as those who unceasingly pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate your holy memorial with love!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having thee, O Bride of God, who alone art blessed among women, the boast and ornament of Adam, an unconquerable help in battles, the confirmation of the weak, and swift visitation for those in sorrow, today we fall down in thy most pure temple and offer our prayers unto thee: that thou mayest pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto us who piously bow before thy most holy birthgiving.

*Songs of Ascents, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4:*
*Prokeimenon: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel (Ps. 67:36 [LXX 67:36]).*
*Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel (Ps. 67:27 [LXX 67:27]).*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Verse: Praise God in His saints.*
*Then, Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 10 (Matthew 4:23–5:1):[^11]*
At that time, great multitudes followed Jesus...
*After Psalm 50, Glory...*
Through the prayers of our venerable fathers of the Caves, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions.
*Both now...*
Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions.
*Then:*
Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression (Ps. 50:1–2 [LXX 50:1–2]).
*Sticheron, Tone 8:*
Who shall tell the mighty acts of the Lord... (seek on "Lord, I Call").

*(Left page — Folio 3 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
*The Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos, to 6; and of the Venerable, to 8.*
*Tone 8, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us send up a song, O ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who delivered Israel from servitude, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparia:*
At the memorial of Thy venerable ones, grant, O my Christ, help from on high, and guide me, giving me wisdom through Thy Holy Paraclete, that I may offer a hymn of praise unto Thee, the only Master.

The synaxis of God-bearing fathers hath assembled in God; come, O people, in worship, bearing with yourselves living sacrifices, for behold, they summon us that we may offer glory unto the King of all, the only Master.

Embracing the fruits of those whom the East and the Ruthenian land brought forth, and whom the Cave hill reared up like trees planted by God, assemble, O people, to honor them, and let us offer a hymn unto the Master.

Thou art a noetic paradise, O holy Cave hill, that hast multiplied noetic trees, the blessed fathers, whom we cannot enumerate one by one; therefore together for them all we offer praise and song unto the only Master.

A fruitful tree and olive branch, brought forth from that tree unto this hill is the great Anthony, with whose fruits cheering our souls, we offer a song unto Thee, O Christ, Master of all.

Stephen, full of faith and piety, builder of the church founded by God, and Nikon, who adorned it with icons, have shone forth like great luminaries; honoring them, we offer a song unto Thee alone, O Christ, Master of all.

*Glory...*
Offering praise unto Thee, O Most Holy Father, we bring forward as intercessor the marvelous Barlaam,[^12] who despised the glory of boyar lineage; for his sake turn my glory in this world into bitterness, that there I may find glory from Thee, the Master of all.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bring the human race unto its primal nobility through thy holy childbearing: lead me, who have been dishonored by the deceits of demons, unto noetic glory, that I may unceasingly sing unto thee: Rejoice, O glory of the Orthodox, pure Virgin Mother!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, of Whom all creation sings: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.

*Troparia:*
Though there is no beauty of song upon my lips, yet do I bring praise unto thee, O God-bearing Ephraim; for having ceased to serve an earthly prince,

*(Right page — Folio 4 recto, running header: OF THE NEAR CAVES:)*
prince, thou didst follow the Heavenly King and didst receive reward from the hand of the Lord.

How shall I speak of Isaiah, loud-voiced in the gift of miracles?[^13] Entreating him at least as holding the hierarchic throne of Rostov, may he through his prayers unite me, a straying sheep, unto the flock of the Shepherd on high, and lead me into His fold.

In the beauty of the most radiant and among healers I will praise the blessed Damian; for burning with love toward all, he healed through oil and prayer: through his remedy heal me also, O Master and Lover of mankind!

It is meet to honor thee with praises, O Jeremiah, for honored from on high with the gift of prophecy, thou alone by the revelation of God wast a seer of the hidden things of men: remember me, who am in the oblivion of countless sins, O blessed one, and be a guide unto my repentance.

O Matthew, who didst manifestly behold the assaults of demons and didst correct those who gave cause unto passionate suggestions: as one seeing my uncorrected state with noetic eyes, guide me, that I may unceasingly magnify thee, O venerable one!

O good shepherd and luminary of Suzdal, Simon, together with Nikita the Recluse: as lovers of the great Lavra and zealots for monastic rectitude, correct me who have fallen, O fathers, through your chastening!

*Glory...*
Preserving Thy glory in their soul in this life, O Holy Father, the venerable ones were thereby counted worthy of everlasting glory from Thee; that we also may not be deprived thereof, we present their synaxis unto Thee today in prayer.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Lady, who by the Word art the ineffable dwelling of the venerable, make me a partaker of their joy and deprive me not of the glory of the saints, for all things are possible for thee in answer, and thou doest whatsoever thou desirest, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
I venture to honor the synaxis of the God-bearing fathers, the noetic stars that illuminate the Ruthenian land: but having a soul darkened by passions, I am at a loss in my mind; dispel the darkness of inconstant thoughts, that I may honorably sing a hymn unto you, O most blessed fathers!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of the unsetting Star and dawn of the mystical day, for through thee have we beheld the Light and Sun of true righteousness, illuminated by whose rays we unceasingly sing of thee, O Maiden, who alone art blessed!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
The Prophet in divine vision perceived Thee, O Word, intending to become incarnate from the overshadowed mountain, the only Theotokos, and with fear glorified Thy power.

*(Left page — Folio 4 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
From the hill, according unto the foreknowledge of God, the God-bearing fathers appeared as luminaries upon earth, and illuminated the Ruthenian land with the brightness of their life: honoring which, we are filled with wonder, and glorify Thy power, O Christ!

We marvel how, being in the flesh, ye took upon yourselves an ascetic labor beyond nature and became like unto the angels; for having winged your souls with the love of God, ye preferred nothing in the world above it, whence even in weak flesh ye received Thy power, O Christ!

Stephen was steadfast in fasting, and Anastasius in boldness toward God, for in their petition they obtained all things; honoring their love of labor, I glorify Thy power, O my Christ!

Isaac, deceived in light by darkness, yet having overthrown the darkness by the light of rectitude, so that even now the sons of darkness fear his name: for our deliverance from such temptation Thy venerable one prays, O Christ, that we may unceasingly glorify Thy power!

I venture to honor the ascetic labors of Ilya of Murom, the invincible warrior:[^14] who having in His hand a wound from a weapon, yet having love toward Thee, O Christ, in His heart, which wrought a yet deeper wound within him: he is crowned all the more, glorifying Thy power.

We praise the steadfastness of Nikon in temptation,[^15] for he endured much from the infidels; but through patience he acquired even more humility and salvation: through his prayers may we also be saved, O Almighty Christ, as we glorify Thy power!

*Glory...*
In one essence we glorify the Tri-hypostatic Godhead, Father, and Son, and Most Holy Spirit; and we sing of Thee as the one God, and bowing before Thee at the memorial of our venerable fathers, we glorify Thy power!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having hope in thee, I fear not the assaults of demons; and having gained boldness in thee, I withstand the terrors of Satan, knowing that thou art the Good One Who through thunder terrifies the enemies.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Delivering me from the gloom of passions as from deep night, make my spirit worthy to keep early vigil, I pray Thee, in the light of the day of Thy commandments, O Christ!

*Troparia:*
Having cast aside all the gloom of passions and harmful lust, Thy venerable ones, O Lord, ran with patience unto the ascetic struggle set before them; therefore were they illumined with grace from Thee, walking in the light of Thy commandments, O Savior!

We glorify the memory of Titus the Priest, who through humility trampled down the demon of anger, and Laurence the Recluse, who possessed boldness against the subtle

*(Right page — Folio 5 recto, running header: OF THE NEAR CAVES:)*
spirits; for they kept Thy commandments and are glorified in Thy light, O Savior!

Though I venture to praise him who shone sacredly in fasting, yet I cannot beatify him worthily; therefore unto Thee alone do I flee: grant me strength from Thy gifts, that I may praise Thy favorite, O Savior!

Theophilus, eminent in wonders, is praised in song, for while yet in this life he astonished many through the working of miracles; and now having received a greater gift to assist others, because he kept Thy commandments, O Savior!

We honor Basil and Theodore with the martyrs, for slandered by a demon, they innocently received torment from the prince: the one an end by an arrow, the other by fire and smoke; but together they are glorified in Thy kingdom, O Savior!

Prokhor is praised in his working of miracles and in his labors,[^16] for like a bird he fed upon unsown herbs, and we learn of him as the maker of bread from orache, who also gave salt transformed from ashes: marveling at his working of miracles, we glorify Thee, O Savior!

*Glory...*
Guided by the Spirit, named a son of the Most High, John was illuminated through fasting; for he was nourished by the Word of God rather than by food: therefore lightly, as if winged, did he pass through this life, receiving Thy glory from Thee, O Savior!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
All thy glory is within, O King's Daughter: adorned and arrayed, thou standest before the most pure throne; whence shining brighter than the sun, look down upon us with the rays of thy goodness, that we may unceasingly sing of thy power, O Virgin Mother!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Encompassed with many sins, receive me, O Lover of mankind, who fall down before Thy compassions, as Thou didst the Prophet, O Lord, and save me!

*Troparia:*
I am constrained by love to praise your synaxis, O God-bearing fathers; for ye are praised by God and men, because in this life ye despised the glory of this world, that ye might fulfill the commandments of Christ.

What tongue shall tell your glory, O venerable ones, which ye acquired through many labors and brought unto perfection through grace! And these signs make manifest your marvelous benefactions and healings, wherewith despise not us who are weak, that we may be able to fulfill the commandments of Christ!

O Agapitus rich in miracles, who dispensest healings abundantly, and venerable Helladius with the other Abraham: as possessing much boldness before the Master of all, despise not your servants, but pray *(Left page — Folio 5 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
pray that we may be delivered from the snares of the enemy, and ever do the will of God!

O John of many virtues, who even after death by obedience unto thy brother didst show forth an image of love; and Theophilus, who didst wash away the defilement of vainglory with tears: as ye are undivided both here and there, pray together unto God, that we also may have true love toward Him and unfeigned love toward our neighbor!

O Damian, who through prayer and oil didst heal bodily passions, with Sabbas the Wonderworker, despise not us who are exhausted by spiritual passions; but from the healing gift of your prayers cure our wounds of heart, that we may offer acceptable prayers unto God!

O Nectarius the Obedient, with Sergius of one mind with thee: having yielded your souls under the yoke of the obedience of Christ, bow down our stubbornness through your prayer under the yoke of Jesus Who sent you forth, that we may diligently fulfill His commandments!

*Glory...*
The glory of the city of Smolensk, Mercurius the Shepherd,[^17] who well pastured the sheep of his Chief Shepherd, and in spirit was exalted on high to be inseparably of the choir of the Caves venerable ones: therefore after death he miraculously floated in a wooden coffin, and in the holy cave rests with his body, while in his soul he rejoices with the venerable ones unto eternal glory.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having no ascent from good works unto the dwelling of the venerable, I flee unto thee, O Good Theotokos: for thou art the door of contemplation and the ladder beheld by the prophets; according unto thy goodness, therefore, grant an ascent in my heart, and open unto me the doors of thy mercy!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Beholding with noetic eyes the choir of ascetics gathered in mind, come, O people, let us honor them with praises, and bowing before their relics, offer gifts of love; for they are intercessors before God and pleaders for our souls.

*Ikos:*
Who can praise Thy saints one by one, O Good One? I will number them and they shall be multiplied above the sand; but do Thou Thyself, O Master Christ, Who countest the multitude of the stars and callest them all by name, receive the prayers of them all for us, and show Thy compassions upon Thy people: for we know that they have much boldness before Thy majesty, and intercede with Thee as unceasing pleaders for our souls!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
O Hebrew children in the furnace, Who didst preserve for her who bore Thee her virginity after childbearing: blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Help me to offer a childlike hymn, O Anyfey, divinely beautiful

*(Right page — Folio 6 recto, running header: OF THE NEAR CAVES:)*
among the fathers of the Caves, that through your instruction, with the people named after Christ, I may dispassionately sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

O Pimen the Long-Ailing, who not only didst pass through this life in illness with thanksgiving, but didst also grant relief in sicknesses unto others through prayer: do thou, who art now in endless rest, relieve me from sickness, that I may sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Pure gold and silver tried sevenfold in the gifts, O righteous Eustathius: thou wast a righteous worker, and didst cross over into the working of noetic virtues; wherewith having adorned thy soul, thou hast presented an offering as gold, and singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

O glorious Nestor,[^18] who by thy writing didst declare unto us the times and years of the most precious Church, and the lives and virtues of the first fathers, and wast a diligent emulator of their deeds: together with them do we now beatify thee, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

O Eustratius, beauty of martyrs,[^19] thou didst appear as a second Elijah the charioteer: but he was taken up as unto heaven, while thou into the very heavenly gates didst enter on a fiery chariot, a voice summoning thee from the majesty of glory, where with a voice of joy thou singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

O glorious and blessed fathers, Helladius and Luke the Steward, renowned in gracious miracles: having been good laborers of the commandments of the Master, ye have now received recompense from the torrent of delight, and joyfully sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
The Holy Spirit assists our spirit, O God-bearing fathers, for ye are the children of God and heirs of Christ; and Him Whom ye served, with Him are ye also glorified, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Holy Spirit came upon Thee, and the power of the Most High overshadowed Thee, showing Thee to be an honorable Mother even after childbearing: delivered through whom from the servitude of the stranger, all generations bless Thee, for the blessed God of our fathers hath beatified Thee!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin through fire: praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Those who lived upon the holy Cave hill, you, the great luminaries, O God-bearing fathers, whence ye stretched out rays of grace and illuminated the whole Ruthenian land with miracles: therefore we praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Not the stormy sea, but the holy cave reveals your relics *(Left page — Folio 6 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
relics as precious pearls, O favorites of God, and enrich with outpourings of miracles all who come unto you with faith, and supremely exalt the Lord with praises unto the ages.

Matthew the clairvoyant of things to come,[^20] and John, radiant above others in many sufferings, with Moses, the mirror of purity, as precious stones hidden in the cave, are revealed through miracles unto all who come unto them with faith, and supremely exalt the Lord with praises unto the ages.

Ephraim the Eunuch, a noetic dove,[^21] who brought the olive branch of the Studite Rule into this monastery, and who was exceedingly adorned with elder-like manners, together with Jeremiah the clairvoyant, let them be praised, who taught all to glorify the Lord unto the ages.

Mark the Grave-Digger was shown forth as a mirror unto lovers of labor,[^22] an instructor unto the repentant, a raiser of the dead: whose word they obeyed with fear as the roar of a lion; and even now he is dreadful unto demons, for he lives with the Lord unto the ages.

Gregory was shown forth as a wonderworker, not from Caesarea, but from the Pechersk Lavra,[^23] who through prayer held the thieves motionless with the fruits for three days and three nights; and having ended his life by drowning in the water, he now rejoices with the venerable Sergius by the waters of rest, singing unto the Lord unto the ages.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Glory unto Erasmus alone, who adorned the Pechersk church with icons, and unto Onesimus of renowned profit,[^24] unto whom is numbered Matthew, who in our day possessed the prophetic gift: in these three the name of the Trinity is glorified unto all ages.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Whosoever will not ever praise thee, O pure Virgin, is a stranger unto his salvation; for thy glory hath reached out unto the salvation of all who glorify thee, O Mary, Bride of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The childbearing of the Ever-Virgin revealed in fire and the bush upon the lawgiving mountain unto the salvation of us the faithful, let us magnify with unceasing hymns.

*Troparia:*
Upon the holy mountain, in the dark cave, ye sanctified your relics with the water of blessing, O most blessed fathers; ye made a paradise of God, and made it blossom with virtues and miracles as with fruitful trees, from which they that come with faith receive the fruits of healings, and magnify you with praises.

Spyridon, lily of guilelessness, and Nicodemus named after victory, both holy prosphora-bakers,[^25] making pure loaves as an offering unto God, also made themselves a sweet-smelling sacrifice; and unto them is Anatolius also numbered, whom we all magnify.

*(Right page — Folio 7 recto, running header: OF THE NEAR CAVES:)*
Angel-like Alipius with Gregory the Byzantine, most skilled iconographers,[^26] correct our unwritten deeds with the drops of good actions, that standing radiant in form before Christ, we may magnify you in glory.

Pimen the Faster, a fragrant blossom, and Ephraim the Priest, these are a good olive tree,[^27] anointing themselves with the gift of whose grace the faithful give thanks, and ever magnify the Creator of all with doxologies.

Onuphrius the lover of silence, and Isaiah, a desert-loving turtledove, with the blessed Sylvester, a threefold cord against the demons,[^28] having utterly strangled the passionate assaults brought on by them, possess the gifts to assist others also; therefore do all magnify them.

Equal-to-the-Apostles Vladimir, with divinely wise Roman and David, and with them Nikola Sviatosha, a foursome of Ruthenian princes is sung,[^29] and upon them as upon a noetic chariot God rested; therefore do all magnify them.

*Glory...*
The glory of martyrs, radiant Barbara, and Euphrosyne of Polotsk, a radiant pair, and Juliana, who pours out the oil of healing grace upon the infirm:[^30] for their sake, O Christ, Lover of mankind, save all who act righteously and who magnify Thee with worship.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O glory of the saints, most pure Virgin! Rejoice, O ship for those who wish to be saved in this tempest-tossed life! Rejoice, O rock that gave drink unto those thirsting for eternal blessings! This "Rejoice" do we all offer unto thee, and magnify thee with hymns.

*Exapostilarion:*
In songs of praise let us sing of the hierarchs of the Ruthenian land:[^31] among whom the foremost is Michael, Hilarion, Peter, Alexis, and Jonah; Photius and Cyprian, Ephraim, Nicholas, and Nikita; Macarius the Hieromartyr, Leontius, and Simon; and the whole pastoral assembly do we bring forward in prayer: for their sake, O Christ, save our souls.

*Theotokion:*
O true Mother of the spotless Lamb and the Chief Shepherd on high: I am a sheep of thy holy sheepfold, but by passionate actions I have turned aside into foul lawlessness; guide me, I pray thee, and save my soul from the noetic wolves.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
With one-minded soul and unanimous heart did ye agree together, O most blessed fathers, having cast away all worldly attachments and taken up your cross according unto the word of the Lord, to follow with voluntary poverty Christ God Who became poor for our sake: from Whom ye are now enriched with incorruptible *(Left page — Folio 7 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
treasures in the heavens: from which send down unto us also, that we too may be enriched, and through your intercession receive great mercy from the Giver of all good things.

Rejoice, O choir of God-gathered fathers, for having lived an angelic life with one soul in this world, ye made your dwelling in the dark cave, and having united yourselves unto God through many labors, ye received adoption from Him; but as standing before the Merciful One, look down mercifully upon us, and by your intercession propitiate Christ the Lover of mankind, that we may find remission of sins and obtain mercy on the day of judgment, we pray you as we fall down before you.

Today hath shone forth brighter than the sun the all-festal memorial of the God-bearing fathers, and illumines the assembly of feast-lovers with the rays of the grace of manifold miracles; therefore do we also, O most blessed ones, flee unto you with faith, and fall down before your relics, asking in our necessities for your swift visitation: despise not our supplication, but as having boldness before the Good One, entreat great mercy for us.

*Glory... Tone 8:*
The hymnographic gifts of great honors, O our most blessed fathers of the Caves, do we offer unto you with love, saying: Rejoice, O ornament of the Ruthenian land! Rejoice, O boast of monastics! Rejoice, O good armor-bearers of the Heavenly King! Rejoice, O radiant stars of the noetic firmament! Rejoice, O citizens of the Sion on high! Rejoice, O myrrh-fragrant flowers of paradise! Rejoice, O our protectors in afflictions and intercessors for salvation! And as having boldness before the Good One, pray diligently for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Lady, receive the supplications of thy servants...

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6 [LXX 115:6]).*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3 [LXX 115:3]).*
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 176 (2 Cor. 11:21–12:9).[^32]*
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he shall greatly delight (Ps. 111:1 [LXX 111:1]).*
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth (Ps. 111:2 [LXX 111:2]).*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matthew 11:27–30).[^33]*
*Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous; praise is becoming to the upright (Ps. 32:1 [LXX 32:1]).*

*(Right page — Folio 8 recto, full-width architectural woodcut border and engraving of Saint Michael, First Metropolitan of Kyiv, vested in sakkos and omophorion with miter, blessing with his right hand and holding a patriarchal cross-staff in his left, standing before the Kyiv Pechersk Lavra and city skyline; Cyrillic inscription around the halo: "Saint Michael of Christ, First Metropolitan of Kyiv":)[^34]*

*Month of September, on the 30th Day:*
*Service to our Father among the Saints, Michael, First Metropolitan of Kyiv and All Russia:*
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I Call," Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O Container of Ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*
Thee as first among the hierarchs doth the Ruthenian land crown with hymns, O venerable Michael, most blessed of the fathers of Christ, for thou didst first perfectly enlighten her with divine baptism; thou didst first bring the light of the Gospel into the darkness; thou didst first destroy the idol-sacrifices with the sign of the Cross, and thereby didst astonish the unbelieving people; and now as a preacher, standing with the first hierarchs before thy Master, pray diligently that all may obtain the unwaning Light, and receive great mercy from Christ God.

*(Left page — Folio 8 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
A gift truly brighter than the sun, higher than all honor, a most precious pearl that hath enriched the whole universe: the Gospel of Christ, unto the Ruthenian land held fast by the poverty of unbelief, didst thou bring from Tsargrad (Constantinople),[^35] and didst grant this unto her; adorned wherewith as with golden raiment, like a beautiful virgin she stands before Christ in the faith, and delights in gladness in the heavenly bridal chamber; where standing, O Hierarch Michael, pray that the heirs of thy preaching may receive great mercy from Christ God.

Borne along by the gentle winds of help from on high, like a winged and swift-flying eagle carrying the Gospel of Christ, Thou didst come into the Ruthenian land; and the birds of deceitful ravens that nested there under the shadow of idolatry Thou didst utterly crush by thy divine teaching; and having tamed by marvelous miracles those who were tossed about, and having nourished with the evangelical word those who were exhausted by spiritual famine, Thou didst lead them unto Christ: unto Whom pray, O hierarch, to grant great mercy unto all.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Let us, O ye people, praise Michael the hierarch, a true witness and preacher of the Gospel of Christ; for being a disciple of Christ his Teacher, sent unto an unbelieving people, he came like an apostle and fulfilled the prophecy of the First-Called;[^36] having captured the deceived ones by a marvelous sign, and having converted them, he brought them unto God; and now standing before the throne of the Most High, he prays for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the Tone.*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And 3 Readings for the Hierarch (see fol. 151).[^37]*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
At thy coming, O holy one, the Ruthenian land was made glad, and the glorious city of Kyiv piously celebrates, having been enlightened by divine baptism; those who formerly offered sacrifices unto demons, behold, they now bring their souls as an offering in purity of heart, having thee as a preceding intercessor and pleader for all who honor thy memorial.

*Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice (Ps. 131:9 [LXX 131:9]).*
At thy coming, O most blessed Michael, like unto John's coming, [the demons] cry aloud with sobbing: "Woe to our wretchedness with our cunning! We who were previously honored with the veneration of majesty and worshipped by all as good, how do we now endure mockery from a single stranger, who bears the image of the marvelous Jesus Who grants renewal unto the whole world!"[^38]

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6 [LXX 115:6]).*
By the anointing of thy spiritual chrism, O Father Michael, the most glorious city of Kyiv is blessed and receives the seal of adoption; and those who had fallen under the noetic slayer, darkened by the darkness of idols, being enlightened by the oil of baptism, come unto the light of the knowledge of God as children of light and a newly chosen people.

*(Right page — Folio 9 recto, running header: TO MICHAEL, METROPOLITAN OF KYIV:)*
*Glory... Tone 6:*
The Divine Spirit, desiring to choose a new people for Himself, first brought unto divine knowledge Vladimir, great among princes; and he, illumined with noetic brightness from grace from on high in the holy font, received thee as shepherd through a revelation that took place, and brought thee into Rus', unto the sheep that had strayed in unbelief; whom, having washed with water by thy teaching and baptism, thou didst lead into the fold of the sheep of Christ; standing before Whose throne with them today, remember us who perform thy memorial on this day, O Michael, first-enthroned hierarch of Rus'!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true vine...

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Today the prophecy of the First-Called among the Apostles hath been fulfilled:[^39] for behold, upon these hills grace hath shone forth and faith hath multiplied; and they who were grown old through unbelief were reborn through the divine font, and became a people of renewal, a royal priesthood, a holy nation, the flock of Christ, unto which thou wast shown forth as the first shepherd, having first ministered through baptism. And now, standing before the Master, Christ God, pray that all the sons of Rus' may be saved: for thou hast boldness, as a hierarch and sacred minister of God!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The Mystery hidden from all ages...

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*
Thou wast a chosen vessel, O Hierarch Michael, to bear the name of Christ before unbelieving men, unto whom thou wast sent in the Ruthenian land; whom having enlightened with holy baptism, thou didst guide unto the knowledge of God by thy teachings: by these guide us also into the path of the commandments of the Lord, as we pray thee, O most blessed favorite of God!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O golden-wrought cup, most pure Virgin, in which the wine of our salvation was mingled by the Holy Spirit: cheer our hearts with this, for thou art the cup that ladles out eternal joy unto us!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word Co-Unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
The Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit was brought by thee, O hierarch, into the Ruthenian land, Whom thou didst preach without doubting with thy mouth, rebuking the madness of idols, and didst lead a new people into the heritage of the Lord; with whom as thou standest, O sacred initiate of mysteries and minister of God, ever pray for the souls of those who sing of thine honorable falling asleep, O father!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As being the true Mother of the Word co-unoriginate, for us who are to give an account in the hour of judgment *(Left page — Folio 9 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
word in the hour of judgment, stand before Him and entreat thy Son with thy fervent supplications: for we know that thy maternal prayers can do much, and for their sake we hope to find mercy on the day of dread trial.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Leaning upon the Breast" (Возле́гъ на пе́рси):*
As unto the leader of hierarchs, and planter of the faith of Christ in Rus', destroyer of the evil belief in idols, and shepherd of a newly chosen people, we offer the first-fruits of hymns unto thee, O most blessed Hierarch Michael, performing thy memorial on this present day, and crying unto thee with love: as standing before the throne of the Most High, pray that remission of transgressions be granted unto those who piously bow before thine honorable tomb.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As the true consolation of all who are in sorrow, and right guidance for those who have strayed from the true path, speedily stand before us, O most pure Mother of God, and deliver from calamity those who are severely wronged; for thou canst do all things that thou willest, having boldness before thy Son and God: unto Whom pray, that He grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who honor thy childbearing in a manner worthy of thy pious name.

*Songs of Ascents, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6 [LXX 115:6]).*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3 [LXX 115:3]).*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 36 (John 10:9–16).[^40]*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6: Let us, O ye people, praise Michael the hierarch... (seek on "Lord, I Call," Glory).*

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos, to 6; and of the Saint, to 8.*
*Tone 6, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, and beholding the pursuer Pharaoh engulfed, Israel cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*
As a hierarch and minister of God, standing before the throne of the Most High with the choirs of the saints in heaven, pray for us who minister unto Him with spiritual hymns, that we may stand with thee where the gladness of the righteous is.

As one chosen unto the Lord for the ministry of His glory, unto the work of the Gospel of Christ, pray for us who are gathered at thy memorial, O Hierarch Michael, that we may labor in the evangelical commandments, and receive the reward, the gladness of the venerable.

Standing like a lamp upon the lampstand, thou didst shine forth far and wide with the light of thy good deeds, so that

*(Right page — Folio 10 recto, running header: TO MICHAEL, METROPOLITAN OF KYIV:)*
even the unbelieving people, through thine instruction, O father, beheld the light of the vision of God, and the country enlightened through thee rejoices.

*Theotokion:*
O thou who didst manifest unto the world from thy womb the true Light: illuminate us who walk in the darkness of passions, and guide us into the way, O Mary, Bride of God, who didst bring forth the Way for the Christian race!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*
Faith was not planted in Rus', nor was piety rooted, until thou, O Hierarch Michael, as a most wise husbandman, having destroyed the thicket of polytheism, didst sow the fruitful seed of the knowledge of God.

With light from on high was great Vladimir illuminated, who brought thee, O holy hierarch of God, as a guiding dawn into the Ruthenian land darkened by idolatry, that there also the people might be enlightened through thy coming, and come to the knowledge of God through the recognition of the true faith.

The Sun of righteousness, Christ God, sent thee, O Michael, as a radiant ray unto the Ruthenian land: which having enlightened with the light of the knowledge of the divine faith, and cleansed with the washing of regeneration, thou didst bring together as a new people, renewed by the good confession of faith.

*Theotokion:*
The Sun of righteousness, Christ our true God, having borrowed flesh from thee, shone forth upon the world and utterly consumed the corruption of polytheism; O dwelling of Light, Virgin Mary, illuminate me who am darkened by despondency, and guide me into the path of well-doing!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
By the teaching of the evangelical instruction thou didst cut down the sculpted idols, and cast the shrines down to the earth, planting piety: adorned wherewith, the city of Kyiv cries unto thee with love: Rejoice, O Michael, first-enthroned archbishop of Rus'!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Deliver from barbarian servitude and from the wounds of alien bondage those who sing unto thee: "Rejoice!"; and having crushed in our minds the passionate thoughts, plant the knowledge of God in the fear of God; for thou art the cause, beginning, and fulfillment of all good.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying out with a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparia:*
The idols, sensing thee, O holy hierarch, to be the servant of Christ and a pious builder, wept bitterly over their destruction; but the people renewed by baptism were glad, celebrating the feast of the hierarch in the Lord.

When thou, O father, didst come unto the hills of Kyiv, the foremost idol, Perun, was cast down, dragged by horses, torn to pieces in the whirlwinds and winds;[^44] but the people of renewal were glad, celebrating in the Lord.

*(Left page — Folio 10 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
Every sex and age of people formerly darkened came in haste to the river for enlightenment; and there was a countless multitude having thee as their leader: whom having ministered unto with the bath of baptism, thou didst guide unto the knowledge of God, O Michael, hierarch of God!

*Theotokion:*
All generations bless thee, O Mary, Queen of all; and we, beatifying thee with hymns, bow down before thy face depicted on the icon, celebrating the feast of the hierarch in the memorial of thy holy one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate the souls of those who keep early vigil for Thee with love, I pray Thee, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest us forth from the gloom of sin.

*Troparia:*
Illumined with the divine light, the newly baptized people cried out with thee, O Michael: O Christ God, grant us to know Thee, the only Word of God, Who deliverest us from the gloom of demonic idolatry!

The walls of Jericho fell when the Ark was carried around by Joshua the son of Nun;[^41] and the idols also fell when the Cross was carried before thee, O holy hierarch: and the people glorified the Word of God, Thee, O Christ, Who deliverest men from the gloom of unbelief.

The city of Kyiv is adorned even to this day, clothed by thee, O holy hierarch, with the garment of baptism; and it wears upon its neck, like a golden chain,[^42] the honorable Gospel of Christ: which kissing, we glorify the Word of God, Who delivers us from the darkness of sin.

*Theotokion:*
We all exult in thy beauty, O Mary, Bride of God, for thou art truly fair; and the Most High, having desired thy beauty, borrowed flesh from thee; and having become man, for thy sake, O pure Virgin, He deified us who had fallen into corruption.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*Troparia:*
The foul sea of demonic idolatry, like Moses with his glorious rod, thou didst cut through with the Cross, O Hierarch Michael; and having washed the people with the water of the knowledge of God, thou didst lead them unto the haven, singing: Glory to Thee, O Christ, Most Merciful!

The Ruthenian land that formerly strayed, being unstable, hath now through thee, O Hierarch Michael, been guided and come unto the knowledge of God; and having received the ring of adoption, it rejoices, crying aloud: Glory to Thee, O my Christ, Most Merciful!

Having tamed in the Ruthenian land the people who raged with unbelief, and laid upon them the yoke of obedience to the Church, thou didst bring many laborers, O Michael, into the vineyard of the Heavenly Master: make us also, O holy one, laborers even at the eleventh hour, that we may receive the reward with the last.

*Theotokion:*
Tame the passions raging within me, and lay upon me the yoke of humility, O Theotokos, that therein I may labor for the rest of my time, and find

*(Right page — Folio 11 recto, running header: TO MICHAEL, METROPOLITAN OF KYIV:)*
mercy on the day of judgment.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Thou wast shown forth as a second Moses of Rus', O father,[^43] having transplanted the noetic vineyard from Egyptian idolatry into the land foreseen through prophecy, concerning which it may fitly be said: the confirmation of the faith shall be upon the earth, and upon the tops of the hills of Kyiv the fruit that nourishes the whole world shall be exalted above Lebanon; tasting whereof, we beatify thee, O Michael, holy hierarch of God!

*Ikos:*
Having gathered the scattered people into one understanding of the knowledge of God, and having washed with baptism the oldness of the ignorance of the Ruthenian land, thou didst enlighten it with the Cross; and having taught them to glorify the Most Holy Trinity, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, thou didst unite a rational flock unto Christ God: standing before Whom in unshaken faith unto this day, it cries aloud: Deliver from every evil adversity the Empress, city, and people who beatify thee, O Michael, holy hierarch of God!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Angel made the furnace pour forth dew for the venerable children, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Through the dew-giving grace in thee, O holy hierarch, Rus' was washed with water, and the scales of its noetic eyes fell away at the holy font; and having recognized its only Creator, it cries with faith: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

When the glorious city of Kyiv rejoiced after baptism, the foul idols, among whom the foremost was Perun, being dragged along, wept bitterly; whence the people, recognizing their former deception, cried out with thee, O Michael: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

A golden ring of new grace didst thou place on the hand of Rus', O Hierarch Michael, and betrothed the bride of Christ, whence the Church was shown forth; which rejoicing spiritually even unto this day, cries: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
We call thee a golden ring, O pure Virgin, adorned as with a precious diamond by the union of the Godhead with flesh borrowed from thee for Christ; therefore with love do we cry: Blessed art thou alone, who didst beforehand adorn the unseemliness of our nature!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one with water: for Thou doest all things, O Christ, solely by willing them. Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Snatching the Ruthenian land most wisely out of the flame of the deceit of idols, thou didst refresh it with the dew of baptism, and once already by the turning back of the flame upon Perun; supremely exalting Christ, and beatifying Thee, its shepherd, unto the ages.

Having well understood all things concerning *(Left page — Folio 11 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
the newly chosen people, thou didst appoint pastors and teachers throughout the cities; and having entrusted that newly chosen flock unto them, thou didst depart, to exist unto the ages with Christ God, the Chief of Shepherds.

Great weeping and lamentation was made in the city of Kyiv at thy departure unto God, O father; but the Jerusalem on high, receiving thee into its bosom, was glad; where living, forget not thy flock, and pray that it may rejoice with thee unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
Much gladness and joy do I work for demons by evil deeds, bringing sorrow upon my guardian angel; behold, O pure Mary, Mother of Christ God, guide me unto repentance, that there may be weeping for them, but joy for my angel, and for me with him unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate was made manifest unto men, Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we beatify thee.

*Troparia:*
As a palm tree hast thou blossomed in the house of God, O father, and the incorruption of thy relics reveals the fruits unto all;[^45] whence we, having understood that thou standest before Christ, magnify thee with hymns.

Like a fragrant cypress thy relics breathe sweet fragrance, O holy hierarch, and give delight unto those who bow before thy shrine; ever fill them with the sweet fragrance of thy prayers, that they may faithfully glorify thy memory with hymns.

O choir of the hierarchs of Rus', and assemblies formed like choirs, eagerly gather at the holy shrine of the first-placed shepherd, and ye, O people, honor your father with hymns, all together beatifying his memory!

*Theotokion:*
All creation sings hymns unto thee, O Virgin, who didst pass through all the heavenly gates and didst sit at the right hand of the fiery throne of the Most High: make us also worthy to stand at the right hand of thy Son, as with hymns we magnify Him Who was born of thee, and thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
Before all others dost thou stand, O Hierarch Michael, and thy head is crowned with a blessing from the right hand of the Most High; stretch out thy hand and bless us, as once thou didst walk beforehand toward the knowledge of God; for thou art not dead, but livest unto the ages; therefore doth the Ruthenian Church sing of thy memorial with hymns, in which we also glorify thee.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of the King of the ages, Queen and Lady, show forth the kings who sing of thee to be rulers over passions; for behold, we bring forward unto thee in prayer the marvelous Hierarch Michael, in whose memory celebrating, we magnify thee.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Today thy memorial is celebrated, O holy hierarch, by Kyiv, the most renowned mother of cities, and the Ruthenian people

*(Right page — Folio 12 recto, running header: TO MICHAEL, METROPOLITAN OF KYIV:)*
honor thy translation unto eternal life with praises; for thou didst depart unto the heavenly ones, yet didst not abandon looking upon the earthly ones, as an unceasing intercessor and pleader for our souls.

Beginning the first rotation of the new year,[^46] as unto thee, O blessed one, the first and chief hierarch of the Ruthenian land, we offer the sheaves of our first hymns: receive them, therefore, as an honorable gift, and reward us with thy blessing, as ever being a pleader for our souls.

Sweetened with the desire of the love of Christ, Thou didst take up noetic wings, flying from the lower things unto the higher; and the gates of paradise were opened unto thee, entering into which Thou delightest in the vision of God: with that sweetness cheer the souls of those who sing of thy memory, O Hierarch Michael!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
By the path of those spiritual anointings, O most blessed Michael, thou wast like a fruitful olive tree in the house of God; and sweetened by Thy fruits, Basil, great among kings,[^47] received thee with love for the governance of the Ruthenian land; which having cleansed from idolatry, thou didst lead unto the knowledge of God; therefore, worthily performing Thy memorial, and falling down before the shrine of Thy relics, we pray: ask from Christ God strength in reign for the Empress, peace and good stability for the holy people, and for all remission of sins and great mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save from perils thy servants, O Theotokos...

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the Hierarch.*

*(Left page — Folio 12 verso, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: Saint Damian the Presbyter and Healer, haloed and vested in epitrachelion, standing in prayer before an icon of the Mother of God; in the lower register, Saint Damian lies upon his sickbed while an angel in the likeness of Saint Theodosius appears to promise him the Kingdom of Heaven; Cyrillic inscriptions: "Venerable Damian, Presbyter and Healer" and "Unto Damian came an Angel in the likeness of Theodosius promising him the Kingdom":)[^48]*

---

# MONTH OF OCTOBER

*On the 5th Day:*
*Commemoration of our Venerable Fathers: Damian the Presbyter, Matthew, and Jeremiah the Clairvoyant:*
*At Vespers, on "Lord, I Call," Stichera, to 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Being held fast by spiritual and bodily sicknesses, through the oil of thy mercy, as possessing sacred anointing from on high, O venerable Damian, speedily heal me who cry unto thee from the heart; and through fervent prayers unto God grant humility unto my heart, and cleanse the festering wounds of my soul, that with pure

*(Right page — Folio 13 recto, running header: TO DAMIAN, JEREMIAH, AND MATTHEW:)*
lips I may offer praise unto God Who is glorified in His saints.

Having illuminated thy soul with the grace of the Most Holy Spirit, O all-lauded Jeremiah,[^49] thou didst receive the gift from God to foretell future things as though present; and imitating the ancient prophet, thou didst unerringly predict all things, and didst foresee the secrets of the human heart: with thine illumination enlighten our hearts also, and ask great mercy from Christ God!

Thou didst follow Matthew, the preacher of the Word of God, O marvelous one;[^50] for as he boldly rebuked the disobedient Egyptian people, so also thou, O blessed one, didst put to shame the cunningly woven wiles of the subterranean wicked Ethiopians; and now we pray thee, O father: drive away the demonic legions that noetically darken our minds, and deliver our souls from afflictions!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Come, O ye people, let us honor with praises the three most blessed fathers, three protectors: Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew; and let us offer doxology unto God Who sits on high, Who hath given His favorites unto the Ruthenian land for help and as pleaders for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*At the Aposticha, Glory... Tone 6:*
Bright and radiant hath your memorial shone forth today, O our all-blessed fathers, for ye brightly shine with the rays of miracles upon those who come unto you and ask fervent intercession from you; therefore do we also fall down before your relics, eagerly kissing them, and we glorify God Who gave you such gifts, wherewith enrich us also, and ask great mercy from Christ!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having enlightened your hearts with the light of the commandments of Christ, ye drove away the darkness of passions; therefore did ye become the dwelling of the Trinity, O thrice-numbered fathers: Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew; having received grace from Whom, ye heal the sick, foretell things to come, and as partakers with the angels, pray Christ God to grant us a portion with the saints!

*Canon, Tone 5, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto God our Savior, Who guided the people in the sea with dry feet, and engulfed Pharaoh with all his host, unto Him alone let us sing: for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*
Let us offer praise unto God our Savior, glorifying His most blessed favorite Damian, *(Left page — Folio 13 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
the wise physician of the sick, and let us cry aloud unto Him Who gave such gifts: for He is glorified!

Thou didst imitate the angel-like life of thine instructor with unceasing zeal; instructed by whom, thou wast adorned with virtue, unceasingly remaining without sleep in doxology through all nights, and didst bless God: for He is glorified!

Standing now with the prophets before God the Savior, as possessing boldness, pray, O divinely inspired Jeremiah, that hearts may be illumined with grace, of those who beatify thee with faith and cry unto the Savior: for He is glorified!

*Glory...*
The all-seeing eye of God, beholding thy pure heart, O blessed Matthew, gave thee the gift to know the secrets of men, and to turn them unto profit for all, that guided by thee we may sing unto God: for He is glorified!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Being the noetic Sun, O pure Virgin, illuminate our minds darkened by passions and pleasures, that enlightened by thee, we may sing unto God: for He is glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my understanding, that I may sing and glorify Thy saving Ascension.

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by the power of God, thou wast meek and silent, O blessed one, ever with fervent prayers unto God, and didst occupy thyself with the diligent reading of the Holy Scriptures, and thereby wast established in the Lord.

The Word of God was thy food and drink, wherewith thou didst nourish thy soul, O venerable one, and thy body with bread only and water; and on this didst thou feed until the day of thy death, subjecting unto the spirit all the passions that arose.

Having fulfilled thy years in ripe old age, O Jeremiah, thou didst receive the gift to foresee the secrets of the heart, and didst rebuke in private the evil thoughts of men, and didst instruct all those who were influenced by the counsel of the devil.

*Glory...*
Having come unto the first fathers of the Caves, thou didst settle with them, and lived in monastic life conquering all carnal lusts; therefore didst thou receive the gift from God to see visibly the faces of the subterranean Ethiopians, O marvelous Matthew!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Terrify and drive away by the visitation of thy countenance, O Theotokos, the wicked Ethiopians who transform themselves into various forms and act within our members, that without confusion we may sing of thy saving power!

*(Right page — Folio 14 recto, running header: TO DAMIAN, JEREMIAH, AND MATTHEW:)*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Having received twofold grace from God, Thou healest twofold passions: spiritual ones through diligent prayers, and bodily ones through the anointing of holy oil; heal us who suffer from both sicknesses, and drive away the passions that oppress us, and pray Christ God that eternal life may be obtained by those who honor thy holy falling asleep.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
All-spotless Bride, spacious dwelling of the Word of God: cleanse me who am a foul dwelling of passions, and show me forth as a bright habitation of virtues; drive away the darkness of lawlessness, and make me worthy of the communion of the unwaning Light through thy prayers, that I may sing of thine ineffable mercy!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard the report of the power of the Cross, how paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Thou wast shown forth as a sweet-smelling cluster of the vineyard of the Mother of God, watered with the dew of the teachings of the venerable fathers, and didst distill the wine of healings unto all who flee unto thee with faith, O Damian, minister of God!

Protected by the armor of faith and the flame of love, O blessed one, thou didst drive away the enemies who harm men with sicknesses; for thy diligent prayer, passing through the clouds, drove away all diseases, O marvelous Damian!

As a clairvoyant of the thoughts of men, thou didst foretell those who would turn their backs to the enemy and who desired to depart from this holy sheepfold; and though rebuking them with a sharp word, O Jeremiah, thou didst teach them to stand firmly in the battle and be unshaken against the enemies.

*Glory...*
With open eyes thou didst visibly behold once in the church a demon walking about in the likeness of a soldier and throwing onto the hems of the brethren's robes sticky flowers, that they might depart before the blessing, darkened by sleep:[^51] those, O father, didst thou instruct to stand unsluggishly and protect themselves with the weapon of the Cross!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Surrounded by demonic legions and wounded by the passions that flatter the heart, strengthen me, O Mother of God, and grant me grace to conquer their deceitful wiles, that I may flee under thy pure protection!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Keeping early vigil we cry unto Thee, O Lord: save us, for Thou art our God, and we know none other beside Thee.

*Troparia:*
Thy blessed instructor Theodosius commanded thee to pray over those who came in common sickness; and thou, being humble and well-obedient, *(Left page — Folio 14 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
considered himself unworthy of the gift of healing; but fulfilling the command of his father, through prayer and oil he granted health unto the sick.

As a good traveler, thou didst well traverse the path of this life, confirming thyself with monastic ascetic labors; and when thou didst attain unto thine end, having cleansed with sickness that which was lacking, purer than gold didst thou depart into painless rest.

By the foresight given unto thee by God, whatsoever thou didst see, O blessed one, thou didst tell truly unto every man, and thy word was fulfilled in truth; therefore, as the glory of prophets and companion of the venerable, we worthily beatify thee, O Jeremiah!

*Glory...*
Never didst thou leave the church before the conclusion of the singing, but wast always the last, having an insatiable desire to abide in the doxology of God; for this reason God glorified thee before angels and men, O God-bearing Matthew!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Knowing thee, O pure Virgin, to be the holy mountain of God and the house of the glory of the Most High, falling down in humility, I pray: lead me up unto the height of virtuous life, and make me a pure temple for the reception of divine gifts!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The abyss encompassed me, the sea-monster was a tomb for me; but I cried unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Having walked the straight paths upon earth, at thy departure thou didst mingle prayers unto the Crown-Giver with tears, asking His goodness that He deprive thee not of the Kingdom of Heaven and of dwelling together with thy father, the venerable Theodosius.[^52]

Desiring not to be deprived of communion with the venerable in the unapproachable Light, with humility didst thou send up supplications unto Christ, and didst obtain that which thou didst ask: for the wise Creator informed thee with a marvelous assurance, that with good hope thou mightest depart to rest in the repose of the righteous.

In deep old age thy youth was spiritually renewed, O venerable Jeremiah; for having left the old bodily covering, thou didst enter with thy soul into a new dwelling, unto the ancient prophets for the vision of the glory of God.

*Glory...*
Sitting once beneath the church semantron (bilo),[^53] thou didst behold a great crowd of dark demons; and having questioned them, thou didst learn that they were going unto a certain brother; for this reason thou thyself didst lovingly instruct many of the brethren how to beware of them: teach us also, O blessed one, that they may not enter

*(Right page — Folio 15 recto, running header: TO DAMIAN, JEREMIAH, AND MATTHEW:)*
into the doors of our hearts!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Deliver me, O Good One, from the demonic whispering of passions, that their deceitful darkness may not enter into the house of my soul; bar the noetic doors of the heart with fear, and grant me to be vigilant and attend unto my salvation!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
As the ancient prophets, having received the grace of prophecy, unerringly foretold in the Spirit the things that were to come, so also ye in our life foretold the evil things that come upon us because of our sins: and now, that evils may not overtake us, ask from Christ God the correction of our deeds and great mercy!

*Ikos:*
Imitating those whose names ye bear, and having lived in an angelic manner upon the earth, ye were counted worthy of great gifts, O Jeremiah and Matthew; for foretelling things to come, ye led many unto correction, and astonished all men by the putting to shame [of the demons]; and now living after this temporal life in the endless life with the angelic choirs, ask from Christ God the correction of our deeds and great mercy!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
He Who saved the children that sang hymns in the furnace of fire: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
An angel of God stood before thee, O holy Damian, in the likeness of the venerable Theodosius, and falling upon thy breast, lovingly kissed thee as one beloved of God and marvelous even unto the angels; and thou didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

He who appeared said: "Behold, child, concerning what thou didst pray unto the Lord, I am sent to announce unto thee that according unto thy petition it shall be unto thee, and thou shalt dwell in the Kingdom of God with the saints": hearing these words, thou didst joyfully sing, O Damian: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

What is sweeter, and what is greater, than to live together with the saints, where Christ lives in the unwaning Light? There also a place of rest was prepared for thee, O Damian; pray that we also who sing may obtain it: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

In the choir with the saints, in honor with the prophets, in standing with the angels, thou wast counted worthy, O Jeremiah, to be appointed by God: unto Whom pray that we also may stand before Him, purely singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
Ever having love toward the brethren, O Matthew, whatsoever was revealed unto thee thou didst disclose for the benefit of all, and didst instruct them, coming unto their cells, to weep over their sins, *(Left page — Folio 15 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
and to sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Delivered through thee from all demonic wiles, O most pure one, unceasingly unto Him Who was born of thee do we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who of the Father before the ages was begotten as Son and God, and in the last times was incarnate of the Virgin Mother, praise, O ye priests, and supremely exalt Him, O ye people, unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Recognizing him who appeared unto thee, sent from the Father of lights, to be thy God-bearing instructor Theodosius, having summoned him, thou didst inquire concerning what he who stood there said, and what he promised thee, O Damian; and he was astonished at what was told, and supremely exalted the Lord unto the ages.

With joy didst thou question the promise from thine instructor and father, saying: "Shall it be, O father, as thou didst promise me upon my petition?" And he answered: "I know not, child, whereof thou questionest me; for an angel of God was he who promised thee, and he shall settle thee there where the saints sing unto the Lord unto the ages."
