Being joyful and of good hope from such an assurance, thou didst depart unto the light-bearing places, escorted by light-bearing angels, with whom thou also singest unto the Lord unto the ages.

All thy knowledge was for instruction unto those being saved, O venerable Matthew, nor did the superior hide thine instruction: awakened wherewith, he quickly attained unto beatitude, where the saints supremely exalt the Lord unto the ages.

*Glory...*
After many revelations didst thou depart in deep old age, O Matthew, to stand with unveiled face before the throne of God: pray for us also, O father, that with thee we may be counted worthy to see the Lord and supremely exalt Him with praises unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
He Who is one in essence with the Father, God the Word, took up His abode in thy pure womb, O Virgin, and was shown forth consubstantial with us in the flesh: Whom blessing, we supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee who above mind and word didst ineffably bring forth in time the timeless Mother of God, with one mind do we the faithful magnify.

*Troparia:*
What shall we call thee, O blessed place? A heavenly firmament, where the Trinity of fathers shine like stars with miracles, and enlighten those who celebrate their honorable memory and piously beatify them with hymns!

Having departed from the temporal light unto the eternal light, with your light-bearing rays illuminate our hearts; and ask through your prayers that walking in the light of the commandments of the Lord, with you we may behold the unwaning Light!

*(Right page — Folio 16 recto, running header: TO DAMIAN, JEREMIAH, AND MATTHEW:)*
Having received the grace of healings from God, speedily heal us who are held fast by passions; for with faith we flee unto your relics, and praying, magnify you with hymns!

*Glory...*
Let us glorify God, Who hath glorified His saints, Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew, who having lived in their bodies like angels glorified Him; Whom we also pray, that He may not deprive us of His heavenly glory together with them, who today crown their memory with praises!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Remembering my end, when the time shall come for me to be loosed from the bonds of the flesh, I tremble, for the day of judgment approaches: from which who shall deliver me, if not thou, O pure Virgin? Therefore do I ever flee unto thee in doxology!

*Exapostilarion:*
O fathers who lived upon earth, ye are holy in heaven also, and stand with the angelic choirs before God: having received from Him recompense for your labors, forget us not, but guiding us, direct us into the right way, that walking therein we may find mercy on the day of judgment!

*Theotokion:*
Behold, thy temple is here, and truly marvelous;[^54] for being thyself the temple of the glory of God, by thy providence thou didst build it and didst adorn it with the angel-like life of the holy fathers for the sake of our way of life: transform our deformity into goodliness, that the King may desire the beauty of our souls, and pour out His great mercy upon us!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Ye became a radiant receptacle of the Most Holy Spirit, O all-blessed fathers, and enlightened by Him, in a manner worthy of the vision of God ye were enriched with the gift of prophecy; ye acquired foreknowledge of things not yet come to pass, and true understanding: therefore we pray you: pray for us unto Jesus the Lover of mankind, and the Savior of our souls!

Like true prophets, by unerring indications ye foretold all things that were to come, and proclaimed them, O venerable fathers: Matthew, and Jeremiah with marvelous Damian, by deed *(Left page — Folio 16 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE OF THE CAVES:)*
for the Holy Spirit knows how to enlighten pure hearts, and to grant the grace of revelation: therefore do we glorify Him Who glorified you, Jesus the Lover of mankind, and the Savior of our souls.

Kindled with divine love and with desire for the blessings to come, with affection ye traversed the narrow path, counting its afflictions as nothing; therefore now rejoicing together in refreshment, unto those who honor you with love, grant a portion of your gifts, and pray for us, O Damian and Jeremiah with Matthew, unto Jesus the Lover of mankind, and the Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Gathered together, O ye faithful, let us beatify the trinity of venerable fathers: the one as having received the gift of healings, the other as being filled with true prophecy, and the third as perceiving things invisible; honoring the day of whose festal triumph, we glorify our God, and pray that He make peace in the world, and save our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true vine...

*(Right page — Folio 17 recto, woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Nikola Sviatosha, Prince of Chernihiv; inscribed above: "Ven: Nikola, SVIATOSHA PRINCE OF CHERNIHIV"; the saint is depicted haloed and vested in monastic habit holding a water-bucket and vessel before the monastery gate, church, and garden; below are two narrative panels inscribed: "The Calling of the Venerable Nikola Sviatosha" and "The Saint does not enter into strife with the enemy at his appointment":)[^55]*

*On the 14th Day:*
*Commemoration of our Venerable Father Nikola Sviatosha, Prince of Chernihiv:*
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the first antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I Call," Stichera, to 6, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O divinely wise, blessed Nikola, seeing that the fashion of this world passes away, and that all things are subject to corruption, thou didst quickly exchange the image of thy glory; thou didst forsake diadem and scepter, and looking toward the heavenly kingdom, didst choose to walk the narrow path; and now *(Left page — Folio 17 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
dwelling in the heavenly mansions, pray Christ God that we may obtain the blessedness of the eternal kingdom!

O Venerable Nikola, desiring to acquire a life corresponding unto thy name, thou didst conquer this world; thou didst forsake the throne of princely rule, counting wealth, glory, and earthly happiness as refuse; clothed in the poor monastic habit, thou didst follow Christ Who became poor for our sake: standing before Whom now, pray fervently for us, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

O Father Nikola, father most blessed, foreseeing with the eye of thine understanding that kings and princes are deprived of their glory and depart naked into the tomb, thou didst cast away the matching honor of the authority of this age, following the footsteps of Christ, for unto Him in humility didst thou labor: therefore thou didst obtain unfading blessedness, wherein remember us also who honor thy memory!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Foreseeing, O Christ God, that Thy dominion abides unto the ages, Thy favorite Nikola cast away scepter and diadem, and distanced himself from manifold cares, running unto the tranquil life of those who practice monasticism: he was a true and faithful servant, having ministered unto Thee his Savior in holiness and righteousness.

*Both now... Dogmatikon: The shadow of the Law passed away... Then, the Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And 3 Readings for the Venerable One: see folio 30 verso.[^56]*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Kindled with divine desire, O venerable Father Nikola, thou didst despise worldly glory, and loved monastic life, becoming of one zeal with the venerable fathers: dwelling together with them now, pray for those who honor thy translation!

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord (Ps. 111:1 [LXX 111:1]).*
Thou wast wholly enslaved unto Christ, directing all thy desire toward Him; for the sake of His love thou didst renounce this world, loving Him Who loved the human race: therefore with hymns we beatify thee, and with love bow down before the shrine of thy relics.

*Verse: Blessed are all they that fear the Lord (Ps. 127:1 [LXX 127:1]).*
Deprive not of thine intercession, O father, all those who reign now from thy race, and the people who praise thee; but stand before all as a fellow-helper, and assist in afflictions and tribulations, granting profitable things through thy prayers!

*(Right page — Folio 18 recto, running header: TO NIKOLA SVIATOSHA:)*
*Glory... Tone 5:*
O venerable father, thou didst not give rest unto thy body until thou didst cleanse the passions of thy heart; fulfilling the commandments of Christ, thou didst leave house, kinsmen, and friends; running unto the tranquil haven of the Mother of God, thou didst subject thy greatness and glory under the feet of all; shining forth in fasting, thou didst mortify the body, and being ever guided by the Spirit of God, thou wast shown forth as a son and heir of the unwaning Light: where living now, pray that we also may obtain mercy on the day of judgment!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who art full of grace, intercede through thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions, and cleansing of our many transgressions, we pray thee!

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion, Tone 8:*
Having left the homeland and glory of thy princely rule, a prince of humility, thou didst fervently follow Christ, O our venerable father Nikola;[^57] therefore thou didst receive from Him the eternal kingdom and glory in heaven, where keeping festival, remember us who faithfully honor thy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: All beyond understanding...*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The Soldiers Standing Guard" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
The Ruthenian people are filled with gladness, and joyfully sing of thy memory, O blessed one, having thee as a swift helper in afflictions and deliverance in all diseases: therefore, as one who received from God the grace to drive away sicknesses by a word, cease not even now to assist all who honor thy venerable falling asleep!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Knowing thee to be a fountain of healings, O Virgin, we thy sinful servants flee unto the warmth of thy heart; and possessing natural care for us, forget not those who are tossed about by tribulations, but stretching out thy hands, pray unto thy Son, that we may receive swift relief from them!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Having hated that which passes away in the light upon earth, O blessed one, and loved from the heart that which is ever-existing in heaven, these things after thy many labors didst thou inherit: where living, pray unto Christ for all of us, O venerable one, who in hymns honor thy memory!

*(Left page — Folio 18 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy holy childbearing, O Virgin, thou didst renew the nature of the earthborn decayed by passions, and didst raise it up from death unto incorruptible life: therefore as is due, O pure Mother, we ever beatify thee, as blessed throughout all generations of the faithful.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was Mystically Commanded" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*
Let thy holy forefather rejoice today, the autocratic Vladimir sitting on the height of the throne, for from his root thou didst sprout as a good branch, O most blessed Nikola, bearing upon thyself fitting fruit, as an image unto all who desire to be saved and in humility to follow Christ, even as thou didst do, O venerable father; and now as they rejoice in thy glory, the fruit of thy monastic labors abides unto the ages!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Let David the hymnographer rejoice today, for from his root the noetic Tree grew up for us, the Virgin Mary, bearing the Fruit, Christ our God: by Whom the ends of the whole world are nourished, and become partakers of eternal life: therefore all we the faithful piously beatify her as God.

*Sticheron after the Gospel, Tone 6:*
The humility of heart of thy life, O our father: for having forsaken all earthly things—race, and the throne of thy princely rule—thou didst love Christ alone, and serving Him with a pure conscience, didst live in an angelic manner upon earth; thou didst drive away the demonic legions, having ascended unto the height of virtues. Blessed art thou who didst thus labor unto Christ, and we, having recognized thy labors, beatify thee with love!

*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 4, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour out a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing of her wonders.

*Troparia:*
Open my lips, O my Christ, send down the Paraclete, that I may be able to sing of Thy favorite, versifying the height of virtues, unto which in purity of heart he ascended through humility.

Truly wast Thou named Sviatosha: for being full of holiness, Thou didst keep far away from every unvenerable deed, and winging thine understanding toward the vision of God, thence didst Thou recognize all bright things that are in the world.

Thou didst remember those who were before thee, from how great glory they fell in vain, and with dishonor the sickle of death reaped their heart: therefore that thou mightest be delivered,

*(Right page — Folio 19 recto, running header: TO NIKOLA SVIATOSHA:)*
thou didst leave the princely throne on earth, and didst receive a throne in heaven, which abides ever with the saints!

*Glory...*
Thou didst emulate the blessed Joasaph of India;[^58] for as he left an earthly kingdom and sought the heavenly, so also thou, O blessed one, having forsaken burdensome princely rule, didst receive the eternal!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Deprive me not of the Kingdom on high, O Lady who rulest over heavenly and earthly things; for seeking this, I keep aloof, being overcome by passions that ever wage war against me: behold, I flee unto thee with unhesitating hope!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish Thy singers, O Theotokos, living and unfailing fountain, who have assembled themselves as a spiritual company, and in Thy divine glory count them worthy of crowns of glory!

*Troparia:*
That thou mightest be a servant unto the only God, thou didst not desire to labor unto the world, from which having departed like a second Abraham, thou didst leave thy house and land; and coming as unto Haran, into the most honorable Caves monastery of the Mother of God, thou didst behold, as upon a ladder, the actions and contemplation of the blessed fathers ascending unto heaven.

Being once lord of thy principality, for the love of thy Lord thou didst become a servant, ministering unto the servants of Christ in the kitchen;[^59] that thou mightest follow Him Who became obedient for our sake even unto death.

Clothed in purple and holding a scepter in hand, thou wast not ashamed to carry firewood upon thy shoulders and chop it with thine own hands, that thou mightest surrender all thy members into servitude unto the Master on high, from Whom as recompense thou didst receive eternal life.

*Glory...*
Worldly men minding worldly things, thy brethren, O blessed one, thought thy work in the kitchen to be dishonorable; therefore they dissuaded thee, that thou mightest leave this: but thou, loving this obedience, didst ask for thyself yet another year to abide therein.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O my Sovereign Lady, convince me who do not obey the commandments of my Lord, nor keep the rules of obedience, unto the practice thereof, and strengthen me during the rest of my time to be corrected perfectly in both: that having died not in sluggishness, with the unprofitable servants I may not receive punishment according unto my deeds!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
As a true hearer of the evangelical word, and lover of monks, and emulator of the venerable, come, O brethren, let us in songs of praise sing of the blessed Nikola: for he his body *(Left page — Folio 19 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
subjected his body unto God, that He might liberate his soul from passions, and shining forth like a sun with the rays of his virtues, he illumines all of us who sing: Glory to God Who calls His servants unto eternal salvation!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Show me forth as a citizen of heaven on high, O pure Virgin, who flee unto thy protection, and ask fervent intercession from thee: that having found grace through thee, I may sing: Glory to God Who saves the human race!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, in a light cloud Jesus the Most Divine came with incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparia:*
Sitting in glory previously upon the throne, and with noble magnates standing before thee, thou wast not ashamed, O blessed one, to sit at the monastery gates for three years,[^60] as one faithfully guarding their entrance from those who enter unruly.

When previously a table of lavish food was set before thee and many servants served thee as lord, thou didst not disdain afterwards, O holy one, to be a servant unto the servants of Christ,[^61] ministering unto them and presenting food from God's gift.

As men having ascended by degrees unto the height of worldly glory have rest after labors: so also thou, O venerable one, by the counsel of the superior and brethren having passed through all the orderly obediences, wast constrained to practice silence in peace in thy cell, and attend unto thy salvation.

*Glory...*
As a labor-loving servant multiplying the talent unto his master, thou didst not give rest unto thy much-laboring body, but with thine own hands didst plant an orchard;[^62] and the brethren beheld the fruits of thy labors.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Awaken me unto virtues, O Mother of God, who am sluggish, and grant me to plant a spiritual orchard with noetic trees, to water it with tears of contrition, and to bring forth hundredfold fruit unto my Master, that I also with His faithful servants may receive from His hands the reward of my labors!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb Him Who is God over all, and didst bring forth the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who sing of thee.

*Troparia:*
Day and night in thy labor-loving toil, O blessed one, thou wast never seen idle, but work was in thy hands, and in thy mouth thou didst ever have the Jesus Prayer: and by this didst thou repel the demon who assaults through love of idleness.

*(Right page — Folio 20 recto, running header: TO NIKOLA SVIATOSHA:)*
That thou mightest be a partaker of heavenly food, thou didst cast away self-indulgence: for thou didst taste nothing else save a little from the common food left upon the table; and thus didst thou preserve the purity of soul and body.

After thy many fasting labors, thou didst receive from God the gift to heal diseases by a single word and assist in sorrows: we pray thee, transform into health also me who am held fast by sickness, speedily!

*Glory...*
Attempting with woven nets to turn away thy God-loving soul from the good continuance of the way, the enemy once brought the physician who served thee;[^63] but by exhortation thou didst exhort him, that he attend not unto thee, but unto his own salvation.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I have become corrupt in heart and soul, and having cast away the grace given unto me, I walked in unfitting ways: but before I fall into error before the enemies, do thou, O Mother of God, guide me unto the former blessedness: for I am a sheep of thy saving fold!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, O divinely wise, come, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her!

*Troparia:*
Rushing with divine desire toward the love of God, all the afflictions that came upon thee, O blessed one, thou didst sweetly endure, considering what was spoken: that the sufferings of this present age are not worthy to be compared with the glory that shall be revealed in us.

Unto the physician Peter who exhorted thee to cease from monastic labors, thou didst many times chastely answer: "Cease turning me away from such an undertaking: for neither tribulation, nor famine, nor distress shall separate me from the love of Christ, for Whose sake I endure all things that come upon me."

That thou mightest find eternal life in Christ, thou didst mortify thy body with much abstinence and labor, and wast not deprived of that which thou didst desire: for behold, now thou livest with the saints in the heavenly courts.

*Glory...*
Having Christ as the only physician, thou didst never permit thyself to be treated in sickness, but didst say with the Apostle: "For to me to live is Christ, and to die is gain"; and by this word didst thou thyself heal thine own diseases.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Heal my soul that is enfeebled by many sins, O Lady, by thy visitation, and give me to drink of the water of thy grace: that being freed from spiritual and bodily diseases, I may labor unto thee, my Sovereign Lady, *(Left page — Folio 20 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
with diligence always!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Having counted as nothing all the fair things of this world and corruptible wealth, thou wast enriched with many wonders and signs from Christ God: standing before Whom in gladness, remember us who honor thy memory with love, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Nikola most marvelous!

*Ikos:*
Having lived like an angel upon earth, and now with the angelic ranks radiantly keeping festival in the heavens, we beatify thee, O blessed one, with these hymns: Rejoice, brightness of thy race, who didst despise princely rule! Rejoice, who didst trample upon vain wealth and glory! Rejoice, who having left all, didst follow the Lord of the poor and needy! Rejoice, who didst cast down pride and acquire humility! Rejoice, who didst pass through all monastic obediences! Rejoice, who didst abide in them with all humility! Rejoice, who didst not disdain cooking and menial labor! Rejoice, who didst renounce dwelling together with the princes, thy brethren! Rejoice, who being thyself infirm didst prevail, and healed the physician! Rejoice, who didst obtain salvation for the physician through thy prayers, and didst foresee the end of death! Rejoice, who didst grant healing unto thy brother after thy death! Rejoice, marvelous and swift helper unto all who fervently call upon thee: Rejoice, O Nikola most marvelous!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The divinely wise worshipped not the creation rather than the Creator; but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers, praised above all!

*Troparia:*
"O from how great glory hast thou passed over, O prince!" Peter said unto thee, rebuking thee, O blessed one, that thou didst work and carry the refuse-basket; but thou didst answer him: "I have not lost mine honor, but rather by this am I glorified, following Christ Who came to minister unto all; unto Whom also I cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!"

When Peter once attempted to treat thee in sickness, he himself fell sick; whom thou didst heal not with herbs, but with prayer, and didst instruct him not to rely on medical craft, but only to hope in God, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers, supremely praised!

Completing thine obedience from the brethren, thou didst foresee the end of thy life after three months; therefore thou didst summon Peter, and didst exhort him to receive here the monastic habit, and in thy stead minister unto God, Who is blessed unto the ages.

*(Right page — Folio 21 recto, running header: TO NIKOLA SVIATOSHA:)*
*Glory...*
Hearing of the change of thy life, Peter fell with tears at thy feet, O venerable one, sobbing that he was being deprived of his master; but thou, raising him up, didst comfort him, and didst exhort him not to grieve over this, but to cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having no change of my life because of foul deeds, who will not weep for me? For I depart into an unknown path: I have turned aside from the way of the commandments of thy Son. Therefore with tears I call upon thee, O Sovereign Lady: show me the path, and I will walk in truth, singing: Supremely praised art thou, O Theotokos, and blessed from generation unto generation!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The childbearing of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace: then indeed being figured, but now being actualized, it arouses the whole inhabited earth to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, O ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Having lived according unto thy pious name, and ever foreseeing thine own departure, O Venerable Nikola, thou didst prepare for thyself a place in the holy cave, which at his petition thou didst grant unto Peter,[^64] saying: "Let it be unto thee, if thou willest, and as the Lord will, Who is supremely exalted unto the ages."

Possessing boldness before God and an unshaken hope according unto faith, thou didst receive the gift of clairvoyance, wherewith having learned of Peter's departure unto God after three days, thou didst command him to prepare himself for such an exit: and he, having received the Holy Spirit according unto thy word, departed rejoicing and singing: O God supremely exalted unto the ages!

Having lived thirty years after the death of this physician,[^65] and blessedly labored unto the Lord, thou didst bring forth hundredfold fruits, with which thou didst depart, O blessed one, unto the assembly of the saints, singing unto the Lord Jesus and supremely exalting Him unto the ages.

Hearing of thy glorious and venerable falling asleep, the people assembled from the whole city to give thee the last kiss, and buried thee with tears, being borne with the joy of the angels unto the bosom of Abraham, that there with all the saints thou mayest stand before the Lord unto the ages.

*Glory...*
Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Temporal life comes to an end, woe is me! Unto Whom shall I be brought, I who am the most wretched of all men? The Judge waits, the books are opened, and according unto my deeds, it is certain that I shall inherit the portion of the wicked: but before I depart hence, pray, O Lady, with Thy favorite Nikola unto Thy Son, that I may inherit the portion of the good!

*(Left page — Folio 21 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit, being enlightened, and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparia:*
Let every earthborn mortal emulate the life of the God-praised father: for having despised burdensome glory upon earth, and having attained unto eternal glory, he now rejoices, having an immovable throne in the heavenly mansions, where he also prays for us, that we may become heirs of the blessed life.

"Whosoever loves Me, let him follow Me, that he may be with Me": hearing these evangelical words, O most blessed Sviatosha, thou didst love God, didst follow Him, and didst acquire nothing in the world above His love: therefore now thou livest with Christ in unapproachable light.

He Who said: "Them that glorify Me I will glorify," fulfilled the promise upon thee, O holy one; for not only did He glorify thee thyself with gifts, but also thy cross, thy headrest, and thy hairshirt are glorified, and are remembered even until now,[^66] for glorious things were wrought through them.

*Glory...*
From generation to generation thy memory is glorified, O venerable father; the Church together with us praises thy labors and ascetic feats, and the shrine containing thy relics in the holy cave reveals them, and summons all to pray unto thee now as a fervent intercessor and protector in our weakness.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Glorified by all generations, and honored by the Most Holy Trinity, be an intercessor and swift protectress from the assaults of the enemy for us who praise thee in hymns, O pure Virgin, that we may ever sing unto thee: Rejoice, O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin!

*Exapostilarion:*
Thou wast a temple of the Most Holy Spirit, and the Father with the Son having come made Their abode in thee, and thereby through communion of the divine mysteries showed thee forth as a citizen of paradise: wherein now being nourished by the rational Tree and delighting in incorruptible glory, remember us also who with faith bow down before the shrine of thy relics, O venerable father!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou wast the all-pure temple of the Word Who was incarnate from thee, O Virgin: make me, who am a temple of all lawlessness, a temple of the lawful commandments of thy Son through thine intercession, that having become an abode of the grace of God, I may worthily sing of thee as one who can do all things through God, O Maiden of God!

*(Right page — Folio 22 recto, running header: TO NIKOLA SVIATOSHA:)*
*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
The great corrections of fasting labors of the great Caves fathers drew thee, O blessed Nikola, unto the fasting assembly: whose life thou didst fervently emulate; and now living with them in heaven, pray for those who sing of thee!

*Twice:*
Upon the holy mountain, where the Mother of God established her dwelling, thou didst blossom like a sacred, mystical palm tree: with the branches of thy labors, cover from affliction all who call upon thee for help, and grant healing from thy fruits unto those who piously honor thine all-venerable memory, O all-blessed Nikola!

Today at thy memory, O venerable one, the Pechersk Lavra rejoices, and the honorable assembly of monastics coming unto thy tomb spiritually dances in psalms, praising God Who dwells in unapproachable light and is praised in His saints, with whom thou also now livest, receiving honor from Him.

*Glory... Tone 5:*
Being a fair-branched tree, like a cedar in Lebanon, thou wast planted in the house of the Mother of God, O most blessed Father Nikola, and watered at the living waters from the teachings of the Caves fathers: with thy shadow, therefore, overshadow the people who flee unto thee; defend them from barbarian invasions, casting down their uprisings; for the Lord appointed thee as a prince of the Ruthenian land: therefore falling down we pray: preserve the Ruthenian dominion in peace, and well keep this monastery through thy prayers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save from perils thy servants, O most holy Theotokos Virgin: for all we after God fervently flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable wall and intercession.

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*(Left page — Folio 22 verso, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Nestor the Chronicler, haloed and seated at a desk writing in a book with a quill, with books on a shelf and the monastery church visible through the window; in the lower left foreground, a kneeling monk reads a book; inscribed above: "Ven: Nestor, CHRONICLER OF RUS'":)[^67]*

*On the 27th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Nestor the Chronicler of the Caves:*
*At Vespers, on "Lord, I Call," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having written down the times and years of memorable deeds, the ascetic struggles and labors of the God-bearing fathers, O blessed Nestor, thou wast kindled with love toward noetic desire, and wast shown forth as a good disciple, following the footsteps of the first-founders: moreover in this life thou didst choose the good

*(Right page — Folio 23 recto, running header: TO NESTOR THE CHRONICLER:)*
portion that was with thee. Of which make us also partakers through thy prayers, who fervently sing of thy memory!

Having written down the words of the divine Scriptures, the struggles of those who lived in an angelic manner, in the book of incorruption, thou didst write the memory of future recompense in the book of thy heart: therefore thou hast thy name written in the book of life; but O father most blessed, pray for us, that we also may be found written in the book of life, who honor the commemoration of thy memory!

Desiring to have understanding of spiritual wisdom, O venerable one, thou didst come unto the great fathers, from whom thou didst learn heavenly understanding: therefore having lived in an angelic manner in the flesh, thou didst shine forth like the noetic dawn: and now being a son of the day and a partaker of the Light, cease not fervently praying, that they may be delivered from corruption and afflictions, who in faith sing of thine all-venerable memory!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having acquired a prepared heart for the Holy Spirit, O Nestor our father, thou didst adorn thy soul with purity and humility, by the choice of voluntary poverty, by vigilance and obedience, and unceasing prayer: and with the reed-pen of thy chastity thou didst write down the holy lives of the first-founding Caves fathers; having an unceasing desire toward God, thou didst find Him Whom thou didst seek, and now as a son of light in the unending Light, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 5:*
Thou didst fulfill the prophetic saying, O Venerable Nestor, proclaiming unto us the works of God by writing, saying with David in the psalm: "I remembered the works of the Lord, and Thy wonders from the beginning, O God" (Ps. 76:12 [LXX 76:12]), which in the beginning of the monastery of Thy Mother I saw resting; I make known unto the last generation Thy power in the venerable ones: these things having learned from thy writings, O most blessed one, we kiss thy labors, beatify thy memory with hymns, and on high offer the psalm-singing: "Thou art the God that doest wonders" (Ps. 76:15 [LXX 76:15])!

*Both now... Theotokion according to the Tone, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Canon to the Saint, Tone 4, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The choirs of Israel with unwetted steps, having passed through the Red Sea and the watery depth, beholding the enemy captains engulfed therein, with gladness sang: Let us sing unto our God, for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*
O monastic choirs, in the yearly memory of the God-bearing father assemble yourselves fervently *(Left page — Folio 23 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
assemble yourselves fervently in the holy cave, and bowing down with love before the shrine of the relics of wise Nestor, sing unto God, for He is gloriously glorified in His saints!

Joined unto the monastic choirs, unto me who venture on this present day to sing of thy memory, and who have fallen into sin more than all men, assist through thy prayers, O holy Nestor, for God is glorified in thee today!

Desiring to be numbered with the angelic choirs, thou wast sealed in the flesh among those who were equal to the angels, O father: from whom having learned angelic life, thou didst ascend unto noetic desire: therefore do we honor thee, for God is wonderfully glorified in thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O choirs of saints and venerable fathers, standing with the angelic choirs, glorify the Word Most Holy of all saints Who was born of her: and help us also, earthborn mortals, today to glorify the Mother of God, for she is wonderfully glorified over all!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the infirm have girded themselves with strength (1 Sam. 2:4 [LXX 1 Kings 2:4]): therefore my heart is established in the Lord.

*Troparia:*
The disposition of thine understanding was like a bent bow, O father, for living in this vale of tears, thou didst ever have an ascent in thy heart toward God: and unto me who lead my life ever in sorrows, O Nestor, help, praying for me, that my heart may rejoice in the Lord!

Being seventeen years of age, mentally thou didst receive the stature of a perfect man;[^68] for thou didst shine forth, as with elder-like gray hairs, in good deeds: correct these things in me also, O blessed one, and my heart shall rejoice in the Lord!

*Glory...*
Having received purity and deep humility unto poverty, thou didst fly as with wings unto the mountain of monastic virtues: whence having perceived in the twinkling of an eye the passing fashion of this world, thou didst all the more wing thy heart into the love of God.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bring forth the Garden of purity, with the fruit thereof thou didst abundantly nourish those who believe thee to be the Mother of God: and grant unto me who bow before thee with faith, O Bride of God and Virgin, to taste even a little of this fruit, that my sorrowful heart may rejoice!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Directed by the great fathers in good manners, and in youth wholly united unto God, thou didst mortify carnal lusts by strict fasting: thou didst enliven the spirit with the hope of eternal blessings: having lived in an angelic manner upon earth, thou now dwellest with the angels: therefore do we honor thee, O blessed Nestor!

*(Right page — Folio 24 recto, running header: TO NESTOR THE CHRONICLER:)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
An ineffable image of dread wonders do I behold in thee, O all-pure Virgin, for thou didst conceive God, and didst contain in thy womb Him Who is uncircumscribed; but how this mystery came to pass is unsearchable: rather with faith and with fear I beatify thee, mindful: Rejoice, spacious dwelling of the uncontainable God!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, that Thou wast born of the Virgin, to deliver from deceit those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Having heard of the glorious and marvelous things upon the hill of the Caves, thou didst come thither, O venerable one, and having beheld the grace of God descending with fire and dew at the beginning of the foundation of the church,[^69] being yet outside with the elders, thou didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Making a greater and more perfect tabernacle in thy soul for the Holy Spirit, by the affectionate reception of the commandments from the God-bearing fathers thou didst adorn it, O holy Nestor: therefore as into a fair bridal chamber, the Father with the Son and the Spirit came and made an abode in thee.

*Glory...*
Having received the teaching of the God-bearing fathers like sweet milk, thou didst fulfill the word by deed, O blessed one, and wast filled with wisdom from on high: which we also reading from thy writings, cheer our souls with the knowledge of the grace of God that was upon these hills.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Sweet milk of repentance, and heavenly nectar of contrition, dost thou grant unto those who offer hymns unto thee with love, O pure Maiden: which I also, held fast by fear yet moved by love, offer unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, and pray: sweeten my heart, O thou who didst bring forth sweet Jesus my Christ!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst sever the first-created light from darkness, that in light the works may sing of Thee, O Christ the Creator: in Thy light direct our paths!

*Troparia:*
Having removed thyself from the love of the deceitful world, with divine desire thou didst withdraw, O Nestor, to follow the footsteps, manners, and teachings of the great fathers: grant us also to attend unto these, and in the light of their deeds, through thy prayers, direct our paths!

Being walled about with deep humility, O venerable one, thou didst cast down and trample underfoot the demon of vainglory that assaulted the brother; through thine intercession with God, O father, grant us also humility, and direct our steps therein!

*Glory...*
Directing thy life in an angelic manner, all men held thee as an angel of God: therefore also unto the diaconal *(Left page — Folio 24 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
thou wast counted worthy to receive the rank of the diaconate,[^70] wherein having well directed thy course, direct also our paths in holiness, O Nestor!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having brought forth the Way of life, O all-venerable Virgin, by the paths of the diligent as merciful thou dost guide us unto the paths of righteousness: and despise me not, O good one, who stray, but as having brought forth the Guide for the straying, direct my paths unto His light!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
When I was in affliction, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me (Jonah 2:3 [LXX Jonah 2:3]).

*Troparia:*
Directing thine order in holiness and righteousness, thou didst become wholly a temple for the Holy Spirit, and having entered into the temple not made with hands, thou standest before the throne of the Most High: and unto me who desire to be there, through thy prayers, O father, help, as the pleader for my salvation!

A laborer of the fair heavenly garden, thou wast a fair sprout: for adorned with fair deeds, thou wast enriched with good fruits, whose fragrance we now smell. I pray thee, therefore, O blessed one, grant these as a gift also unto me from God my Savior!

*Glory...*
From the labor of thine ascetic struggles, O Venerable Nestor, having poured out manifold sweats, thou didst water the field of thy soul, and having grown up ears of virtues, thou now reapest sheaves, living unto God in heavenly habitations: wherein that I also may be, pray Christ my Savior!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O holy earth sown by the Spirit of the Father, rejoice, for thou didst sprout for us the tree of fair shade: scorched by the heat of passions, lead me under its shadow, O Sovereign Lady, I pray thee, as the mediatress of my salvation!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
As a disciple of the God-bearing Theodosius, and a true emulator of his life, thou wast counted worthy to be the first eyewitness of his honorable relics;[^71] which having translated in a godly manner with the rest, thou didst inherit with them the Kingdom of Heaven: which that we also who honor thee may obtain, pray unto the Lord!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamic youths once in Babylon trampled upon the flame of the furnace, crying out in hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Following the footsteps of Abraham, O holy one, by the fulfillment of the commandments thou didst offer, as he did Isaac, a pure mind unto God; for this reason from this life thou didst pass over unto the land that distills unending sweetness, where pray that I also may be, O Nestor, companion of our God-bearing fathers!

*(Right page — Folio 25 recto, running header: TO NESTOR THE CHRONICLER:)*
Having been translated from this world of passions, thou didst pass over into the passionless world, where living in peace with the hosts on high, depart not from us who are in this world, praying thy Creator that we may become partakers with thee, singing: O God, blessed art Thou!

Thou didst not incline thy heart unto words of malice in this tempest-tossed life, O venerable one: therefore also having passed untroubled through the malice of aerial spirits, thou didst attain unto the heavenly gates: which make haste to open unto us also through thy prayers, as thou art blessed!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I have turned aside from the path of righteousness, and walked not with my feet in right ways, but wandering according unto the assaults of passions, my bonds are tightened by the endurance of sin: yet even though limping, I flee unto thy goodness, and pray thee, O Sovereign Lady, heal me: for thou art the medicine of my soul!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The divinely eloquent youths, kindled with divine zeal, manfully trampled upon the threat of the tyrant and of the flame, and cast into the midst of the fire, being refreshed with dew, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by spiritual power, thou didst fly as with wings of thy virtues unto the highest things, and after these years thou now livest where years fail not: unto which pray that we also may attain, who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

From the house of the Mother of God thou didst pass over, O Nestor, into the all-radiant house of God on high, and didst dwell together with the angels in the number of heavenly citizens; intercede for us also, that we may be numbered with them, who in thy memory sing in hymns of the Lord. Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.

*Glory...*
The Trinity, one nature: O Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit, one origin, having power and authority: deliver us from the authority of the prince of darkness, through the prayers of our God-bearing Caves fathers, with whom we also bring Nestor unto Thee in prayer today, blessing Thee, the Lord, unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Standing before the throne of the Trinity, O Virgin Mother, and ever beholding the glory of the Tri-Hypostatic Godhead: pray for us sinners, that we also may not be deprived of heavenly glory, who praise thee in hymns, and the Lord Who was born of thee!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thy childbearing was shown forth incorrupt: God came forth from thy womb, as He appeared in the flesh upon earth, and lived with men: thee, O Theotokos, with hymns do we magnify.

*Troparia:*
Thou didst depart unto the mansions of the righteous, and in spirit standest *(Left page — Folio 25 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
standest before the ever-existing Light: look down, therefore, upon us who dwell in the villages of the tempest-tossed world, and assist those who are in battles, O blessed Nestor: as with hymns we magnify thee now!

With whom thou didst acquire love while living in the body, with them also having departed in the spirit thou dwellest undivided, as thy relics show forth, O venerable one, lying with the saints in the cave, with whom stand before God praying for us who magnify thy memory!

*Glory...*
A mother truly loves the Lord: and as she is pleasing unto Him, O most blessed one, be a prayerful intercessor for our salvation, that having found grace through thee, we may not fall away from eternal glory, as with hymns we magnify thy holy falling asleep!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Master of all loves thine intercession, O all-pure Virgin, and accepts thy prayers for the human race: pray, therefore, for us thy servants, O Queen of all, that we may obtain mercy on the day of judgment: for bowing down before thee as our intercessor, we magnify thee!

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 2:*
Having beforehand purified thyself by monastic deeds, O venerable one, thou wast made a precious vessel of the Holy Spirit: from Whom thou didst receive unfailing grace, to assist in afflictions all who flee unto thee with faith. And now be an unfailing helper unto us who fall down before the shrine of thy relics, invisibly assisting against those who war against us, and pray the Creator of all and God, to grant us remission of sins, and that we may obtain mercy on the day of judgment, who beatify thy memory, O Nestor divinely wise!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*(Right page — Folio 26 recto, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Barlaam, Abbot of the Caves; depicted haloed and vested in monastic mantle and epitrachelion, holding a prayer-cord; surrounded by panels depicting scenes from his Life: "Going unto the entrance into the cave," "Barlaam tonsured," "Barlaam casts off his garments," "His wife having adorned herself came [to entice him]," and "Falling asleep of the Venerable Barlaam of the Caves"; inscribed above: "Ven: Barlaam of the Caves":)[^72]*

---

# MONTH OF NOVEMBER

*On the 19th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Barlaam, Abbot of the Caves:*
*At Small Vespers, Stichera: Tone 5:*
O venerable Father Barlaam, taking the yoke of Christ upon thy shoulder, valiantly didst thou bear the assault of the enemy, and having unswervingly traversed the narrow path, didst attain unto the prize of the high calling: but O most blessed one, boast of monastics, unceasingly pray Christ to grant great mercy unto our souls!

O Father Barlaam divinely wise, having loved Christ from thy youth, and by divine com- *(Left page — Folio 26 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
-mandments of the Holy Spirit having illumined thy heart, thou wast truly a beholder of God: therefore fulfill for the profit of salvation the petitions of those who flee unto thy church, and pray Christ God to grant great mercy unto us!

O Father Barlaam, taking thy cross upon thy shoulder, and having lovingly run the beautiful course of Christ thy King, thou hast stood before Him Who dwells in the holy heavenly city: whence look down now upon those who honor thy struggles, O holy one; pray Christ God to direct our steps also into the way of peace!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having adorned thy life with much abstinence, O venerable one, thou hast shown forth sweat leading into life through the acquisition of virtues: renouncing the world, and despising all the fair things that are in the world, by an austere life thou didst wear out thy flesh: therefore dwelling in heaven, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (Доме Евфрафов):*
Transitory life which is on earth didst thou despise, O blessed one, that thou mightest obtain endless life.

*Verse:* Blessed is the man that fears the Lord: in His commandments he will have great delight.[^73]
For the sake of the love of Christ, O venerable Barlaam, thou didst put to death the passionate wisdom of the flesh.

*Verse:* Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^74]
By thy watchful prayers, assist in tribulations those who lovingly sing of thy memory, O divinely blessed one!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Having entered into the cave, O venerable one, thence for the sake of thy virtues thou didst pass over unto the heavenly city, and didst attain unto the rest of the righteous and the rejoicing of the saints: therefore with them fervently pray for all who honor thy memory with faith, that we may be delivered from every adverse circumstance, O Barlaam divinely wise!

*Both now... Theotokion.*

*At Great Vespers:*
*Blessed is the man... the 1st Antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Званный свыше):*
Beholding the ever-bubbling fountain of honey-flowing words coming forth from the mouth of the great father,[^75] O blessed Barlaam, as a hart unto the fountains of streams of living water,[^76] thou didst come thirsting with unspeakable love. Whence having drenched thy soul with compunction, thou didst despise all the fair things of this world, and in word didst follow the Word: before Whom now with rejoicing standing

*(Right page — Folio 27 recto, running header: TO BARLAAM, ABBOT OF THE CAVES:)*
standing, pray that He may save and enlighten our souls!

When with the eyes of the mind thou didst behold the great luminaries hidden beneath the bushel of the holy cave, O venerable one,[^77] then having illumined thy heart in youth with the light of virtues, and kindled with divine fire for dwelling together with them, thou didst leave the house of thy parents and thy friends, and becamest a partaker with the monastics. And now, numbered with the radiant choirs in heaven, enlighten us upon earth with the rays of grace, and grant gifts, praying Christ to save and enlighten our souls!

When the father worthy of beatitude showed thee the divine love of good things to come, and the ever-shining glory in the heavenly bridal chamber: immediately thou didst account earthly good things as dung:[^78] despising riches, glory, and the greatness of thy lineage, the beautiful chamber, and the bride betrothed unto thee,[^79] thou didst love Christ alone, and conforming thyself unto His poverty, didst traverse the narrow path in struggles and labors, and after thy falling asleep didst inherit the things desired: enjoying which now, pray the Good One to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Living upon earth in bodily form, thou didst emulate the life of the bodiless hosts, O venerable Father Barlaam: by the renunciation of thy glory thou didst astonish men, by poverty and abstinence thou didst put the demons to shame, by purity thou didst make the angels rejoice, and by thy great struggles thou wast an exemplar unto monastics: therefore worthily do we beatify thee, adorned by the Sovereign Master on high: Whom ever pray, O blessed one, to grant peace unto our EMPRESS,[^80] and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*The King of heaven...*

*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three Readings (see fol. 30 verso).[^81]*

*At the Litiya, Stichera: Tone 1:*
Today the holy cave joyfully rejoices, having thy holy relics, O blessed Barlaam, for they grant health unto those who with faith flee unto thy precious shrine: whence we also, being enlightened, say: make us worthy of thy gifts, O blessed one, and preserve thy flock by thy prayers, granting salvation unto our souls!

O venerable Father Barlaam, having from youth loved the beauty of the Master Christ, and having an unswerving desire for Him, thou didst leave thy fatherland and lineage, thou didst hate riches and glory, and unto Him alone *(Left page — Folio 27 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
didst follow Him alone, living in the cave as an equal of the angels: therefore now standing before Him in the heavens with the angels, pray with boldness for our souls!

*Tone 3:*
Strengthened by the almighty right hand, O venerable Father Barlaam, thou didst acquire abstinence in food, and by unceasing prayer and standing through the night wast accounted worthy to become a partaker with blessed Anthony;[^82] by streams of tears thou didst drown all the armies of demons: therefore also Christ, Whom thou didst desire and for Whose sake thou didst bear the burden of the day, glorified thee with the gift of wonders: Whom pray that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 3:*
Come, all ye assemblies of monastics, with psalms and hymns let us piously praise today the precious festival of the venerable one: who, even though he shone forth in the latter days, yet was accounted worthy of the honor of the ancient fathers, and prays Christ that our souls may be saved!

Thou wast a precious receptacle of the Holy Spirit, O venerable Barlaam: for imitating the bodiless hosts in the flesh, thou didst emulate their life: for this cause thou wast crowned by the almighty right hand, and even though thou didst pass away from the earth, yet living unto God thou standest before Him with the saints, praying for our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having from youth hated the beauties of this world, O venerable one, thou didst love from thy heart Him Who is fairer than the sons of men,[^83] and having cleaved unto Him with all thy soul, as one bodiless upon earth, living in a mortal and corruptible body, thou didst obtain the honor of incorruption: and now purely beholding the Holy Trinity, fervently pray, O venerable one, to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*O Virgin unwedded...*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones?" (Что васъ наречемъ святии):*
What shall we call thee, O venerable one? An angel in the flesh, for as an angel thou wast known upon earth? A luminary unto the venerable, for by their struggles thou didst astonish? Thou didst put demons to shame, having subdued the spirit over bodily passions; and now standing before Christ, pray that He may save and enlighten our souls!

*Verse:* Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^84]
How shall we address thee, O glorious one? An animate pillar, for thou didst remain unshaken in trials? A river of living water, pouring forth streams of spiritual grace

*(Right page — Folio 28 recto, running header: TO BARLAAM, ABBOT OF THE CAVES:)*
grace? An excellent guide of monastics, of one mind with the bodiless hosts, and a fervent intercessor for our souls!

*Verse:* The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.[^85]
What shall we name thee, O holy one? An instructor of the undisciplined, and a true physician unto those in sickness, a graceful offspring of the cave, a confirmation of monastics, an ever-flowing fountain of wonders, and a good ascetic, a swift protector unto all who come unto thee with faith!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O venerable father, having loved God from thy youth, thou didst prefer nothing above Him: having fled the turmoil of the world and of this life, thou didst attain unto the quiet haven of the holy cave, and having settled therein as in a fair bridal chamber, thou didst betroth thy soul unto God alone, having purified it beforehand with fasting and prayers: therefore thou wast a beautiful palace of the Holy Trinity, before Whom now joyfully standing, remember those who honor thine all-honorable memory!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Be thou the true vine...*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord":*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Leaving the fair things of the world and its glory, thou didst hasten unto the cave, where advancing from strength to strength, thou wast a leader of the flock of Christ, O all-glorious Barlaam: having tended which so well, shepherd us also, we pray thee, preserving us from all evils, that we may beatify thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same tone.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn: Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (Собезначальное):*
Come, ye Ruthenian assemblies of monastics, and beholding in His saints the glory of God, open your lips in praise, blessing the Sovereign Master your Creator, Who glorifies Himself in His saints, and sing in the manner of psalms, saying: Exceedingly precious unto us are Thy relics, O Christ God, for exceedingly strengthened have their dominions become, concerning Thee, the Savior and Redeemer of our souls![^86]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
My heart is confirmed to sing, O all-immaculate Virgin, of thy most pure childbearing, whereby thou didst deliver us from the ancestral curse, and didst lift up fallen human nature unto the heights: therefore as is our duty we sing of thee and magnify thee!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn: Tone 4, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ликъ ангельский):*
The spiritual wings of knowledge and action didst thou receive from the words of the great father, O Barlaam divinely wise, and by these raising thy mind unto the heights *(Left page — Folio 28 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
thou didst escape as a bird from the snare of the fowlers,[^87] and unto the dwelling where the choirs of the righteous abide, thou didst attain unto beatitude: glory unto Him Who gave thee this true desire, glory unto Him Who gave thee victory over passions: glory unto Him Who brought thee into the eternal dwelling!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having received from the Holy Spirit into thy womb, O pure Virgin, and having brought forth the Word begotten of the Father before the ages: bring us who are likened unto unreasoning beasts into understanding, and grant amendment of life, that we may ever magnify thee!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn: Tone 8, Podoben: "Having leaned upon Wisdom" (Премудрости возлег):*
Illumined with the dawn of the radiance of divine words, thou didst leave what is small in this life: and hastening unto the noetic Light, in the flesh thou didst receive the immaterial life with the bodiless hosts: whence being purified beforehand by the grace of the Spirit, thou wast an instructor of monastics, O Barlaam divinely wise; therefore with praises do we cry unto thee: pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
I have fallen into the mire of sin, and have no hope of rising; grievously have I sunk in despair: but as thou didst bring forth the Hope of salvation, look upon me thy servant, and deliver me from the sin that holds me fast, and from soul-corrupting passions, and from the perpetual malice of the crafty enemy, O Virgin unwedded; pray Christ God to grant me remission of sins: for I thy servant have thee as my hope!

*Degrees, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.[^88]
*Verse:* In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.[^89]
*Gospel of a Venerable Father.*
*Sticheron after the Gospel: Tone 6:*
O boast and ornament of the venerable, citizen of the Sion on high, earthly angel and heavenly man, helper of ascetics, and praise of virgins, leader and beautification of monastics: come, ye assemblies of feast-keepers, let us sing today with them, having lovingly woven crowns of praises, let us form a spiritual choir at the precious tomb of our God-bearing father, and let us bring worthy hymns, singing in the manner of psalms: Precious in the sight of the Lord is thy death, O most blessed Barlaam:[^90] for even though in the spirit thou hast departed unto goodness, yet in the body thou abidest ever with us, and fillest with spiritual gifts those who bow down before thy relics: and now, as one having great boldness before the Lord, pray for those who sing of thy precious falling asleep,

*(Right page — Folio 29 recto, running header: TO BARLAAM, ABBOT OF THE CAVES:)*
falling asleep, that He may grant us unanimity, strength unto our Orthodox EMPRESS,[^91] and confirmation unto all the world!

*Canon to the Venerable One:*
*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*
Delivered from bitter slavery, Israel traversed the impassable sea as dry land, and beholding the enemy drowned, sang a hymn unto God as Benefactor, Who worked wonders with a high arm, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparia:*
Grieved by the fierce slavery of this world, seeing many led captive, O blessed one, thou didst reflect that there is no deliverance from it, except one follow the steps of Christ the Redeemer: therefore thou wast an unflagging emulator of Him. By thy watchful prayer, O father, stir me up well from despondency unto the praising of thy struggles, that I may swiftly sing a hymn unto God, for He hath been glorified!

Drenched with honey-flowing words from the great father, thou didst no longer desire to behold wealth, but forgetting the things behind, as one winged thou didst press forward unto the things ahead,[^92] pondering one thing: to seek God with all Thy heart, and ever to sing unto Him a hymn, for He hath been glorified!

From the discourse of the venerable Anthony, thy heart being kindled with fervent love toward God as with a flame, O blessed one, rejoicing as one who had found a treasure,[^93] thou didst return unto thy house, yet the resolution of thy will thou didst leave at the feet of the father worthy of beatitude, after which hastening back unto him, thou didst ever sing unto God: for He hath been glorified!

*Glory...*
Heeding the divinely inspired discourse of great Anthony, thou didst tear asunder the entangling snares of love for the world, and hastening unto him, thou wast his true disciple and hearer, crying aloud with him unto God: for He hath been glorified!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Dispel by thine intercession, O pure Virgin, the afflictions and assaults of the enemy that ever harass me: and put me not to shame from mine expectation, O Mother of God: for thou together with thy Son art the only hope of the hopeless!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The stone which the builders rejected, the same became the head of the corner: this is the stone upon which Christ confirmed the Church, which He redeemed from among the nations.[^94]

*Troparia:*
O Christ the unyielding Stone, Thou wast made the head of the corner, and upon Thee Thy venerable one, desiring to build a spiritual house, resolved beforehand to lay the walls of humility, poverty, and other virtues: therefore coming unto the great father, he prayed him, saying: Confirm me upon the rock of the commandments of the Lord, O venerable one!

*(Left page — Folio 29 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
In glory and riches, having come with many servants unto the holy cave, thou didst cast down before the feet of the elder all thy fair adornments and thyself also, together casting down the pride of the crafty one!

Perceiving, O holy one, that a spiritual treasure was hidden in the holy cave, thou didst sell unto Anthony all the riches brought by thee and thyself also, and as a wise merchant thou didst buy that field wherein thou didst become rich with this, whereby we also glorify thee after thy death!

*Glory...*
Judging the wealth, glory, and all the light of this age to be like a fleeting shadow, O venerable one: therefore counting all things as dung that Thou mightest gain Christ alone, Thou didst desire to walk toward Him along the path of monastics!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The path of my life being seized by unlawful passions, do thou, O pure one, with the right hand wherewith thou didst bear the Creator on high, clear and cleanse, that the path of virtues may be marked out for me also, and a right spirit renewed within mine inward parts.[^95]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Seeing thy desire to be unfeigned, the great one among the fathers placed upon thee the helmet of salvation, clothed thee in the breastplate of righteousness, and numbered thee with the monastic company; and thou, appointed a warrior in the heavenly kingdom, didst struggle well against visible and invisible enemies, overcoming whom thou didst receive the crown of heavenly honor, which pray that we also may obtain, O venerable Barlaam, who lovingly honor thy holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Against the enemies warring with me grant help from on high, O Virgin unwedded of God, and teach me how, fleeing their crafty wiles, to overcome them: for nothing shall vanquish them unless thou assist those who especially honor with love thy most immaculate childbearing!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Of old Habakkuk heard Thy marvelous report, O Christ, and with fear cried aloud: God shall come from the south, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed with leafy thickets, to save His anointed ones: glory to Thy power, O Lord![^96]

*Troparia:*
When the three warring ranks of darkness heard that they were stripped of thy love when thou wast clothed in the garment of salvation, they grieved against thee, and raging they stirred up thy father against thee; but thou didst set thy heart upon the power of the Lord!

Thy senseless father ran against the holy flock with many servants unto the cave, and having driven all away, he dragged thee out who wast bound with warmest love unto God, hoping in Whom thou didst cry aloud:

*(Right page — Folio 30 recto, running header: TO BARLAAM, ABBOT OF THE CAVES:)*
glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Dragging thee outside, O blessed one, thy father stripped off the holy mantle and cast it into the ravine, likewise also taking the helmet of salvation which was upon thy head he cast it away; then he clothed thee in a bright and costly garment.[^97] But thou, despising all these things, didst unswervingly emulate the angelic life, crying aloud and shouting: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Glory...*
Forcibly clad in a most radiant garment, thou didst trample it into the mire, together with it trampling the proud enemy, who craftily through various natures drew men to sing: glory to Thy power, O Lord![^98]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Strip me, O Mother of God, who am covered with the shame of fierce iniquities, and my defiled garment, granting me repentance and compunction, do thou make clean by thy grace, that gratefully I may stand before thee, crying: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, garment of boldness for the naked!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Make Thine unsetting light to dawn, O Christ, in the hearts of those who faithfully sing of Thee, granting us peace which passes understanding: therefore from the night of ignorance toward the day running in Thy light, we glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
When thou wast forcibly led home in radiant garments, O blessed one, the unsetting light of God illumined thy heart; therefore thou didst not look upon deceptive light, but beholding God alone at thy right hand, thou didst glorify Him as Lover of mankind!

When he who had prepared a feast called thee unto an immortal banquet, O venerable one, thou didst not taste of the delicacies of the table, but looking down with thine eyes, thou didst raise thy mind unto the heights, contemplating the table of the heavenly King, and the beatitude of him who shall eat bread in the kingdom of heaven!

Preparing in thy heart a fair palace for the Spirit, thou didst not sit in an honorable place in thy palace, but in a single corner, choosing rather to select a narrow place of life for the glorification of the Lord, Lover of mankind!

*Glory...*
Thou didst not incline thy heart unto the crafty words of thy betrothed, nor didst thou ascend her bed, taking heed unto the word spoken: The venerable ones shall rejoice in glory upon their beds,[^99] of whom thou art now a partaker and inheritor!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Raise me up, O Mother of God, who lie upon the bed of evils, and by thy guidance direct my life, that I may spend the rest of my time in repentance, and glorify thee, the Mother of Christ our God, Who is abundant in tender mercy!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I am wholly held fast by boundless passions, and fall into the monster of evils; but lead me up from corruption, O God, *(Left page — Folio 30 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
as of old Thou didst lead up Jonah,[^100] and through faith grant me passionlessness, that with the voice of praise and in the spirit of salvation I may sacrifice unto Thee!

*Troparia:*
Wholly surrounded by distressing trials, thou didst cry aloud unto the Lover of mankind, O venerable one, to deliver thee from the snare of the fowlers, and thou didst remain fasting for three days, nourishing thy soul with prayer alone, and saying with the prophet: Lead me up from this corruption, O God, and I will live the rest of my years serving Thee in true deeds!

Concerning thee, O blessed one, when thou wast in such great trial, fervent prayer was made by the holy ones in the cave; for the righteous cried, and the Lord heard them,[^101] and delivered thee from sorrow, inclining the heart of thy father unto mercy, who had pity on thy patient endurance, and having kissed thee lovingly, released thee in peace unto the holy cave!

A mournful voice was then heard in the house of thy father: thy parents wept for thee their beloved son; thy wife bitterly lamented thee her betrothed; menservants and maidservants groaned with weeping for their master, and all accompanied thee with tears; but thou, having departed rejoicing as from Egypt into the promised land,[^102] O holy one, didst offer thanksgiving unto God, that He delivered thee from the fierce ruler of this world!

*Glory...*
Glory to Thee, God alone praised in the Holy Trinity, that Thou hast saved Thy servant from the manifold adversary: whom save also through the prayers of Thy pleasing one, who honor his memory!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save, O Sovereign Lady, those who are destroyed by passions, and stretch forth thy hand unto the despairing, that being saved through thee, in a true spirit we may offer praise unto thee with thine Only-begotten Son and God!

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*
Thou didst not fear thy father's threats, thou didst not yield unto his flatteries, neither wast thou cast down by womanly deceit, O all-honorable Barlaam, but didst remain invincible against them, hoping in thy Lord, Whom pray for our souls!

*Ikos:*
Beholding thy voluntary suffering, who will not be astonished, O most blessed Barlaam! For thou didst expose the demonic wiles, and having manfully stood against passionate lusts, how much didst thou suffer from distress, that thou mightest present thy soul pure unto God: Whom also fervently pray for us, O holy one, that we may be delivered from carnal furies and invisible

*(Right page — Folio 31 recto, running header: TO BARLAAM, ABBOT OF THE CAVES:)*
invisible enemies through thy prayers unto God assisting our souls!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The holy children who of old showed themselves for piety's sake, passed through the unbearable flame of the furnace as through a bridal chamber, and singing in concord, sang the hymn: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^103]`

*Troparia:*
Thou didst pass through the flame of temptation, O venerable one, and remaining unburned by it, thou didst rather burn the enemy who invisibly warred with thee, against whom the prayers of thine instructor assisted thee; for he unceasingly cried: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Anthony was amazed at thy patient endurance, that warring against fierce Belial, thou didst not fear his manifold wiles, whereby even if thou wast tripped, yet by patience and humility thou didst conquer, and like the youths didst sing the hymn: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

When thou didst return with victory unto the fathers in the cave, all things were filled with joy when they saw thee crowned, and thine elder also rejoiced receiving thee in health; therefore they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
Seeing, O venerable one, that upon the root of his teachings thou hadst grown up with virtues like a fair-flowering tree, and the shoot of thy youth had borne the fruits of aged grace, Anthony marveled, and thankfully cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Sprinkle with the dew of thy grace, O pure Virgin, the tree of my life rotting with sins, that at least it may put forth small branches, and bring thee fair, immortal fruit of humility and prayer, crying: Rejoice, acquisition of immortal fruit!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The furnace moist with dew showed forth the image of a supernatural wonder of old, for the fire burned not the young children, showing forth the seedless childbearing of Christ: therefore singing let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
A wonder! That the gift began to operate while thou wast yet in thy youth, seeing which Anthony appointed thee leader over the brethren in his own place;[^104] and thou wast a good shepherd unto the fathers who sing and highly exalt the Lord unto the ages!

Having received the beginning of leadership, thou didst also receive anew a beginning for thy life, O holy one: for thou didst add labors unto labors, and ascetic deeds unto vigilance for God: so that all who desire to sing of the Lord unto the ages might have Thee as an exemplar!

*(Left page — Folio 31 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
A good instructor and father wast thou, O blessed one: for as thou thyself didst never alter thy rule, so also thou didst instruct and entreat the brethren to have care for salvation, and to attend unto the soul, an immortal thing, if they desire to highly exalt the Lord with the angels unto the ages!

Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.

Seeking the noetic Jerusalem, thou didst journey unto the sensible one, sweetly enduring all manner of labors on the way for Christ's sake:[^105] and having lovingly kissed His life-giving Tomb, thou didst return singing of the Lord unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
All-hymned Mother, Sovereign Lady of the heavenly ranks, Queen of heaven and earth, tear me away from earthly things, and grant me to seek the things on high, and make me worthy in the heavenly Jerusalem to sing of thee, and to highly exalt the Lord unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The ineffable mystery of the Virgin: for she was shown forth as heaven, the cherubic throne, and the light-bearing bridal chamber of Christ God the Almighty: her piously as Theotokos do we magnify!

*Troparia:*
Thou enjoyest ineffable joy, O father, now: for with the angelic ranks standing before the unwaning Light, direct us also by thy prayers to walk in the light of the commandments of the Lord!

From this life thou hast departed unto eternal life, O venerable one, and hast received the place of rest: preserve our life from harm, beset by the assaults of adversaries, and grant that through thy guidance we may attain unto the dwelling of paradise!

Returning from the city of Constantine,[^106] thou didst depart in soul unto the all-radiant city of the heavenly King, and thy relics thou didst leave in the monastery of the Holy Mountain, which thou didst command to translate unto the cave, where now they lie incorrupt, pouring forth an ever-flowing fountain of wonders!

*Glory...*
O only God Who art glorified in Thy saints, glorify all who faithfully bow down before Thy magnificent glory: for behold, we bring Thy pleasing one unto Thee in prayer, for whose sake save us all!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
We glorify thee, O Virgin, with unceasing lips, and beatify thee as is our duty: for thou alone art blessed among women, having brought forth the Father's Only-begotten Word: with Whom the faithful together magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
Having wounded thy soul with sweet desire for the love of Christ, O venerable one, and desiring ever to be filled with it, thou didst renounce the corruptible world with its lusts, thou didst leave house and parents, thou didst disregard thy betrothed: therefore a hundredfold hast thou received, and dost inherit eternal life: of which that we also may not be deprived, grant help through thy prayers!

*(Right page — Folio 32 recto, running header: TO BARLAAM, ABBOT OF THE CAVES:)*
*Theotokion:*
Fair palace, Mary Bride of God, whose beauty the Most High desired, by the virginity of the All-Holy Spirit He dwelt in thine all-consecrated womb, and took flesh for our sake: make my soul a palace for the dwelling of the heavenly King, that I, thine unprofitable servant, may ever magnify thee!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 3, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Далъ еси знамение):*[^107]
Having acquired a spotless life, patient endurance, meekness, and unfeigned love toward God, great abstinence, and divine compunction, O father, as an angel in the flesh didst thou live, O blessed Barlaam, an intercessor for our souls!

Preparing the beauty of thy soul as a house for the Holy Spirit, thou didst despise the beauty of this world, and having laid an immovable foundation upon the rock of the commandments of the Lord, thou didst complete it with the height of humility, and becamest a dwelling of the All-Holy Trinity; Whom pray for us, to give beauty unto our souls, and that we may become a spiritual receptacle of grace!

Having caused the noetic tree of thy life to grow by renunciation of the world, thou didst bring forth fruits of virtues unto God, O most blessed father; receiving from Whom the recompense of thy labors, thou now enjoyest the tree of life in paradise: of whose sweetness that we also may be accounted worthy, pray Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
