The instructor of monastics, the great one among the fathers,[^108] seeing thy warmth toward God, O father, filled with many virtues, entrusted unto thee the God-gathered flock, which having tended well upon the pastures of thine instruction, thou didst lead whole unto Christ God, the Chief of shepherds: Whom now entreat, to preserve His spiritual flock in peace, and to keep it from the wolves that destroy it!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, accept the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow!

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service is common to a Venerable Father.*

*(Left page — Folio 32 verso, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves, seated or kneeling in a rock-hewn cave cell before an open book on a lectern, with a skull on a rocky shelf and a hanging oil lamp radiating light; inscribed in his halo: "Ven: Athanasius the Recl:"):*[^109]

---

# MONTH OF DECEMBER

*On the 2nd Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves:*
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Яко добля):*
Desiring to mortify thy flesh, thou didst enter into the narrow reclusion as into a grave, O father, and didst bury it unyieldingly through ascetic deeds; but before the general resurrection thou didst show forth a certain rising of the dead, having risen from the dead after two days: and living now in immortality,

*(Right page — Folio 33 recto, running header: TO ATHANASIUS THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
living, pray that we also may escape eternal death, and obtain great mercy from Christ God!

"He that believes in Me, as the Scripture hath said, though he were dead, yet shall he live forever,"[^110] was fulfilled on thee in deed, O venerable Athanasius: for having died unto the world and the flesh, yet cooperating with God by good deeds, He raised thee up unto a better life, and thou wast an astonishing wonder unto all, and rather unto profitable amendment: which grant unto us also through thy prayers, who pray, celebrating thy memory!

Declaring the paths of eternal life, Thou didst not suffer Thy venerable one to see corruption,[^111] raising him up after two days, as Thou didst four-day-dead Lazarus, having broken the bonds of death by the calling of Thy divine voice: by this call us up also, O Christ the King, and raise us up from dead deeds, we pray Thee, O Giver of life and Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Tone 3:*
After Thy rising, O Christ the Savior, the bars of death were shattered, and faithful people are no longer held under its dominion, as Athanasius, marvelous among the venerable, showed us by his terrifying rising, and by three deeds gave profitable amendment unto monastics; confirm this also in our hearts, O Savior, we pray Thee, who honor the yearly memory of Thy venerable one!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 2:*
With showers of tears, O venerable one, thou didst drown the death that held sway in Judea, and resembling the Giver of life Who abode three days in the tomb, having risen from the dead after two days, thou didst choose the narrowest path unto eternal life; having attained unto which in the land of immortality, look down upon us thence without delay, drive away the passions that slay us, and make us partakers of thine immortality, who on this present day celebrate thy memory, O father Athanasius most blessed!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Desiring to obtain the beatitude of those who weep,[^112] day and night thou didst unceasingly weep, having in mind the hour of judgment: therefore after thy falling asleep thou didst find consolation in heaven, O venerable Athanasius. Grant that we also may ever weep here for our sins, so that escaping endless weeping, we may receive eternal consolation there!

*Canon, Tone 1:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He hath been glorified!

*(Left page — Folio 33 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
Desiring now with clayey lips to sing of thy dread falling asleep, O father, despise me not, but be bountiful unto me, who cry out to pray now on the day of thy solemnity, on which thy memory hath been glorified!

Perceiving human life to be cut down in this world as by the shadow of death, O venerable one, that having crushed it within thyself thou mightest hasten unto the firm Rock: and having drowned the assaults of death by virtues, thou singest unto God, for He hath been glorified!

*Glory...*
Having died first unto the world by the renunciation of its many-passioned deeds, O holy one, thou didst direct the steps of thy journeyings unto unshakable life; therefore being now a son of life, look upon us who sing unto God in thy memory: for He hath been glorified!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
My soul slain by voluptuous passions, do thou make haste to bring to life, O pure Virgin: for thou art the Mother of Life, and dost grant resurrection unto the dead!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The stone which the builders rejected, the same became the head of the corner: this is the stone upon which Christ confirmed the Church, which He redeemed from among the nations.

*Troparia:*
Bound with the cord of a life pleasing unto God, O most blessed one, death as a captive had no power over thee: for even if it seized thee, yet it was commanded to return thee again for the profit of many, through Him Who by His death redeemed all from death!

Dead and bound with the edge of the mantle thou didst lie upon the bed, O father, and the Providence on high sought to work wonders through thee: for after two days He called back thy soul, and alive thou wast found, unto the astonishment of all who looked upon thee, who had seen thy falling asleep!

*Glory...*
Thy sickness, O venerable one, was not unto death, but unto the glory of God; for having died, thou didst receive death as a sleep for the relief of thy sickness, and after two days as from sleep awaking, thou didst wash thy face with streams of tears!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having cut off the sickness of death by thy holy childbearing, and given life unto all the world, I alone am left dead by defiled deeds: O Mother of God, lay thy hand upon me, and I shall find a better life!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Looking unto immortality, that thou mightest escape eternal death, thou didst choose a narrow life, O most blessed Athanasius, in thy mortified body: therefore marveling at the marvelous dispensation shown upon thee, we glorify the only Lover of mankind, Who hath given life and incorruption unto all!

*(Right page — Folio 34 recto, running header: TO ATHANASIUS THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Those who were subject to the slavery of death hast thou freed by thy childbearing, O Virgin Bride of God; therefore the generations of Christians praise thee with hymns, for thou hast given us life and incorruption!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
The sun was lifted up, and the moon stood still in its order: Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, and didst establish Thy Church upon it.[^113]

*Troparia:*
Lying lifeless, thou didst rise, and having received breath, thou didst come back unto existence, O father: seeing which those who stood by glorified the power of God with fear!

Seeing how terrible is the hour of death and judgment, O blessed one, thou didst weep bitterly, and pour forth silence and groaning, and thy tears became for many a depiction of the dread judgment!

*Glory...*
Ever remembering thy departure, O holy one, thou didst weep in this painful life: and thine existing lamentations moved thee, having sensibly seen what manner of fear and trembling those who depart possess: which that we also may escape, pray, O venerable father!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I fear not the dread judgment, nor do the bitter torments of Gehenna terrify me, for from many passions my heart is hardened: by the substance of thy tender mercy, O pure Virgin Mother, melt it, that it may become sensible through the remembrance of dread torments!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God, for other than Thee we know no God, we call upon Thy name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead!

*Troparia:*
Seeing thy weeping and disconsolate lamentation, those who stood by, terrified, asked that thou mightest speak a word unto them for their profit: and thou didst speak three profitable things unto them: to keep obedience, repentance, and prayer,[^114] as hearing the beginnings of virtues; in which that we also may be confirmed, pray, O father Athanasius!

Being thyself obedient unto the commandments of Christ, thou didst tell the brethren that obedience is above all virtues, O venerable one: for even after death it dies not, but preserves life with God, Who is appointed Judge of the living and of the dead!

*Glory...*
Repentance and prayer are two fiery wings, and by these thou didst declare an unhindered passage through the aerial toll-houses,[^115] O blessed one; thou didst choose the path of silence, whereby thou didst hasten unto the highest things, leaving instruction unto those who asked: grant amendment unto us also, who celebrate the day of thy rejoicing!

*(Left page — Folio 34 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O swift intercessor for the Christian race, Virgin Mary, having received the prayers of thy singers into thy most pure hands, bring them unto thy Son, and ask remission of sins for us, and grant tranquility unto those who are in passions!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou didst cast me into the depths of the heart of the sea, and didst save me, O Savior, from the slavery of death, and didst loose the bonds of mine iniquities!

*Troparia:*
Having utterly renounced life and seeing the sensible light, thou didst enter into a dark reclusion, exchanging the sensible for the vision of noetic light:[^116] which that we also may obtain through thy prayers, O father, having renounced our iniquities!

That thou mightest bring to life the flesh mortified by ascetic labors, O holy one, thou didst ever drench it with streams of thy tears, remaining twelve years in the cave buried alive, brought to life by the hope of immortal life!

*Glory...*
The tree of life, thy flesh, O venerable Athanasius, brought forth fruits of repentance for all, of which grant that through thy love I also may taste, that I may be strengthened to loose the bonds of mine iniquities!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou art the ever-flowering Tree, O most pure Virgin, covering with thy shadow those scorched by the heat of iniquities; cover us also with it, who are burned by passions, and let us sing: Rejoice, tree with fair and leafy shade, beneath which many are sheltered!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
As one named for immortality,[^117] after thy death thou didst rise alive; and having shown how exceedingly bitter is death, thou didst weep bitterly; now having found unending joy, pray for us, that we may escape eternal weeping!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire touched not, neither did it distress Thy children in the furnace, O Savior; then the three as with one mouth sang and blessed, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Having spent thy life in weeping and groaning, and having made white the robe of the angelic habit with streams of thy tears, thou didst depart unto the tearless regions, rejoicing and singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The angelic ranks arrived on the day of thy falling asleep, and having peacefully received thy spirit, they conducted it unto the paradise mansions, where living, remember us,

*(Right page — Folio 35 recto, running header: TO ATHANASIUS THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
O venerable father, who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
Twice wast thou a dead man in this life, O holy one: and first indeed all saw thee returning again unto bodily form, and now loosed a second time, thou rejoicest with the angelic ranks; where forget not us also who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Make haste to bring me to life, who am dead by evil deeds, O Sovereign Lady: for in thee have I placed my hope without doubting, as having brought forth the immortal King and Giver of life unto all the world!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O heaven of heavens, and ye waters that are above the heavens, bless, sing of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The heavens have joyfully received thy spirit into their bosom, and we rejoice bowing down before thy relics, O venerable one; from them grant grace unto those in need, and make us worthy to sing of the Lord unto the ages!

The angels celebrate having thee as a fellow citizen, and the Caves mountain rejoices, containing the shrine of thy relics as a most radiant pearl; by this enlighten us who are held fast by the darkness of passions, and grant that we may sing of the Lord unto the ages!

Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.

Thou wast well-pleasing in this life unto thy Master, O father, and didst receive the gift of healing sicknesses: visit us who are held fast by passions, as through prayers thou didst heal the sick Babylas,[^118] and make us worthy to sing of the Lord unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I am in sorrow and grief, reflecting upon my defiled life, and have no hope: unto thee alone, the Joy of all the world, do I flee, O pure Virgin, and pray that joy may shine forth upon my soul unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
He hath showed strength with His arm, for He hath put down the mighty from their seats, and exalted the humble, the God of Israel; wherein the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and hath guided us into the way of peace.[^119]

*Troparia:*
Show love unto us who pray unto thee, O father, and by thy prayer grant healing unto those who suffer with sicknesses of soul: especially direct into the way of peace those who are warred upon by the assaults of the enemy!

The Great Lavra sings of thy labors and ascetic deeds, O venerable one, and the cave ever glorifies thee, preserving thy wonderworking relics: bowing down before which we also pray thee: Direct our steps into the way of peace!

*(Left page — Folio 35 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
Arise swiftly, and come, heal my soul, O father Athanasius most blessed: for behold, out of great sickness of soul I cry unto thee: having no relief, I rather pray thee: guide me into the peaceful path of salvation!

*Glory...*
Glory to Thee, All-Holy Trinity, O Father unoriginate, with the co-eternal Son, and the Spirit of Thine Holy Spirit: Whom grant unto my heart, through the intercession of Thy pleasing one, that I may find the peaceful path!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
If anyone glorifies not thy pure conception, and sings not of thine ineffable childbearing, O Mary Bride of God, let him fall away from eternal glory, and let him not be a partaker of the Light and a son of the ever-existing peace!

*Exapostilarion:*
With hymns let the people praise Athanasius, marvelous among the venerable, for alive he died, and after two days he rose, and again fell asleep; he lives no longer held by death, but began life anew as a partaker, as a son sharing in life, and an heir of the resurrection of the righteous!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Who will tell of thy marvelous mysteries, O pure one? Or who will declare the coming to pass of ineffable things in thee? For in time thou didst bring forth the Son begotten of the Father before the ages, the Firstborn of life and resurrection; entreat Him therefore, that we also may become heirs of resurrection and of life!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда от древа):*[^120]
Come, ye people, let us sing of daytime Athanasius, who is sung among the angels in heaven, the beauty of ascetics, and the radiance of the venerable; whose life emulating, he unflaggingly traversed the narrow sweat, having wounded every passion by abstinence; he had uncontainable desire toward God, before Whom he now stands with the angels, and prays for our souls!

Come, brethren and assemblies of feast-keepers, every company of God-bearing fathers, let us bring crowns of hymn-sung flowers, having journeyed unto the tomb of the most blessed father, and let us fall down, saying: O venerable and all-glorious one, fellow citizen with the angels, forget not thine intercession unto God, the Good of all, that we may obtain eternal life, and mercy on the day of judgment!

Come, ye choirs of monastics, unto the shrine of the many-miraculous relics of venerable Athanasius, and let us honor the divinely eloquent lips that instruct us unto obedience, and unto perpetual repentance and prayer, and by these virtues ascending unto God as upon fiery wings, may we find grace and great mercy!

*(Right page — Folio 36 recto, running header: TO ATHANASIUS THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
*Glory... Tone 8:*
O most blessed father,[^121] holy among the saints Athanasius, thine instruction became an amendment for many, and a raising up from falling through the remembrance of the judgment to come, which expecting thyself also after thy resurrection, thou didst pour forth inconsolable tears from thine eyes: standing now with joy before Christ, pray for those who sing of thy precious falling asleep, that they may receive remission of sins, and great mercy on the day of judgment!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, accept the entreaties of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow!

*(Left page — Folio 36 verso, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Nikon the Dry of the Caves standing bound in chains in the midst of a Polovtsian encampment, with an inscribed cartouche: "The holy one was brought invisibly into the Caves church of the Most Holy Mother of God"):*

*On the 11th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Nikon the Dry:*[^122]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Далъ еси знамение):*
Pillar of patient endurance, O Nikon glorious and God-bearing, firmly set in the love of God, wherein thou didst remain unmoved through many trials: therefore we glorify thee, O father, on this present day, celebrating thy memory, as one glorified in the heavens by Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!

Tried with fire and afflicted in every way,

*(Right page — Folio 37 recto, running header: TO THE VENERABLE NIKON THE DRY:)*
thou didst cry aloud thanksgiving, O blessed one, unto the Creator and God: therefore delivered from the hand of the impious, thou didst stand in the holy church,[^123] and now standing before Him in the church of those who keep festival, pray Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls, that we may be delivered from tribulations!

The fire of thy love toward God extinguished the sensible fire, and the streams of thy blood drowned the hosts of the noetic enemy, and thou didst conquer those who tortured thee, O venerable one: therefore also Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls, granted thee the honor of the heavenly ranks!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
The multitudes of monastics are amazed at thy patient endurance, how being afflicted by the infidels, tried by fire, and cut with knives, enduring heat and scorching, hunger and thirst, and having the hope of thy salvation in God, thou didst easily traverse the sweat of the body, and didst attain unto ultimate beatitude: blessed art thou having thus labored for Christ, and thou hast received the reward of thy labor, a dwelling with the angels, and communion in the divine Light: therefore we pray thee, O most blessed Nikon, as having boldness before Christ God, ask peace for the world, and great mercy for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 6:*
Come, assemblies named for Christ and loving Christ, and gatherings of monastic ranks: come, let us piously bring philosophic songs of praise as a gift unto the holy cave, and let us bow down before the relics of glorious Nikon, so that by his holy prayers we may receive the crown of unfading glory from Christ God, the Savior of our souls!

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Having loved the good God, Thou didst receive the monastic life, and having struggled well therein, Thou wast taken captive by the Polovtsians;[^124] being much tortured by them, O stout-hearted Nikon, by the grace of Christ Thou wast delivered from them invisibly: and now abiding in the unapproachable Light, pray that we may be saved who honor thy memory!

*Canon to the Saint, Tone 4:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast delivered Thy people from the bondage of Egypt, and covered the chariots and power of Pharaoh![^125]

*Troparion:*
Enable me, O Lord my God, to sing of the all-honorable memory of Thy venerable one, *(Left page — Folio 37 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
and to honor glorious Nikon in hymnody, send the all-holy Paraclete, that girded with vigilance I may offer a hymn unto Thee, Who givest strength unto all!

Who will worthily praise thee, O most blessed father, who from youth didst love thy Lord, and followed Him with voluntary poverty even unto death? Therefore living now with the angels, pray that strength unto patient endurance may be sent also unto us!

*Glory...*
Having crossed without stumbling through the darkness and gloom of the lustful world by burning love for Christ, and having been numbered with the brightness of the angelic habit, thou wast as if outside the flesh: therefore thou singest unto Him Who hath given thee strength!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Drive away, I pray thee, O pure Virgin, the darkness that has blinded my noetic eyes with passions: for thou art the light-receiving candle unto those who are in darkness, kindled by immaterial fire, and thou dost guide unto the understanding of the divine draught: Rejoice, lamp of unwaning Light!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
As the barren church from among the nations brought forth, and the assembly multiple in children languished,[^126] let us cry aloud unto our marvelous God: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
As she who was barren rejoices when she conceives in her womb, so the holy Caves mountain rejoices, having received thee as a light-bearer, and with monastic ranks sings: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

Emulating the first fathers, having joyfully laid the cross upon thy shoulder, O glorious Nikon, thou didst bear it magnanimously even unto death for the sake of the love of thy Lord, unto Whom thou now singest: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Glory...*
Armed with the shield of firm faith, thou didst go into captivity, O venerable one, and through magnanimity thou didst bind the enemy who sought to lead thee captive there into destruction, trampling the adversary with thy foot, and unto God Who strengthens thee thou didst sing: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Unable to overcome the enemy who rises up against me through the strength of passions, I flee unto thee, O Mother of God, help me: for thou art a strong pillar, a weapon in battle, and an unconquerable general!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Desiring to cultivate the field of thy soul, thou didst lay upon thyself the cross like a plow, and with this as with a plow thou didst cultivate the soil barren of passions, sowing with tears and prostrations of ascetic labors,[^127] from which now thou reapest joyful

*(Right page — Folio 38 recto, running header: TO NIKON THE DRY:)*
sheaves,[^128] standing before Christ God: Whom pray, O glorious Nikon, as being an unceasing intercessor, for those who lovingly bless thy holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, untilled field, that hast brought forth the incorrupt grain of wheat, from which the saving Bread was made for the world: with this nourish the souls of those who lovingly honor thine immaculate conception, O pure Virgin!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Merciful One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away: for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise!

*Troparia:*
Thy love toward God was strong, O father, for Whose sake desiring to suffer from the infidels, thou in no wise didst desire to be ransomed;[^129] for being beloved of the Lord Who loved thee, thou didst set thy hope upon Him: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

Having lovingly received the chastening of the Lord, sweetly didst thou pass through the trial of sicknesses, O blessed one, saying with David: Blessed is the man whom Thou chastenest, O Lord;[^130] therefore also thou criest: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Glory...*
Looking unto the things to come, thou didst count present pains as nothing, O beloved Nikon; though fire and frost and iron touched thee, yet all these flowed away: but thou didst remain like a strong adamant, saying: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Not looking unto the things to come, my soul has cleaved unto the earth, and I am become like senseless beasts:[^131] but do thou, O pure Virgin, open my noetic eyes, and make marvelous thy mercy upon me, that rejoicing I may cry: Holy art thou and marvelous in righteousness!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, O Lord my Light, art come into the world, the holy Light that turns from gloomy ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith!

*Troparia:*
Being afflicted in every way by the infidels, O father, thou didst not care for this, but didst cry aloud thanksgiving unto God, saying: Save me, O God, for the waters are entered into my soul:[^132] for Thou alone art He Who brings out those who are bound with courage, who sing of Thee in faith![^133]

Thy patient endurance, O most blessed one, confounded the Polovtsian who had taken thee captive; therefore for three years he continued torturing thee every day, afflicting thee with hunger, thirst, cold, and heat: but thou, O holy one, didst have hope in God, that He will deliver those who sing of Him in faith!

*Glory...*
The noetic enemy, seeing the streams of thy blood, the cutting of the sinews of thy shins,[^134] and other torments, caused the Polovtsian to hope; but neither hearing that deliverance was about to come unto thee, he placed heavy fetters *(Left page — Folio 38 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
to be placed upon thee: but thou, O father, didst cry aloud in the words of the Psalm: O God, attend unto my help![^135] For Thou alone art He Who savest those who sing of Thee in faith!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
With the arrows of thy help, O Virgin Bride of God, drown the invisible enemies who drag my soul into the abyss of Hades with the chains of passions, that being free from them I may cry unto thee: Rejoice, sea that hast drowned the noetic Pharaoh!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah prayed in the sea monster: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparia:*
The iron of sorrows pierced thy soul, O glorious Nikon, until through suffering the martyr gave the deliverance of release sent unto thee swiftly from God, unto Whom thou didst cry aloud: In Thee, O Lord, have I hoped; in Thy righteousness deliver me, O Jesus, King of powers![^136]

*Glory...*
As once with Habakkuk,[^137] the right hand of the Most High seized thee invisibly; and those who guarded thee were amazed, hearing a voice from on high, and being unable to overtake God, for He alone is King of powers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O strong Deliverer, deliver me from the pit of the adversaries, for behold, my head is swallowed into the abyss, and I shall not come forth from the violent bars of Hades, unless thou grant me help, who art the Mother of the King of powers!

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Emulating the First Martyr,[^138] thou didst pray for those who tortured thee, and having delivered him from death, thou didst lead him unto Christ, O venerable martyr, glorious Nikon: therefore with the first sufferer beholding Christ at the right hand of the Father, pray for us, that we may not be cast outside the bridal chamber of Christ!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamite children of old in Babylon trampled the flame of the furnace, crying aloud in hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^139]

*Troparia:*
While the time of the dread celebration of the mysteries was taking place in the church, seized by divine power from captivity, thou didst stand in the midst of the brethren, O glorious Nikon, and thy fetters and the blood dripping from thy shins struck all with awe, who lifted up their hands for thy deliverance, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

He who formerly held thee in captivity, O father, seeing thee afterward free in the midst of the brethren by the right hand of the Most High, came to compunction, and being baptized, made himself captive unto the obedience of Christ, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Glory...*
Blessed are the captives who have thee as a wise priest: for through thee, freed from sickness, they were delivered from the hands of the impious, and that which is said in the Scripture was fulfilled:

*(Right page — Folio 39 recto, running header: TO NIKON THE DRY:)*
The prayer of a righteous man avails much, working with power.[^140]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Taken captive by noetic enemies, and bound with the chains of passions by fierce Belial, unto thee do I cry, O unwedded Mother: tear me away from them, that being free I may cry: Rejoice, thou who didst bring forth the Deliverer of captives!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
All-powerful Deliverer of all, coming down in the midst of the flame Thou didst moisten those who were pious, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and sing of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Fearing the Deliverer of all and thine own Savior, in meekness of feet, O father, thou didst fear the torturer who sought to crucify thee: but rendering good for evil, thou didst heal him lying in sickness, so that he also, turning, might bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

In the earthen vessel of thy body,[^141] how great a treasure of wonderworking do we honor, O blessed one, and we glorify the heavenly Father Who hath given thee such things for our profit; with this heal us also, we pray thee, that through thee we may sing of Him Who works marvelous things, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.

Thou didst stand against the world and the flesh, and having bound both, thou didst subject them unto the spirit: therefore having ascended unto the world on high, thou rejoicest, and with the angels singest, and highly exaltest the Lord unto all ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Stand by to help us who swiftly call upon thee, O most pure Virgin, and strengthen us against the world and the flesh that lust against the spirit: for behold, we bring glorious Nikon unto thee in prayer, for whose sake grant us to highly exalt thee in the light of the saints unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed through the disease of disobedience introduced the curse, but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the budding of the childbearing of thy womb, didst cause blessing to blossom forth upon the world: therefore we all magnify thee!

*Troparia:*
Desiring to heal the sickness of the noetic eye of their soul, the company of valiant fathers assembled on the Caves mountain, among whom glorious Nikon, shining like a star, enlightens us with the rays of his prayers: therefore we magnify him!

Thou wast illumined with noetic light, who formerly, through the holding fast of passions, didst turn away thine eyes from seeing vanity: therefore we magnify Christ, Who hath confirmed His word unto His servant in Thy fear!

*Glory...*
Marvelous are Thy works, O uncreated Trinity, and Thou art glorified in Thy saints, O God of Israel: through the prayers of Thy pleasing one Nikon, make marvelous Thy grace also upon us, *(Left page — Folio 39 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
by the turning away from unfitting deeds, that we may ever magnify Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Marvelous works of His dispensation hath the Most High wrought upon thee, O unwedded Virgin, and all generations of the earth-born magnify thee, blessing thee from generation to generation: through the prayers of the captives of the Caves, grant that we also may magnify thee unto all ages!

*Exapostilarion:*
By strong abstinence and by suffering from the infidels, thou didst slay the serpent of thy straying flesh, O father, and after thy falling asleep thou didst receive a double crown of thy virtues; and living now in the heavens, as a glory of the venerable and a praise of the martyrs, pray for us, that we also may be partakers of thy glory, O wise Nikon!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Our race having dwelt in darkness through intemperance, thou who by thy childbearing didst lead us unto the light, deliver us from intemperance, and guide us unto the light of the commandments of thy Son, that we may sing unto thee, O Theotokos: Rejoice, unfading crown of temperance!

*At the Aposticha, Glory... Tone 5:*
Come, ye people, let us bless the earthly angel and heavenly citizen, equal of the venerable, glorious Nikon; for having struggled in tribulations free from harm, and having received double crowns through his ascetic struggle, he stands before Christ God, unceasingly praying for this city and monastery, that it may obtain great mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, in the same Tone.*

*(Right page — Folio 40 recto, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Mark the Caves Gravedigger in a cave digging a grave with a shovel, with two lower vignettes: a deceased monk pouring oil upon himself, and a second deceased monk arising and waiting until morning):*

*On the 24th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Caves Fathers Mark and Theophilus:*[^142]
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Радуйся):*
Desiring to build a house of virtues, thou didst lay the foundation of patient endurance, O all-blessed father Mark, and the stormy sea of passions thou didst tame with the calm winds of humility; and the enemy who burns with furious lusts and sensual pleasures thou didst drown in the abyss of fasts, and with the help *(Left page — Folio 40 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES:)*
of the grace of God, having crossed the untroubled deep of this life, thou didst attain unto the desired goal: where standing now before Christ, pray that He may grant great mercy unto our souls!

When thou didst hear the voice of the Lord calling unto the heavenly banquet, O venerable one, while many wander on the path of life and through hardness of heart reject the heavenly calling, thou didst leave the turmoil of this age, and having run swiftly, having thy lamp pre-adorned with love, thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal chamber; that we also may be accounted worthy to enter therein, we pray thee: pray fervently unto the Good One, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and great mercy!

Despising the passing love of this age, O venerable father, and the flesh that is pampered with sensual pleasures, by ever-abiding love thou didst fix thy mind upon God, for Whose sake taking up thy cross, thou didst crucify all thy members, and having slain the ruler of this world, thou wast a radiance of ascetics and an adornment of the venerable: with whom standing before the Good One, delay not to pray Him to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехвальнии мученицы):*
O most blessed Mark, for the sake of the love of Christ having left the spacious life, thou didst settle in a narrow cave, wherein, that thou mightest ever have the memory and hour of death before thine eyes, thou didst prepare graves with thine own hands for the deceased brethren: standing now before Him, pray that He may grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

O venerable father, the dead, hearing thy voice, obeyed thy command, and having received breath again, came to life, until thou didst command them to go unto the desired Good One; before Whom thou also standing after thy falling asleep, fervently pray to grant our souls unending life and great mercy!

O father worthy of wonder, having burdened thyself with many labors, thou didst humble thine evil-working domestic enemy,[^143] and having bound him with chains, thou didst subject him unto the spirit; therefore thou wast a praise of monastics, with whom pray to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory... Tone 5:*
Who will worthily tell of thy labors and ascetic deeds, O venerable father! For since thou didst receive the angelic habit upon thyself, thou didst not give rest unto thy body, but didst lift up thine eyes unto the Lord, and didst ever direct thy lips unto prayer,

*(Right page — Folio 41 recto, running header: TO MARK AND THEOPHILUS:)*
thou didst stretch out thine hearing unto the listening to the word of God, and didst strengthen thy hands unto the digging of graves: blessed art thou having in all ways labored for Christ, before Whom now standing, pray to bring peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4:*
Glory to God in the highest, I hear today in Bethlehem from the bodiless hosts, on earth peace unto Him Who hath been pleased to be born! Now the Virgin is wider than the heavens: for the Light hath dawned upon those in darkness, and hath exalted the humble, who sing in angelic fashion: Glory to God in the highest![^144]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three Readings (see fol. 30v).*[^145]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having abode three days" (Тридневен):*
*I behold an awesome mystery... (see in the Menaion on the same day).*

*Glory... to the Venerable Ones, Tone 8:*
Come, ye people, assembled today in the yearly memory of the God-bearing father, behold how, being in this mortal body, he held sway over death: by a word he called back the dead man, and having obeyed his voice, he himself poured the oil upon himself;[^146] another also by his command yielded his place unto another, and yet another after his falling asleep revived again, remaining until a place was prepared for him.[^147] Therefore, praising him with hymns of praise as having received so great grace from God, we pray that he may entreat Christ to raise us up from our transgressions, and deliver our souls from tribulations!

*Both now... of the Feast, the same Tone:*
O Lord, coming unto Bethlehem, Thou didst dwell in a cave; having heaven as a throne, Thou wast laid in a manger; Thou Whom hosts of angels surround, art come down unto the shepherds, that as Compassionate Thou mightest save our race: glory to Thee!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having mortified carnal lusts by much abstinence, and by digging graves for the holy ones, living in the cave even as one dead, by thy command thou didst raise up the dead, O all-praised Mark! Mortify our carnal mind also, and guide us into the course of virtues, praying for us unto the only Lover of mankind!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast.*
*Glory... of the Venerable One. Both now... of the Feast.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ликъ ангельский):*
Having received the divine voice into thy heart, having left the world, thou didst follow the calling of thy Lord: therefore taking the cross as a staff into thy hands, *(Left page — Folio 41 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES:)*
thou didst gently cross the sea of this life upon it; and the noetic Pharaoh thou didst drown in the abyss of thy labors, and thou wast an adornment of the venerable, O Mark worthy of wonder!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
The voice of Gabriel do we offer unto thee, O pure Virgin: Rejoice, thou who didst conceive God the Creator of all, and didst renew the aged human nature; Rejoice, for thou wast a Virgin in childbearing, and after childbearing thou remainest a Virgin still!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate" (Собезначальное):*
Who will worthily declare Thy love toward mankind, O Master, shown unto those on earth, that Thou hast given us Thy Cross unto help, which Thy pleasing one having taken into his hands, conquered all the demonic hosts? For this became a breastplate unto him and a helmet of salvation; this was food and sweet drink; this was his glory and an adornment before the venerable!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Who will worthily praise thy childbearing, O most pure one? For seedlessly didst thou conceive of the Holy Spirit, and in time didst bring forth the Word begotten of the Father before the ages: therefore honoring thee in hymns as the Mother of our true God, we sing and magnify thee!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премудрости):*
Who will declare thy labors and ascetic deeds, O all-blessed one! For having been a faithful servant unto thy Lord, and faithfully serving Him, thou didst multiply the talent entrusted unto thee, and with the good laborers thou didst receive the denarius of eternal life:[^148] therefore as having boldness, pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Spending the time of my life in slothfulness, I have lost the talent entrusted unto me by my Lord, and now stripped of the grace of God, I cry unto thee, O pure one: by thy maternal prayers be a good intercessor for me unto God the Lover of mankind; remember thy kindred providence for thy servants, and ask forgiveness of sins for those who lovingly honor and piously bow down before thine all-holy childbearing!

*Gradual Antiphon: 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 44.*[^149]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 2 (see the whole at Vespers):*
*Who will worthily tell of thy labors and ascetic deeds...*

*(Right page — Folio 42 recto, running header: TO MARK AND THEOPHILUS:)*
*Canon of the Feast, to 6.*
*And of the Venerable Ones, to 6.*
*Tone 4, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us all the people sing a song of victory unto Him Who delivered Israel from bitter servitude to Pharaoh, and led them dryshod through the depth of the sea, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparia:*
Let us all sing, having assembled in this all-honorable temple, to praise the memory of the pleasing one of God, for He hath been gloriously glorified in the name of Christ His Lord!

Having received the angelic habit upon thyself, thou didst struggle in the cave in angelic fashion, digging with thine own hands many places for the deceased brethren; from these thou didst pass into fair and marvelous places, to sing unto the Lord: He hath been gloriously glorified!

That thou mightest have death before thine eyes, O father, thou didst dig graves for the brethren who departed unto Christ, carrying out the earth upon thy shoulders: therefore after thy falling asleep thy death was precious in the sight of the Lord, O blessed one, and with the angels thou singest unto God Who is glorified in His saints: For He hath been glorified!

*Glory...*
Laboring out of love toward all, thou didst prepare graves freely, receiving nothing from anyone, but only recompense from God; therefore now Thy reward is great in the heavens, O venerable one, where thou singest unto God: For He hath been glorified!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Who can worthily praise thee, the all-glorious Mother of God, throughout all generations? Therefore neither do I dare beyond my mind, but only offer the angelic greeting: Rejoice!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish me, O Christ, upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, and enlighten me with the light of Thy countenance: for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Established upon the rock of the commandments of the Lord, desiring to be a house of the Holy Spirit, thou didst bind thy loins with irons, O blessed one, which passing through thy soul every day, kindled thee to sing: There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!

Setting clean prayer as a gate and severe fasting as a strong wall—[for instead of sweet mead, thou didst drink only water]—[^150] thou wast shown forth as an unapproachable and fearsome temple unto enemies, O father, whom having conquered to the end, with joy thou didst cry aloud: There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!

Taking vigilance, fasting, and the girding of the loins with irons as weapons upon thyself, thou didst go forth unto the root of death that burned thee with carnal passions: therefore the Lord granted thee to have dominion *(Left page — Folio 42 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES:)*
over them, as thou didst cry aloud: There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!

*Glory...*
Death was unto thee as a captive, O holy one, for obeying thee against its will, it returned the dead, and they remained alive until thou didst command them to fall asleep again, and to remain in the graves until the general resurrection!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Being taken captive by invisible enemies, deliver me, O Sovereign Lady, by thy swift intercession, driving them away by power as by fire, so that being saved through thee, I may ever sing unto thee: There is no other intercessor save thee, O most pure Sovereign Lady of the world!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Living in abstinence, thou didst subject natural passions unto the spirit, having become an angel in the flesh, and fulfilling in deed that which was commanded in the Law: therefore thou didst receive from God the gift of wonderworking, to raise the dead unto life by a word; and having thyself departed unto eternal life, pray for us, O holy one, that we who lovingly honor thy holy memory may be delivered from eternal death, O Mark worthy of wonder!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having conceived beyond nature and beyond nature brought forth thy Creator and God, pray for us who ever sin, that being delivered, with love we may sing unto thee, O pure Virgin Mary!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Great is the mystery of Thy dispensation, O Christ: for perceiving this from on high with divinely-beholding vision, Habakkuk cried aloud unto Thee: Unto the salvation of Thy people, O Lover of mankind![^151]

*Troparia:*
Great is the mystery of Thy word, O Christ, for Thou didst say: He that believes in Me, the works that I do shall he do also;[^152] as Thou Thyself didst raise dead Lazarus by Thy voice, so unto Thy pleasing one also Thou didst give power to raise the dead by a word, and to proclaim glory unto the garden,[^153] unto the astonishment of all who stood by, O Lover of mankind!

Our God is merciful, and He Who breaks down the middle wall of enmity,[^154] so that there might be no enmity against His servant on account of the grave not prepared for the deceased, took away this malice through a wonder; for He moved the spirit of His pleasing one to say unto the dead man: Brother, the place is narrow; move thyself, and pour oil upon thyself! And thus when the word was fulfilled in deed, all cried aloud: Glory to Thee, O Lover of mankind!

Being in simplicity of heart and deep humility, the venerable one gave command unto the dead man's spirit to return and perform that which was commanded, for he poured oil upon himself, and returned the vessel

*(Right page — Folio 43 recto, running header: TO MARK AND THEOPHILUS:)*
and fell asleep again; which all seeing with fear and awe, cried aloud: Glory to Thy dispensation, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory...*
The prayer of Thy humble cave-dweller, O Lord, passed through the clouds: whom we also bring unto Thee for propitiation, and we pray: Be merciful unto us Thy servants, take away enmity from Thy people, and grant us Thy peace unto our salvation, O Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou who didst bring forth eternal Life, and by thy childbearing didst give life unto all, bring to life us who are slain by the poison of sin, and with the oil of thy mercy deal bountifully with us, that we may cry unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: Thou art the cause and deliverance of our life!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early from the night we sing of Thee, O Christ, Co-unoriginate with the Father, and Savior of our souls: grant peace unto the world, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Rising early from the night unto prayer to God, O blessed one, spending the time, thou didst not prepare the grave for the deceased; therefore thou didst say: Go, tell the deceased to wait until morning, until I dig the place, and we will bury him, glorifying the Lover of mankind!

According to thy word, O venerable one, when he that was sent said: Brother, Mark says unto thee: live yet a little while, immediately the dead man opened his eyes, in the sight of all who looked on, and remained until morning: O marvelous wonder! As Thou Thyself, O God, hast dominion over life and death, so Thou grantest Thy servants also to hold dominion, O Lover of mankind!

Laboring night and day, thou didst prepare the place for burial, and didst say, O holy one: Tell the deceased: yield up thy spirit unto God! And he, having obeyed thee, closed his eyes and departed; and all, being astonished at the power of thy word, glorified God, the Lover of mankind!

*Glory...*
A strange wonder, that the dead should raise the dead! But unto those who heed God, all things work together for good;[^155] therefore all things come to pass unto our building up, that with warmer love we may flee unto the Master, and with the venerable ones glorify Him as the compassionate Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
A strange wonder, how in a bodily womb thou didst receive the divine Fire, O Virgin, and didst remain unconsumed, even as of old the bush was unburned,[^156] which all took place that the mystery of divine dispensation might be fulfilled in thee, O pure one!

*(Left page — Folio 43 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES:)*
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou didst save the Prophet from the sea monster, O Lover of mankind; and lead me up from the depth of transgressions, I pray Thee![^157]

*Troparia:*
Cleansing the defilement of self-indulgence, O blessed one, thou didst command deceased John to yield the higher place unto Theophilus: O wonder! The dead man, having obeyed, moved unto the lower place, and all were filled with fear; but thou didst thus bow down with humility, saying: Lead me up from the corruption of enmity, O God, I pray Thee![^158]

When Theophilus fell down at thy feet, asking forgiveness, thou didst say, O venerable one: The Lord Himself, deriding enmity, hath commanded the dead man out of love to yield seniority unto thee; but that thou mayest be delivered from the noetic sea monster who awaits thee, pray unto the Lover of mankind with hymnody!

Thy word was like thunder, O father, from the fear of which Theophilus was terrified, thinking that he had fallen and would die; but coming a little to himself, and entering his cell, he spent the rest of his years weeping, saying: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Glory...*
Thy chastening, O most blessed father, brought Theophilus unto such great humility that no one was able to raise him up from weeping, but tears became food and drink unto him; for day and night he cried aloud: Lead me up from my fall into sin, O God, I pray Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having slipped into the corruption of self-indulgence, and possessing no compunction, grant me streams of tears, O Sovereign Lady, that having washed away the defilement of my soul, I may appear pure before the countenance of God, when His glory shall appear unto me!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
With love let us the faithful bless the excellent physician and wonderworker, asking him that by his prayers he may heal our passions, both of soul and body; for he hath grace from God for this, and drives away deceitful spirits from all who faithfully flee unto his tomb and cry aloud: Rejoice, Mark, healer of our infirmities!

*Ikos:*
Hymning the Creator of the angels and of all creation, and having ever served Him upon earth with an angelic life, with the angels we now offer the thrice-holy hymn unto thee, O all-blessed one, crying thus: Rejoice, thou who didst renounce all earthly things! Rejoice, thou who didst fix all thy mind on high! Rejoice, for as one dead while living thou didst bury thyself in the cave! Rejoice, for therein thou didst accomplish many labors and sweats! Rejoice, thou who didst dig graves for many holy ones therein! Rejoice, thou who by thy command didst raise up the dead!

*(Right page — Folio 44 recto, running header: TO MARK AND THEOPHILUS:)*
Rejoice, thou who didst cause dead John to move from his place! Rejoice, thou who didst guide Theophilus unto salvation! Rejoice, thou who without heaviness didst bear a great cross and an iron belt upon thyself! Rejoice, thou who by thine own cross didst heal the humble body with a single prosphora and measured water! Rejoice, thou who didst heal many passions! Rejoice, thou who dispensest help in needs unto all who flee unto thy tomb! Rejoice, Mark, healer of our infirmities!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the concord of martyrs summoned the people to serve the image, the youths of David, having suffered in the flame of Sion, destroyed the impious decree of the tyrant, and turned the flame into dew, singing the hymn: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art highly exalted!

*Troparia:*
When the time for thee to appear before the countenance of God was drawing near, O father, thou didst call the blessed Theophilus, saying: Forgive me, for I have grieved thee; for behold, I am already departing, but do thou pray for me, that we both may be accounted worthy to sing with the saints: Blessed art Thou, O God!

Thy words, O holy one, added lamentation unto him that was weeping; for unable to endure being left without thee, he said: I know that the Lord will hear thee, if Thou wilt to take me with thee, to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

Comforting him that was weeping, thou didst say, O venerable one, that he should not grieve, nor seek to see sensible light; for weeping for sins with noetic eyes, he shall see intelligible Light, and be accounted worthy of the beatitude of those who weep, for they that weep shall be comforted, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Glory...*
For the consolation of him who was sorrowing, O father, thou didst make known unto him this sign of his departure unto God: that three days before his end he should regain his sight, and thus pass unto the tearless places, to sing with the saints in unapproachable Light: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Being in the sorrow of my despair, I have no consolation; therefore unto thee, the Sovereign Lady of the world, do I flee, saying: O my Joy, deliver me from those that have surrounded me, for many dogs have encompassed me, and there is none able to deliver me save thee, O blessed Virgin!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sing of the Lord and highly exalt Him unto all ages, Who preserved the children in the fiery burning furnace, and came down unto them in the form of an angel!

*Troparia:*
The Holy Spirit, Who grants the gift of prophecy, brought thy words unto fulfillment, *(Left page — Folio 44 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES:)*
O venerable one: after thy falling asleep, thou didst commit thy spirit into the hands of God; before Whom now standing, thou singest with the prophets: Sing of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

Thy words were unto blessed Theophilus more than refined silver, O venerable one; remembering which, though he wept in his heart, yet he was not able to extinguish them, for they were kindled in his heart: for all his days he awaited his end, desiring to go unto the Lord, to highly exalt Him in praises unto all ages!

By thy words, O blessed one, thou didst dig in the heart of Theophilus a great gift of compunction and an inexhaustible fountain: for as much as he poured into the vessel, so much more did the streams of tears multiply,[^159] wherewith he extinguished the passionate flame that hindered from singing of the Lord unto the ages!

Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.

Thrice-radiant Light, All-Holy Trinity, fulfilling the promise of Thy pleasing one Mark, Thou didst enlighten the pupils of the weeping one, and he rightly beheld the light with sensible eyes, and with the noetic eyes his journey unto Thee his God, Whom with all his heart he desired to sing of with the saints unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou who didst bring forth the true Light, O most pure one, enlighten me with thy grace, who wander in sinful darkness and have no vision of repentance: for thou art the light-giver, having appeared swiftly as a light unto those in darkness, O Virgin Theotokos!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, the Light of lights and Mother of God, the most marvelous glory, higher than all creatures, in hymns do we magnify!

*Troparia:*
According to thy word, O holy one, Theophilus, having learned of his departure three days before, uttered words of repentance; and immediately, for the soothing of his lamentation, an angel stood before him, showing him a vessel filled with tears, blessing his repentance as acceptable unto God!

By thine instruction, O father, divinely-wise Theophilus, having sown with tears in his life, departed unto the heavenly dwellings, and reaps joyful sheaves, and now receives the recompense of his labors, blessed by all unto the ages!

In the valley of weeping, O all-blessed fathers, ye chose to dig and labor with tears all the years of your life, that ye might find the pearl of great price, Jesus,[^160] with Whom having also ransomed yourselves from eternal death, ye now rejoice, being blessed in a place of light!

*Glory...*
The pair of fathers, Mark and Theophilus, do we bring unto Thee in prayer, O Christ God, Who existest in Two Wills and Operations;[^161] receiving whose entreaty, and preserving us from the crafty wiles of the enemy by Thy Holy Spirit, direct us to magnify Thee unto all ages!

*(Right page — Folio 45 recto, running header: TO MARK AND THEOPHILUS:)*
*Both now... Theotokion:*
How shall we bless thee, O pure Virgin, whom the heavenly hosts are not able worthily to bless? How shall we glorify her who is glorified throughout all generations, and the blessed Mother of all Christians?

*Exapostilarion:*
Thy tears became like fragrant myrrh, wherewith thou didst cleanse the defilement of self-indulgence; and shining now in the dwellings on high like a most radiant star, illumine us who live in sinful darkness, and pray, O Theophilus our father, with blessed Mark unto Christ the Lover of mankind, that walking here in the light of His commandments, we may be accounted worthy to behold the light of heavenly glory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
Our Savior, the Dayspring from on high, hath visited us, and we who were in darkness and the shadow have found the truth: for the Lord was born of the Virgin![^162]

*At the Praises, Stichera, to 6: of the Feast, to 3; and of the Venerable Ones, Tone 5, Automelon:*
Every sweetness of this life didst thou despise for the sake of the sweetness on high, having emulated the hard path of the all-blessed fathers, and by thy many labors thou didst make haste to extinguish the furnace of passions, having become an honorable adornment of those who have embraced monastic life: therefore rejoicing now with the angels on high, pray for us, O venerable Mark!

By the narrow and hard path didst thou journey from earth unto heavenly glory, surrendering thyself unto many ascetic deeds and sorrows for the sake of the love of God: therefore now standing ever before the stream, thou dost enjoy its splendors; and unto us also who honor thy memory, grant through thy prayers communion in divine things, and the receiving of eternal joy, O venerable Mark!

Ascending in mind unto the unwaning Light, O glorious one, and delivered from the darkness of this life, thou didst stand before the All-ruler, the Thrice-radiant Light, with the ranks on high; therefore pray through thy prayers, O venerable Mark, that we also who bow down before the shrine of thy relics and glorify thy precious falling asleep, may be illumined with the beam of the brightness sent down thence!

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Good branches of the vineyard of Christ, who have brought forth profitable clusters of grapes for us, from which fragrant wine pours forth, making glad *(Left page — Folio 45 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES:)*
the hearts of those who draw near: let us make haste to praise them with love! Beholding the cross and iron chains of the one, we kiss the relics of him who bore them, and receive healing of passions; and contemplating the other, we remember his tears, and receive compunction! O good company of all-blessed fathers! Therefore we pray you, O God-bearing Mark with Theophilus, as standing before Christ God, ask for your servants to receive remission of sins and great mercy!

*Both now... of the Feast, in the same Tone:*
Heaven and earth are united today, Christ being born; today God is come upon earth, and man hath ascended unto the heavens; today He Who is invisible by nature is seen in the flesh for the sake of man:[^163] therefore we also, glorifying Him, cry aloud: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace! For Thy coming hath granted it, O our Savior: glory to Thee!

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service is common to Venerable Fathers.*

*(Right page — Folio 46 recto, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: The Venerable Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves in monastic habit, with a lower cartouche vignette: Gregory drowned in the river by command of Prince Rostislav, with a heavy stone tied to his neck):*

---

# MONTH OF JANUARY

*On the 8th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves:*[^164]
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," we sing Stichera, to 6: Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехвальнии мученицы):*
O father Gregory, kindled by the fire of love toward God, thou didst burn up the passions that rise up against the spirit; therefore living in frail flesh, thou wast joined unto the angelic life, with whom now taking up thy dwelling, pray Christ God to grant unto our souls *(Left page — Folio 46 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...peace and great mercy!

O father Gregory, giving heed unto the words of David, thou gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thy eyelids,[^165] until, having cleansed thyself of passions, and having received the ladder of the vision of God, thou didst ascend unto the purest Light, where now living, pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls!

O father Gregory, the working of thy miracles has traversed all the Russian land, crying aloud like a loud-sounding trumpet, and summoning the choirs of feast-lovers to praise thy precious memory; with whom we also, offering praise and bowing down before the shrine of thy relics, pray to obtain peace and great mercy through thee!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
O instructor of the multitudes of monastics, honoring thy life we marvel, and we glorify God Who hath glorified thee, for He granted thee abundant working of miracles, whereby thou didst drive away demons from men, and didst lead those who sinned unto correction; entreat Him also on our behalf, who honor the day of thy precious falling asleep, that He may grant mercy on the Day of Judgment!

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*
The hand of the Baptist trembled when it touched Thy most pure head; the Jordan river turned backward, not daring to minister unto Thee: for as it stood in awe before Joshua the son of Nun, how was it not to be terrified of its Creator? But Thou didst fulfill the whole dispensation, O our Savior, that Thou mightest save the world by Thy manifestation, O only Lover of mankind!

*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and 3 Readings (see fol. 1v):*[^166]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 7, Podoben: "On the third day" (Тридневен):*
Having fallen through passions, our nature the Master renews, having gone down into the waters; and thee, O holy one, who camest unto the streams of waters, the Lover of mankind renewed, sending thee before unto life eternal.

*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.*
Unto Christ Who renewed our human nature with water and the Spirit, thou wast zealous, O venerable one; therefore for the sake of truth thou wast drowned in water; having finished thy course therein, thou now rejoicest with Christ beside the waters of rest.

*Verse: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.*
The Master Who stands in the streams of Jordan breaks the bonds of hell from the human race, Who also released thee, O blessed one, through water, when He brought forth thy spirit from the prison of the flesh.

*Glory... Tone 5:*
Thy life, adorned with the brightness of divine virtues, journeys throughout the Russian land, and the shrine of thy holy relics sends forth beams of miracles unto all who draw near with faith. Grant therefore unto us also a token of thy love: for though thou hast departed, yet spiritually thou dost ever look down, and seeing the warm entreaty of all, through thy prayers thou bestowest grace and great mercy from Christ God!

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Beholding Thee Who cleansest the sin of the world in water and fire coming unto him, the Baptist was seized with awe, and trembling cried aloud, saying: I dare not touch Thy most pure head; do Thou sanctify me, O Master, by Thy divine manifestation, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O recipient of great gifts, and by the power of God worker of great miracles, most marvelous father Gregory,[^167] as having great boldness before Christ God, unceasingly pray Him for us, that He may grant remission of sins unto our souls!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*(Right page — Folio 47 recto, running header: TO GREGORY THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord":*
*Troparion of the Feast, twice.*
*Glory... of the Saint. Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Скоро предвари):*
Desiring to inherit the beatitude of those who fear the Lord, in deed didst thou fulfill the commandments of God: all thy possessions that thou hadst, thou didst distribute for the glory of God, even unto those who asked deceitfully, O divinely-wise, most rich Gregory: therefore thy memory and righteousness abide unto ages of ages!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "The beauty of virginity" (Красотѣ девства):*
Unto Thee, O Master Christ, when Thou camest unto Jordan, the servant who faithfully joined himself unto Thee and followed in righteousness, being drowned by command of the prince, departed unto life that grows not old. Having him as a swift intercessor before Thy majesty, we glorify Thee, our God, in his memory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom" (Премудрости):*
Desiring the beatitude of the righteous and the choir of the angelic ranks, having despised all that is in the world, thou didst take no thought even for thy life itself: therefore thou wast not put to shame even before him who wore the diadem *(Left page — Folio 47 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...thou wast not put to shame, but didst instruct him like a father, bringing him unto repentance: being innocently drowned in water by him, thou didst depart unto Him Who covers Himself with waters, singing like a psalm: We went through fire and water, and Thou, O God, didst bring us out into Thy rest![^168] Where now living, pray that we also who honor thy holy memory with love may not be deprived thereof, O worthy of all wonder, Gregory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Anabathmoi, 1st Antiphon of Tone 1.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath...*
*Gospel according to Luke, composite pericope 24.*[^169]
*After Psalm 50, evening Sticheron: Tone 8, Multitudes of monastics:*

*Canon of the Feast, with its Heirmos, to 6; and of the Venerable One, to 6: Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The deep uncovered its bed, and through dry ground He leads His own, covering the adversaries therein, the Lord strong in battles: for He hath been glorified![^170]

*Troparia:*
Enlightener of Thine own righteous ones with the light of Thy grace, O Christ, Who hast illumined the ends of the world with the rays of their deeds: enlighten my mind, darkened by ignorance, that purely in the memory of Thy favored one I may sing a hymn unto Thee: For He hath been glorified!

With the fire of love Thou didst kindle the heart of Thy venerable one to run after Thee; and having found Him Whom he desired, he now joyfully sings: For He hath been glorified!

Desiring to emulate the life of the great fathers, thou camest, O Gregory, unto the monastery of the Mother of God, where taking upon thyself the angelic schema, thou didst form within thyself the likeness of virtues, and thy soul became a fair bride unto the immortal Bridegroom, Who hath now appeared at the Jordan!

*Glory...*
For the sake of the many ascetic deeds and labors performed by thee, though not numbered by count of time, thou didst receive power from God over demons, O venerable one, even as they cried aloud: Unapproachable art thou unto us, Gregory, hedged about by prayer and the weapon of the Cross!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
For the sake of the prayers of thy favored one, hedge me about with thy power, O pure Virgin, and show me unapproachable unto demons, as thou didst give birth unto the unapproachable God, Who in human nature now stands naked at the Jordan for my sake!

*(Right page — Folio 48 recto, running header: TO GREGORY THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The Lord Who gives strength unto our kings, and lifts up the horn of His anointed ones, is born of the Virgin, and comes unto baptism; unto Him let us, the faithful, cry aloud: None is holy like our God![^171]

*Troparia:*
Beholding the strength of thy prayer, the celestial spirits of wickedness fiercely raged against thee, and pondered how they might do thee harm: but thou, O father, didst not fear their terror, but cried aloud unto Him Who strengthens thee: The Lord is my strength unto salvation!

The rulers of darkness and sons of Gehenna gnashed their teeth against thee who wast illumined with divine Light, setting various snares for thy feet, if only they might afflict thee a little, but they could not: for unceasingly thou didst cry aloud: None is holy like our God!

The crafty demons who fall upon the servants of God in the manner of thieves incited evil men to rob thee, O blessed one; but what could they take from thee who hadst nothing, save the books kept for the praise of God?

*Glory...*
Darkened by the darkness of evil-doing, the thieves, coming to thy cell at night, O father, waited until thou shouldest go out into the church for Matins, that they might then take thy belongings; but having perceived their coming, thou didst pray, and they were weighed down with sleep for five days and five nights.[^172]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Drive away, O Mother of God, the gloom of my soul wherewith the demons darken me: for thou didst give birth unto the Giver of light, Who enlightens every man that comes into the world with the brightness of His face!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*
*Then, Sessional Hymn to the Venerable One, Tone 4:*
The divine Light ever shining upon thee stretched forth its rays and drove away those who walk in darkness, suffering them not to draw near unto thee: for everyone who does evil deeds does not come unto the light, lest he be reproved; but the adversary, having fled from this, fell down and was trodden under foot: pray Christ God, O venerable father, that we may be preserved from him!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*
He Who was born of the Virgin Mary, and baptized in the Jordan by John: the Holy Spirit descended upon Him in the form of a dove; therefore the Prophet cried aloud with the angels: Glory to Thy coming, O Christ! Glory to Thy kingdom! Glory to Thy dispensation, O only Lover of mankind!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, Thy voice which Thou didst utter, the voice of one crying in the wilderness, for Thou didst thunder over many waters, testifying *(Left page — Folio 48 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...testifying unto the Son; being wholly filled with the Spirit Who descended, he cried aloud: Thou art Christ, the Wisdom and Power of God![^173]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast heard by God, O father, for the prayer of thy humility passed through the clouds; therefore the Most High, receiving thy prayers, preserved thee from all evil: for thou didst confess the power and wisdom of God to be one!

Having fallen asleep with their sleep, the first thieves were able to snatch nothing because of thy prohibition, O blessed one, whom after five days and five nights thou didst awaken, saying: How long will ye suffer? Depart! But they were unable, held fast by the invisible power of God!

Seeing this, that the thieves were perishing with spiritual and bodily hunger, thou didst not render evil for evil, but fulfilling that which is written, thou didst feed bread unto the hungry enemies,[^174] and didst let them go unto their homes, that at least thus they might become sober-minded, and clearly confess that the wisdom and power of God truly work within thee!

*Glory...*
Thou didst commit the judgment of those who offended thee unto God, Whose vengeance did not pardon the thieves, but delivered them unto the ruler for trial, who, having seized them, was about to deliver them unto torments; but thou, taking human frailty into account, gavest thy books unto the judge for their ransom, and deliveredst them from bitter torments![^175]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Cruelly my adversary, conscience, contending with me concerning sins, with whom I did not agree on the way of life, now drags me unto the Judge, that I may receive judgment according to my deeds, and not go out thence until I have paid the uttermost farthing. Woe is me! Who will deliver me from this evil, unless thou, O pure one, give me help?

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Jesus, the Prince of life, comes to loose the condemnation of Adam the first-formed; needing no cleansing as God, He cleanses the fallen in the Jordan, wherein having slain the enmity, He grants peace which surpasses all understanding![^176]

*Troparia:*
Jesus, the Prince of life, directing sinners unto the right path, gave a right spirit unto the thieves, wherewith having been moved to compunction, they came unto repentance, and surrendered themselves unto labor for the monastery of the Mother of God, mindful of God, Who grants peace unto those who love His law!

Having loved Jesus, the only Prince of life, thou didst fulfill His word in deed, O father: for thou didst sell all that thou hadst, lest anyone should fall into misfortune again for their sake, and thou didst distribute that price unto the poor, and through their hands didst deposit it in the treasury where no thief approaches, but it remains safe!

*(Right page — Folio 49 recto, running header: TO GREGORY THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
Again the enemy sought to do thee harm, O venerable one, through other thieves, who having entered into thy orchard, filled their sacks with fragrant fruit, but wishing to go, they were unable, held fast by an invisible chain, and weighed down by the burden, they remained so for two days and two nights![^177]

*Glory...*
Being unable to conceal their heavy evil deed, behold, having the spoils of unrighteousness upon their shoulders for reproof, they cried aloud, asking forgiveness of thee, O father, promising to spend the rest of their years in repentance!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Though I wish to hide from the all-seeing God by my evil deeds, yet that which is in the reproof of inward things, like a great burden, covers my shame; not knowing therefore what else to do, I cry unto thee: Have mercy on me, O compassionate Mother, even though shame covers my face!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The voice of the words, the lamp of the Light, the morning star of the Sun, the Forerunner in the wilderness cries aloud repentance unto all people, and to be cleansed beforehand: for behold, Christ is at hand, Who delivers the world from corruption![^178]

*Troparia:*
The voice of thy words, O father, troubled those who came to steal, and their soul fainted from fear, so that they were as dead men in one place, and there was none to deliver them from so great an evil!

When the thieves heard the voice of the preacher calling unto repentance, they fell down before thee, O blessed one, with tears, asking forgiveness; and thou, moved to compunction at this, saidst with a gentle voice: Blessed is God Who strengthens you unto good work, and may He Who delivers all from corruption set you free!

When Christ appeared at the Jordan, publicans and all sinners came unto Him, and received the renewal of their life; emulating whom by thine instruction, O father, the thieves also began repentance in newness of life, and completed it by labor!

*Glory...*
Thou gavest an example of Thyself unto Thy servants, O Christ, that they be not tempted by Satan; therefore Thy favored one, being tempted a third time by evil men, was not tempted, but being guileless gave what they demanded, foreseeing the destruction of the one from which there was none to deliver him![^179]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Drawn by my own will, I have fallen into the pit of destruction, and there is *(Left page — Folio 49 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...there is no hope of being raised up for me: unto thee do I stretch forth my voice, O Lady, pray thy Son for me, Who today appeared at the Jordan, delivering not me only, but also the whole world from corruption!

*Kontakion, Tone 7:*
Rebuking the lawless that they should not act lawlessly, thou wast cast by them into the streams of the river, O Gregory worthy of all wonder;[^180] where having blessedly received thy end, thou now standest before the throne of the Master with the angels, praying for our souls!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The dewy spirit that refreshed and the descent of the angel of God preserved harmless the pious youths who were joined together in the fiery furnace; therefore, bedewed in the flame, they thankfully sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, praised above all![^181]

*Troparia:*
