Not having emulated the youths who were once in the furnace, the wretches who courageously trampled the flame and firmly resisted the tormentor, but yielding themselves unto the passionate flame at the counsel of the enemy, thought to hide their lie before thee, O father, unto whom the blessed God revealed it!

Seeing by the Spirit prophetically that which was inward in the hearts of the thieves, that it would truly come to pass, thou didst weep, O blessed one, foreseeing the misery of him whose day of destruction had arrived, and the blessed God did not grant everything unto him who loved it!

The Holy Spirit knows how to open even unclean mouths, that they may speak the truth for the correction of others; therefore when thou didst ask, O venerable one, by what death the son of perdition should die, the thieves, not knowing it, prophesied that he would be hanged upon a tree: which also came to pass in its own time![^182]

*Glory...*
The sting of death is sin, which kills man, and there is no ransom for this, until life is preserved by repentance; for though thou gavest thy books as a ransom for the thieves, O father, yet he who was guilty of destruction was not ransomed, but rather was his memory blotted out of the books of the living!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O noetic book, Mary Bride of God, in whom by the finger of the all-holy Spirit the Word of the Father was written: account all of us worthy to be written in the books of the living with the blessed!

*(Right page — Folio 50 recto, running header: TO GREGORY THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Babylonian furnace showed forth a most marvelous mystery, having poured forth dew, for the Jordan was about to receive the immaterial Fire in its streams, and to embrace the Creator being baptized in the flesh: Whom the people bless, and exalt unto all ages![^183]

*Troparia:*
Secretly foreseeing their crafty hearts, O father, the thieves shut thee up in the cave, and opened their thought unto evil-doing; but when they intended to gather the fruits of others' labors, the judgment of God overtook him of whom thou didst prophesy: that he should not bless the Lord with the living unto the ages![^184]

The wild boar of a thief, wishing to devour thy labors, O father, reached forth his hand, stepping upon a tree, but did not hold fast: for the branch broke, and immediately by hanging the tree choked out his life, so that he should no more bring forth the fruit of repentance unto the ages!

Let no man judge an unrighteous judgment, nor speak a lie: for God is not mocked. Mocking, the thieves condemned the one who was worthy of hanging upon a tree, and that which was spoken came to pass; then, O all-blessed one, thou dost exalt the Lord unto the ages!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*
Nothing being hidden from thee, O blessed father, falling down they asked forgiveness, that a like destruction might not come upon them also; but thou didst instruct those who stole not to steal any more, but rather to labor for the brethren, and to eat the blessed fruit, if they wish to exalt the Lord unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I have touched the fruit that is not blessed for me, and behold, I die with spiritual death; seeing thee therefore, O Mother of life, I stretch forth my voice: apply unto me the plaster of living repentance, that having come to myself at least a little, I may find life, and exalt thee, O Mother of God, unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee worthily, and even a transcendent mind is dazed to sing of thee, O Mother of God; yet being good, accept our faith: for thou knowest our divine love, for thou art the intercessor of Christians: thee do we magnify![^185]

*Troparia:*
At a loss how to afflict thee, O father, the enemy brought the foolish servants of the prince, who, when thou wast drawing water in the river, mocked thee, uttering gatherings of insults; but thou, O venerable one, foreseeing their destruction by the Spirit, didst instruct them not to speak foul things, but rather with their tongue to magnify God![^186]

The boastful men were not corrected by thine instruction, but rather mocked thee, insulting thee; therefore again thou didst instruct them with compassion, that they might rather have need of prayer, since they would soon be drowned with their prince Rostislav!

Reprove not a fool, lest he hate thee, says the Scripture,[^187] which also came to pass in thee, O all-blessed one: for the prince, having heard the death foretold concerning him by thee, counting it an insult, was enraged *(Left page — Folio 50 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...was enraged against thee, and seized with fearlessness, commanded that thou be drowned in water![^188]

*Glory...*
Unto Christ Who is now covered with waters, thou didst pass through water, O father; and death cut down the tree of thy temporal life, which now fairly blossoms beside the streams of living waters, and the foliage of thy prayers gladdens the Church: therefore, O holy one, we magnify thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
In the temple of thy glory, preserve in peace those who offer Gabriel's voice unto thee, O pure Virgin, and strengthen the scepters of the kingdom, granting them the power of thy grace to drive away all pestilential passions, that we all may magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
O blessed one, who in this life wast made like unto the angels by a blameless life, even after thy falling asleep thou wast not parted from them: for the King on high commanded His angels to bring thy much-suffering body out of the water into thy cell after three days, and to settle thy holy soul in the dwellings of the saints, where now living, remember us who honor thy memory, O most sacred father![^189]

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
The Savior, grace and truth, hath appeared in the streams of Jordan, and hath illumined those who sleep in darkness and the shadow: for the unapproachable Light hath come and appeared!

*At the Praises, Stichera, to 4: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небесных чинов):*
Having ended thy life with water and a stone bound unto thy neck, O blessed one, thou didst depart unto Christ Who stands upon the waters, common cornerstone unto that Stone upon which the Jerusalem on high is founded: there now having thy dwelling with the angels, remember those who with love honor thy all-honorable memory! *(Twice)*

Having obeyed the law of God in this life, O venerable one, thou didst struggle lawfully as was worthy, and having ended the life of the present age like a martyr, thou didst pass over unto Christ, the Lawgiver and Crowner, from Whom having received the incorruptible crown, being now separated with the angelic choirs: account us also worthy through thy prayers to be partakers of the dwellings with them and of their rejoicing!

Being bound by hand and foot, and cast into the streams of water, thou wast released from the bonds of the flesh; the Lord therefore brought forth thy soul from this prison unto the unutterable Light, where the spirits of the righteous enjoy the vision of God...

*(Right page — Folio 51 recto, running header: TO GREGORY THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
...now put to rest: which we also pray to obtain through thy mediation, O most blessed father!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having received the unfailing gift of prophecy, O venerable one, thou didst foresee the destruction of thy murderer, who being instructed by thy words was not corrected, nor did he desire thy blessing and prayer which aids in battles. Therefore, like Pharaoh, he sank into the water with his warriors, and was deprived of both lives, receiving reproach sevenfold into his bosom, and his memory perished. But do thou, O father, standing before the throne of God, grant blessing unto thy servants, and prayer unto those who bow down before thy wonderworking relics and honor thy memory with praises!

*Both now... of the Feast, in the same Tone:*
O Lord, desiring to fulfill that which Thou didst ordain from the ages, from all creation Thou didst receive the ministers of Thy mystery: from the angels Gabriel, from men the Virgin, from the heavens the star, and from the waters the Jordan, wherein Thou didst destroy the iniquity of the world: O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service is common to Venerable Fathers.*

*(Vignette occupying lower half of page: woodcut engraving of the Dormition Cathedral of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra with radiance and clouds) (Left page — Folio 51 verso, full-page woodcut engraving within an ornamental border: Saint Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslav, in sakkos, omophorion, and mitre, holding dikirion and trikirion, with vignettes of his journey to Tsargrad and his return to the Kyiv Caves Monastery):*

*In the Month of January, on the 28th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Ephraim of the Caves, former Eunuch of Prince Izyaslav of Kyiv, and afterwards Bishop of Pereyaslav:*[^190]
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Званный свыше):*
When by the counsel of Jesus, the Chief Shepherd on high, Thou wast called, O venerable one, then counting the corruptible things of the world as nothing, and having left all the fair possessions of power, Thou didst go by the irrevocable, hard...

*(Right page — Folio 52 recto, running header: TO EPHRAIM, BISHOP OF PEREYASLAV:)*
...hard path leading unto life, which having reached by the help of thy God, thou now rejoicest in the heavenly dwellings, taking up thy abode with the ranks of the venerable; where make haste to offer supplication unto Christ God to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

When by the anointing of the Holy Spirit thou wast chosen, O venerable Ephraim, to shepherd the rational flock of the King on high, then as a good shepherd and teacher, unceasingly guiding the sheep entrusted unto thee with good instruction, thou didst give them understanding, and nourishing them with the word of comfort, thou didst confirm them, showing thyself as a pattern unto them in word and deed; and having shepherded them well upon spiritual pastures, thou didst bring them whole unto Christ God, the Chief Shepherd Who passed through the heavens: Whom pray to preserve and save us also from invisible enemies!

When the good pleasure of God kindled the hearts of all unto love for thee, O all-blessed father, then according to the desire of him who wore the diadem, by the common counsel of all, thou wast appointed hierarch of the glorious city, wherein, that thou mightest faithfully serve thy Master as a faithful servant, thou hadst great zeal for the building of churches in thy bishopric, and this thou didst count as a deed more honorable and lovely than all else: to multiply praise unto the glory of Christ our God, Whom pray to save and enlighten our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Adornment of hierarchs, O most sacred and all-blessed father, excellent disciple of Christ, good shepherd who didst feed thy flock upon spiritual pastures, O Ephraim worthy of all wonder: now standing before the throne of Christ with the hierarchs, cease not to pray for us who sing of thy precious falling asleep!

*Both now... Theotokion: Who will not bless thee...*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and Readings:*
*Reading from the Book of Proverbs:*[^191]
The memory of the righteous is praised, and the blessing of the Lord is upon his head. Blessed is the man that finds wisdom, and the mortal that knows understanding. For the merchandise of it is better than the merchandise of silver, and the gain thereof than fine gold. She is more precious than costly stones: and all honorable things are not worthy of her. Length of days and years of life are in her right hand, and in her left hand riches and glory. From her mouth proceeds *(Left page — Folio 52 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...proceeds righteousness, and she bears law and mercy upon her tongue. Hear me therefore, O children, for I will speak honorable things, and blessed is the man that shall keep my ways: for my goings out are the goings out of life, and the will is prepared from the Lord. For this cause I entreat you, and put forth my voice unto the sons of men: for I, wisdom, have set things in order, and counsel, and understanding, and knowledge have I called; mine are counsel and confirmation, mine is understanding, and mine is strength. I love those that love me, and those that seek me shall find grace. Understand craftiness therefore, O simple ones, and apply your hearts, ye uninstructed. Hear me again, for I will speak honorable things: and I will open right things from my lips, for my palate shall meditate truth, and deceitful lips are an abomination before me. All the words of my mouth are with righteousness; there is nothing crooked or perverse in them. They are all right to those that understand, and simple to those that find knowledge. For I teach you the truth, that your hope may be in the Lord, and that ye may be filled with the Spirit.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon:*[^192]
The mouth of the righteous drops wisdom, and the lips of wise men know grace. The mouth of the wise meditates wisdom, and righteousness delivers from death. When a righteous man dies, hope perishes not. For a righteous son is born unto life, and in his good things he shall reap the fruit of righteousness. Light is ever with the righteous, and from the Lord they shall find grace and glory. The tongue of the wise knows good things, and in their heart wisdom rests. The Lord loves venerable hearts, and acceptable unto Him are all that are undefiled in the way. The wisdom of the Lord enlightens the face of the prudent. For she overtakes those that desire her, before they know her, and she is easily seen of those that love her. He that rises early to her shall have no great travail, and he that watches for her sake shall quickly be without care. For she goes about herself seeking such as are worthy of her, and she shows herself favorably unto them in the ways. Malice shall never overcome wisdom. For these things I was a lover of her beauty, and I loved her, and sought her out from my youth: and I sought to bring her to myself as a bride, for the Master of all loved her. For she is privy to the mystery of the knowledge of God, and a chooser of His works. Her labors are virtues: for she teaches temperance and prudence, righteousness and fortitude, than which men can have nothing more profitable in life.

*(Right page — Folio 53 recto, running header: TO EPHRAIM, BISHOP OF PEREYASLAV:)*
If a man desire much experience, she knows things of old, and conjectures what is to come: she knows the subtleties of speeches, and can expound dark sentences: she foresees signs and wonders, and the events of seasons and times: and she is a good counselor unto all, for immortality is in her, and good report in communion with her words. Therefore I spoke unto the Lord, and prayed Him, and with my whole heart I said: O God of the fathers, and Lord of mercy, Who hast made all things with Thy word, and ordained man through Thy wisdom, that he should have dominion over the creatures which were made by Thee, and order the world in holiness and righteousness: give me wisdom, that sits by Thy thrones, and reject me not from among Thy servants, for I am Thy servant and the son of Thine handmaid. O send her out of the heavens, from the dwelling of Thy holiness, and from the throne of Thy glory, that being present with me she may teach me what is well-pleasing before Thee: and guide me in understanding, and preserve me in her glory. For the thoughts of mortal men are all fearful, and their devices fallible.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon:*[^193]
When the righteous is praised, the people shall rejoice: for the memory of him is immortal, because it is known with the Lord and with men, and his soul is well-pleasing unto the Lord. Desire wisdom therefore, O men, and live; desire her, and ye shall be instructed: for the beginning of her is love, and the keeping of the law. Honor wisdom, that ye may reign for ever. I will tell you, and will not hide God's mysteries from you: for He is the leader of wisdom, and the directer of the wise, and the artificer of every understanding and work. And wisdom will teach all understanding: for in her is a spirit intelligent and holy, the brightness of the everlasting Light, and the image of the goodness of God. She makes friends of God and prophets. She is more beautiful than the sun, and above all the order of stars; being compared with the light, she is found prior. She delivers from pains those that please her, and guides them in the right paths. She gave them to have holy understanding, and preserved them from those that lay in wait, and gave them a stout struggle, that all may understand that godliness is stronger than all. And malice shall never prevail against wisdom, neither shall judgment pass away convicting the wicked. For they said among themselves, reasoning unrighteously: Let us oppress the righteous man, and let us not spare his holiness, nor reverence the gray hairs of the ancient in years. But let our strength be the law: and let us lie in wait *(Left page — Folio 53 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...and let us lie in wait for the righteous, because he is unserviceable unto us, and contrary to our doings, and reproaches us with the fall of the law, and objects to our infamy the transgressions of our training. He professes to have the knowledge of God, and he calls himself the child of the Lord. He was made to reprove our thoughts, and he is grievous unto us even to behold, for his life is not like other men's, and his ways are of another fashion: we are esteemed of him as a mockery, and he abstains from our ways as from filthiness, and pronounces the end of the righteous to be blessed. Let us see if his words be true, and let us prove what shall happen in the end of him. Let us examine him with rebuke and torture, that we may know his meekness, and prove his patience. Let us condemn him with a shameful death, for that there shall be a visitation from his words. Such things did they imagine, and were deceived, for their own malice blinded them, neither knew they the mysteries of God, nor discerned that Thou art God alone, Who hast the power of life and death, and savest in the time of tribulation, and deliverest from every evil: bountiful and merciful, and giving grace unto Thy venerable ones, and with Thine arm resisting the proud!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Тридневен):*
By the purity of thy life and the brightness of thy manifold ascetic labors, having illumined the beauty of thy face unto those that beheld it, thou didst draw all men unto love of thee; for thou wast like a radiant sun, shining with the rays of the unwaning Light, and thou didst enter where unapproachable Light shines: therefore as having boldness before Christ God, pray for those who honorably celebrate thy memory, O most sacred father Ephraim!

*Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.*
By the gentleness and understanding of thy lowliness of mind having ascended unto the supreme Mind, thou didst enter into the Holy of Holies as a venerable hierarch, and as a blessed minister of mysteries thou didst partake of the Holy Mysteries, whence thou wast illumined with the light of the vision of God: therefore with boldness pray for us who sing of thy memory, O Ephraim worthy of all wonder!

*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.*
All understanding and zeal didst thou receive for the goodly adornment of the Church of Christ, which as a bride...

*(Right page — Folio 54 recto, running header: TO EPHRAIM, BISHOP OF PEREYASLAV:)*
...as a bride thou didst adorn with the golden necklace of thy teaching, and having filled it with thy divinely-inspired words, thou didst present it fair unto thy Master: Whom now diligently pray that those who celebrate thy all-honorable memory with faith may be delivered from corruption and tribulations, O all-blessed hierarch Ephraim!

*Glory... in the same Tone:*
On the auspicious day of Thy festival, O venerable father, the Russian assemblies rejoice with one accord and leap for joy, and the Church of Christ mystically keeps festival, offering songs of praise unto thee, and glorifying Christ God in Thy memory!

*Both now... Theotokion: O Mother of God, thou art the true Vine...*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Burning in spirit with a journey unto holy places, thou didst travel into the land of the Greeks, O hierarch Ephraim, whence returning with great profit, having copied the Rule of the holy Studite Monastery,[^194] thou didst bring it hither as a most precious gift, wherewith the Russian Church is guided even unto this day, and glorifies thee, her true provider!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: He Who for our sake...*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord":*
*Troparion of the Saint, twice.*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гроб твой):*
Standing now in the highest before unapproachable Light, pray, O most sacred father Ephraim, that grace and cleansing of transgressions may be obtained for us who praise thy memory, and great mercy on the day of fearful trial, that we all may be accounted worthy to glorify the only Lover of mankind together with thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The Creator and Savior of the world of all, Who before the ages was born from the Father's bosom, the Same in time having come forth from thee, O Virgin Bride of God, renewed corrupted human nature, and showed thee unto all as the mediatress of our salvation!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Удивися Иосиф):*[^195]
The hard path in this life didst thou traverse, O father, and by thy many labors didst thou reach the longed-for haven, and having entered into the heavenly expanse on high, thou hast left unto us the ladder of thy virtuous ascent, O most blessed shepherd Ephraim!

*Glory (Left page — Folio 54 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Unto me walking in the broad way of various stumbling-blocks and unable to reach the saving haven, swiftly appear, O most pure Virgin: for thou art the good guide unto all that call upon thee!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-beginningless" (Собезначалное слово):*[^196]
By the cutting off of spiritual and bodily passions, thou wast truly named a eunuch, O venerable one; therefore as having escaped the drowning of the sea of passions, make haste to offer supplication unto Christ God for us who honor Thy memory with love, that we may receive grace and mercy, and deliverance from all passions!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Diligently we pray thee, O Virgin Bride of God, as having boldness before thy Son and God, ever pray for us, thy unprofitable servants, that we may find grace and mercy on the Day of Judgment!

*Then, Anabathmoi, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, in the same Tone: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.*
*Verse: Hear these things, all ye nations, give ear, all ye that inhabit the world.*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Common Gospel for a Hierarch.*[^197]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6: Adornment of hierarchs... (Look for it at "Lord, I have cried").*

*Canon to the Mother of God with its Heirmos to 6, and to the Saint to 6: Tone 2.*[^198]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt: for He hath been glorified!

*Troparia:*
Come, Church of Christ assembled by God, and with poetic crowns crown the adornment of hierarchs crowned from on high with grace, marvelous Ephraim, offering a song of victory and praise unto God: For He hath been glorified!

Bearing unto teachers the pattern of gentleness and humility, O most sacred father, thou wast a father unto the poor, orphans, and widows, and a swift protector unto the wronged!

Worthily wast thou called by the great High Priest Who sat down on high at the right hand of the Father, to shepherd His chosen flock which He acquired with His own Blood; and having shepherded it well, thou didst enter into the heavens to stand with the hierarchs before Christ, the Chief Shepherd!

*Glory...*
Seeing thy head encircled with a pastoral crown, O holy one, the cherub...

*(Right page — Folio 55 recto, running header: TO EPHRAIM, BISHOP OF PEREYASLAV:)*
...the cherub opened the gates of Eden unto thee, and thou didst enter where the tree of life and the stream of endless sweetness satisfy the chosen!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother and Virgin, who through thy childbearing didst open the heavenly gates unto all: open unto us the doors of thy mercy, and lead us into the heavenly homeland!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty is broken by Thy dominion, O Christ, and the weak have girded themselves with strength![^200]

*Troparia:*
Having thy mind as a bent bow from youth, O father, to emulate the angelic life, therefore having left serving Prince Izyaslav, thou camest unto Antony in the cave, and learned from him how to gird thyself with strength against the enemy!

Inflamed with love for Christ and stripped of the world, thou didst clothe thyself in the robe of salvation, and placing a helmet upon thy head by the hands of blessed Nikon,[^199] thou didst live as an angel upon earth, being outside the world and the flesh!

Seeing thee, O venerable one, that having despised the world thou wast numbered with the holy flock of God-bearing fathers, fierce Belial devised to scatter it; but having taken up weapons—prayer, fasting, and vigilance—thou didst gird thyself against him with spiritual strength!

*Glory...*
Thou wast obedient in all things unto the instruction of thy father, the venerable Antony, as a true follower of Jesus, Who was obedient even unto death; therefore through him thou wast exalted in virtues, girded with strength!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Languishing in soul and body, unto thee as unto a strong and all-powerful help, O Mother of God, do I wholeheartedly flee; and I pray thee: be pleased to gird me with strength from on high unto virtues, that thankfully I may sing of thy goodness, O all-hymned one!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*
A fervent intercessor and lover of churchly splendor, the adornment of hierarchs, the hierarch of God Ephraim let us honor today with praises; for on earth in the flesh having emulated the bodiless ones, by the word of fervent prayer he cut down all fleshly passions, and received boldness to aid all in their necessities!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Unto thee, our fervent intercessor and unbreakable wall, do we wholeheartedly flee, O blessed Virgin Mother of God, asking help: deliver us from tribulations and immeasurable sorrows: for thou hast grace and power to help all in their necessities *(Left page — Folio 55 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...to help all in their necessities!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I have glorified Thine incomprehensible power, O Lover of mankind![^201]

*Troparia:*
Having heard of the angelic life of the God-bearing fathers, O father, thou wast inflamed in spirit toward the holy places, desiring to be an eyewitness of their life and an emulator of their virtues!

Wishing to journey unto the imperial city of the land of Greece, thou didst desire the blessing and prayer of thy guide, O venerable one, knowing that he that sows with blessing reaps blessed sheaves!

Having received the two wings of the blessing and prayer of thy father, O father, like a dove from the ark thou didst fly forth unto the Greeks, seeking the spiritual branches of the virtues and profit of the God-bearing fathers!

*Glory...*
Having found that which thou didst desire, O all-wise one, beholding the pillar-dwelling fathers flourishing like cedars in the paradise of God, from their blossoms thou didst bring unto us the fruits: the Rule of the Studite Monastery!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Through slothfulness the enemy hath made the tree of my soul fruitless of sweet fragrances; but that I may not be cut down like that fig tree, water me from the fountain of thy goodness, O Virgin Mother, that in its season I may bring forth the fruit of an amended life!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments: for other than Thee we know no other God![^202]

*Troparia:*
Jesus, the Giver of light, seeing thy soul shining with purity, suffered thee not to be under a bushel, but set thee upon the candlestick of hierarchical office, that thou mightest give light in His Church, which He acquired with His precious Blood!

The city of Pereyaslav rejoiced, having received thee as a luminary unto its citizens, and wove a crown of praise for thee, for thou didst enlighten it with divinely-inspired words, and even more by the pattern of thy pure life!

With the pastoral staff thou didst drive the noetic wolf far away from thy flock, O father; which having quietly led out unto the spiritual pasture of teaching, thou didst confirm by thine active and divinely-inspired traditions!

*Glory...*
Like an eagle thou didst fly round the circuit of thy bishopric, driving away the noetic ravens, the spirits of wickedness in the high places, and protecting thine own chicks in like-mindedness from all wiles of the enemy!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The noetic enemy...

*(Right page — Folio 56 recto, running header: TO EPHRAIM, BISHOP OF PEREYASLAV:)*
...The noetic enemy often attacks us with evil wiles, making haste to snatch us for food unto himself; but do thou, O Mother loving thy children, deliver us not unto his trapping: for like a bird thou hast built a chamber for thyself here, and like a turtledove a nest, this monastery, wherein thou hast placed us like nestlings, thy singers!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
From the whale Jonah cried aloud unto the Lord: Do Thou lead me up from the depth of hell, I pray Thee, that as unto a Deliverer in the voice of praise, and in the spirit of truth, I may sacrifice unto Thee![^203]

*Troparia:*
Inflamed with love and supplication unto the Lord, thou didst erect many churches, wherein thou didst offer bloodless sacrifices for all thy rational sheep; pray now also that we may pray, so that in the hidden day we may offer praise here unto our Master!

Having adorned the Church of Christ like a fair bride with goodly beauty and various pigments, thou wast in it like a golden-beaming lamp, shining with virtues, O divinely-wise Ephraim; with the light-bearing rays of thy prayers, in that which is worthy of praises unto thee, enlighten the mind of those that praise thee!

Sitting upon thy throne, O most sacred father, many who were sitting in sinful darkness thou didst raise up unto repentance by thy teaching; raise up us also who have fallen through transgressions, that in the voice of praise and in the spirit of truth we may offer an amended life unto our Master!

*Glory...*
Many labors and ascetic deeds didst thou endure for thy flock, O blessed one, until thou didst cleanse it from the stumbling-blocks of the noetic enemy; guide us who are darkened by sinful gloom unto the light of repentance through thy prayers, O Ephraim worthy of all wonder!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Held fast by fierce and countless passions, we cry aloud unto thee who art blessed among all generations, O Mother of our God: lead us up unto the light of dispassion, that in the voice of praise we may offer our humble prayers unto thee!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Unto the Orthodox Russian Church, according to the desire of God-bearing Theodosius, having brought the Studite Rule,[^204] having shone forth as a luminary among hierarchs, and having shepherded his flock well: we praise thee, O Ephraim, as one who prays unto the Lord for all of us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the golden image was worshiped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded[^205] *(Left page — Folio 56 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...the godless decree, and cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Adorning thy soul with sweet-smelling flowers—fasting, prayer, and humility of mind—thou didst present it as a pure bride unto thy Master, standing before Whom thou singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

As in this life thou wast bound by love unto thy teacher, the blessed Theodosius, so also after his falling asleep remaining inseparable, thou camest with the other bishops unto the translation of his relics,[^206] and seeing their incorruption, thou didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

From youth having followed the calling of thy Master, after thy many labors which thou didst endure upon the cathedra of the Bishopric of Pereyaslav, thou wast summoned unto the heavenly throne into the paradise-dwelling, where with hierarchs and the choirs of the venerable thou singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
The aerial demons gnashed their teeth, seeing thee led by angels from earthly things unto eternal life; but they stood from afar: for thou wast unapproachable unto them, fenced round with heavenly light, where also pray for us, O most sacred father Ephraim!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Extinguish the enemies burning against me, O Virgin Bride of God, shatter their teeth, and crush the mental wounds of my members, I pray thee, that without fear and their assault, I may sing: Blessed art thou, O Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Disregarding the golden image, the three blessed youths, seeing the unchanging and living image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all creation that hath been sanctified sing to the Lord, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
As thou didst adorn the Church of Christ with goodly splendor, O hierarch, therefore Christ also after thy falling asleep adorned the temple of thy soul, and honored thy body with incorruption; bowing down before whose reliquary, we sing with those that sing: The Lord unto all ages!

Into the temple of the triumphs of the heavenly hosts' Supreme Commander,[^207] in whose name thou didst erect a fair temple, thou hast been led, O Ephraim, to sing unto the Lord unto the ages!

The First-Called of the Apostles, unto whom with the brethren thou didst erect a house of prayer, together with the Great Martyr Theodore, came to meet thee, O father, and graciously received thee into the heavenly house of God, to sing unto the Lord unto the ages with them!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*
For us, O hierarch Ephraim, as having boldness...

*(Right page — Folio 57 recto, running header: TO EPHRAIM, BISHOP OF PEREYASLAV:)*
...boldness before the Life-Originating Trinity, cease not praying, that we may be accounted worthy to reach the place of the marvelous tabernacle even unto the house of God, where with the voice of rejoicing and with most sweet sound they sing unto the Lord unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Holy Trinity, one Godhead, by the supplication of the most pure Mother of God and of Thy favored one Ephraim, grant that in the paradise-dwellings we may sing to Thee unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Luminary and God Who shone forth before the sun, Who came unto us in the flesh, ineffably incarnate from the Virgin's womb: O blessed and pure one, thee, O Mother of God, do we magnify!

*Troparia:*
Having left earthly bounds, thou hast entered into the heavenly dwellings, where enjoying the tree of life, pray that we also be not deprived of communion therein, O most blessed Ephraim!

The city of Pereyaslav rejoices, remembering thee, its shepherd; and we, delighting in festival, bow down before thy relics, seeing their incorruption, O most sacred father!

The cave cannot hide thy relics, O venerable one, for the burning lamp within it is not extinguished: for thou art its praise and adornment!

*Glory...*
Seeing the incorruption of thy relics, O hierarch, we remember the light of thy virtues, and we glorify the Heavenly Father, for He hath granted thee to be glorified before angels and men!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having been delivered from corruption through thy incorrupt childbearing, O most pure one, we sing with unceasing voices, and bowing down before thy childbearing, thee, O Mother of God, do we magnify!

*Exapostilarion:*
By yearning for thy purity, O father, thou didst drive away the darkness of fleshly passions, and didst show thyself as a light-bearing star, enlightening our thoughts and hearts, O wise Ephraim, glory of the fathers and adornment of the venerable!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Being all-fair and without blemish, O Virgin more honorable than the angels, by thy grace transform the ugliness of my soul into goodly form: for thou art the adornment and praise unto all!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небесных чинов):*
Having received the sacred anointing with myrrh from on high, O divinely-wise one, thou wast a shepherd unto Christ's flock, which having shepherded upon the pastures of the Gospel teaching and brought whole unto the Chief Shepherd, thou didst receive the recompense of good laborers: with whom now rejoicing, remember *(Left page — Folio 57 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...remember those who with love honor thy memory, O hierarch Ephraim! *(Twice)*

A temple of the All-Holy Spirit wast Thou, which having first cleansed beforehand with the water of humility, and anointed with the myrrh of grace and spiritual purity, Thou didst enlighten with the candle of virtues; and the Father coming with the Son made an abode in thee: therefore we pray thee, O most sacred father, pray Christ God that we also may receive grace through thee, and great mercy!

Gathering today on thy annual memory, O ye people, let us bless in hymns and songs the teacher of piety, the faithful laborer of Christ's vineyard, and the most honorable hierarch, saying: O venerable father Ephraim, forget not us who with love honor thy falling asleep, but pray Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Come today, ye assemblies of feast-lovers, let us sing in hymnodic praises of the hierarch of Christ, our God-bearing father Ephraim, saying: Rejoice, shepherd and most chosen teacher of the rational sheep of Christ! Rejoice, honorable adornment of hierarchs! Rejoice, most radiant luminary and beautifier of the Church! Rejoice, fervent petitioner for all who faithfully flee unto thee! By thy prayers deliver from tribulations us who now celebrate thy memory with love and fall down with joyful soul before the reliquary of thy relics, and pray Christ God to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save Thy servants from tribulations...
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*(Vignette occupying lower half of page: decorative baroque tailpiece)*

*(Right page — Folio 58 recto, headpiece woodcut engraving with two panels: upper panel depicting Saint Prochor the Orache-Eater holding a loaf of bread, surrounded by the famine-stricken people of Kyiv; lower panel depicting Prince Svyatopolk carrying Saint Prochor to the cave with his own hands)*
