# MONTH OF FEBRUARY

*On the 10th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Prochor the Wonderworker of the Caves:*[^208]
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Дал еси знамение):*
O marvelous Prochor, passing through the angelic life, thou didst flee from all worldly pleasures, and wast fed by the Word of God rather than with food; therefore thou wast a radiance unto fasters, with whom also Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls, granted thee a dwelling in the heavens *(Left page — Folio 58 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls!

O glorious Prochor, fleeing from human glory, like a soaring bird thou didst hasten unto the hill of the Caves and didst present thyself, distinguishing thyself by vision and deed of the law of the Lord; whence having ascended unto the summit of virtues, thou didst receive the gift of wonderworking from Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls!

O most blessed Prochor, following the footsteps of the blessed fathers, thou didst acquire a narrow manner of life in this world, so that thou didst not even taste ordinary bread, but wast satisfied with growing herbs; therefore now abiding in blessed life, thou art fed with the food of heavenly joy, which Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls, granted thee according to His goodness!

*Glory... Tone 5:*
Who will not marvel at thy life, or who will not recount thy patience, O most blessed father Prochor? For passing through life in great abstinence, thou didst disregard the body and didst crush the pleasure-loving serpent unto the end, establishing thy feet upon the steps of ascetic virtues. Therefore today honoring thy memory, we confess thy struggles and pray, falling down before the reliquary of thy relics: as having boldness before Christ God, ask peace for the world and great mercy for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Oktoechos.*
*Glory... to the Venerable One, Tone 6:*
O venerable one, the multitude of monks having formed a choir in praise at thy memory, we honor thy labors and struggles, and marvel how, while thyself leading a narrow and poor life, thou didst preserve others living in abundance in time of need: for those wasting away with hunger thou didst feed with bread made from orache, and unto those in need thou didst give salt from ashes; and now strengthen us who are ailing with spiritual hunger by thy prayers, and ask great mercy from Christ God!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Troparion, Tone 1:*
O venerable and wonderworking father Prochor, who in famine didst feed the hungry with bread from herbs and salt from ashes: do thou also nourish us with spiritual food, praying Christ God for our souls!

*Canon, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, once engulfed the chariot-driver Pharaoh, but saved the fleeing Israelite as a foot-traveler, as he sang a song unto God![^209]

*(Right page — Folio 59 recto, running header: TO PROCHOR THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
*Troparia:*
Gird us, O Master Christ, with spiritual weapons against invisible enemies, and teach us how to struggle and overcome them, that we may lovingly offer a festal song unto Thee our God in the memory of Thy favored one!

Having their loins girded with vigilance, the choirs of monastics remember thee, O father, and celebrate thy annual memory with praises unto thee, offering a song unto God!

Remembering eternal blessedness, O blessed one, thou didst leave the corruptible things of this world, and that thou mightest be a converser with angels, thou didst clothe thyself in the angelic habit; therefore we honor thy ascetic life, offering a song unto God!

*Glory...*
Having taken upon thyself the good yoke of abstinence, O divinely-wise one, thou didst acquire the harsh life of ascetic deeds, wherein wrestling with the invisible enemy, thou didst conquer him; therefore we glorify thee, offering a song unto God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, guide us who walk in the broad way and run by pleasures into the abyss of hell, unto the narrow way leading unto blessed life: for behold, thy gathered servants today fall down before thee, offering prayers!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Christ, Who in the beginning established the heavens with understanding, and founded the earth upon the waters: establish me upon the rock of Thy commandments, for there is none holy save Thee, the only Lover of mankind![^210]

*Troparia:*
Having established thy feet upon the rock of Christ's commandments, O father, thou didst remain unshaken by demonic assaults, ever singing praise unto God: There is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

By the rain of abstinence thou didst extinguish the kindle of fleshly passions, for eating neither bread, but orache herbs, thou didst abide having rather the Word of God as food, and this strengthened Thee, O venerable one!

Thou didst count hunger as nothing, O blessed one, looking unto Him Who feeds the birds; and He gave thee to eat bread not from sown grain, which was sweeter unto thee than honey and the honeycomb!

*Glory...*
We marvel at thy marvelous humility, O holy one, that even for orache bread thou didst condemn thyself, judging thyself unworthy even of such food, by which thou didst lightly traverse the sorrowful ascetic way!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, cast down the demons that exalt themselves with pride against our humility: for thou alone didst say: Behold, the handmaid of the Lord; and thou reignest unto ages over all generations that call thee blessed!

*(Left page — Folio 59 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Emulating the great fathers, thou didst dearly love the utmost asceticism, for being fed with herbs alone and water, thou didst not faint from fasting, but didst remain stronger than adamant. And now having received the fruits of thine abstinence, recompense from God, pray for us, O venerable one, that through thee we also may receive mercy, as we lovingly call thy memory blessed, O worthy of all wonder!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed who didst bless the human race, and we glorify thee who didst transcend all creation, honoring in hymns thy seedless conception and thine ineffable childbearing, O Mother, Mother, pure Virgin!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who leaving not the Father's bosom, didst visit our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^211]

*Troparia:*
Having from God the grace of wonderworking, O father, thou didst feed many with orache bread in time of famine, and making salt from ashes, didst generously give it unto those who came. O glory unto the Lover of mankind Who gave thee unto us as an intercessor!

By thy prayer, O venerable one, thou didst grant sweetness unto the orache bread, for it was sweet to eat, bright and pure, like wheat unto those who received it with a blessing. O grant unto us also of that blessing of thine, O most sacred father, as we have thee as a mediator before Christ the Lover of mankind!

Thy orache bread, O most blessed father, was sweet and bright unto those who received it with a blessing, but unto those who took it secretly, it appeared black as ash, and bitter. Bless us with thy blessing, and grant unto us of thy sweetness, as thou sittest at the heavenly table, O glorious Prochor!

*Glory...*
Not secretly, but openly bringing hymns of praise, fainting with spiritual hunger, we come unto thee, O father, on this day of thy memory, and we pray: grant unto us of thy grace, that in thee we may glorify Christ the Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Who that flees unto thee departs from thee ashamed, O pure Virgin? Or who that asks receives not of thy goodness, O Lady? Therefore we also, believing and confessing this, offer supplication unto thee, O Queen: grant unto us thy compassions, as being the good Mother of the Good One!

*(Right page — Folio 60 recto, running header: TO PROCHOR THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and why hath a strange darkness covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and guide my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^212]

*Troparia:*
O glorious Prochor, tarry not to come, and account us worthy of thy blessing, as once in time past thou didst give it unhindered; and direct the paths of those who lovingly glorify thee from afar unto the commandments of the Lord, we pray thee!

The adversary enemy, troubling the earth and shaking the people, brought war upon us, and from every side spiritual hunger came upon us; but do thou, O most blessed one, anticipate us quickly, and by thy prayer feed us with spiritual food, and direct our paths, we pray thee!

Seeing thy beneficence, O holy one, many came unto thee in time of famine, unto whom thou didst unstintingly give bread from herbs and salt made from ashes by thine hands; look upon us now also, and by thy prayer grant every abundance unto our monastery, we pray thee, O venerable father!

*Glory...*
Many enemies hating us have risen up against us, striving to deprive us of spiritual and bodily food; but do thou come unto us and rebuke them, and by thy prayer grant unto our monastery all things profitable, we pray thee, O venerable Prochor!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The adversary enemy, envying thy flock, ever stirs up war against us; but do thou, O most pure one, as Mother of the Holy King, forbid him, and direct our paths unto the commandments of the Lord, we pray thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation![^213]

*Troparia:*
Cleanse the mire of my soul by thy prayer, O glorious Prochor, and heal the festered wounds of my heart with the plaster of thy grace: for unto thee do I cry this day, as unto a petitioner for my salvation!

Overcome by the thought of greed, the prince commanded all the salt made from ashes by thy prayer to be plundered, which turning back into ashes was found bitter in his mouth; deliver me from such malice and passion, O father, as a helper of my salvation!

Shame covered the face of those who slandered the food of the hungry: for seeing the transformation of thy salt into ashes, O father, they poured it out with shame; but thou didst command the cast-out ashes to be gathered *(Left page — Folio 60 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...to be gathered, and it became pure salt again by thy prayer, as before. Grant now also thy prayer unto me, O prayerful helper of my salvation!

*Glory...*
Thinking to hide their shame, they commanded the salt that had turned into ashes to be poured out by night; but the night cried aloud witnessing thy wonders: for the ashes turned back into salt, and by thy command those in need gathered it, blessing thee, O venerable one, as their protector!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
A night of slothfulness holds me fast, and I walk in the darkness of estrangement, not drawing near unto the noetic light of vigilance; O Mother of God, enlighten my understanding, guiding me unto doxology, that I may ever sing to thee: Rejoice, candle kindled with the divine flame!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Wasting thy flesh with the abstinence of herbs, O holy one, being deprived of human food, herbs and grass were in place of bread for thee, and thou wast fed with ashes as with salt, O wonderworking Prochor; seeing which, the Lord transformed the herbs into bread and the ashes into salt, wherewith thou didst feed a multitude of the hungry; therefore we honor and bless thee![^214]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire of the descent of God in Babylon was once ashamed; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^215]

*Troparia:*
Remembering the descent of God and the fearful judgment, the prince was afraid, for he had afflicted thee, O venerable Prochor, not knowing the workings of grace that had come to pass in thee; but coming to himself he cried aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Knowing thee to be a servant of God, O father, the ruler afterwards called thy wonderworkings blessed, and having conceived the warmest love toward the wonder-filled monastery, he repented of his greed, and asking forgiveness for the evil he had done, he sang: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

As a true minister and servant of the guileless Lamb, the Son of God, having shown thy guilelessness, thou didst grant forgiveness unto him who had afflicted thee, O blessed one; and thou didst confirm a word with him, that the one should lay the other in the grave, whenever the God of our fathers should command one of you to depart from the body!

*Glory...*
Having lived in holiness and ordered this life well, thou didst reach a blessed end, O holy one; therefore looking joyfully upon the angelic choirs, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I have not emulated those who lived in holiness, but have spent my life in slothfulness; drawing near unto the end and reflecting upon it, unto whom wilt thou look, O my soul? Having the Mother of God as mine only hope, cry aloud: O Lady, help me, and deliver me from eternal condemnation!

*(Right page — Folio 61 recto, running header: TO PROCHOR THE WONDERWORKER OF THE CAVES:)*
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tormentor furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, exalt Him unto all ages![^216]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst slay the seven-headed serpent with the spear of ascetic deeds, and preparing thyself to receive the crown, with joy thou didst cry aloud, O Prochor: Ye people, sing unto the Lord, the Creator and Deliverer, and exalt Him unto the ages!

Hearing of thy blessed falling asleep from thy message, O venerable one, the prince left the war and came with haste unto thee, that he might be accounted worthy of thy blessing; and he was not deprived of that which he desired, but receiving thy blessing, he sang: Ye people, exalt Christ unto the ages!

As formerly in prayers thou didst stretch forth thine hands, O venerable one, so at thy falling asleep, having given the kiss of peace unto all and made a lifting up of thy hands on high, thou didst yield up thy spirit, and all sang: Ye people, glorify your protector unto the ages!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*
Thy joyful departure to dwell with the heavenly ones filled all with mourning even while rejoicing them: for all wept, the poor for their feeder, the orphans for their father; and we on this day, glorifying thy falling asleep, O father, cry aloud: Ye people, exalt Christ unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bear the cause of our joy and the rejoicing of all the venerable ones, by thine intercession account me worthy of eternal joy, O Mother of God, and account me worthy to glorify thy loving-kindness unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth marveled, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, O Mother of God![^217]

*Troparia:*
Every man marveled, remembering thy life, O father, that living in the body thou didst make thyself like unto the angels by ascetic deeds; therefore all magnify thee, O Prochor!

Though thou wast poor in spirit, O venerable one, he who wore the diadem was not ashamed to lay thee in the grave with his own hands; and we now remembering thy falling asleep, bless thee, whom also the angelic choirs bless in the dwellings on high!

Bearing the cross upon thy shoulders in this life, the prince was not ashamed to carry thee upon his shoulders into the cave, and poured out warm tears upon thy grave, repenting that he had formerly afflicted thee; but he amended both by thy blessing, whereof account us also worthy, as we lovingly magnify thee with hymns!

*(Left page — Folio 61 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Glory...*
Glory to Thee, O All-Holy Trinity, Father, Son, and Spirit, only God: for by Thy goodness Thou givest eternal glory unto those who love Thee from the heart, whereof account us also worthy, by the prayers of the venerable Prochor and communion of the saints, where their choirs ever magnify Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Glory to thee, O Mother of God, the only refuge of faithful servants, the only confidence, the only hope and salvation of the Christian race; therefore as is due, we all magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
Having left the corruptible things of the world, thou didst reach the heavenly realms by the narrow way, and standing before the unoriginate Light with the choirs of the venerable, pray, as having boldness to help in necessities, both for the pacification of the world and for the power of Christian rulers, who glorify thy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O mediatress before the Holy Trinity, stand in thy help unto those who suffer in the abyss of worldly passions, and by the calm waves of thy tender-heartedness bring them unto the blessed haven, as a swift helper unto all who glorify thy name!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Oktoechos.*
*Then, to the Venerable One:*
*Verse: Tone 4:*
*The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.*
In angelic manner let us keep festival, O ye people, and offer a song unto God on the annual memory of the God-bearing father, exalting His most holy name, and calling aloud in seraphic manner: Holy art Thou, O Father unoriginate; holy art Thou, O Son co-beginningless; holy art Thou, O Spirit co-everlasting!

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Having formed an honorable choir, emulating the choirs of the bodiless ones, come, ye people, let us now lovingly bring mental gifts unto the holy cave, and stooping down into the grave of the most blessed father, let us praise him, crying: Rejoice, O Prochor of glorious name, and petitioner for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*(Vignette occupying lower half of page: decorative baroque tailpiece)*

*(Right page — Folio 62 recto, headpiece woodcut engraving with six narrative panels surrounding a large central figure of Saint Isaac the Recluse: top left: demons appearing as angels of light; top right: demons sitting around Isaac; center left: Antony finding Isaac prostrated and paralyzed; center right: Isaac seized by demons; bottom left: demons exhausting Isaac with dancing; bottom right: Isaac prostrating before the demon chief)*

*On the 14th Day of the Month of February:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Isaac the Recluse of the Caves:*[^218]
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (О преславнаго чудесе):*[^219]
O venerable father Isaac, though at first the dark enemy appeared in the form of light and darkened thee through ignorance, and thou wast struck down by demonic wiles, yet afterwards thou didst amend thyself, and wast shown fearful unto him: for from Christ God thou didst receive victory over all the power of the enemy, which *(Left page — Folio 62 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...which cease not praying that we also may be delivered from all enemy assaults and grievous circumstances!

O divinely-wise father Isaac, though the enemy deceived thee, appearing as light, yet afterwards deceived he fell by thine amendment; for having entrapped him by thy holy folly, thou didst utterly cast down his wiles, and trampling him underfoot, thou didst cry aloud: I have no fear of your fright, for God is with me, and His strength is my confirmation against all your terrors!

O most blessed father Isaac, though in ignorance thou didst bow down before him as unto Christ's person, yet after this fall thou didst amend thyself again, and burned up the demonic legions, filling their faces with dishonor: who, being put to shame, vanish away like smoke, and as by the fire of love toward God, thy name drives away their assaults and helps all who invoke thee!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
O father Isaac, in thyself thou gavest a pattern unto ascetics, that they believe not appearing phantoms: for Satan knows how to transform himself into an angel of light; but let them test them with the sign of the mighty weapon, upon which the spirits of wickedness cannot look, but as dust is driven away by the wind from the earth, so scorched by its rays they vanish away: let us not fear the enemy's wiles, but rather escaping their strange craftiness, from Christ God let us receive peace in our bones, and great mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Desiring to enjoy the splendors of the saints, thou didst settle in a dark cave, O all-praised one, and didst struggle well, O Isaac; and being deceived by the enemy, again thou didst firmly trample underfoot all his wiles; and now standing as a victor in magnificence before Christ God, ask peace and great mercy for us!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the threefold part of my soul in the depth of dispassion, as Thou didst the mighty captains of three,[^220] that like a timbrel in the mortification of the body I may sing a song of victory unto Thee!

*Troparia:*
O almighty Master Christ, in the manifestation of Thy compassions drown my sins as Pharaoh, I pray Thee, that with earthen lips glorifying Thee in the memory of Thy favored one, I may worthily offer a song of victory!

*(Right page — Folio 63 recto, running header: TO ISAAC THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
The present mighty ruler of this world, seeking to cover all with the depth of passions, could not submerge thee, O father; for having escaped his manifold-woven nets, thou didst flee from the world like a bird from snares, and didst offer a song of victory unto God!

A skilled merchant wast thou, O venerable one: for by corruptible things thou didst acquire incorruptible, and with perishable didst buy the imperishable pearl, wherewith thou didst adorn the crown of thy patience, and now rejoicing thou standest before Christ, offering a song of victory!

*Glory...*
Thou didst distribute thy possessions unto the poor, O blessed one, and didst not spare thine own will, but didst entrust it unto the great Antony, from whom receiving in exchange the angelic habit, thou wast enriched with the heavenly treasure, and singest unceasing praise unto Christ!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Burdened with the yoke of sin, I come unto thee, O Mother of God, praying: take away my passionate torment, that at least from henceforth I may amend myself by repentance, and offer warm prayers and my contrite song unto thee, O Lady!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
From the heights Thou camest down willingly upon earth, O Thou Who art above all principality, and hast raised up lowly human nature from nethermost hell: for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind![^221]

*Troparia:*
Having received the calling from on high by the exchange of the angelic habit, thou wast crucified unto the world, crucifying together with it all its lustful passions, treading upon them by a harsh manner of life, O venerable one, having the only God Who loves mankind as thy helper!

That thou mightest not inherit a portion with the goats in the world to come, O father, mortifying thy flesh in this life, thou didst clothe thyself in a single garment of haircloth and in a goatskin,[^222] wherein thou didst traverse the narrow way of monastic life, calling upon God, the only Lover of mankind, for help!

Thou didst settle in the mount, and shut thyself up in a narrow cave; and reaching forth unto things on high by the ladder of virtues, O wise one, thou didst set an ascent in thy heart, ever to hymn God the Lover of mankind!

*Glory...*
The narrow way of humility and poverty didst thou traverse, O wise one, dashing the crafty enemy like an infant against the rock of the love of God *(Left page — Folio 63 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...the love of God, seeking which, having cleansed thy soul by fasting, by all-night vigilance thou didst humble the body, that it might stand without slothfulness before God the Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Overcome by slothfulness, I cannot dash the passions born within me like infants against the rock of the commandments; thee do I call upon for help, O Mother of God: help me as Mother of God the Lover of mankind, and they shall fall under my feet, conquered by thy power!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Mortifying thy flesh by abstinence, thou didst disregard corruptible food, satisfying thyself every other day with a single prosphora; thou didst abide in abstinence, and didst not rest upon thy ribs; thus struggling against the invisible enemy, thou didst cry aloud: O Christ God, grant help unto those who are in warfare!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Darkened by the sleep of slothfulness, I stretch forth my soul unto thee, O pure one; by thy wakeful prayer and unsearchable goodness raise me up unto hymnody, and grant help unto me who am in warfare!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Bountiful One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away: for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise![^223]

*Troparia:*
For the sake of the love of God in many labors and fastings, Thou didst abide, O venerable one, until the seventh year not going forth from the cell, ever remembering thy fearful departure, but unto Christ Who strengthened Thee Thou didst cry aloud: Thou, O God, art my strength and praise!

Behold, how good and how pleasant it is for brethren to dwell together in unity: a brother helped by a brother is like a strong city, but he that contends alone against the enemy falls; which came to pass in thee also, O father, that thou shouldest not rely upon thyself, but upon the living God: for He alone is strength and praise unto all!

*Glory...*
Wishing to darken thy mind, O holy one, the devil came with a darkness of bright demons, and he shone forth like the sun, but covered thee with the darkness of deceit; from which being delivered, thou didst cry aloud unto God: Thou, O God, art my strength and praise!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having a mind darkened by passions, I am held fast by many sins; but unto thee do I pray, who beyond mind didst bear the Light of the whole world: take up the weapon of thy power, O Mother of God, and stand by me for help, that I may see the light of true knowledge of God!

*(Right page — Folio 64 recto, running header: TO ISAAC THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Make the light of Thy commandments to shine upon me, O Lord, for my spirit rises early unto Thee, and sings to Thee: for Thou art our God, unto Thee I flee, O King of peace![^224]

*Troparia:*
Shining forth in the form of light, the sons of eternal darkness made a great cry, calling thee their own, O father Isaac, and compassed thee about like many dogs with their assaults; escaping from which, thou didst flee unto God the King of peace!

He who once ordained the sound of music to be before the gold-beaten idol for the deception of those who knew not God, the same commanded to sound with pipes and timbrels for thy deception; from which, though thou wast deceived, thou wast delivered by the help of God the King of peace!

Seeing thee exhausted by ascetic labors, the devil commanded to wear thee out with dancing; fulfilling whose command, the evil spirits leaped with thee for a long space of time, and having mocked thee, left thee scarcely alive; but afterwards amending thyself, thou didst mock him, and trampled him underfoot unto the end, by the help of God the King of peace!

*Glory...*
The venerable fathers carried thee out before the cave, terrified by the enemy's assault, speechless, breathing only a little; and knowing that this had happened from demonic working, they diligently aided thee with prayer; and the Lord the King of peace gave ear unto their prayer, and had mercy on thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having a heart wounded by the darkness of demonic assaults, unto thee do I pray, O Lady: apply the good plaster of mercy unto it, and save my darkened soul from falling: for thee alone do I have as a swift visitation, O Queen of light!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Engulfed in the tempest of sin, as in the belly of the sea-monster, with the Prophet I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Lord, and save me![^225]

*Troparia:*
Assaulted by the tempest of demonic temptations, O blessed one, thou wast paralyzed in mind and body, and knowing nothing concerning thyself, thou didst lie upon a bed for two years, neither eating bread nor drinking water; but being entreated by the prayer of the venerable Theodosius, the Lord led up thy life from corruption, and saved thee!

A strange wonder it is for human nature to be without food; yet this was fulfilled in thee, O blessed one: for where God wills, the order of nature is overcome, for in His hand is the breath of all creation, and He does whatever He wills!

Thy Cross, O Christ, is a mighty weapon against enemies, wherewith if we fence not ourselves, we shall be tempted *(Left page — Folio 64 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...we shall be tempted like Isaac; fencing ourselves therefore with this, we pray Thee: as Thou didst not suffer Isaac to be tempted unto the end, so also suffer not us to be tempted above our strength, and save us!

*Glory...*
Glory to Thee, O Unoriginate Trinity, Cause of goodness, Who by Thy compassions liftest up all from falling: have compassion upon us Thy servants, and raise up those who have fallen into corruption, as Thou didst save Thy favored one Isaac, granting him greater strength against demons!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Glory to thee, O Mother of God, refuge of the faithful: for through thee the fallen rise up, the slain are quickened, they receive strength against the devil, and are accounted worthy of eternal life in the glory of thy Son and God!

*Kontakion, Tone 5:*
Having cast down the deception of the enemy, and shone forth radiantly in fasting, come, ye faithful, let us praise Isaac the victorious, who shone forth as gold in temptations, and enlightens the hearts of those who faithfully flee unto him, praying unceasingly unto the Lord for our souls![^226]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Deliver us not up unto the end for Thy name's sake, and destroy not Thy covenant, and take not away Thy mercy from us, O Lord God of our fathers, praised above all unto ages![^227]

*Troparia:*
Having passed through many temptations, O venerable one, thou didst abound the more unto the glory of God, and standing firmly against the enemy, in psalmody thou didst sing: When I was fallen in distress, Thou hast set my feet in a spacious place, O Christ God, praised above all unto the ages!

Thou didst bind thyself tightly with blessed bonds, firmly binding also the enemy by steadfast obedience in the kitchen; and thou didst mock him who had mocked thee, driving him away by prayer, and anticipating all by obedience and ascetic labors!

Restoring the fallen temple of thy soul by temptations, thou didst run before all others unto the temple of God's Mother, and with thy feet frozen unto the stone from winter, thou didst stand until the end of the rule, the fire of thy heart vehemently kindling thee unto the love of God!

*Glory...*
Having formerly found grace after the demonic deception, again thou didst attain it, O father, which the bird caught by thee signified; seeing which, they knew the grace of God that was with thee, and all marveled and glorified Christ Who glorified thee, and honored thee as Christ's favored one!

*(Right page — Folio 65 recto, running header: TO ISAAC THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Darkened by holy folly through demonic wiles, and doing things unseemly, I transgress the commandments of my Lord; but do thou, O pure Mother, grant me understanding, that I may discern the right way, and escaping the hand of Belial who hunts me, I may praise thee unto the ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who was nailed in the flesh upon the Cross, and showed forth a weapon of salvation unto us: ye youths, exalt Christ our God unto all ages![^228]

*Troparia:*
Having ascended the cross of patience, thou wast crucified unto the world, O most blessed Isaac: for thou didst nail thy members by the cutting off of passions, and with the spear of mockery, those who knew not thy holy folly for God's sake pierced thee; but thou didst joyfully endure all things, praising Christ unto the ages!

Stepping with bare feet upon the flame of a kindled furnace, Thou didst remain standing motionless until it was extinguished; and this signified the quenching of demonic assaults, which Thou didst swiftly cool by the power of the Cross of Christ, and in this Thou boastest unto the ages!

Many times the demons assaulted thee, O father, calling thee their own, because thou hadst bowed down before their prince; but by the power of Christ thou didst drive them away like flies, and with shame they fled from thee, unable to endure thy rebuke; and thy latter victory over them was greater than the first!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Tempted by the assaults of fierce passions, unto thy protection do I flee, O all-immaculate Virgin: for the sake of thy venerable Isaac, cover me, and save me from these assaults, that I may exalt thee with Christ unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
All of us magnify Thy love for mankind, O Christ our Savior, the glory of Thy servants and crown of the faithful, Who hast magnified the memory of her that bare Thee![^229]

*Troparia:*
All of us magnify God the Lover of mankind, Who so strengthened His servant, that he turned all demonic terrors into flight, and remained henceforth unconquered by any weapon, like an adamant, firm, whole, and unbreakable!

Thy adversary grew weak, O venerable one, though many times by night he brought many demons unto his aid, with mattocks and spades, and they cried aloud: Let us dig up this cave, and bury him here, for already he overcomes us, calling upon the unconquerable name of the Most High and her that bare Him for help!

Having driven away the nocturnal darkness, O blessed one, thou didst drive away those who came by night with the light-bearing sign of the Cross, wherewith *(Left page — Folio 65 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...wherewith being scorched, unable to endure, they turned to flight, leaving the crown of victory unto thee, as unto a good soldier and unconquerable ascetic!

*Glory...*
The serpent and fierce beast wrestling with thee for three years, O father, was transformed into four-footed beasts, wild animals, creeping things, and mice; but not overcoming thee by his phantasms, against his will he confessed thee as victor: for thou didst cast him down by the name of Christ, Whom with her that bare Him we magnify!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
All the time of my life, the enemy transforming himself into various forms conquers me; unable to stand against him, thee, O Mother of God, do I call upon: send me strength, as having borne the Mighty One, Whom with thee I magnify!

*Exapostilarion:*
Having endured many labors in life, struggling with enemies, thou didst reach a blessed end, and wast translated unto things on high into transcendent rest; but thy memory abides among us, and the Church confesses thy labors: remembering which we pray thee, visit us, and grant a blessing unto those who honor thy translation on this day, O our father Isaac!

*Theotokion:*
O swift intercessor for Christians, make haste to stand before those who are tormented by many misfortunes, that hoping in thee we may not fall away from hope, but delivered from misfortunes through thee, we may glorify thy honorable childbearing, O all-immaculate one!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 3:*
Thou didst do good according to the word of the Lord, taking the furrows of thy heart without pain with the plow of thy narrow life, O venerable one; having tilled the field of thy soul, thou didst water it with a stream of tears, and sowing labors like pure wheat, thou didst grow ears of monastic virtues, and didst reap sheaves of unceasing prayers, which thou didst bring into the heavenly granary; and now receiving praise with the good servants, remember us, O all-blessed father, who lovingly honor thy memory!

Having walked by the narrow path in this temporal age with sorrows, tears, and unceasing vigilance, with groanings and afflictions, O worthy of glory, thou didst turn away from the broad, ruinous way, and didst reach the heavenly expanse, where having rest...

*(Right page — Folio 66 recto, running header: TO ISAAC THE RECLUSE OF THE CAVES:)*
...rest without sorrow, forget not us also in thy goodness, as an intercessor for our souls!

Having become poor for God's sake, disregarding all worldly possessions, thou didst follow Christ Who became poor for our sake, from Whom having received the perfection of virtues, thou wast enriched with incorruptible treasure: forget not us who are impoverished in good deeds, but grant unto us of thy fullness, O venerable father, who honor thy honorable falling asleep on this day!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having cast off the world and all voluptuousness, O venerable one, taking thy cross upon thy shoulder in Gospel manner, thou didst follow Christ: having lived in a narrow cave, by the extreme sharpness of monastic labors thou didst conquer him who had formerly conquered thee; and after thy falling asleep thou wast shown so fearful unto him, that he trembles even at thy name, unable to endure thy rebuke: are not these indeed the spirits of wickedness under heaven? Drive them away from us also, O blessed Isaac, and ask great mercy for us from Christ God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save Thy servants from misfortunes, O most holy Mother of God!
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*(Vignette occupying lower half of page: decorative baroque tailpiece) (Left page — Folio 66 verso, full-page woodcut engraving with decorative floral border, depicting the martyrdom of Saint Eustratius: Saint Eustratius nailed and bound upon a wooden cross in the center, inscribed with his name; below, a Jewish captor piercing his side with a spear while another points mockingly; a dog resting at the base of the cross; in the background, a lake/river and buildings with observers on a balcony)*

---

# MONTH OF MARCH

*On the 28th Day:*
*Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr Eustratius of the Caves:*[^230]
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "Of the heavenly hosts" (Небесных чинов):*[^231]
O thou who didst first crucify thyself willingly unto the world by the cutting off of all passions, thou now at last, having become a partaker of the eternal suffering, sufferest the passion of ultimate mortification, being nailed unto a cross by the Judeans; as by a ladder...

*(Right page — Folio 67 recto, running header: TO EUSTRATIUS OF THE CAVES:)*
...as by a ladder thou ascendest unto heaven; whence, O blessed Eustratius, look down, raise up our minds unto the heights, and pray unto Christ God to grant us the cutting off of all passions!

A second chariot-driver Elijah, by zeal for Christ thou wast shown forth, O marvelous one: for even as he rebuked the madness of the reigning king, so didst thou also censure the God-killing madness of the foolish Judean; and from him thou didst receive to be crucified upon a cross in the body, but thy soul ran unto the heavenly realms in a chariot of fire, where thou rejoicest, illumined by unwaning light: whereof may we also not be deprived, through thy prayers!

Portraying the passion of thy Master, thou wast crucified upon a cross by a Judean, by whom also thou wast pierced in the ribs with a spear; ascending upon a chariot of fire, thou didst signify thy fervent love toward God. Standing before Him now in rejoicing, pray for those who hymn thee, O blessed father Eustratius!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Ever looking unto the heavenly glory, O venerable one, thou didst first crucify thy body by abstinence; and afterwards, having thy hands and feet nailed by the Judeans, thou didst find the ladder of desire, whereby having ascended unto the hosts on high, a magnificent voice calling thee: This is a good citizen of the heavenly Sion! Rejoicing now in it with the saints, remember us who honor thy holy memory, that we also may become heirs thereof with thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The shadow hath passed...

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And Readings: Three for a Venerable Monk. (Seek folio 30, on the reverse).*[^232]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехвальнии):*[^233]
O all-praised venerable-martyr Eustratius, thou didst commit thy life unto the care of virtues; for with hymnody the faithful praise Christ in faith, being instructed by thy teachings: standing before Him, entreat the Divine Spirit that we may be confirmed in peace!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
O divinely-inspired martyr Eustratius, thy life indeed was shown like unto the angels unto earthly men; and unto the heavenly ones thy confession was offered as a sweet-smelling sacrifice and good incense. And now pray, that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls!

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord (Left page — Folio 67 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HOLY VENERABLE-MARTYR:)*
...the man that fears the Lord, he shall greatly delight in His commandments.

O exceedingly rich venerable-martyr Eustratius, having become a conqueror of passions, thou didst conquer the tormentor. Dancing in choir with the crown-bearers, O blessed one, remember all who celebrate thy memory! And now pray unto Christ, to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory... Tone 4:*
The Judeans did not understand the words of the prophets, going astray from the womb, speaking a lie against our Savior, and with the reproach of a curse condemned the Master's death. But thou, O blessed one, rebuking their God-killing, didst show forth the blessing given unto all the world through the Crucifixion. Therefore the lawless ones nailed thee to a cross, and pierced thy ribs with a spear, not knowing that for the sake of thy cross thou receivest a reward from God, while the ungodly shall quickly perish. They inherited a curse through the tree, but thou enjoyest the Tree of Life: whereof that we also may be partakers with thee, we glorify the Savior and Lord in thy memory, O father Eustratius!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Receive the supplications of thy servants...

*Troparion, Tone 7:*
Thou wast shown forth as the praise of ascetics and martyrs, O venerable Eustratius: for having first mortified the flesh by abstinence, thou didst follow Christ, bearing thy cross. Afterwards thou thyself wast also lifted up upon a cross, and pierced in the ribs with a spear, and didst commit thy soul into the hands of God. Carried up into the heavens in a chariot of fire, whence thou wast crowned by Christ God and the Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the same Troparion.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn: Tone 4, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Лик ангельский):*[^234]
Emulating the passion of thy Master, thou didst endure the wood of the cross, O all-blessed one, and didst put to shame the blaspheming Judean with thy divinely-inspired lips: therefore the ungodly one pierces thy ribs with a spear, but thou rejoicing ascendest unto thy Lord; pray unto Him unceasingly for us, who lovingly honor thy sufferings, O valiantly-wise Eustratius!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
I offer angelic hymnody unto thee, O unwedded Virgin: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace and blessed among women, who didst receive in thy womb...

*(Right page — Folio 68 recto, running header: TO EUSTRATIUS OF THE CAVES:)*
...who didst receive in thy womb the Word of God seedlessly, Who delivered mankind from irrational deeds!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn: Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (Собезначальное):*[^235]
Let us, O ye people, honor with hymns the citizen of the Jerusalem on high, the true witness, and good soldier of Christ, the valiantly-wise Eustratius, fellow-dweller of the venerable and fellow-chorister of the martyrs, singing his memory: for standing before Christ God, he unceasingly prays for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Queen of the Jerusalem on high, only immaculate Mother among women, crown of virgins, and holiest of the holy, unsearchable intercessor before the Holy Trinity: have mercy upon us who have strayed far away, O Mother of God, and deliver from misfortunes those who hymn thee!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn: Tone 3, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Скоро предвари):*[^236]
Kindled with the flame of burning love toward the crucified Christ, O blessed one, thou didst not fear the Hebrew God-killer breathing with fury, but didst valiantly rebuke him, and didst preach the confirmation of the legal Passover from the Scriptures when grace had come: therefore thou wast nailed unto a cross by the Judeans, and having received the crown of confession, thou didst enter into the heavenly courts. Where forget not us also, O father Eustratius!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
When we are burned by the flame of passions from destructive assaults, make haste, O most holy Virgin, to send down the water of grace to quench the fire burning in our members, that leading this life in tranquility, we may radiantly enjoy the Resurrection of Christ for thy sake!

*Anabathmoi, the First Antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Mark, pericope 37.*[^237]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron: Tone 6:*
This day the hill of the Caves rejoices, holding thy relics as a priceless treasure, and is enlightened by the rays of thy sufferings, bringing flowers of spiritual songs unto thee and saying: O favored one of Christ, our father Eustratius, standing before the Master of all, unceasingly pray unto the Lord for us, to send down great mercy upon our souls!

*(Left page — Folio 68 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HOLY VENERABLE-MARTYR:)*
*Canon to the Venerable-Martyr.*
*Tone 1.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in power in a manner befitting God: for it, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, fashioning a new path through the deep for the Israelites![^238]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to be a partaker of the light and day of the Resurrection, O venerable one, on the day of the Master's Passion thou didst ascend upon a cross, and wast conformed unto Christ's Passion, having conquered the world; and being now a partaker of ineffable glory, thou rejoicest, singing a song of victory!

Nothing in this turbulent world didst thou love, O venerable one, like the Cross of Christ, and having it as thy boast, with the Apostle thou didst cry aloud: God forbid that I should boast, save in the Cross of the Lord, whereon thou rejoicest, singing a song of victory![^239]

Being nailed unto a cross by the Judeans, O most blessed father, thou didst nail down all bodily passions, so that with the Apostle thou couldest say: The world hath been crucified unto me, and I unto the world; therefore worthily now thou rejoicest with the Risen One, singing a song of victory!

*Glory...*
Having departed from the lustful Egypt of this world, by the cross thou didst untroubled cross its stormy sea, O holy one; and having written over it with thy nailed palms, thou didst drown the noetic Pharaoh with his aerial armies, and now rejoicest, singing a song of victory!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having departed from the lustful Egypt of this world, I return unto former things, and pleasing the belly, feed in beastly manner; grant unto me, O Virgin Bride of God, to turn away at least a little from these things, that being set free I may offer praise unto thee, singing a song of victory!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and mercifully didst take form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry unto Thee: Holy is the living temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind![^240]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst follow Him Who said: Come unto Me, all ye that labor; and by His Cross thou didst disperse the noetic enemies, who being utterly conquered returned with shame; pray for us also, that we may stand against their ranks: for in this we are confirmed in Christ!

Having laid the cross upon thy shoulder, O holy one, by undoubting faith and love thou didst bind thyself unto the One, that thou mightest gain Christ; and having found Him according to thy desire by the narrow path, thou didst traverse it...

*(Right page — Folio 69 recto, running header: TO EUSTRATIUS OF THE CAVES:)*
...thou didst traverse it without stumbling, having the Cross as thy strength and confirmation!

Clothed in the monastic habit, thou didst strip thyself of the world, O blessed one, and having traversed the path lightly into the promised land, into the monastery of the Caves, which pours forth the teachings of the God-bearing fathers like honey and milk, wherewith being fed thou wast made strong: by their teachings grant that we also may be confirmed!

*Glory...*
Having subjected the sensible enemy unto the spirit by the word of prayer and fasting, and by humility and obedience having conquered the noetic Amalek, thou wast pleasing unto thy Master, O father, and from Him didst receive the Cross as an unconquerable weapon, whereon we also are confirmed![^241]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Ever shaken by the enemy's assaults, having no confirmation, I, the passionate one, flee unto thee, O Mother of God: for thou art an impregnable wall unto the faithful, wherein we are confirmed!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*
Come, ye people, let us hymn the memory of the victorious sufferer, passing the day of his rejoicing with songs and hymnody: for from this vale of tears he hath passed unto unending joy, and standing before the Master's throne, cries aloud unto us: Be ye imitators of me, even as I am of Christ, Who grants His rich mercy unto all!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having a passionate life in the world, burdened with waves of deadly sins, O thou who didst bear the Helmsman, Christ: direct my life unto the calm haven, I pray thee, and grant me thy rich mercy!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Seeing with his prophetic eyes the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal![^242]

*Troparia:*
Knowing, O venerable one, that because of intemperance the first man settled in hades, thou didst give thyself unto abstinence and severe fasting, and from this didst acquire the name of faster: for thou wast fed by the Word of God rather than with food, singing unto Christ: For Thou art all-powerful!

By permission from on high having been taken captive by the Polovtsians, O holy one, thou wast in apostolic manner: Sorrow it is that brings every thought into captivity unto the obedience of Christ; therefore in sorrows, O blessed one, thou didst not faint, but rather marveling at the judgments of God, didst glorify Christ: For Thou art all-powerful![^243]

*(Left page — Folio 69 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HOLY VENERABLE-MARTYR:)*
Ever bearing upon thy body the image of the betrayed Jesus, O blessed father, thou wast sold into the Greek land, into the city of Korsun,[^244] unto a certain God-killing Judean, who sought to turn thee away from the love of God, for the sake of which thou wast ready even unto death, singing: Christ is risen, for He is all-powerful!

*Glory...*
Shut up in prison with other Christians, and tormented with hunger by the Hebrew, thou didst warmly and sweetly say: Who shall separate me from the love of Christ? Neither hunger, nor death, nor distress: for all these things are small compared to the glory that is to be revealed in us, who sing: Christ is risen, for He is all-powerful![^245]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having been taken captive by noetic enemies, and being already near the prison of hades, turning unto thee I cry aloud: O Mother of God, when thou shalt turn back the captivity of my soul, then shall my tongue be filled with rejoicing, and shall sing: Thy Son is risen, for He is all-powerful!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Christ Who didst enlighten with the radiance of Thy coming, and by Thy Cross didst illuminate the ends of the world: enlighten the hearts of those who orthodoxly hymn Thee with the light of Thy knowledge of God![^246]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst rise early with love toward Christ, O valiant Eustratius, opposing the Hebrew who compelled thee to depart from thy Christ, Who is the Sun of Righteousness, shining forth ever-existing life unto all!

By thy teaching, O father, all the captives who were in prison spat upon the madness of the God-killing Hebrew, and chose rather to die of hunger and thirst than to deny Christ, Who grants life unto all!

A company of martyrs fifty in number, starved by hunger and thirst by the Judean, thou didst send before thee unto thy Christ, O blessed one, thyself afterwards desiring to suffer the same for the love of Jesus, Who grants life unto all![^247]

*Glory...*
Seeing, O wretched one, that when all the prisoners had died in the dungeon his gold was lost, and his hope of drawing them unto his impiety had vanished, he resolved to crucify thee upon a cross, unto which thou didst run with haste, O venerable one, crying aloud: O my Christ, lead up my soul from the bodily prison!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O most blessed Virgin, have compassion in thy goodness upon my soul that is perishing with hunger, and satisfy it with the angelic bread, and give it to drink from the torrent of invisible sweetness, that thence I may find peace for my soul!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The nethermost abyss hath compassed us, there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter: save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration![^248]

*(Right page — Folio 70 recto, running header: TO EUSTRATIUS OF THE CAVES:)*
*Troparia:*
Like that of a voracious beast was the belly of the lawless Judean, who sought to swallow thee up and bring thee into the number of the darkened Jewish synagogue; but thou, O father, didst boldly stand against his malice, and didst conquer him, being strengthened by the help of the Most High!

On the very day whereon the Judeans raged and hung the Giver of Life upon a cross, then also the wretched Hebrew crucified thee: that with Him with Whom thou didst suffer, with Him thou mayest also be glorified, O blessed one, in unwaning light, having received a reward from thy Savior!

Remembering the Master's Passion with mocking, the defiled God-killers, even as their fathers did Christ, so these, mocking at the wood of the cross, nailed thee thereto, O father; but unto them the wood of the cross became a curse, while unto thee it became the saving power of God!

*Glory...*
Rebuking the feasts of the defiled Judeans, while upon the cross, O holy one, thou didst not fear their curse: for it was abolished by Christ God nailed in the flesh, Who enlightened the world by His three-day Resurrection!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure Mother, who by thy blessed childbearing didst bury our forefather's curse: account me worthy to be given the blessing of God's chosen ones, O blessed and glorified one!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Being a zealot of the Master's Passion and an ardent follower of fasters, having instructed the captives by thy teaching, thou didst bring them unto Christ, O Eustratius; and deliver us who are held captive by passions through thy prayers, and show us to be imitators of thy honorable life, who cry aloud: Rejoice, thou who didst sweetly drink the cup of Christ's sufferings![^249]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
We faithful contemplate thee, O Mother of God, as a spiritual furnace: for even as the Exalted One saved the three youths, the All-Powerful One renewed the world in thy womb, the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers![^250]

*Troparia:*
Being nailed upon a cross, O father, thou didst rebuke the foolish Judean command by the prophetic Scriptures, and didst not desire to taste of the legal passover, desiring to be a partaker of the Passover which was sacrificed for the whole world: Christ God, Who is blessed!

Thou didst declare the abolishing of the curse that came from the tree from the prophets, O blessed one, and didst preach the entrance of life with Life hanging before their eyes upon the wood of the cross, and didst put to shame the Judeans who knew not how to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*(Left page — Folio 70 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HOLY VENERABLE-MARTYR:)*
Unto the Judeans who crucified thee thou didst prophesy, O venerable father: Swiftly shall vengeance come upon you for my blood and that of the other Christians whom ye have slain innocently, and your feasts shall be turned into mourning: for the Lord hates your sabbaths, wherein ye were accustomed to sing: O Christ God, blessed art Thou![^251]

*Glory...*
Thy words were sharp arrows unto the lawless ones, wherewith being pierced they could not endure, but pierced thy ribs with a spear; and thou didst depart unto the springs of life for all the world from the pierced side, to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Unto me who am pierced with the sinful spear of fierce Belial, swiftly apply the plaster of thy mercy, O Virgin Mother, Bride of God, that being healed I may ever sing to thee, O Lady: Blessed art thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible, shone brighter than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him unto all ages![^252]

*Troparia:*
Honor, O ye people, the good sufferer, who having first mentally crucified all his members by himself, afterwards being sensibly crucified by the Judeans, cried aloud unto Christ: Behold, I bear Thy marks upon my body, and I sing to Thee unto all ages![^253]

Unto thee, O father, caught up unto heaven in a chariot of fire and with horses, the choirs of martyrs and righteous went forth to meet thee, and praised the wounds of thy hands and feet and thy pierced side, receiving thee into the dwelling of the saints, to praise the Lord unto all ages!

A voice was brought unto thee, O holy one, from the magnificent glory: This good heavenly citizen, of whom the whole world was not worthy, ascends from the earthly world unto the transcendent dwellings, to sing unto the Lord unto all ages!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*
Thy relics, a priceless treasure, O all-blessed one, the lawless Hebrew wishing to hide, cast into the sea; but by God's providence they were found here in the cave, where venerating them, we glorify the Lord Who glorified thee, O blessed one, with incorruption and wonders unto all ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bring forth the priceless treasure, the most precious Jesus, from thy maternal womb: redeem my soul from the captivity of the lustful world, that I may sing to thee here...

*(Right page — Folio 71 recto, running header: TO EUSTRATIUS OF THE CAVES:)*
...that I may sing to thee here, and in the unwaning light unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The unburned bush showed forth an image of thy pure childbearing: and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of misfortunes raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Mother of God![^254]

*Troparia:*
Shine, shine, O monastery of the Caves of the Mother of God! For in thee flourished the marvelous Eustratius, a blossoming tree adorned with fragrant flowers of suffering: that we also may become partakers of his beauty, we venerate his relics and magnify him with hymns!

God, Who reveals secrets, revealed the defiled murderers unto the pious emperor, showing that the Judeans who pretended to be citizens secretly slay Christians, and their iniquity came down upon their own head: for they received a death equal to their deed according to thy prophecy, O most blessed father![^255]

Thy blood innocently poured out by the Judeans, like Abel's, O father, cried aloud unto God, Who by righteous judgment brought vengeance for it; and thy tormentor being hanged upon a tree, received his portion with Judas in gehenna: therefore we magnify the portion of the Lord, Whom we venerate on this day![^256]

*Glory...*
Thy blood, O holy one, and thy unfailing prophecy afterwards brought many unto the faith: for seeing its fulfillment, truly repenting they believed in Christ, and being baptized lived well, and were accounted worthy of the blessed life, whereof that we also may not be deprived, we bow down before the reliquary of thy relics, and magnify thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O immaculate Mother, who didst embrace the ever-existing Life in thy womb: direct us from temporal life unto eternal by thy mediation, and grant unto all to behold the unwaning day of the kingdom of thy Son!

*Exapostilarion:*
Foreseeing the ever-existing life, O blessed one, thou didst disregard temporal life, enduring all things inflicted by the Judeans with valor, and not wishing to taste of the legal passover, thou wast nailed unto a cross, and ascending from earth in a chariot of fire didst testify: Christ is the Passover of incorruption, the salvation of the world!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Being the mediatress of the ever-existing Light, O Virgin Bride of God: stand by us who dwell in darkness, and direct our life, that without stumbling we may reach *(Left page — Folio 71 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HOLY VENERABLE-MARTYR:)*
...where Christ is, the Passover of incorruption, the salvation of the world!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 6:*
Having reddened thy sacred robe with martyr's blood, O venerable one, thou didst worthily enter into the Holy of Holies, O glorious one, where is ineffable light, where is divine glory, where is the voice of those that keep festival: from which, for thy martyr's labors, thou didst receive victories, and an unfading crown, and eternal glory of good things, and the life of paradise, O all-wise Eustratius; with boldness pray for our souls! *(Twice)*

The storm of torments did not shake thy soul, nor did long confinement in a dark prison, which hid thee away, O glorious sufferer: but like light thou didst shine forth, giving light unto those in distress in darkness, whom thou didst show forth as sons of the day by the laver of baptism, an exalted and saving birth, and by divine conversation, as a God-pleasing sufferer, O all-wise Eustratius!

Who shall worthily recount the victories of thy virtues? What lips shall utter thy torments and patience? For in both thou wast well-pleasing unto Christ, O Eustratius; and now cease not praying, O favored one, to save our souls, as having great boldness, O venerable-martyr!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
The Cherub, seeing thee coming on a chariot of fire unto the heavens, did not bar thee from tasting of the Tree of Life with his flaming sword, but with joy received thee, O martyr of Christ, Eustratius! Therefore now enjoying the food of paradise, forget not us who honor thy memory, but stretch forth thy palms, wherewith thou wast nailed unto the tree, and pray unto the Creator and Master of all for all, that we also may become partakers with thee, O most blessed father!

*Both now... Theotokion.*
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*(Vignette occupying lower half of page: decorative baroque tailpiece)*

*(Right page — Folio 72 recto, full-page woodcut engraving with decorative floral border, depicting Saint Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod: Saint Niphont in episcopal vestments, mitre, and omophorion, holding a pastoral crosier in his right hand; inscribed beside him "С: Нїфонтъ єппкъ новгородскїй"; in the background, a cathedral with domes and belltowers on the left, and monastic buildings on the right)*
