# MONTH OF MAY

*On the 3rd Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Theodosius, Hegumen of the Caves Monastery in Kyiv, and Initiator of the Monastic Coenobitic Life in the Holy Russian Land:*[^281]
*At Small Vespers, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^282]
True and blameless, venerable and guileless, keeping thyself back from every evil thing, thou wast known, O blessed one, truly adorned with virtues and shining with piety; therefore we call thee blessed, and celebrating today thy holy and honorable falling asleep, we rejoice!

*(Right page — Folio 77 recto, running header: TO THEODOSIUS, HEGUMEN OF THE CAVES:)*
Blameless and most honorable, a most lofty pillar and unconquerable by the wiles of the enemy, we all call thee blessed, and faithfully praise Theodosius the great, who wast a favorite of Christ and of His most pure Mother, and who diligently prayest unto Him to save our souls from misfortunes!

Thou didst clarify the monastic life, having shone forth like the sun in the Russian land: for having hedged about thy body with abstinence, thou didst mortify the stirrings of passion by prayers and vigils; therefore we pray thee, O all-blessed Theodosius: cease not helping us, and visiting thy servants!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Come, ye assembly of the faithful, merrily this day; come, let us all sing with joy in the honorable temple unto the God-bearing fathers, and clap our hands on their most radiant memory, whom Christ our God hath glorified; therefore with a voice of joy and a pure conscience let us cry aloud, saying: Rejoice, earthly angel and heavenly man, all-blessed Anthony, dwelling-place of the venerable! Rejoice, golden-beaming eastern star, Father Theodosius, luminary and shepherd of monastics! Rejoice, our boast, joy and helpers of the universe! Dwelling with the angels in the light of the Holy Trinity that crowned you, diligently pray to deliver from enemies visible and invisible, and from every misfortune, us who celebrate with faith and love your all-festal memory!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*[^283]
*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "House of Ephratha" (До́ме Єѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^284]
Morning and radiant day hath thy feast appeared, enlightening our hearts who faithfully praise thine honorable ascetic struggles, O Father Theodosius!
*Verse: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^285]
The day of salvation and feast of gladness hath appeared: let us gather together, ye faithful, having purified our souls, to hymn the all-blessed Theodosius!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; he shall greatly delight in His commandments.*[^286]
Having found Anthony as a sun upon earth, shining with virtues, thou didst show thyself a most radiant star: do thou therefore lead those who remember thee unto good things, O venerable Theodosius!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O thou who didst receive the Joy of the world, Christ the Giver of life, and who didst assuage our foremother's grief, O Virgin!

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Having been elevated unto virtue, from thy youth having loved the monastic life, having valiantly attained unto thy desire, thou didst settle in a cave; and adorning thy life with fasting and radiance *(Left page — Folio 77 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...and radiance, in prayers thou didst abide as one bodiless; having shone forth in the Russian land like a radiant luminary, O Father Theodosius, pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved![^287]

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast.*
*At Great Vespers:*
*We sing "Blessed is the man".*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera: Tone 5, Podoben: "Venerable father" (Преподо́бне ѻ́ч҃е):*[^288]
O venerable father, God-bearer Theodosius, having striven greatly in temporal life with hymns, fasting, and vigil, thou wast an example unto thy disciples; and now indeed thou dancest with the bodiless hosts, unceasingly glorifying Christ, the Word of God from God, the Deliverer, Who bowed His head upon the Cross, and delivered the human race from deception: pray unto Him, entreat Him, O venerable one, to grant peace and great mercy unto the universe!

O venerable father, God-bearer Theodosius, truly wast thou accounted worthy of divine life, having found it by purity and fasting: for while living thou didst pass over unto true life, unto the King of all, unceasingly glorifying Christ with the bodiless hosts, Who ineffably received flesh from the Virgin, Who overthrew hades, and raised up the dead from the ages: pray unto Him, entreat Him, O venerable one, to grant peace and great mercy unto the universe!

O venerable father, God-bearer Theodosius, thou hast found that which was beloved unto thine honorable soul: for the grace of the All-Holy Spirit made His abode in thee, as a most pure light, by Whose visitation being radiantly illumined, thou didst unceasingly glorify Christ, the one God in two essences, Who suffered in the flesh upon the Cross, and remained impassible in His nature: pray unto Him, entreat Him, O venerable one, to grant peace and great mercy unto the universe!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Come, let us all gather together at the divine memory of our Father Theodosius: for having received a calling from on high from youth, he was named by the Greeks "God-given gift";[^289] therefore unto Christ-named princes he was a teacher of the right faith, unto nobles a firm defense, unto orphans a merciful father, unto widows a warm protector, unto the sorrowing a consolation, unto the poor a treasure, unto the monastic choir a ladder ascending unto heavenly height, and simply unto those who have recourse unto him he was a fountain of ever-flowing water; he unceasingly prays that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*
*And three readings.*

*(Right page — Folio 78 recto, running header: TO THEODOSIUS, HEGUMEN OF THE CAVES:)*
*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon:*
The righteous live unto the ages, and in the Lord is their reward, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive the kingdom of majesty, and the crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord: for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take His zeal for full armor, and shall make the creation His weapon for vengeance on His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall take unfeigned judgment for a helmet. He shall take holiness for an invincible shield; He shall sharpen His severe wrath for a weapon, and the world shall fight with Him against the senseless. Right-aiming shafts of lightnings shall go forth, and as from a well-drawn bow of the clouds they shall fly unto the mark. And from His stone-hurling wrath shall hailstones full of fury be cast. The water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall mercilessly overflow. A spirit of power shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall scatter them; and iniquity shall make the whole earth desolate, and evildoing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty. Give ear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and that boast yourselves in crowds of nations: for power is given you from the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High.[^290]

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon:*
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was accounted an affliction, and their going from us destruction; but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly benefited: for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace He tried them, and as a whole burnt-offering He accepted them. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and like sparks through stubble they shall run to and fro. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign over them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand truth, and the faithful in love shall abide with Him: for grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and visitation in His chosen.[^291]

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon:*
Though the righteous happen to die, he shall be in rest. For venerable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years. But wisdom is gray hairs unto men, and an unspotted life is ripe old age. Being well-pleasing unto God, he was beloved, and living among sinners he was translated. He was caught away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding *(Left page — Folio 78 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...or deceit should beguile his soul. For the bewitching of naughtiness obscures good things, and the wandering of concupiscence undermines the simple mind. Being made perfect in a short space, he fulfilled long years: for his soul was pleasing unto the Lord: therefore He hastened to take him away from the midst of wickedness. And the peoples saw, and understood it not, neither laid they up such a thing in their mind, that grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and visitation in His chosen.[^292]

*At the Litiya, Stichera: Tone 1:*
Come, ye feast-lovers this day, having assembled a choir in piety, let us celebrate the memory of the great father, the all-world luminary, honorable and glorious, praising him and saying: Rejoice, Theodosius, kinsman of the venerable and righteous! Rejoice, thou who didst assemble an honorable flock of the most sacred! Rejoice, faithful minister and builder of the monastery of the Mother of God, which excellent church thou didst erect for the glory of God: therefore now we pray thee: pray that we may be delivered from every assault and misfortune, and from barbarian invasion, and that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 2:*
O disciple of the Savior, God-wise Theodosius, God-bearing mouth, God-revealed mind, spiritual commander, good shepherd, boast of the venerable: Thou didst seek God, and Him Thou didst proclaim, being illuminated with rays of light; with God Thou didst converse, being unceasingly purified and illuminated; with streams of tears Thou didst make thy mind radiant; as a pillar of fire and a cloud of light Thou didst appear, O all-blessed one, boast of monastics, glory of fathers, and minister of the Trinity: and now preserve us all by thine intercession!

O venerable Father Theodosius, thou wast bountiful unto those melting in the heat of thirst; thou didst rule divinely with wonders of reward, as an excellent minister imitating thy Master: thou didst multiply the wine, and by thy command didst fill the empty granaries with flour, and didst make the failing vessel of honey to abound; knowing hidden things, such things thou didst clarify, showing forth martyr-like sufferings; thou didst grant multitudes of healings; thou didst gladden the poor by thy child-loving ways; and thou didst constrict thy belly with bread and water, in the flesh imitating the life of the bodiless: therefore we offer glory unto thee, praying: Forsake not us thy servants, preserving us by thy prayers!

Two most radiant luminaries have shone forth in the Russian land, Anthony and Theodosius: for the one by divine word brought down heavenly dew upon the earth, and brought down fire from heaven...

*(Right page — Folio 79 recto, running header: TO THEODOSIUS, HEGUMEN OF THE CAVES:)*
...brought down fire from heaven, and clearly prophesied things to come, having received prophetic grace; and the other asked rain from heaven, and rebuked the prince concerning brother-hatred and unrighteous flattery,[^293] and saw the Lord face-to-face, and in the air, like Elijah, was seen over the monastery in flaming light, lifting up his hands and making prayers unto God; and now rejoicing with the angels, they pray for us, that with them we may be accounted worthy of the true light, and that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Preserving that which is according to the image unhurt, having ascetically set the mind as master over destructive passions, thou didst ascend, as far as was possible, unto that which is according to the likeness: for having manfully constrained nature, thou didst hasten to subject the worse unto the better, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit; therefore thou wast shown forth as the head of monastics, a desert-dweller, a trainer of those that run well, a most certain rule of virtue; and now in the heavens, the mirrors being done away, O our Father Theodosius, thou purely beholdest the Holy Trinity, praying unceasingly for those who honor thee with faith and love!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*[^294]
O God-wise Father Theodosius, having joined thy mind unto God from the womb, thou didst utterly disregard worldly things, O King all-ruler,[^295] offering worthy fruits unto Him from thy sweaty ascetic struggles, gathered by virtuous good things; therefore thou hast found the reception of good things, O wondrously worthy!
*Verse: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^296]
Thou art the instructor of monastics, and a most radiant star of the Russian land, illuminating all this country with wonders: for God chose thee from youth, O father, as shepherd unto rational sheep; therefore the choir of the venerable received thee: with whom, we pray thee, pray that forgiveness of sins may be granted unto thy flock!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; he shall greatly delight in His commandments.*[^297]
Rejoice, O all-hymned Virgin, who by a pillar of fire didst show the pasture where the divine flock was to be pastured: and that church Theodosius straightway erected, banquet-like and all-marvelous unto thee: wherein all we faithful celebrate thy falling asleep, O most pure Mother of God, standing around the reliquary of thy favorite![^298]

*Glory... Tone 8:*
We honor thee, O our Father Theodosius, as the instructor of multitudes of monastics: for by thy path we have truly learned to walk uprightly! Blessed art thou, who didst work for Christ, and didst expose the enemy's power, converser with angels, companion of the venerable and righteous: with whom pray unto the Lord, that our souls may be saved!
*Both now (Left page — Folio 79 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...And now... Of the feast.
At the blessing of the loaves, the troparion of the saint, twice; or else: once.[^299]
At "God is the Lord", the troparion of the feast, twice; Glory... Of the saint; Both now... Of the feast.
After the 1st Stasis of the Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Go thou before" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):[^300]
Thou didst follow the calling of thy Lord, having forsaken the world and all the beautiful things of the world, O all-blessed Theodosius; thou didst endure bodily affliction, and in a cave in a sorrowful and narrow place thou didst strengthen thyself zealously; and thou didst manfully drive away the regiments of demons; therefore we ever faithfully praise thy memory with hymns! *(Twice)*
Glory... Both now... Of the feast.

After the 2nd Stasis of the Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):[^301]
Having richly watered thy soul with streams of tears, O wise father, thou didst reap a fruitful ear of thy virtues; and having become a shepherd unto the sheep, O venerable one, thou didst nourish them in the pasture of abstinence; therefore having been gloriously released from life, thou wast united unto the fatherly choirs, O Theodosius; for this cause we cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory! *(Twice)*
Glory... Both now... Of the feast.

After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):[^302]
Being truly sanctified from the womb, O blessed Theodosius, thou didst live upon earth like an angel: for having mortified the flesh, and worthily emulated Anthony's life in the cave, thou wast shown forth as their favorite, O blessed one; therefore also unto zeal for thy divine life thou didst gather choirs of monastics, O glorious one; O our God-bearing father, pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*
Glory... Both now... Of the feast.

Gradual hymn, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone.
Prokeimenon: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?[^303]
Let every breath...
Gospel according to Luke, pericope 24.[^304]
After Psalm 50, Sticheron: Tone 6:
Having ascended the course unto heaven by virtues, a divine seat, thou didst reach the outermost city by fasting, ascending from earth unto the most beautiful higher Jerusalem; and receiving worthy honor for thy sufferings and ascetic struggles, thou rejoicest with the heavenly ranks, O all-blessed one, having become an heir of eternal good things and a citizen of the Kingdom; but pray, O God-bearing Theodosius, unto the Savior of all, to give peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

Canon of the feast, with the heirmos to 6; and two canons to the saint to 8: Tone 8 and Tone 6.[^305]
*Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking the sea in the form of a cross and dividing it, once drowned the chariot-driving Pharaoh, but saved the fleeing people of Israel as they marched on foot, who sang a song unto God![^306]
*Troparia:*
Having acquired a God-pleasing struggle, O Father Theodosius, thou wast shown forth as a beholder of God; therefore pray unto God, Whom thou didst please, to drive away the darkness of my ignorance, that being divinely inspired I may hymn thee!
From thy mother's womb thou didst cleave unto God, O venerable one, like the all-wise Paul and the prophet Jeremiah of old; therefore divine grace named thee through the lips of Theodosius the servant of God![^307]
Having followed the Savior's commandments from youth, O wise one, having accounted this life as nothing, desiring to bear the cross upon thy shoulders, thou didst hate all earthly things, looking unto the heavenly, O Theodosius!
*Theotokion:*
Having become incarnate from thy most pure womb, O most holy Virgin, the Word of God enlightened all with the light of the knowledge of God, and accounted the festival of men worthy of the angelic choir, and led them up unto the heavens!

*Second Canon, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God![^308]
*Troparia:*
Illumined with most radiant divine rays, O Father Theodosius, enlighten my mind by thy prayers, that I may radiantly hymn thy divine memory!
Unto the one God thou didst offer thyself from youth, from Whom thou didst receive the rich grace of wonders; therefore thou wast an incorruptible treasure unto thy flock!
A new Abraham hast thou appeared unto us, bringing a sacrifice unto God, truly the Lamb that takes away the sins of the whole world, making the loaves with thine own clean hands![^309]
*Theotokion:*
He Who is before the morning star, the Word of God, appeared as a man unto us, having become incarnate for our sake from the most pure Virgin; and having come forth from her, He preserved her unhurt!

*Katavasia of the feast.*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord, and there is none righteous; do Thou establish me in Thy love, O desire of edges, the establishment of the faithful, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind![^310]
*Troparia:*
Having enriched thy divine and blessed soul with incorruptible and spiritual gifts, O holy one, thou didst desire to see the Lord's Sepulchre; but from on high thou wast restrained by divine providence![^311]
Desiring to be a perfect, true, and blameless sacrifice unto the Lamb that takes away the sins of the whole world by His most pure blood, O blessed one, thou didst offer thyself as a bloodless sacrifice!
Thou wast an author of the rule and an acquirer of the measure of youth, O father, and a master of the mind, having fled worldly origins, thou didst receive instruction from the Divine Spirit!
*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O thou who alone didst bring forth the Lord of all, who didst herald joy unto men! Rejoice, tabernacle and unhewn mountain, establishment of the faithful, only all-hymned one!

*(Left page — Folio 80 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...only all-hymned one.
*Another Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession![^312]
*Troparia:*
Theodosius gave ear unto the Gospel voice, and ran swiftly unto the church; desiring to take the cross of the Lord upon His shoulder, having forsaken all worldly attachment, he followed Him!
Thy mother who bore thee, having tried thee, commanded thee to guard thy garments; and she saw a dreadful sight: thy loins gushing with blood from the iron girdle![^313]
Greatly desiring to see the Lord's Sepulchre, O blessed Theodosius, thou wast restrained by the Master's providence, and becamest a companion of the venerable Anthony!
*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding didst thou conceive, O all-hymned Virgin, and alone from the ages didst bring forth the Creator and God and Maker of all, the Word of God!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the feast.*
*Then, Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4:*
Beyond human mind didst thou appear upon earth, O venerable one, a divine garden planted standing in the midst of the house of the Lord; and thou dispensest the fruit of thy labors unto thy disciples: for from thy lips thou didst make the grace of the Spirit to spring forth, having been an example of struggles and a rule unto those that love the Lord; Whom cease not now to entreat, that those who hymn thee may be saved! *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Of the feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the Father's bosom and didst visit our poverty; therefore with the prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^314]
*Troparia:*
The grace of the Spirit grew in thee, O father, when thou didst see her that bore thee weeping and wailing bitterly: standing before her as she suffered, pouring out tears, thou didst remain an unshakable and unmoved pillar!
By cruel men the all-cunning enemy sought to tear thee away, O Theodosius, from the nest adorned and desired by thee; but thou didst account this as nothing, driving away his regiments by thy prayers, and singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!
Having sprinkled thyself with spiritual rain, and being watered by the living spring through the tongue of the instructor Anthony, thou didst sprout forth a divine ear of corn, and dost feed multitudes, having truly become Joseph's inexhaustible granary!
*Theotokion:*
Who can worthily tell the sum of thy wonders, O pure one? For through thee the whole world, offering praise unto thee, is shown mercy, and delivered from cruel misfortunes and various evils, enemies and assaults!

*Another Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord![^315]
*Troparia:*
*(Right page — Folio 81 recto, running header: TO THEODOSIUS, HEGUMEN OF THE CAVES:)*
Thy mother having learned, O wise one, that thou wast living with the blessed Anthony in the cave, went thither; yet coming and weeping, she was comforted, having been persuaded by worldly words to flee.[^316]
Thou wast truly a guileless priest of God, O father, offering bloodless sacrifices, O sacred one, and singing with a pure heart unto the Lord, keeping festival evermore!
Ask for us deliverance from sins, O servant and defender of God, we thy flock cry unto thee; and preserve from the snares of the enemy those who hymn thee!
*Theotokion:*
The Word of God, Who shone forth from the Father, sanctified thy most pure womb, and for His great goodness delivered the human race from the ancient curse!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray![^317]
*Troparia:*
The accomplishment of thy virtuous struggles hath shone forth, O father: for the dwelling which of old was mean, thou didst make spacious, having brought many worshipers unto God, Who gives every sufficiency unto them through thy prayers!
By the radiant aspects of thy struggles the multitude of demons was driven away from thy dwelling: for the grace of the Spirit of the Creator of all went with thee, and showed thee forth glorious, O Theodosius!
Thou didst hymn the merciful Savior in the midst of the church; therefore He sent thee His rich mercies, dissolving the sorrow of need, and gushing forth ever-flowing gifts unto thy flock!
*Theotokion:*
Thou wast higher than the heavenly powers, O woman full of the grace of God, having conceived the Word Who made all things by a word, and having brought forth Him Who was ineffably begotten of the Father before the ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate with love the souls of those who rise early unto Thee, I pray, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin![^318]
*Troparia:*
Thou wast a pure vessel adorned with virtuous flowers, O Father Theodosius, from which thou dost pour forth a fountain of instruction unto thy flock, sweeter than honey and the honeycomb!
Thou didst mortify carnal nature by virtuous struggles, O wise Father Theodosius; therefore Christ showed thee forth as a shepherd, where He previously chose a pasture for the most pure Virgin!
Thou wast an instructor unto monastics and a shepherd unto the sheep pastured in the pasture of the Mother of God; therefore He grants thee twofold joy, O Theodosius, by the prayers of His pure Mother!
*Theotokion:*
Finding thee a consecrated house, He Who dwells in the heavens willingly made His abode in thy womb; therefore with thankful voices we ever hymn thy most pure childbearing, standing at thy house *(Left page — Folio 81 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...standing at thy house.
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^319]
*Troparia:*
By prayers and vigil, and by unceasing psalmody, thou didst make thy soul a divine paradise, having clearly sprouted the honorable life-bearing tree, the Savior and Lord!
Thou didst fulfill hidden humility, imitating the Master's poverty, O blessed one: for by thine own will, on a journey, at the command of a lazy servant, thou didst take upon thyself to drive the cart unto the divine place; where being bowed down to by those that met thee, thou wast recognized![^320]
Imitating Him Who washed the feet of His disciples, O God-wise one, thou didst choose to draw water and bear it upon thy shoulder, and bringing firewood with thine own hands, thou didst show thyself as a beginner of labors unto monastics![^321]
*Theotokion:*
The manifoldly wily enemy was slain through thee, O Mother of God, all creation having previously been buried in corruption; and we the dead have ascended unto life through thee: for truly thou didst bring forth Life, the Lord of all!

*Another Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^322]
*Troparia:*
Having truly erected a church of the Mother of God, in it thou didst gather a multitude of monastics, a choir who lovingly hymn thine honorable memory!
Unto those who flee unto thy shelter with faith, thou dost pour forth a fountain of healings unto all, and pourest out gifts, O venerable one, and dost enlighten souls, driving away demonic deceit!
Lift up thy hands unto Christ God, O venerable one, and thy flock which thou didst assemble, O father, preserve by thy prayers, radiantly hymning thy memory!
*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O all-hymned one, who didst bring forth the all-hymned God of all, and make those who hymn thee partakers of light, O pure one, and deliver them from eternal fire!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*[^323]
Today let us honor the Russian star that shone forth from the east and came unto the west; which enriched all this country with wonders and goodness, and all of us with the work and grace of the monastic rule, the blessed Theodosius!
*Ikos:*
Who can utter thy struggles, O father? Or who shall recount the multitude of thy labors, and daily wonders? For while yet in the flesh, thou didst see a bodiless one in bodily form conversing with thee, and bringing thee a gift, gold sent by God;[^324]

*(Right page — Folio 82 recto, running header: TO THEODOSIUS, HEGUMEN OF THE CAVES:)*
above man didst thou appear in humility and gentle character, and filled with spiritual wisdom; therefore having received the Spirit in thyself, thou wast seen in light shining like the sun; and me who hymn thee enlighten with thy radiance, O venerable one, divine Theodosius of the monastic rule!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The children that came down from Judea in Babylon of old trampled down the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^325]
*Troparia:*
Every one who has recourse unto thy prayers with faith, O blessed one, shall never be put to shame: for he receives the grace of his petition, O father, and returning leaps for joy, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Thou wast shown forth as a father and defender of orphans, and a hope of widows, and unto all the sick thou wast a restoration, O father; therefore we sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Thou wast the adornment of monastics and a path of salvation unto men: for having enriched all with thy words of divine mysteries, thou didst teach them to say: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Theotokion:*
Incarnate from the virginal womb, O Christ, Thou didst appear for our salvation; therefore knowing thy Mother to be the Mother of God, we thankfully sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos:*
An angel made the furnace dew-dropping for the venerable youths, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^326]
*Troparia:*
The grace of the Divine Spirit overshadowing thee grew in thee, enlightening thy heart, O father, wherewith thou didst teach to sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
The declarations of the providence of thy words, the mysteries went forth into all countries, unto those who ever sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
The manifold grace of the Spirit rested upon thee, O father, and showed forth most glorious wonders unto those who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Theotokion:*
Fleeing under thy protection, O Sovereign Lady, deliverance from cruel things, having hope, we all the faithful pray: Despise not thy servants, O Virgin Mother of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^327]
*Troparia:*
As a divine prophet penetrating by the Spirit, O all-blessed Theodosius, unto him who brought from heaven the hidden Gospel,[^328] who conquered darkness to fulfill that which was promised to the most holy Mother of God: and he, having wondered, confessed with a pure mind, glorifying God!
Running unto thy dwelling like a hart unto the water-springs, to obtain ever-flowing incorruptible food, the pious prince holding this land, wishing indeed *(Left page — Folio 82 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...indeed to sup with him, O blessed one; but when the steward reported a shortage, at thy command thy whole vessel was filled with honey![^329]
The angelic powers praised thy struggles, O Theodosius, and the choir of the venerable praised thy life, the radiant and adorned rejoicing of monastics; therefore now rejoicing with him and keeping festival together, thou singest with one accord: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!
*Theotokion:*
Thee, O most pure Mother of God, I set before me as the guardian of my life and an unconquerable defender: be thou a helper unto me, delivering thine unworthy servant from misfortunes and sorrows, who lovingly sings: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt the pure one unto the ages!

*Second Canon, Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Thou didst not cause the flame to drop dew for the venerable ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages![^330]
*Troparia:*
The choir of thy disciples with the instructor hymns Christ, standing around thy reliquary, having received thy labors worthy of God, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!
Rejoicing, thou didst follow Christ God Who was crucified: for having mortified the body with many labors and afflictions, thou didst acquire incorruptible riches and love, having cast away the world and loved Christ alone!
The reliquary of thy relics pours forth health by divine power, O Father Theodosius, and heals passions of soul and flesh; therefore we worthily hymn thy memory!
*Theotokion:*
We now cry out unto thee the voice of Gabriel: Rejoice, all-blessed key, full of the grace of God, through whom we were delivered from the ancient curse: for thou art the intercessor of the salvation of all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, being made man from the virginal womb; therefore, ye faithful, we magnify the most pure Mother of God![^331]
*Troparia:*
Thy memory hath shone forth unto us this day like the sun, O venerable one: kissing thy reliquary, and standing as it were around the divine ark, we faithfully sing thy falling asleep with the higher ranks, O Theodosius!
Rejoice, O Russian land, having received an unstolen treasure from the Lord, a great helper, the divinely-blessed and fervent defender Theodosius; and rejoice thou also, first of Russian cities, with the ascetic choir![^332]
A festival hath assembled this day at thy memory to hymn the Lord, Who gave thee good struggles which thou didst endure; therefore pray unto Him, O holy one, that thy flock which thou didst acquire by thy prayers, O wise one, may be delivered from the snares of the enemy!

*(Right page — Folio 83 recto, running header: TO THEODOSIUS, HEGUMEN OF THE CAVES:)*
Save me, O only Deliverer, Who didst receive the fervent repentance of the harlot and the prodigal, and this sighing of the publican; and be merciful, O Christ, unto me the prodigal, through the prayers of Thy favorite, driving away the multitude of my sins!
*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Savior, Who wast born and didst preserve her that bore Thee incorrupt after childbearing, when Thou shalt sit to judge my deeds, overlooking my sins and iniquities, being without sin and of great mercy, as the Lover of mankind!

*Another Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word was made manifest unto men incarnate: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed![^333]
*Troparia:*
O father, having gathered thy children in faith, account them worthy to be enlightened with virtues, a shepherd worthy of shepherds: pray, O venerable one, that forgiveness of sins and amendment of life may be granted unto us!
Having lived equally with the holy fathers, thou wast a servant of the most pure Virgin, O blessed one, and an excellent shepherd of monastics, truly a pillar of Orthodoxy, the hope and expectation of the faithful, who sing in the house of the Mother of God!
Being strengthened by the power of the Almighty, thou didst conquer the wiles of the enemy, and didst drive away their regiments, and didst grant health unto the sick; therefore rejoicing, we ever honor thy memory!
*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably conceived Him Who shone forth from the Father without a mother, thou didst bring Him forth, O Mother of God, all-hymned and pure Virgin: Him, as thy Son and our God, pray, O most holy one, for thy servants!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оуслы́шите):*[^334]
Having received the light of the Consubstantial Trinity in thy heart, O Theodosius, father of fathers, thou didst shine forth with piety, being strengthened in the cave with the great Anthony, kinsman of angels: with them remember those who hymn thee with faith! *(Twice)*
Glory... Both now... Of the feast.

*At the Praises, Stichera: on 4, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Єгда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^335]
Being all-wounded with love for Christ, O blessed one, thou didst follow Him, having cast away the world, thy homeland and kin, and didst flee unto Anthony the venerable; from whom thou didst learn ineffable things, which eye hath not seen, and which entered into thy heart; with whom we sacredly hymn thee, honoring Theodosius! *(Twice)*
Having desired a spotless life, patience, love, and humility, all-night standing and abstinence, wherewith thou didst mortify carnal lusts; therefore receiving divine attainment, thou rejoicest with the choirs of the venerable: forget not, O blessed one, to ask forgiveness of sins for those who worthily hymn thee!

*(Left page — Folio 83 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
When the malicious enemy brought robbers against thy flock, O venerable one, seeking to destroy those who were therein, then seeing the church hanging aloft in the air, they departed, fearing the grace given unto thee from God, O venerable one: for they heard a voice in the church, of ineffable angelic singing; with them now rejoicing, remember us who faithfully hymn thy light-bearing and honorable memory![^336]

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O venerable father, into all the earth went forth the sound of thy right deeds; therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors: thou didst destroy the demonic regiments, and reached the angelic ranks, whose life thou didst faultlessly emulate; having boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for our souls!

*Both now... Of the feast.*
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy:*
*The Beatitudes of the saint: Ode 3 and Ode 6 from both canons.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^337]
*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericope 334.*[^338]
*Alleluia, Tone 2: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.*[^339]
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 33.*[^340]
*Communion Verse: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be.*[^341]

*(Vignette occupying lower half of page: magnificent large decorative baroque tailpiece)*

*(Right page — Folio 84 recto, full-page woodcut engraving with decorative border, depicting Saint Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal: Saint Simon in episcopal vestments, mitre, and omophorion, blessing with his right hand, holding a pastoral crosier in his left hand, and holding a model of a church in his right arm; above Him in a halo of clouds is the Holy Spirit as a dove; inscribed beside Him "С: Симонъ Епкпъ. Владимерскій и Суждалскій"; in the background on the left and right, stone cathedrals, city walls, and monastic buildings)*

*Month of May, on the 10th Day:*
*Service to our Father among the Saints Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal:*[^342]
*At Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man", the first antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera: Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, life-bearing Cross" (Ра́дꙋйся живоно́сный крⷭтѐ):*[^343]
Rejoice, adornment of the Church, beauty of hierarchs, most sacred father, good shepherd, who having loved Jesus the Chief Shepherd from thy youth, with all thy soul didst uprightly work for Him: us who are enslaved *(Left page — Folio 84 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
...us who are enslaved by passions, who fall down before thee from our soul, lead up unto our first freedom by thy prayers!
Rejoice, most fervent lover of Christ thy Lord, O Simon worthy of wonder, who being ignited with the warmth of spiritual grace into divine love, didst disregard all the beautiful things of this world, and didst captivate thy mind into obedience unto Christ; therefore now, living as a free man in free places, liberate our minds that are captivated by the assaults of the enemy, O blessed father, and by thine intercession with God deliver us from misfortunes, we pray thee, O father, who honor thy memory!
O Simon, most wise hierarch, guiding and instructing by thy wise teaching those who are held by the billows of this life and go astray on the path of passions, thou didst lead them unto the path of salvation, having been a guide unto the heavenly height; where also thyself now dwelling with the angels, guide by thy invisible governance unto life and salvation for our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Thy swift tongue for teaching even now raises up sluggish hearts, and calls them to work the commandments of the Lord; and thy shepherd's flute proclaims divinely-uttered words, wherewith, as by a ladder, those who hear are lifted up, and made God's own; therefore, O Simon, venerable father, as having boldness toward Christ God, cease not praying that we who lovingly honor thy holy memory may be enlightened with the light of understanding and good deeds!

*Both now... Theotokion.*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings; seek them in the service to the Venerable Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslav, Folio 52.*[^344]
*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*[^345]
Having come together this day, we praise thee with hymns, most sacred Simon, as a rational mirror of theology and virtues, and as an initiate of grace; and we pray: Pray unto Christ God to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!
*Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.*[^346]
Having received illumination from the noetic Light, O blessed one, thou wast as a radiant luminary in the midst of the ascetic choir; from which, chosen from on high and entrusted with the episcopal throne, thou wast a good shepherd of the rational sheep of Christ God, before Whom now standing, pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls!

*(Right page — Folio 85 recto, running header: TO SIMON, BISHOP OF SUZDAL:)*
*Verse: The mouth of the righteous shall meditate wisdom, and his tongue shall speak of judgment.*[^347]
Having found love toward heaven by thy noetic mind through thy many labors, O blessed hierarch, thou didst ascend unto the unwaning Light, and livest rejoicing with thy first-founders;[^348] with whom also pray for us, that in eternal life we may be accounted worthy of abiding with thee and with all the saints!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
The example of thy radiant life, most sacred Simon, corrected many unto virtues, and the holy saving teaching of thine instructed souls held by despondency unto vigilance, and raised up the sluggish unto the praising of God; therefore honoring thine all-honorable memory, we fall down and pray, that by thy guidance we may direct our life unto peace, and receive great mercy from Christ God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save from misfortunes thy servants, O most holy Mother of God!
*Troparion, Tone 1:*
O Simon all-blessed, reliable chronicler of the lives of the venerable fathers of the Caves, and hierarch of Christ: diligently pray with them unto Christ God, that He may direct our life in peace, and write us in the books of the living, saving our souls![^349]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the hierarch; Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*
*After the 1st Stasis of the Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou didst appear" (Яви́лся єсѝ):*[^350]
As with a golden-woven purple robe the church of Vladimir was clothed by thy orthodox teaching, and the choirs of monastics are led afar by thy guidance, glorifying the labor of the saints in one community, which came to pass concerning thee, O Hierarch Simon!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O most pure Virgin Mother of God, despise not us who take refuge in thy goodness and seek thy swift protection, but bow down unto prayer; help us in our necessities, and deliver our souls from misfortunes!

*After the 2nd Stasis of the Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "At the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^351]
Having received the spiritual treasure from our first-founding fathers *(Left page — Folio 85 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
...having received the spiritual treasure from our first-founding fathers, O father, venerable Simon, most wise hierarch, thou didst multiply it a hundredfold into talents, and didst leave it without lack unto those who came after thee; and thyself, as a good servant, didst enter into the joy of thy Lord: whereof account us also worthy through thy prayers, who honor thy memory with praises!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We have buried the silver talents given us from God through slothfulness, and we are ashamed to appear empty before our Lord: unto thee do we flee, O Sovereign Lady: give us at least one coin from the unfailing treasure of thy mercy, that we may not be put to shame on the day of fearful trial, and unceasingly magnify thee with praises!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Let us hymn the Word co-beginningless" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^352]
Let us, ye people, praise in hymns the most sacred Simon, the wise hierarch and unfaltering shepherd, the faithful chronicler of the lives of the saints, and the pious emulator of them: for he now stands in the highest before the Master's throne, purely beholding the unapproachable Light, and unceasingly prays for those who lovingly hymn his memory and glorify Christ God!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, Mother of the Word Who is co-beginningless with the Father, swift helper of sinners and defender of those in misfortunes: stretch forth thy most pure hands, wherewith thou didst bear the Ageless One as an Infant in time, and entreat His goodness on behalf of us the much-sinning, that we may be delivered from pressing necessities and sorrows, so that we may purely magnify thy blameless childbearing!

*Gradual hymn, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon:*
My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding. Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.[^353]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to John, pericope 36.*[^354]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron: Tone 6:*
The labors of thy deeds, O venerable father, were an example unto monastics: for who seeing thy measureless humility would not be corrected in his own life? Or who seeing thy unmurmuring obedience would not marvel at thy patient soul? Thou didst teach all by thyself; but even after thy departure thou dost not cease teaching, admonishing and instructing with quiet guidance; therefore, honoring thy memory, we call thee blessed as a shepherd and teacher, and kinsman of the venerable!

*(Right page — Folio 86 recto, running header: TO SIMON, BISHOP OF SUZDAL:)*
*Canon of the Mother of God, with the heirmos to 6; and of the Hierarch to 6:*[^355]
*Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking the sea in the form of a cross and dividing it, once drowned the chariot-driving Pharaoh, but saved the fleeing people of Israel as they marched on foot, who sang a song unto God![^356]
*Troparia:*
Direct me, I pray, O Christ, as I hasten to hymn with praises the virtues of Thy hierarch, as one traversing a sea in its course; and having cleansed my mind from the stormy thoughts of this life, save me as I sing hymns unto Thee my God!
Being rude and senseless, yet drawn with love toward thee, I dare to praise thy memory, O Simon, hierarch of Christ; do thou therefore assist me by thy prayers, and guide me now to sing a song unto God my Master concerning thee!
Who reading thy life, O venerable one, will not marvel, seeing thy zeal to emulate the first fathers of the Caves? Therefore I also, marveling, humbly send up a song unto my God concerning thee!
*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having received the angelic habit in the beautiful Lavra of the Caves, thou didst also receive the angelic life, following Anthony the great and Theodosius the marvelous: with whom now living thou rejoicest, unceasingly singing a song unto God!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
I have changed my habit, but I have not changed my deeds, O Mother of God, who through the grace of Him Who was born of thee didst save the human race; by thy grace save me, as I send up a song unto God concerning thee my Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Maker of the vault of heaven and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, establishment of the faithful, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind![^357]
*Troparia:*
Thy virtues have shone forth like stars of heaven, O father, with whose rays illuminate also us who walk in the darkness of passions, that concerning thee we may sing unto the Creator of the vault of heaven: Glory to Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!
Desiring to be a partaker of the unwaning Light, thou didst keep far from corrupt worldly things, and having received the angelic habit, thou didst angelically offer praise unto God, crying: Glory to Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!
Emulating the great fathers who shone forth, Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves *(Left page — Folio 86 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
...emulating them, as a bright star thou didst shine forth in this life first in humility and purity; marveling whereat, we sing: Glory to Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!
*Glory...*
Thy obedience, O blessed one, was like a mirror in the midst of the congregation of the brethren; and thereby most of all thou didst draw near unto Jesus Who was obedient even unto death, with Whom thou now livest in the midst of the heavenly citizens, and singest: Glory to Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
I hear the commandments of my Lord, yet I disobey them; but thou, O pure Virgin, who didst say: "Behold the handmaid of the Lord," make me an obedient servant, that I may glorify thee, the Mother of the One Lover of mankind.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Having found the ladder of virtues, thou didst ascend unto the mountain of dispassion; and having seen the deceit of this world, thou didst renounce the voluptuous kingdom that passes away in the twinkling of an eye: not unto world-lovers, but having fled unto the One God, thou didst appear; therefore we honor thee with hymns, O Simon, all-blessed father!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having thee as the hope of salvation and a helper in necessities, O Virgin Bride of God, we shall not fear the assaults of enemies, but in thee we take courage; for thou art our strength and establishment.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the Father's bosom and didst visit our poverty; therefore with the prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^358]
*Troparia:*
Thou, O venerable one, through knowledge and deed didst draw near unto the noetic Light, and wast shown forth radiant in life; guided whereunto, many glorified God, saying: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!
The fame of thy deeds, O father, went forth into diverse countries, and many desired to have thee as a leader and instructor unto themselves, that being guided by thee, they might glorify the Heavenly Father, Who glorified thee with such grace.
More than others the church of Vladimir hastened to call thee, O blessed one,[^359] that by thy coming thou mightest make glad those who were troubled, and shepherd them with the staff of thy governance.
*Glory...*
The citizens of Suzdal also come out to meet thee, composing choirs of spiritual songs, and rejoicing they lead thee up unto the episcopal throne; having ascended which, thou wast a good shepherd unto them.

*(Right page — Folio 87 recto, running header: TO SIMON, BISHOP OF SUZDAL:)*
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Being the Mother of the Good Shepherd, gather thy rational sheep scattered by the noetic wolf, and do thou thyself, O Sovereign Lady, shepherd us, that no longer may the wolf of hades dare to draw near unto thy flock!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray![^360]
*Troparia:*
Unto thee, O Simon, as unto that beloved disciple of His, Simon, the Chief of pastors, Jesus, entrusted the shepherding of the sheep;[^361] which having received, thou didst well guide unto the heavenly height: whereunto also direct my ways by thy prayers!
From youth thou didst love the Master of all, and from Him as a loving recompense thou didst receive to shepherd the sheep redeemed by His precious blood; of the same Master a sheep am I also: look upon me, holy Simon, and direct my ways, I pray thee!
The pastoral reed of thy teachings, O father, gladdened the rational sheep, and no longer did they prefer corruptible food, feeding on spiritual grass: nourish me who hunger with thy teaching, and direct my ways by thy prayers!
*Glory...*
With the staff of governance thou didst well manage the flock entrusted unto thee, giving an example of thyself unto them; which written in part I also read, but cannot emulate: O good shepherd, that I may be an imitator thereof, direct my ways by thy prayers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Maddened is the nature of the earthborn by my transgressive life: O Virgin Mary, weaving a net for our eyes wandering over earthly things, lead us up unto the higher things and unto the seeking of them, we pray thee, direct our ways!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation![^362]
*Troparia:*
Sanctified by the grace of the Holy Spirit, as an initiate entrusted with the throne of God upon earth, O Simon, thou didst minister in holiness and righteousness; unto thee who now dwellest with faithful servants in the joy of thy Lord, I pray: be thou an intercessor for me unto God concerning my salvation!
Thou wast accounted worthy of a twofold calling, O hierarch, as one who took twofold labors upon thyself; for attending unto pastoral deeds *(Left page — Folio 87 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
...pastoral deeds, thou didst never abandon monastic struggles; therefore living now in twofold honor, pray for me, that I may not be deprived of my salvation!
Ever remembering, O venerable one, the labors of the fathers of the Caves, thou didst learn to emulate them; shown forth as co-disciplined and of one mind with them, thou didst gird thyself from worse unto better, that thou mightest be adorned with a twofold crown.
*Glory...*
By the practice of the Lord's commandments thou didst mortify thy flesh, but by the grace of God thou didst give life unto thy soul. Having found the ladder through spiritual knowledge, thou didst ascend by noetic elevation unto God, before Whom standing, pray for those who hymn thee, O all-blessed Simon!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
By slothfulness have I lost action, and by darkening of passions have I lost knowledge; and possessing neither one, I fear twofold condemnation: yet unto thee, O Birthgiver of God, do I flee; as one who art mighty and all-powerful, correct both, and be the helper of my salvation!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^363]
Though thou wast worthily clothed in the vesture of hierarchs, yet not exalting thyself thereby, thou didst desire rather to be cast down as sweepings before the gates of the Lavra of the Caves, O humble-minded Simon;[^364] therefore not as sweepings outside the gates, but as a treasure within the holy cave wast thou accounted worthy to be laid; and now standing before the Lord in heaven, pray for us who honor Thee!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire was put to shame of old in Babylon by the condescension of God; therefore the children, dancing with joyful foot in the furnace as in a meadow, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^365]
*Troparia:*
The grace of God worked in thee, O hierarch; for there went forth continually from thy mouth streams of holy teachings, watering the hearts of the slothful, who having sprouted fruits of virtues by thy sprinkling, cried: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Thou didst sow the seed of the word of God in the orchard of thy flock, planting the lily of purity, prayer like a stem of Lebanon, mercy toward neighbors like an olive tree, and thoughts ever concerning God like a cypress; for what things thou thyself didst do, those also thou didst command others to do.
Having well written upon the tablets of thy heart the virtues of the fathers of the Caves, thou didst not hide them under the bushel of worldly clamor, O father; but moved by zeal, thou didst turn thine entire mind to write their lives for the benefit of others;[^366] reading which, we also cry:

*(Right page — Folio 88 recto, running header: TO SIMON, BISHOP OF SUZDAL:)*
...we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Glory...*
Like a wise bee having gathered the noetic flowers, the lives of the venerable, thou didst give a sweet honeycomb unto thy children that live after thee, wherewith sweetening our souls, with thee, O Simon, we sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
My heart is faint from passions, and my lips are filled with bitterness: do thou, O Sovereign Lady, who didst bring forth sweetest Jesus, sweeten my soul with thy grace, that I may sing: Rejoice, dwelling of holy sweetness, and blessing of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^367]
*Troparia:*
By the sevenfold gifts of the Spirit thou didst crush the head of the seven-headed serpent, O father, and wounded, he trembles unto this day at thy name; which remembering on this present day, concerning thee we cry: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages!
Through love toward the most pure Mother of God and the fathers of the Caves, thou didst seem to be absent in body only, but in spirit thou didst abide with them, and didst emulate their life; therefore in thy love visit us, and pray that we may supremely exalt Christ unto the ages!
Truly, as Simon was the most fervent disciple of Christ Himself, so also thou, O Hierarch Simon, wast shown forth as a zealous champion toward the Mother of God and her monastery, from which thou now livest unto the ages!
*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
Thou didst not compare thy glory and honor with the refuse of the Caves monastery; thou didst desire, O father, that it were better for thee to be cast out as sweepings in the house of the Mother of God for one day, than to be glorious upon a throne for a thousand years: O marvelous humility of thine! Therefore we call thee blessed unto the ages![^368]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy assistance, O pure one, I came unto the co-dwelling of the fathers of the Caves; but through negligence I have fallen away from the vision, and the enemy drags me back into the world unto the former things: O Mother of God, persuade me to abide here and give heed unto my salvation, that I may not fall away and perish unto the ages!

*(Left page — Folio 88 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE HIERARCH:)*
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was terrified at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, the Mother of God.[^369]
*Troparia:*
We marvel at thy holy life, O all-blessed Simon; we glorify thy struggles, and honor also thy memory, which now with the angels we magnify.
Having left the lower things, thou didst depart unto the higher, and thy body according to thy desire was brought from thy throne into the cave,[^370] showing how great love thou hadst toward this place, wherein we all magnify thee.
The cave rejoices, containing thy relics, O hierarch, and calls together the assemblies of monastics and feast-lovers, that all having gathered on this present day may magnify thy memory with praises and hymns.
*Glory...*
Thou hast departed in bodily senses, but thy memory abides with us inexhaustibly; for behold, we thy children, burning with love toward thee, ever remember thee and magnify thee.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Abiding with us unfailingly, O most holy Virgin, strengthen the people gathered in memory of thy faithful servant, drive away sloth, and enlighten our understanding, that we may all vigilantly magnify thee and thy Son!

*Exapostilarion:*
Let us praise the divinely-wise father who wrote most wise words; for his venerable writings are like morning dew, a sweet honeycomb, living bread, heavenly nectar, a fountain of living water, from which one is filled again with spiritual sweetness, and glorifies thy memory, O most blessed Simon!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Virgin, who didst bring forth the inscrutable Fountain of living waters higher than the heavens, give drink unto me who thirst for thy mercy, that being refreshed, I may glorify thee!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^371]
Thou wast a sacred initiate, O all-blessed one, while living in the flesh in this world; and having departed unto the better life, thou wast accounted worthy of the very vision of God, and beholdest Him as unapproachable Light: of which make us also worthy to behold, through thy fervent prayers unto God!
Those who give heed unto thy words, and attend unto thy instruction, O venerable Simon, learn profit in life, and are filled with correction, receive spiritual actions, and draw grace through thy teaching; wherewith enrich also us who honor thy memory with praises on this present day!

*(Right page — Folio 89 recto, running header: TO SIMON, BISHOP OF SUZDAL:)*
Today, ye people, let us honor with hymns the divine instrument of the Holy Spirit, Simon the sacred proclaimer, the first-enthroned hierarch of Suzdal, who rightly proclaimed spiritual corrections, bowing down before the reliquary of his relics; for as a hierarch and fervent intercessor he stands before the Master's throne, praying that we may be saved!
*Glory... Tone 6:*
The assemblies of angels and men marveled at the radiance of thy life and at thy hierarchic correction; for from on high thou wast shown forth as a sacred initiate in election, and wast an excellent mirror unto fasters; therefore as a worthy servant of God, O Simon, most blessed hierarch, we lovingly magnify thee in hymns!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the true vine...
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Common Service for a Hierarch.*
*(Vignette: Large baroque woodcut tailpiece with angel, foliate scrollwork, and urn) (Left page — Folio 89 verso, full-page woodcut engraving with decorative baroque border, in two registers: upper register depicts Saint Agapitus the Unmercenary Physician, tonsured with schema and monastic habit, blessing with his right hand and holding a medicine vessel in his left hand, standing before a monastic cell and church; inscribed "Ven. Agapitus the Unmercenary"; lower register depicts the baptism of the Armenian physician in a font surrounded by priests and monastics, inscribed "After the death of Agapitus, the Armenian was baptized according to his prophecy")*
