# MONTH OF JUNE

*On the 1st Day:*
*Service to the Venerable Agapitus of the Caves, Unmercenary Physician:*[^372]
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried", Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́бля):*[^373]
Having abundantly received from on high the gift of spiritual grace, O venerable Agapitus, thou dost water all as with river streams, and by thy prayers thou grantest healing unto all that flee unto thee with faith;

*(Right page — Folio 90 recto, running header: TO THE UNMERCENARY PHYSICIAN OF THE CAVES:)*
therefore we also, taking courage, bow down before the reliquary of thy relics, and pray: grant thou healing unto our souls and bodies!
Subduing the irrational dart by purity of mind and by unceasing standings in prayer unto the spirit, O all-blessed one, thou didst receive the grace of healing, and dost lavishly grant help unto all that ask of thee; therefore we also, honoring thy falling asleep, pray thee fervently: as an unmercenary physician, grant healing unto our souls and bodies!
The reliquary of thy relics, O father, venerable Agapitus, is shown forth as a radiant heaven, displaying wonders like light-bearing stars, and granting grace-filled healing unto those that flee unto it with hope; therefore having hope, we also pray thee fervently: grant assistance in passions unto our souls and bodies!
*Glory... Tone 2:*
Having received the grace of healing from on high while yet living in this brief life, O all-blessed Agapitus, thou didst grant healing unstintingly unto all that asked; having now been accounted worthy of greater grace, standing before the throne of the Most High, send down fiery gifts upon us who bow down before thy many-wonderworking relics, and who honor thy memory with praises, that through thy prayers unto God, O father, we may receive healing for our infirmities!
*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, from the Oktoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 3:*
Gushing forth streams of healings from spiritual gifts, O glorious one, unto those held by many passions who require thy protection, grant swift help from on high, refreshing with streams of thy wonders and driving away the diseases brought on by the evil one, that drawing healings, we may all glorify the Heavenly Father, and magnify His Co-beginningless Son with His Co-eternal Spirit, and call thee all-blessed, O Father Agapitus!
*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Troparion, Tone 5:*
Emulating the humble-mindedness of the God-bearing Anthony, as with medicine Thou didst heal the sick with edible herbs, O venerable Agapitus;[^374] thereby having also convinced the unbelieving physician, Thou didst guide him unto the path of salvation: heal also our infirmities, and pray unto the Lord on behalf of those who hymn thee![^375]

*(Left page — Folio 90 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE AGAPITUS:)*
*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, and seeing the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, Israel cried: Let us sing a song of victory unto God![^376]
*Troparia:*
As Thou didst valiantly enable the mute to speak uprightly by a touch, so also, O my Christ, loose Thou my tongue and open my mind, that hymning with love, I may offer praise unto the memory of Thy favorite!
I cannot hymn thy grace, O venerable one, unless thou by thy prayers grant me help, that hymning in praise, I may glorify the Good One Who gave thee the grace of healings!
By foreknowledge wast thou called, O father, to walk in the footsteps of thy Master; and following Him, having left all the beautiful things of the world, in love thou didst come unto great Anthony, and didst live with him, singing unceasing praise unto God![^377]
*Glory...*
Following the earthly angel and heavenly man, thou thyself didst acquire the angelic life, O venerable father; therefore we honor thy righteous deeds, and singing a hymn unto Him concerning thee, we glorify the Master of all!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Virgin ever praised by earthly and heavenly hosts, preserve from all assaults those who dare to offer praise unto thee on this present day, that we may ever hymn thee, O woman full of the grace of God!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession![^378]
*Troparia:*
Through many years thou didst pass through manifold labors, O Agapitus, struggling in vigil and fasting, until thou wast shown forth of one mind with thine instructor, and wast established by him upon the rock of a steadfast life.
Possessing nothing else than poverty, following Christ Who impoverished Himself for our sake, O father, and keeping His commandments, thou didst serve the sick, visiting and giving them rest, and granting them health by thy prayer.[^379]
Having lived with the venerable, thou didst acquire holiness, O praiseworthy Agapitus, and wast accounted worthy of gifts of healing, so that by prayer and edible herbs thou didst grant health unto the sick; therefore thou wast also called an unmercenary physician!
*Glory...*

*(Right page — Folio 91 recto, running header: TO THE UNMERCENARY PHYSICIAN OF THE CAVES:)*
Thou didst glorify God in thy members, O venerable one; therefore God also glorified thee with the grace of healing, wherewith heal also our infirmities of soul and body, that we may be established upon the rock of the confession of God!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O most certain living Tree: by the fruit thereof make us who are dead through sin to live again, and grant unto us, O pure Mother, a steadfast confession of thy goodness!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Freely having received the gift of healing from God, thou grantest healing freely unto those that suffer, who flee unto thee with faith; therefore we also, bowing down before the reliquary of thy relics, O venerable father, pray to receive from thee healing for our infirmities!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Fleeing unto thy loving-kindness in sorrows, we pray thee, O all-immaculate one: for the sake of thy venerable one, grant swift healing unto us who are held by infirmities!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord![^380]
*Troparia:*
Fleeing this world, Christ chose thee to put the wise to shame; which also came to pass in thee, O all-blessed father: for by grace and not by cunning didst thou put the most wise physician to shame; therefore keeping festival in the Lord, we glorify thee![^381]
He was deceived who formerly deceived the sick, predicting unto them the day and hour of death; whom reproving, O venerable one, thou didst heal the sick man by prayer and edible herbs, saying: "Give glory unto God, Who holds the key of life and death!"[^382]
Wounded by the arrow of envy, the wicked Armenian reviled thee, O holy one; and having sent a condemned man, he commanded him to be slain before thee by deadly herbs; but thou, having healed him by prayer and herbs, didst put to shame the heretic who wished to put thee to shame![^383]
*Glory...*
The grace given unto thee became a stumbling block for the falling, O all-blessed one: for the wicked Armenian, arming himself against thee most of all, plotted to give thee poison; but though thou didst drink a deadly thing, thou wast unharmed, for the grace of God preserved thee for greater wonderworking![^384]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O vessel that didst bear the myrrh that healed the human race! Give life unto me who am slain by the poison of sin, that having lived sound for the rest of my time, I may praise thee, O Virgin!

*(Left page — Folio 91 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE AGAPITUS:)*
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate with love the souls of those who rise early unto Thee, I pray, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin![^385]
*Troparia:*
Having received the grace of healing from God, Thou didst heal the prince,[^386] who though he was treated by the Armenian physician, obtained no relief; but having tasted of thine herb, he was delivered from his infirmity!
The monastics beholding it were stirred unto the virtuous endurance of the God-bearing father, seeing that with the renunciation of the world he renounced his own will, and never went outside the monastery gates; seeing which, emulate him!
Having one love toward all, and not regarding persons, whether prince or commoner came unto thee, O venerable one, thou didst grant healing freely unto all that came, seeking reward from God alone.
*Glory...*
The prince having received healing through thee, O all-blessed one, understood that thou art a true servant of God, and desired to see thee; therefore he came unto thee with gifts; but thou, having God alone, didst not desire to receive anything from those gifts![^387]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
We praise thee, O most pure Virgin, for thou openest unto us the doors of thy mercy, and grantest unto all an unstinting fountain of healing, delivering from the poison of sin!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^388]
*Troparia:*
Having forsaken all corruptible things of this world, thou didst cast out the gold brought unto thee before the doors of thy cell, O blessed one, fleeing the snares of the money-loving enemy, and didst crush his soul-destroying head![^389]
Having acquired the one pearl of great price, Jesus, thou didst account all things as refuse, O father, and thereby didst purchase for thyself a dwelling in the heavens, which we also pray to obtain by thy prayers, O father![^390]
Fasting through many years, Thou didst grow weak in body, O venerable one, yet in spirit Thou didst remain steadfast like a pillar, enduring all things brought upon thee, and giving thanks unto God, unto Whom Thou didst cry: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!
*Glory...*
Sick in body, in spirit thou didst contend with the wicked Armenian, who foretold death for thee after three days; whom thou, O blessed one, didst overcome, having announced thy departure unto God after three months![^391]

*(Right page — Folio 92 recto, running header: TO THE UNMERCENARY PHYSICIAN OF THE CAVES:)*
*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Word Who dwelt in thy womb, O all-immaculate Virgin, settled the human race in the heavenly courts: entreat Him to translate me from the corruption of this life, whither the saints feed upon the stream of delight!

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*[^392]
Being an excellent physician, O marvelous one, displaying thy unquestioned faith, and reproving the Armenian's unbelief, and bringing him unto piety: when thou thyself wast near unto death, thou didst ask of God a lengthening of life, and having convinced him by this wonder, didst bring him unto Christ; before Whom standing in gladness, pray for us, O venerable one!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
An angel made the furnace dew-dropping for the venerable youths, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^393]
*Troparia:*
Having the gift from on high to heal infirmities, O venerable one, heal me who am sick through negligence of good deeds, as once thou didst heal him that was brought in sickness, granting him health by prayer, and didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
The Creator worked wonders through thy holy deeds: suddenly He set thee sound before the wicked one, who seeing it was amazed at thy swift change from sickness unto health: lead me who am tormented by the wounds of shameful deeds unto change of correction, through thy visitation from on high, O father, that I may sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Thy sickness and the edible herb compelled the physician to confess his impiety, whom thou didst reproach for daring to condemn the saints: behold, I also dare to approach unto thee, O father; but I pray thee, cast me not away in my necessities, that I may sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Glory...*
Having gathered the fruits of thy labors with gladness like a good husbandman, by a blessed end thou didst depart unto the higher things, to receive reward from Christ God, and didst receive unending joy from Him, O many-wonderworking Agapitus!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Fleeing unto thy haven, O Virgin Bride of God, tossed by the waves of worldly passions, do thou calm them, that we may sing unto thee: Rejoice, haven of worldly voyagers!

*(Left page — Folio 92 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE AGAPITUS:)*
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Thou didst cause dew to drop from the flame for the venerable ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages![^394]
*Troparia:*
Thou wast a most marvelous physician, O father, healing bodily diseases while living in the flesh in this life, and after thy falling asleep granting spiritual medicine, as thou didst show forth this upon the Armenian, granting healing unto his soul and instruction unto piety![^395]
Having seen thee in a marvelous vision, O holy one, the Armenian confessed clearly that thou art holy and venerable; and having turned from unbelief unto the faith, he became a monk by thy guidance: of which deprive not us who hymn the Lord unto the ages![^396]
Having a great gift, thou didst work wonders, healing the sick; and having now been accounted worthy of greater gifts, apply a plaster unto the wounds of my soul, that having obtained relief, I may supremely exalt the Lord unto the ages!
*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
O Christ God, Who grantest streams of grace of flame-like light unto the saints, quench the passions that burn me with the dew of Thy mercies, driving away the darkness of passions: the venerable one entreats Thee with us, the Lord Who art supremely exalted by all unto the ages!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst enlighten the world by thy most pure childbearing, dispel the darkness of my evildoing, pouring out upon me the rich mercy of thy compassions unto well-doing, that I may supremely exalt thee, the Mother of God, unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word was made manifest unto men incarnate: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed![^397]
*Troparia:*
The people possessing a divinely-gifted healer, let no one go unto vain physicians who harm property and health; but let him that is in need come with faith unto the reliquary of the God-bearing father, and he shall receive healing freely!
Noetic robbers have now wounded me, having truly incurable wounds: with unhesitating faith we fall down before thy many-wonderworking reliquary, O venerable one, and pray: grant thou healing unto those that need, that we may all praise and magnify thee!
Having ascended with thy soul unto the higher things, into the all-radiant mansions, thou didst leave the reliquary of thy relics in the cave, O all-blessed one:

*(Right page — Folio 93 recto, running header: TO THE UNMERCENARY PHYSICIAN OF THE CAVES:)*
bowing down wherebefore, we pray: grant healing unto our infirmities, that we may magnify thee in hymns!
*Glory...*
Despise not those who ask help of thee in infirmities, but by thy visitation grant healing of soul together with body, O all-blessed Agapitus, as an excellent physician, that we may ever magnify thee!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Despise not those who require help from thee, O Virgin, but grant it unto those who flee unto thee with faith; for thou art an unfailing fountain of healings, that with the heavenly powers we may ever magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
Having forsaken the corruptible things of the world, and been accounted worthy to receive incorruptible things, thou didst hasten to exchange the worse for the better, having been impoverished through passionate love of possessions, but enriched with the gift of healings; and grant these things unto us who honor thy memory this day, O all-blessed Agapitus!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Saved through thee, we confess thee to be the pure Mother; for thou didst bring forth Him Who destroyed death by the Cross, and ascended on high, drawing all things unto Himself, and granting great mercy unto us!

*At the Praises, Stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ єсѝ зна́менїе):*[^398]
Having withered the passionate uprisings of the flesh by ascetic labors, and ascended unto the height of virtues, thou wast shown forth brighter than the sun, shining with the grace of healings, and enlightening with rays those who come unto thee; therefore we pray thee, O venerable one, grant healing of infirmities unto those who lovingly honor thy memory!
Having received from on high a rich gift from the One Lover of mankind, thou didst distribute it unstintingly unto those in need, granting them healing; but now also send down healing in infirmities upon those who fall down before the reliquary of thy relics, as a true physician, O all-blessed one, and intercessor for our souls!
Having assembled in faith and love in memory of the worthy-blessed father, let us praise him with voices of song, that we may be accounted worthy of healing in our weaknesses from him; for he has *(Left page — Folio 93 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE AGAPITUS:)*
...for he has swift compassion, and grants healing as an unmercenary physician and swift helper in sorrows!
*Glory... Tone 2:*
Endless is Thy grace, O Christ the King, and great is the gift which Thou hast bestowed upon Thy venerable one; for not only while living in the body did he heal infirmities, but even after death by Thy divine power the reliquary of his unhidden relics grants healing unto those that come with faith. Therefore we pray Thee, O Good Master, bringing Thy saint unto propitiation, according to Thy great goodness deliver us from infirmities of soul and body, and save our souls, as the Lover of mankind!
*Both now... Theotokion.*
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*(Vignette: Magnificent large baroque woodcut tailpiece)*

*(Right page — Folio 94 recto, full-page woodcut engraving with decorative floral border, depicting Saint Anthony of the Caves: in the center, Saint Anthony standing in full monastic schema, blessing with his right hand and holding a scroll in his left hand inscribed "Lord, let the blessing of the Holy Mountain of Athos be upon this place"; surrounded by four oval vignettes depicting his asceticism in the cave, his tonsure on Athos, his departure for Kyiv, and appointing Theodosius as Hegumen; lower large register depicts the repose and burial of Saint Anthony, inscribed "Falling asleep of our Venerable Father Anthony of the Caves of Kyiv")*

# MONTH OF JULY

*On the 10th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Anthony of the Caves in Kyiv, who was the Founder of all Russian Monastics:*[^399]
*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried", we sing Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^400]
Thou didst follow the footsteps of the great fathers of the Holy Mountain, O Father Anthony; and having struggled as a bodiless one in the flesh, thou wast an example of virtues unto many; wherefrom thou wast also accounted worthy of gifts of wonders from Christ, to heal *(Left page — Folio 94 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...to heal diverse human diseases; and now pray that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*
Having been the leader of monastics, O Father Anthony, thou didst offer thyself as a firstfruit unto God, mortified through abstinence; and by the high wounds of humility and measureless fasting thou didst put the enemy to shame, O venerable one: pray that our souls may be saved!
Thy light-bearing memory, O father, venerable Anthony, is shown forth full of joy and gladness; wherefrom thy sacred children, having come together, keeping festival spiritually, we glorify the Lord Who sincerely glorified thee!
*Glory... Tone 2:*
Today the Holy Mountain of Athos, having wonderfully received thee, O father, like another immigrant Abraham,[^401] rejoices; and thy homeland, O blessed one, having thee again as its fellow-citizen, exceedingly makes merry; and the cave wherein thou didst struggle is adorned concerning thee; the most radiant city of Kyiv, keeping festival at thy memory, sings songs of celebration unto the Lord, Who granted thee once unto it inextricably; and we thy children, instructed by thy teaching, now celebrating thy most glorious falling asleep, pray: pray that our souls may be saved!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the true vine...
*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^402]
Gloriously hast thou departed from earthly unto heavenly mansions, O father, and wast accounted worthy of the honors of the venerable there: with them pray that our souls may be saved!
*Verse: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^403]
By abstinence thou didst mortify the bodily senses, O father; therefore into the dark cave as into a most radiant bridal chamber thou didst enter, whither thou didst bring a multitude of disciples unto Christ!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he shall greatly delight.*[^404]
Thy flock which honors thee, O venerable one, and celebrates thy falling asleep: deliver it from barbarian invasion, and pray that our souls may be saved!
*Glory... Both now... In the same Tone:*
A most pure church of the Mother of God thou didst erect, even as she herself willed, and in it thou didst assemble a multitude of disciples, together to hymn the Mother of God as well; whom pray to intercede, O Venerable Anthony, as thou didst promise, not to abandon thy sacred flock, delivering and saving it from the assaults of adversaries, that we may all unceasingly honor thee with hymns as a father who loves his children, asking through thee to receive great mercy from Christ!

*(Right page — Folio 95 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having gone forth from worldly turmoil, in renunciation of the world thou didst follow Christ according to the Gospel; and having lived an angel-like life, thou didst reach the calm haven of the Holy Mountain of Athos; wherefrom by the blessing of the fathers having come unto the hill of Kyiv, and there having finished thy life in love of labor, thou didst enlighten thy homeland; and having shown unto a multitude of monastics the path leading unto the heavenly kingdom, thou didst bring them unto Christ: Whom entreat, O Venerable Anthony, that He may save our souls![^405]

*At Great Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", we sing Stichera on 8: Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^406]
When divine desire came upon thee, O venerable one, then thou didst account worldly attachment as belonging to the foolish-minded; therefore thou wast an emigrant from thy homeland unto the Holy Mountain, and there in the midst of those fathers thou didst shine forth like a luminary through virtue, O Anthony: pray Christ, Whom thou didst serve from youth, to save and illuminate our souls!
When thou wast inflamed with the love of Christ, O venerable one, then thou didst hate the wisdom and glory of this world as passing away; and having made a cave for thyself, therein thou didst struggle as a bodiless one; therefore from on high thou wast accounted worthy of the honors of the bodiless: with them standing before Christ, pray that He may save and illuminate our souls!
When thou didst receive divine love in thy heart, O father, then into the dark cave as into a most radiant bridal chamber thou didst enter, and wast there enriched with understanding of ineffable things, to behold future things as present, and to speak clearly unto all: pray Christ, Whom thou didst serve from youth, O venerable one, to save and illuminate our souls!
*Other Stichera of the Venerable One, Tone 5, Podoben: "Venerable father" (Прпб́не ѻ́ч҃е):*[^407]
O venerable Father Anthony, having illuminated thy mind by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, thereby as farsighted thou didst prophetically foretell the wrath of God about to come upon Orthodoxy;[^408] wherefrom thou didst also pledge salvation from warriors unto Shimon;[^409] and before the building of the church, thou didst foretell that he would be laid therein: therefore as an intercessor of all good things we ever hymn thee, asking through thee to receive great mercy from Christ!
O venerable Father Anthony, fulfilling the commandments of the true Shepherd and Teacher Christ, displaying thy love even after thy falling asleep, by thy prayers and apparition thou didst alter the deadly disease of a brother who lived slothfully concerning the repentance of possessions;[^410] and granting him time for repentance, thou didst guide others unto chastity: therefore *(Left page — Folio 95 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...therefore pray Christ that our souls may be saved!
O venerable Father Anthony, with God in love for mankind thou didst direct the ship of thy mind, and didst cross the passionate deep of the sea of this world without turmoil; guided by the rudder of the All-Holy Spirit, thou didst reach the calm haven of purity, where is the dwelling of the saints and the rest of the venerable: with them pray, we beseech thee, that we also may obtain entrance there!
*Glory... Tone 6:*
When thy harsh life and labors for God's sake became known unto the fathers of the Holy Mountain, then by divine providence thou wast sent again unto thy homeland: "Go, my child," he said, "that there also through thee God may be made known unto those who know Him not!"[^411] Wherefrom having come, O venerable one, thou didst enlighten thy homeland, and wast a leader of monastics, having brought a multitude of them unto Christ: therefore also upon us who celebrate thy most radiant falling asleep, pray that we may be preserved unassailed by enemies, and that our souls may be saved!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Who will not call thee blessed...
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the three readings; seek them on Folio 51 on the reverse.*[^412]
*At the Litiya, Stichera idiomethe, Tone 4:*
For the sake of thy life of the same name, by divine providence thou wast named Anthony,[^413] receiving thy appellation from deeds; for having gathered a multitude of disciples who follow thy teaching, thou didst bring them unto God: but as a companion of the venerable and with the venerable standing before the Trinity, pray for all who glorify thy most glorious falling asleep in hymns!
Fleeing the passionate darkness of this world, O Venerable Anthony, thou didst make thy dwelling in the cave, O most marvelous one, and didst there penetrate unto the depth of divine understanding; therefore thou wast also accounted worthy as farsighted to discern things afar off, and to speak clearly of things near at hand; and grieving over the sins of men, thou didst prophetically foretell the barbarian invasion about to come for human sins:[^414] but pray, O father, we beseech thee, that we may be delivered from such a threat, and that our souls may be saved!
*Tone 1:*
What human mind or what tongue can utter or worthily praise thy life? For even the angels marveled at thy life in the flesh upon earth, and praised Christ, Who gave thee complete victory over passions, O venerable one: Whom entreat, O all-praised one, to save him that weaves praise for thee as far as he is able, O most marvelous Anthony!

*(Right page — Folio 96 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
Blessed is the cave having within itself thy honorable relics, O venerable one, from which healings pour forth unto the faithful; and blessed are thy disciples who acquired thee as such a teacher, and are called the children of such a father, O Venerable Anthony, intercessor for our souls!
*Tone 4:*
Now thy all-honorable and sacred body, O Venerable Anthony, lying in the cave wherein while yet in this life thou didst labor much, abundantly gushes forth healings unto those who come with faith: therefore we pray thee, through thy prayers ever grant also health of soul unto those who call thee blessed!
*Glory... Both now... Tone 8:*
Great boldness and faith toward God didst thou acquire, O Venerable Anthony; for emulating the prophet Gideon when testing victory by the fleece,[^415] even so thou, O father, didst desire to know by dew where the place was pleasing unto the Master and Queen; therefore thou didst say: "If I have found grace before Thee, let there be dew upon all the earth, but on the place where Thou willest a church to be, let there be drought!" And again: "Let there be dew on the place!" And both of these things, O father, thou didst obtain; therefore thou didst erect a most pure church of the Mother of God:[^416] with her pray Christ, we beseech thee, that our souls may be saved!
*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^417]
Thou didst follow the footsteps of great Anthony, O venerable one: for he dwelt in the desert for the sake of silence, and was accounted worthy to be a converser with angels; while thou didst shut thyself up in the underground places of the cave, and wast accounted worthy to behold the ineffable Light; and having emulated his life, thou didst receive his name and deeds: with him standing before the Holy Trinity, pray that our souls may be saved!
*Verse: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^418]
Like a most radiant luminary thou didst shine forth in a dark place, and like a palm tree thou didst blossom venerably in the house of God, and didst offer thyself as a most holy fruit unto God, a living sacrifice without blood, O father; therefore having come together in love, we all worthily call thee blessed evermore!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he shall greatly delight.*[^419]
The cave wherein thy sacred body lies, O Venerable Anthony, by the power of the All-Holy Spirit heals diverse infirmities of those who come with faith: demons are driven away from men, and the paralyzed depart having received health: while we, praising the Lord, say: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints!
*Glory... Tone 6:*
A marvelous petition and exceedingly great faith didst thou acquire, O venerable *(Left page — Folio 96 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...O Venerable Anthony, emulating the Prophet Elijah: for he brought down fire from heaven for the consuming of the sacrifice; likewise thou also by prayer didst bring down fire from heaven, for the consuming and cleansing of the place where the most glorious church of the Sovereign Lady and Birthgiver of God was to be erected.[^420] Therefore, praising thee as a companion of the venerable and an emulator of the prophets, we pray: pray Christ that our souls may be saved!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
My Maker and Redeemer...

*Having gone forth from worldly turmoil...*

*And: Birthgiver of God Virgin, rejoice... (Once).*
*At Matins:*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^421]
Thy most radiant and light-bearing feast, O God-wise Anthony, hath arrived, summoning the multitudes of monastics to glorify and hymn thy honorable falling asleep, asking from Christ through Thine intercession to receive great mercy! *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth higher than the higher hosts, having given birth unto God, O Birthgiver of God, and thou hast exalted the nature of the earthborn, O Bride of God; therefore, we the faithful glorify thee in soul and tongue as the true Mother of God!
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^422]
Thou wast a converser and fellow-minister with the fathers of the Holy Mountain, O father, and having followed their footsteps, thou didst live as a bodiless one upon the earth; therefore thou wast shown forth a fellow-dweller with the venerable: with them also dancing in the higher realms, pray that our souls may be saved, who unceasingly glorify thee! *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Joseph marveled, beholding that which was above nature, and he considered the rain upon the fleece in thy seedless conception, O Birthgiver of God: the bush unconsumed by fire, the rod of Aaron that budded; and thy betrothed and guardian bore witness, crying unto the priests: "A virgin bears a Child, and after childbirth remains still a virgin!"
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^423]
Thou didst enter into the underground caves, O venerable one, and thence, as a conqueror of passions, having ascended upon the chariot of virtues, even as another Elijah soaring unto the heavens, thou wast righteously crowned there with a most radiant crown, O blessed one! But even as when thou wast with us in this life, thou didst take care and provide what is profitable for us, so also now, O Father Anthony, pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor...

*(Right page — Folio 97 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
...who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As the all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as the unwedded mother of the Redeemer, as being the vessel of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: hasten to deliver me who through iniquity became the plaything of demons in knowledge, from their evildoing, and having made me a radiant dwelling of virtues, O light-bearing and incorruptible one: drive away the cloud of passions, and account me worthy of the higher communion and of the unwaning light, through thy prayers!
*The Gradual Hymns, the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^424] *Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^425] *Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 43 (Matthew 11:27–30).*[^426]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
*When thy harsh life... (Seek on Folio 95 on the reverse).*[^427]
*Canon of the Birthgiver of God with the heirmos on 6, of the same Tone.*
*And two canons of the venerable one on 8.*
*First Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall be seen brightly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders![^428]
*Troparia:*
By fervent abstinence and labors, O Father Anthony, and by all-night standing, thou didst set thy soul above bodily passions; therefore rejoicing with the saints, we sing unto Christ, Who granted thee such victory over passions!
Today the all-honorable day of thy falling asleep hath arrived, O Father Anthony, richly gladdening thy children, O all-honorable one; therefore remembering thine ancient manner of life and angelic conversation, brightly keeping festival, we sing of thee, O father!
Having been snatched away from dark places, thou didst pass over most gloriously unto the supreme brightnesses, O venerable one, and there with the saints, as a conqueror of passions, O father, thou wast crowned!
*Theotokion:*
Unto my soul grievously submerged in the deep of passions, by the tempest of measureless adversities: having stretched forth the hand of salvation unto me, lead me up, O Sovereign Lady, from the depth of my evils!
*Second Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the Pharaoh-led host; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin: the pre-glorified Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified![^429]
*Troparia:*
While yet in tender years, O Venerable Anthony, thou didst acquire great love toward God, and for this sake didst mortify carnal uprisings and passions unto the end; and having winged thyself most gloriously with purity as with wings, thou didst soar unto the heavens, O father!
Emulating the love of pilgrimage, O venerable one, thou thyself didst choose to be a pilgrim, O Anthony; therefore the all-seeing Eye, beholding thy good *(Left page — Folio 97 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...thy good purpose, enriched thee with the gift of wonders, as one most glorious!
Beholding in thee stillness of soul, and love toward God, and the adornment of good manners and customs, thy spiritual instructor, O venerable one, prophetically proclaimed unto all the most glorious things that would afterward come to pass through thee, O Anthony!
*Theotokion:*
Tear asunder the bonds of my transgressions, O good one, entreating thy Son and God, and cast down the sin that assaults and torments me, that I may ever sing of thee, O all-immaculate one, being saved through thee!
*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth...*
*Ode 3. First Canon, Heirmos:*
Establish Thy hymn-singers, O Birthgiver of God, the living and unstinting fountain, who have spiritually assembled themselves as a choir, and in Thy divine glory account them worthy of crowns of glory![^430]
*Troparia:*
Thy sacred and great Lavra, which received its beginning through thee, O father, proclaims it most gloriously and cries aloud louder than a trumpet; and the house of the Mother of God brightly boasts in voice and deed, having been established by thee under God, O Lord!
Thou didst love the One Lord, and taking His Cross upon thy shoulder, O Venerable Anthony, thou didst live wondrously in fasts, vigils, and prayers, having become an example unto thy disciples; therefore thou wast also glorified by Christ!
Having rejected carnal desire, O venerable one, thou didst hate pride; and having been enriched with humility and poverty, thou didst reach the supreme dwellings of the saints!
*Theotokion:*
Have mercy, O only Mother of God, remember, and have compassion to cleanse before the time of my departure my wretched soul, alienated and submerged by demons and deceitful passions!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit hath rejoiced when singing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous more than Thee, O Lord![^431]
*Troparia:*
Foreseeing future recompenses, thou didst forsake temporary things here as though they existed not, O father, and for this sake thou didst receive radiant recompenses, O venerable one; therefore with the angels thou unceasingly singest unto thy Master!
Cease not to pray, O father, for thy flock, which thou didst acquire through many sweats and labors, to deliver it from all circumstances, perils, and sorrows, that we may all hymn thee as a father who loves his children!
In gladness, O venerable one, thou standest before God with the ranks of the saints, delighting in the joy and gladness there: account us also worthy to obtain the rejoicing there, that rejoicing we may magnify thee, O Father Anthony!
*Theotokion:*
Having stretched forth the hands of thy loving-kindness, O Birthgiver of God, deliver from every tempest of burning him that flees unto thee, falls down before thee, and invokes...

*(Right page — Folio 98 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
...and invokes thy help!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^432]
Thy spiritual instructor marveled, beholding great abstinence in thee, and he considered in mind that which would afterward come to pass concerning thee, O Anthony; for he saw thy great life according to God, in no wise shaken by the assaults of the enemy. Therefore, as a father and guardian of thy life, he bore witness, crying aloud unto the fathers of the Holy Mountain: "He who seems lesser in our midst, shall afterward be a great luminary in Russia!" *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Joseph marveled, beholding that which was above nature, and he considered the rain upon the fleece in thy seedless conception, O Birthgiver of God: the bush unconsumed by fire, the rod of Aaron that budded; and thy betrothed and guardian bore witness, crying unto the priests: "A virgin bears a Child, and after childbirth remains still a virgin!"
*Ode 4. First Canon, Heirmos:*
Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Most High, the prophet Habakkuk cried: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^433]
*Troparia:*
From the place wherein thy honorable and sacred body lies, O Venerable Father Anthony, the faithful receive manifold healings, crying unto Christ from the soul: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
The length of the harsh journey terrified thee not, O venerable one, neither did the brigands' assault there; therefore, having reached the Holy Mountain, with the fathers who were there thou didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
The All-Good God, beholding thy steadfast and good purpose, made thee glorious there in the holy places in the midst of the fathers, with whom He accounted thee worthy to exult: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
*Theotokion:*
He that came forth from the loins of another, the King of all, having brought forth and made thee higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O Birthgiver of God, dwelt within thee!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
I sing of Thee, for by hearing, O Lord, have I heard and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who have gone astray: therefore do I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Plenteous in mercy![^434]
*Troparia:*
By divine providence, O blessed one, thou wast sent again unto thy homeland, that there also thou mightest guide many unto the saving path, to sing unto the Creator: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
Strengthened by the hope of future good things, O Anthony, thou didst bridle carnal impulse, and wast an unmerciful enemy unto thy flesh, O father; for this sake thou didst also acquire help from on high!
Having spent thy life in chastity, O venerable one, thou didst exchange earthly things for heavenly, and didst purchase incorruptible things with corruptible; therefore thou singest unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*(Left page — Folio 98 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
*Theotokion:*
I am stripped of all good things, and lie in wickedness: O thou who didst clothe all those stripped of old, do thou also now adorn me with former things, and deliver me from bitter things, O Bride of God!
*Ode 5. First Canon, Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst contain in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst give birth unto the timeless Son, Who grants peace unto all that hymn thee![^435]
*Troparia:*
Thou wast an exemplar and instructor of monastics, O father; therefore thou didst also command the powerful not to desire extortion, but rather to show mercy unto the weak: and for this cause thou wast glorified, O Anthony, and wast shown forth called a great teacher!
Above man, O Anthony, didst thou acquire a manner of life, having lived angelically upon earth; therefore thou wast accounted worthy to receive honor equal unto the angels, and singest with them unto thy Master!
Thy divinely-bestowed gifts of grace, O Father Anthony, amazed every human hearing, and taught men to despise all things that here seem great, as being small and wretched, and to follow thy footsteps: wherefrom pray, O venerable one, that we may be saved!
*Theotokion:*
Thou art my hope, O all-pure one, and salvation and boast; therefore I flee unto thy protection: abhor me not who am now swallowed up by many sins, sicknesses, and sorrows, but anticipating me, save me!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
Thou hast become a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Bestower of light![^436]`
*Troparia:*
Marvelous was thy manner of life, O venerable one, together with the humility which thou didst acquire, O Anthony: for neither of that very light which is in the world, nor of the solar radiance didst thou deem thyself worthy to behold, but didst shut thyself up alone in a dark place!
Thou wast a church and house of the All-Holy Spirit, O Father Anthony, having been adorned with diverse virtues: through humility thou didst inherit the higher summit, and through poverty didst receive heavenly wealth, O venerable one!
Deliver those who perform thy sacred memory from every manner of transgressions, sorrows, and assaults; and grant thy divine intercession, O Father Anthony, standing before the King of all!
*Theotokion:*
As the unwaning Light that shone forth from thy womb, O pure Sovereign Lady, illuminate my darkened soul, and drive away every mist of my heart!
*Ode 6. First Canon, Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, O god-wise ones: come, let us clap our hands, glorifying God Who was born of her![^437]
*Troparia:*
By constant desire toward God, thou wast outside the flesh and the world, O Father Anthony; therefore thou wast also shown forth a worthy dwelling of the All-Holy Spirit, even as Christ Himself said: "Having come with the Father and the Spirit, there will I dwell!"[^438]

*(Right page — Folio 99 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
Having nurtured thee like a most sweet vineyard, O father, the Holy Mountain is most gloriously adorned concerning thee; and rejoicing it glorifies Christ, Who bestowed thee upon it!
Night seemed unto thee as day, and day as night, O Anthony: for according to the prophet, thou gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, nor rest unto thy body,[^439] until thou didst present this pure with thy soul unto God!
*Theotokion:*
Grant me thy help, and deliver me from perils, sorrows, and my transgressions, O thou who didst bring forth the continual deliverance of all!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
Whirled about in the depth of sins, I call upon the unsearchable depth of Thy compassion: lead me up from corruption, O God![^440]
*Troparia:*
Having gone forth from this world, even as thy blessed soul was freed from bodily weight and from the bitter tyrant Pharaoh, it passed without turmoil unto the land of paradise of promise, and abides rejoicing with the saints!
Now thy sacred body lying in the cave, O venerable one, gushes forth manifold healings unto those who come with faith; therefore we pray thee: by thy supplications grant also health of soul unto those who glorify thee!
Thy most honorable memory, O Anthony, hath shone forth brighter than the sun; assembling whereunto, we glorify thy falling asleep and the labors which thou didst endure in this life, asking to receive through thee from Christ remission of sins!
*Theotokion:*
Account me worthy to behold the beauties of thy glory when I am about to be separated from my flesh, for thou art my establishment, O Sovereign Lady, that thence I may receive deliverance!
*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):*[^441]
Having dedicated thyself from youth unto God Who was loved above all, O venerable one, thou didst follow Him with love from all thy soul; and accounting the corruptible things of the world as nothing, thou didst make a cave in the earth, and therein having contended well against the snares of the invisible enemy, thou didst shine forth like a light-bearing sun unto all the ends of the earth: wherefrom rejoicing thou didst pass unto the heavenly mansions! And now standing with the angels before the Master's throne, remember us who honor thy memory, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Anthony, our father!
*Ikos:*
Desiring to enjoy the perpetual, unwaning light of the Holy Trinity, and for this sake having shut thyself up for the space of forty years in a single dark cave,[^442] thou didst abide there without going forth, O father, until thou didst present thy body pure with thy soul unto God: wherefrom also after thy falling asleep *(Left page — Folio 99 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...wherefrom also after thy falling asleep, as living thou didst receive the gifts of wonders from the Lord, to heal diverse diseases of those who suffer grievously, and to drive away demons from men who come unto thee; therefore we pray thee: pray, O father, to preserve unharmed thy fatherland, the city and the people, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Anthony, our father!
*Ode 7. First Canon, Heirmos:*
The god-wise youths worshipped not the creature rather than the Creator, but having manfully trampled underfoot the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, praised above all![^443]
*Troparia:*
By the command of thy spiritual superior, O blessed Anthony, thou wast sent again unto thy homeland; therefore thou didst perform what was spoken unto thee by the father as commanded by God: having arrived whereunto, O father, thou didst bring the souls of many unto the Lord!
Having reached the most holy place, O Venerable Anthony, thou didst amaze many by thy manner of life; for having taken upon thyself a life above man, thereby thou didst cause many to despise worldly things!
Having found the cave of Archbishop Hilarion, thou didst continue dwelling therein, O venerable one, in prayers and fasts, until thou didst make another cave for thyself, wherein thou wast accounted worthy of the upward calling![^444]
*Theotokion:*
Destroy human assaults and counsels, O pure one, by thy divine prayers, and deliver thy servants from painful transgressions, that we may ever call thee blessed!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
The contrary decree of the lawless tyrant lifted up the high flame; but Christ spread spiritual dew upon the pious youths, He Who is blessed and pre-glorified![^445]
*Troparia:*
A great lover of labor and emulator of virtues didst thou acquire, O Venerable Anthony: the blessed Theodosius, who followed thine angelic manner of life; with him dwelling in the highest, remember us!
A new Moses have we known thee to be, O father, a conqueror of passions: for even as he of old, having conquered Amalek, brought out the people,[^446] so thou, O father, hast brought a multitude of monastics into the land of the promise of paradise!
Thou wast a pilgrim from thy city and fatherland, but again by divine providence thou returnest gloriously attested unto thy fatherland, where thou didst receive grace from the Lord, O father!
*Theotokion:*
The furnace prefigured thy childbirth, O all-immaculate one: for it burned not the youths, even as the unendurable Fire burned not thy womb; therefore we pray thee, deliver thy servants from the eternal fire!
*Ode 8. First Canon, Heirmos:*
The Offspring of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace, then indeed figured, but now in act, stirring up the whole inhabited earth to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^447]

*(Right page — Folio 100 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
*Troparia:*
Thou didst emulate the humility of thy Master Christ, O Venerable Anthony, and for this sake didst love a humble and harsh manner of life, renouncing earthly attachment; therefore thou wast accounted worthy of the honors of the saints, and with them singest unto the Lord unto all ages!
Unto those who celebrate thy most glorious falling asleep, O blessed one, pray that we may be delivered from diverse assaults, that with thee we may all glorify, O Anthony, and supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages!
Stretch forth a prayerful eye unto good concerning us, O Anthony, displaying love for thy children, and unseparated abide with us in spirit, O venerable one, even as thou didst promise when thou wast with us, that with thee we may all sing unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!
*Theotokion:*
Have compassion now upon my supplication, and grant me joy in place of sorrow, that I may sing of thee, O good one, singing unto thy Son: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its operation by the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^448]
*Troparia:*
Abundantly and manifestly grant healings, O venerable one, unto those who come with faith unto thy church, and bestow comfort upon those who are in sorrows, that with thee we may all cry unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!
Mercifully look down from on high, O father, upon those who bring thee this thanksgiving hymn, and pray Christ the Redeemer to save all who cry unto Him: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!
A multitude of those being saved was brought as an offering unto the Almighty through thee, who followed thy manner of life, O Anthony; therefore pray that all may be saved who cry with thee unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!
*Theotokion:*
Who will not marvel at the great mystery of thy childbirth, O Birthgiver of God? What tongue of the earthborn, or what mind of the bodiless can utter it? For above nature and understanding, O Birthgiver of God, hast thou brought forth the Creator!
*Ode 9. First Canon, Heirmos:*
Let all earthborn leap in spirit, enlightened, and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Birthgiver of God, pure Ever-Virgin![^449]
*Troparia:*
Thou didst strive in every way to emulate the life of Anthony who shone forth of old:[^450] for he dwelt alone in the desert, but thou didst shut thyself up alone in a cave, and through many years, O father, didst abide without going forth!
Thou wast the founder and instructor of the life in the caves, O father, and didst struggle there as *(Left page — Folio 100 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...and didst struggle there as a bodiless one; therefore Christ also enriched Thee with the gift of wonders, O venerable one!
Accept the uplifting of my hands, O venerable one, emulating the Master's goodness, overlooking my transgressions: for thou didst wound thy soul with love for Christ and for His all-pure Mother of God, with whom pray Christ to save our souls!
By the uplifting of thy hands unto God, O Venerable Anthony, wonderworking was most gloriously wrought: for by thy prayers thou didst bring down fire from heaven for the cleansing of the place of the church of the All-Pure One, whom with thee we all magnify!
*Second Canon, Heirmos:*
The Son of the Unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened, and to gather the dispersed: therefore the all-hymned Birthgiver of God do we magnify![^451]
*Troparia:*
The decaying body of that many-sinning man who ended his life evilly in negligence, after his falling asleep thou couldst not endure, O father, but didst command him to be carried forth from the cave; but again moved by the entreaty of the fathers concerning him, through thy prayers, O father, thou didst transform his foul stench into sweet fragrance![^452]
Emulating the life of Pachomius the Great, who was accounted worthy of an angelic vision,[^453] thou didst live angelically in the cave, O father; therefore thou wast also accounted worthy to receive honor equal unto the angels, O Anthony!
Having followed the path leading from the Holy Mountain, O father, thou didst reach the place of Kyiv, where it was pleasing unto the Sovereign Lady Mother of God for a church to be erected; and there an all-pure church unto the glory of the Mother of God, together with the blessed Theodosius, thou didst erect; therefore with him we all magnify thee!
*Theotokion:*
O Virgin, Mother of the Light: drive away the cloud of my soul, and grant me purely to behold, O Sovereign Lady, the saving beauty that shone forth ineffably from thy most pure womb as a light unto the nations, O all-hymned one!
*Exapostilarion:*
Thou didst mortify worldly voluptuousness, O Anthony, and having recognized temporary glory, thou didst soon depart from it, from the beginning to walk by the narrow path leading unto the spaciousness of paradise; therefore pray, O Anthony, that they may be saved who honor thee! *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We confess thee truly to be the Birthgiver of God, O Sovereign Lady, who are saved through thee; for thou didst give birth unto God, Who destroyed death by the Cross, and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable, with whom we praise thee, O Virgin!
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^454]
Desiring to behold the ineffable joy of the saints, and the rejoicing of the venerable who pleased God, thou didst hate all rejoicing that is in the world; in fasts...

*(Right page — Folio 101 recto, running header: TO ANTHONY OF THE CAVES:)*
...in fasts and tears thou didst spend thy days, until thou didst mortify bodily passions; therefore thou wast also accounted worthy of the rejoicing of the righteous: with them pray that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*
Today accept those who bring a hymn of praise unto thee at thy memory, O Anthony, and anticipate us before God by thy prayers, and deliver us from the standing on the left hand, and pray to unite us unto the portion of the chosen, that we may all worthily call thee blessed as a fervent intercessor!
In a dark place thou didst shine forth like a star, O venerable one, and didst put to shame the dark faces of demons, O all-wise one: wherefrom having ascended unto the heavens, standing with boldness before the throne of Christ, remember those who celebrate thy memory, O venerable one, that we may find mercy in the day of judgment!
*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having forsaken thy fatherland, O Venerable Father Anthony, and accepted voluntary pilgrimage for the Lord's sake, the labors of a long journey, thirst and hunger, cold and heat didst thou endure for Christ's sake; therefore thou also hearest: "Good and faithful servant, enter into the joy of thy Lord!"[^455] Pray, O father, that we also may hear this voice, that we may ever lovingly call thy memory blessed!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, accept the prayers of thy servants...
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes: Ode 3, of the 4th Tone; and Ode 6, of the 2nd Tone. Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? Epistle to the Galatians, pericope 213 (Galatians 5:22–6:2).*[^456] *Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.*[^457] *Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth. Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 10 (Matthew 4:25–5:12).*[^458] *Communion hymn: The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance.*[^459]
*(Tailpiece: A large engraved baroque cartouche depicting the Dormition Cathedral of the Kyiv Caves Lavra) (Left page — Folio 101 verso, full-page woodcut engraving in a decorative border, depicting the Venerable John the Much-Suffering: above, the saint buried in the earth up to his chest, angels visiting him and driving away demons; below, two arched vignettes: left, John in his cave cell before a book and vigil lamp; right, a terrible fiery dragon with gaping jaws scorching the saint's head; inscribed: "John gave a hair of Moses, and the serpent came and burned his head")*

*On the 18th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father John the Much-Suffering:*[^460]
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^462]
O God-wise Father John, running the much-sorrowful path of this life, thou didst trample down the thorn-bearing carnal passions, and by many ascetic labors didst reach the summit of noetic desire: where now rejoicing...

*(Right page — Folio 102 recto, running header: TO JOHN THE MUCH-SUFFERING:)*
...where now rejoicing, pray for us, O venerable one, to trample down the temptations that ever lie in the way, and to reach the place where is the unending joy of all who rejoice!
O blessed Father John, desiring to adorn thy soul for God with the variegations of pure thoughts, thou didst despise this temporary life, having settled in a dark cave, and buried thyself alive therein, desiring to be dissolved from the body and to live with Christ: with Whom now dwelling, forget not us also who honor thy holy memory!
O Venerable Father John, inflamed with desire for eternal good things, thou didst bind the heavy passions of thy flesh by the surrounding of heavy chains, and gavest thyself no relief until thou didst receive relief from the divine Light; wherein now rejoicing, pray for us, to enslave our passions unto the spirit, and lightly to pass over unto unending life!
*Glory... Tone 5:*
O Father John, when the desire of the love of God settled in thy holy soul, then the evil-originating enemy, striving to take this away, brought against thee a grievous tempest of carnal burnings; which perceiving, thou didst stand upon the rock of steadfast suffering, and didst remain unshaken until thou didst tame them by the apparition of the divine Light;[^461] and now rejoicing in the heavenly courts, send down grace unto dispassion also unto us, O holy one, through thy prayers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
The shadow of the Law passed away...[^463]
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and the 3 readings. Look at Folio 51, on the reverse.*[^464]
*At the Aposticha, Stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^465]
O God-wise Father John, pure vessel of virginity, exemplar of monastics: for the sake of purity alone didst thou endure manifold temptation, and remained unharmed, preserved by the grace of God; whereof may we also not be deprived, through thy prayers!
*Verse: Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways.*[^466]
Beholding thy good struggle, O all-blessed one, and thine army arrayed against the enemy, the Creator of the creation sent swift help unto thee in the light of spiritual grace, whereby the darkness of the demonic assault was straightway driven away from thee; while thou, having received peace unto thy soul, didst join thyself unto Christ with warmest love, unto Whom may we also be united through thy prayers!
*Verse: The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon.*[^467]

*(Left page — Folio 102 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...O John the Much-Suffering, through thy swift prayers unto God!
After the darkness of the much-contending passions, a candle of noetic light was kindled for thee, and a voice was brought unto thy rejoicing, which perceiving in the heart of thy much-enduring body, thou wast filled with ineffable gladness: whereof may we also be accounted worthy, O John the Much-Suffering, through thy swift prayers unto God!
*Glory... Tone 6:*
What praise shall we offer thee, O venerable father? Or with what songs shall we hymn thy struggles, O all-wise John? For being in the flesh, by thy struggles thou didst conquer the bodiless enemy, and didst preserve purity, for the sake whereof thou didst suffer much: and now dwelling in the place of rest with the virgins, pray Christ the Master to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Lo, is fulfilled the prophecy of Isaiah...[^468]
*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Truly an unshakeable pillar of endurance, and an unassailable rampart against the enemy's arrows, O equal-to-the-angels and much-suffering father our John:[^469] possessing thee, we the unworthy pray, warmly falling down before thy honorable relics: deliver us from the soul-corrupting carnal passions, and save us by thy prayers!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
When Gabriel announced unto thee, O Virgin: Rejoice...[^470]
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord":*
*Troparion of the saint, twice.*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Today the tidings" (Дне́сь а҆́ггелїя):*[^471]
Possessing a life filled with good deeds, O venerable one, thou didst provoke the deceitful enemy unto envy, who attacked thee all-cunningly in the form of a serpent, and breathing fire most evilly with his mouth,[^472] the noetic and all-wicked one wished to swallow thee; but the grace of God preserved thee unharmed: grant us also to obtain this grace, O John the Much-Suffering, through thine intercessions!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Possessing a life filled with shameful deeds, I have become prey for the deceitful enemy; but unto thee I cry, O pure Virgin: crush the teeth of the insatiable and all-wicked one, and snatch me away from unfitting passions, that I may ever sing unto thee with the voice of rejoicing: Rejoice, thou who snatchest men from defiled deeds!
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^473]
Ceaselessly envying the purity of thy virginity, O venerable father, the all-wicked enemy hurled against thee arrows of unclean burnings, which falling down were shattered against the rock of thine endurance, and herein...

*(Right page — Folio 103 recto, running header: TO JOHN THE MUCH-SUFFERING:)*
...and herein thou didst acquire thy reward, O all-blessed one: whereof may we also be partakers through thy prayers!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Into the mire of sinful passions did the all-cunning enemy cast me down, and I lie in the slime of unfitting deeds; but do thou, O all-pure Virgin, by thy swift intercession draw forth for me the water of contrition, that having washed away the stench of many sins, I may sing: Rejoice, bath that washest the conscience!
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное сло́во):*[^474]
Who will not call thee blessed as a good warrior and lover of purity, O all-wise John? Or who will not hymn in songs thy manifold struggles? How in purity thou didst contend against the invisible enemy, and having conquered him wast shown forth a crown-bearer, and now standing before Christ God in the unapproachable light, pray for those who honor thy much suffering!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Who will not praise thee, O all-pure Virgin, as the beauty among women, and the only immaculate one, who through purity didst surpass the angels and archangels? Or who will not honor in hymns thy childbirth, O unwedded Mother, whom all generations with one accord magnify?
*The Gradual Hymns, the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^475] *Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.*[^476] *Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel of the Venerable One.*
*Sticheron after the Gospel, Tone 6:*
*What praise shall we offer thee... (Seek at Aposticha, Glory).*
*Canon of the Birthgiver of God with the heirmos on 6.*
*And of the venerable one on 8.*
*Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 3.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
He Who of old by divine gesture gathered the waters into one congregation, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, glorified above all; unto Him alone let us sing: For He hath been glorified![^477]
*Troparia:*
O my Christ, Who of old didst give waters unto Thy thirsting people in the wilderness, give also the water of grace unto me who thirst to offer praise unto Thee, that at the memory of Thy venerable one I may sing the hymn: For He hath been glorified!
The narrow path and manifold sorrows hast Thou Thyself shown forth, O Lord, unto those who journey unto Thy kingdom; desiring which, Thy venerable one gladly gave himself over unto sorrows in this life, that there he might rejoice, singing: For He hath been glorified!

*(Left page — Folio 103 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
Ceaselessly remembering the voice of perpetual rejoicing and the sound of those who keep festival, behold, O John, thou didst forsake the delusions that call unto themselves, didst take the yoke of Christ upon thyself, and lightly didst bear it in the hope of swift transformation, singing unto God: For He hath been glorified!
*Glory...*
Having departed far from the passionate world, O blessed one, like a hart thou didst run unto the mount of the Caves, where having given drink unto thy soul with streams of the spiritual torrent of the blessed fathers, thou didst sing unto God: For He hath been glorified!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Submerged with the ship of my soul in the sea of passions, I cry unto thee, O pure one: help me with thy loving-kindness, and deliver me from submersion; for thou art a ship for those who wish to be saved, and a calm haven of worldly voyages!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Most High Almighty, Who from non-existence didst bring all things into being, fashioned by the Word and accomplished by the Spirit: establish me in Thy love![^478]
*Troparia:*
Accounting worldly things as though non-existent, thou didst flee unto the calm haven of monastics, O father, living with whom and loving the One God, thou didst sing: O my Christ, establish me in Thy love!
Seeing that thou desiredst to follow the all-pure and immaculate Lamb in purity, O blessed one, the defiled enemy attacked thee with carnal burning, against which thou didst stand, humbling thy flesh with fasting and thirst, and singing in prayer: O my Christ, establish me in Thy love!
Remembering the fall of the bearer of light that brings fornication, O venerable one, thou didst settle in a dark cave, and keeping silence beside the tomb of the great Anthony,[^479] thou didst ceaselessly remember thy departure, and thus didst find peace in thy bones!
*Glory...*
Not death, but transformation unto a better life is it for the saints of God: for even if they are laid in tombs, yet they ever live unto God, and utter a voice, even as unto thee, O Father John, from the tomb of the great Anthony a voice came forth, calling thee unto silence!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
I remember the hour of judgment, but possess not transformation from passions unto a dispassionate life, O pure Birthgiver of God: for the sake of the prayers of the venerable virgin John, establish me in the love of thy Son!

*(Right page — Folio 104 recto, running header: TO JOHN THE MUCH-SUFFERING:)*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^480]
Emulating the blessedness of those who defiled not themselves with women, that thou mightest follow the Lamb upon Mount Zion, thou didst determine to preserve thy virginity undefiled, and didst preserve it whole, unharmed by many demonic assaults; therefore thou singest a new song, O Venerable John, and thy spirit rejoices with the angels!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The blessedness of virgins have I the wretched one not emulated, having defiled the robe of my baptism with fornication's corruptions, but am far removed from thy grace, O undefiled Lamb: O Son of God, for the sake of Thy holy sufferings and of the all-pure Birthgiver of God, grant me correction, and have mercy upon me!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou hast placed steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst give Thine only-begotten Son unto death for our sake; therefore we cry unto Thee, giving thanks: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^481]
*Troparia:*
Thou didst place thy life in a dark cave without going forth, O father, until the passionate gloom was changed into light through silence and not seeing the sensible light!
Manifold sorrows didst thou endure, O father, contending against passions as against fierce beasts, firmly bridling their harsh assaults by fasting and vigil in thy bodily frame!
Unable to cool thy flesh burned by the fire of demonic inflaming, O blessed one, thou didst choose to live naked in a narrow place, struggling there through cold unto spiritual warmth!
*Glory...*
Desiring to humble thy raging body like a fierce and untamable serpent, thou didst bind it with heavy irons, O venerable one,[^482] that it might not lust against the spirit, but rather be submissive unto the spirit!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Unable to tame the passions that rise up against me through much intemperance, I flee unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: do thou, O all-pure one, humble their foul impurity, and save me from their assault!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Upon earth the Invisible was seen, and with men the Incomprehensible willingly dwelt; and rising early unto Thee, we sing of Thee, O Lover of mankind![^483]
*Troparia:*
Who living upon earth will not marvel at thine endurance, O father? For having dug a pit for thyself, and gone into it naked, thou didst bury thyself up to the shoulders,[^484] by the earth crushing the carnal impulse stirred up by the adversary enemy!
Thy body crushed by the earth grew weary, but the enemy kept vigilant *(Left page — Folio 104 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
...the enemy kept vigilant, rising up powerfully with warfare, and attacking with terrors, wishing thus to prevail over thee, lest he fall, overthrown by thee, O venerable one!
The devil, beholding thy mind burning toward God, O all-honorable John, with malicious flame burned thy members and entrails from below; but taking no heed of this pain, thou didst choose to burn for the Lord's sake rather than to be mocked by demons!
*Glory...*
Unable to wound thy purity with asp's poison, O blessed one, Satan transformed himself into a serpent breathing fire, that at least by thus terrifying thee, he might conquer thee, an unconquerable warrior of Christ!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
With the asp's poison of passionate lust whereby the enemy wounds my heart: heal it with the medicine of thy compassion, O unwedded Virgin, that being saved therefrom, I may bring thee the fruit of repentance, O all-hymned one!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The lowest abyss of sins hath surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy high arm, O Master, as Peter save me, O Steersman![^485]
*Troparia:*
The lowest abyss opened its maw: the unclean devil transformed into a serpent, burning, breathing with flame-bearing gapings, wishing to swallow thee; but thou, O father, wast like an unmoved pillar, singing: O my God, my Steersman, save me!
Standing upon the rock of steadfast faith, O venerable one, thou didst cover thyself with the shield of hope, and accounting the tempest of the deceitful enemy as nothing, thou didst tread upon his head; and being in his maw, thou wast not terrified, crying unto Christ: O Steersman, save me!
The noetic sea-monster placed thy head into his maw, O blessed one,[^486] and thy hair was scorched, but thy heart was not scorched with faint-heartedness, O father; therefore thou didst cry aloud: and the Lord gave ear unto thee, and as a good Steersman in His love for mankind saved thee!
*Glory...*
Thy prayer, O John, became lightning-like arrows against the adversary's power; for thou didst say: "O my God, why hast Thou forsaken me in vain?"[^487] And straightway the deceitful one vanished, wounded, and approached thee no more, driven away by the divine light!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
The arrows of the noetic adversary have wounded my heart with passions; therefore from the bitterness of...

*(Right page — Folio 105 recto, running header: TO JOHN THE MUCH-SUFFERING:)*
...from the bitterness of my soul I cry unto thee, O pure one: O fountain of healings, who didst bring forth Christ my Healer!
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^488]
Being an unshakeable pillar of chastity through extreme roots, like a pillar thou didst vigilantly leave unto us thy much-suffering body, O all-honorable John, bowing before which in infirmities of soul and body, we pray thee, O venerable one: ceaselessly pray Christ God for us all!
*Ikos:*
Having loved the divine commandments of Christ, O venerable one, and hated the pleasures of this world, thou didst zealously come unto Him, and wast a luminary, enlightening the ends of the earth with spiritual brightness; therefore falling down I pray thee: enlighten my noetic eyes, to hymn thy struggles, fasting, vigil, tears, pains, and the exhaustion of thy body for the sake of the blessed future life whereof thou now delightest: ceaselessly pray for us all!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
As of old Thou didst bedew the three pious youths in the Chaldean flame with the bright fire of Divinity, illuminate also us who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^489]
*Troparia:*
Even as dust vanishes before the wind, and smoke before a rain-bearing cloud,[^490] so when the divine light arrived, the wicked one vanished; while thou, O father, didst bow down before the Lord, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
The Lord was near unto those who call upon thee for help, O holy one; perceiving Whose grace, with joyful soul from the depth of thy heart thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
*Glory...*
As an all-wise master imposes a great work upon a steadfast servant, so the Lord permitted thee to endure the day and night burning of carnal inflaming; and thou didst patiently bear it, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
As an all-wise Master, thy Son, O Virgin, permits light passions to come upon me who am slothful, but before they have come, I have grown faint; therefore unto thee I cry: help me, O Mother of the Word, for thou art blessed!

*(Left page — Folio 105 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE:)*
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The youths standing for piety, united in the unendurable fire, yet unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^491]
*Troparia:*
In the unendurable fire of carnal burnings, a voice from on high commands thee, O blessed one, to pray unto the dead man who is able to help against passions of fornication and unto others,[^492] that they may sing unto the Lord unto all ages!
He who accomplished the will of Joseph when, being held captive, he obeyed not the defiled woman,[^493] the same followed thee through intercession unto God, through whom having escaped corrupting passions, thou now singest unto the Lord unto all ages!
The Eye of God beholding thy magnanimity, O father, ten-thousandfold brighter than the sun, sent down the light of grace, and the gloom of ruinous fornication perished utterly from thy bones; and having lived the remaining time in quietness, thou now singest unto the Lord unto all ages!
*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
The Master Who grants noetic joy unto the dispassionate, granted it also unto thee, O Venerable John, after manifold labors; which having received from the divine light, thou didst rejoice, and now makest merry in heaven with those who sing unto the Lord unto all ages!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Virgin who didst conceive outside passions, and without carnal lust didst bring forth the Word of God: destroy the assaults of the wicked one that begin in darkness, that having escaped the fall into sin, I may be accounted worthy to sing unto the Lord unto all ages!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
