A new and divine wonder: for through the shut virginal gate manifestly passes the Lord; naked at His entrance, and flesh-bearing God appeared at His going forth, and the gate remains shut: her ineffably as the Mother of God do we magnify![^494]
*Troparia:*
Thou didst begin a new life when, after much suffering, the tempest of passions was stilled in thee, O father, and thou didst live the remaining time in quietness, magnifying the power of God!
Standing upon the steadfast rock of endurance, thou wast like a strong pillar against the face of the enemy, whose arrows striking against thee were shattered; therefore we magnify thee, O John!
Having lived pure in soul and body upon earth, thou didst enter unto the heavenly realms, to behold the face of God with a pure heart; delighting in the vision Whereof, pray for us, O venerable one, that with thee we may magnify the Trinity!
*Glory...*
Blessed was thy life, O John, blessed was thy falling asleep, but most blessed is thy life in the unwaning light, for the sake whereof thou didst endure manifold sorrows: now rejoicing, pray for us who honor thy memory, O all-wise one!
*Both now... Theotokion:*

*(Right page — Folio 106 recto, running header: TO JOHN THE MUCH-SUFFERING:)*
O Virgin who seedlessly from thy blood didst bring forth the Prince of life: for the sake of the prayers of the virgin John, direct us who bow down before thee unto eternal life, that we may magnify thee who didst bring forth the Fountain of life!
*Exapostilarion:*
For the sake of thy purity having journeyed the sorrowful path, thou didst sow manifold tears; and now with gladness thou reapest blessed joys: pray for us, O venerable one, with the holy fathers, that we also may be partakers of your blessedness; for we are your children, and the sheep of your spiritual teachings!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, portal of the Word, Mother of Christ our God: pray that we thy servants may be saved!
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ єсѝ зна́менїе):*[^495]
Thou hast given desire and ceaseless love for purity unto Thy servant, O Lord; and he, having received instruction from Thee, gave himself over unto much torment from enemies; but having conquered them unto the end, he was shown forth a crown-bearer: for his sake preserve also us, O Jesus, Lover of mankind, and save our souls!
Being pure and possessing the vessel of thy heart, O all-wise John, thou didst kindle the fire of noetic desire, and the fire of the supreme Light scorched the serpent that scorched thee, and drove him away by the power of the supreme Light, so that he dared not draw near unto thy place: where now beholding thy much-enduring relics, we bow down and pray: pray for us, O glorious John, as being ever an intercessor for our souls!
*Glory... Tone 6:*
Come, ye people, let us call blessed John the Much-Suffering, an animate vessel and lover of purity, praising him in hymns and songs: for by his manifold sufferings he crushed the head of the deceitful serpent, and having not burned his body with the flame of fornication, he went forth pure with a pure heart to stand before the immaculate Lamb Christ God; and now ministering unto the Light brighter than the sun, he fervently prays for us who lovingly honor his memory, and grants healing unto those who flee unto him with faith!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Deliver from perils thy servants, O Birthgiver of God...[^496]
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service is common to a Venerable Monastic.*[^497]`
*(Tailpiece: A small floral bar) (Left page — Folio 106 verso, full-page engraving of Venerable Moses the Hungarian and service opening)*[^498]
*(Woodcut engraving: Venerable Moses the Hungarian. Top compartments: "In youth, tonsuring of Venerable Moses the Hungarian" and "Moses went forth unto the nobles." Middle compartments: "The woman kissed Moses" and "She commanded Moses to be beaten." Center: Icon of Venerable Moses the Hungarian standing full-length in monastic habit. Bottom compartments: "The falling asleep of Venerable Moses the Hungarian" and "He was brought unto King Bolesław.")*

*Month of July, on the 26th day.*
*Service to our Venerable Father Moses the Hungarian.*[^498]
*At Vespers, Blessed is the man: the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чино́въ):*[^499]
Attending unto purity of soul, thou didst ever preserve bodily purity, O our Father Moses; adorned wherewith, thou followest now the spotless and all-pure Lamb, the Son of God, Who was slain for the whole world:[^500] fervently pray Him...

*(Right page — Folio 107 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
...fervently pray Him, O venerable one, to keep the garment of our soul undefiled in this life, and to enter into the heavenly bridal chamber!
Thou wast likened unto Moses the God-seer, O holy one: for spiritually beholding God in thy soul as in the bush, thou didst nail thy mind unto His fear, and having Him ever before thine eyes, thou wast kindled with desire as with a flame unto the love of God, from which thou didst never depart.[^501] And now standing before the throne of majesty on high, pray for us, that we may pass through our life without passion, and appear before the face of God!
The grace of the All-Holy Spirit made its dwelling in thy precious soul, and made thee unconquered by all the assaults of the murderer of man; having received victory against him by the help of Christ, thou didst put his crafts to shame, and wast shown forth an invincible warrior; and now standing before His beneficent throne, pray that He grant us peace and great mercy!
*Glory, Tone 6:*
Come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers, having formed a spiritual choir, let us praise the memory of our God-bearing father, Moses the steadfast sufferer, in this summer solemnity! For he contended valiantly against the invisible enemy who sought to consume his purity with unclean passions, and suffered greatly for the sake of purity; but having his hope in God, he was shown forth a crown-bearer, and now standing before the Holy Trinity, he prays for our souls.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Who will not call thee blessed...*
*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 82 on the reverse).*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^502]
Faithfully serving the all-lauded passion-bearer, the Rus' Prince Boris, O our Father Moses, thou didst learn to serve also the Heavenly King in holiness and righteousness;[^503] standing now before Him, forget not to pray also for us, that we may subdue our members unto the Spirit, and receive the heavenly crowns of honor!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord: he shall delight exceedingly in His commandments.*[^504]
Following the servant who feared God, thou didst learn meekness and humility, O our all-blessed Father Moses, and for his love thou wast ready to lay down thy soul, in no wise resisting visible enemies, but rising up only against the invisible ones; having vanquished them unto the end, pray also for us unto the Most High *(Left page — Folio 107 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...pray unto the Master on high, to overcome the spirits of wickedness under heaven!
*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^505]
The grace of the Most High preserved thee, O venerable one: as thy heart fled away from sin, the all-bountiful right hand of Christ God overshadowed thee, and preserved thee as the apple of an eye, until He made thee His faithful servant; having served Him faithfully in this life, thou didst receive the joyful denarius of eternal life:[^506] pray that we also who honor thy memory may not be deprived thereof!
*Glory, Tone 8:*
Ye assemblies of Rus' monastics, and lovers of purity, come unto the holy cave, and behold the many-healing relics of our all-blessed Father Moses, and learn from him humility and purity, patience and magnanimity, and glorify the Heavenly Father Who grants great mercy unto His servants!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of the Creator of all, today in thy most honorable temple, which thou hast built for a dwelling unto thyself, and hast enriched with many miracles: deliver us who have thee as helper from every necessity and sorrow!
*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Another Joseph, great lover of purity and chastity, all-honorable and equal-to-the-angels Moses: we sinners praising thee in sacred hymns pray thee fervently: pray Christ God, that He may heal all our passions and grant great mercy!
*Another Troparion, in the same Tone:*
Beholding thy labors and struggles against passions for the sake of thy purity, O holy one, who will not marvel? For receiving hunger, imprisonment, and wounds, thou wast not enslaved unto the passions of the Polish woman, but as a king having mastery over passions, thou wast glorified then and now by Him Who beholds the hearts of men;[^507] having received from Him grace in temptations, assist also us who honor thy memory, O Venerable Moses!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*(Ornamental divider bar)*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Venerable One.*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^508]
The all-evil enemy envying the beauty of thy countenance, that he might cunningly steal away the soul's fair comeliness...

*(Right page — Folio 108 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
...that he might cunningly steal away the soul's fair comeliness, beset the mind of the lawless woman with passions; then by all deceits he strove to shake thee, O venerable one; but understanding his wiles, as another Joseph thou didst struggle against the Polish woman, and preserved the beauty of both uninjured; therefore thou dost now enjoy in the heavens joy consubstantial with thy labors!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Honoring thy swift protection and intercession, and beholding thine unnumbered goodness and compassion, all sinners run unto thee and receive the healing of repentance; but I, wretch that I am, having despised all these things, have alone been deprived of thy help; therefore summon me unto thy goodness, show it forth, but receive me who fall down before thee, O Sovereign Lady, and guide me into the way of repentance!
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, in the same Tone, Podoben: "Thou wast shown forth" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^509]
All the angelic choirs marveled at thy patience and courage, O venerable one, that being encompassed by the fire of the passion of wantonness, thou wast not burned by the defiled woman, nor didst thou submit unto her desire; for having a heart kindled with the fire of the love of God, thou didst extinguish the flame of passion!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Speedily come unto our help, O all-pure Sovereign Lady: for enemies have risen up against us, warring against us day and night, and lo, being vanquished, we have no shelter where to flee from the destruction of the destroyers; we pray thee, as Mother of God, stretch forth thy mighty right hand and strike them down by thy power, and preserve us uninjured, that we may offer unto thee the angelic hymn: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^510]
Come, ye people, let us hymn in songs the valiant sufferer and unconquerable warrior, the boast of monastics and lover of purity, our God-bearing Father Moses, praising God Who strengthens His servants and grants victory over visible and invisible enemies; for girded by His help, he overcame all the regiments of the ruler of this world, and now standing before the throne of the Master in the heavenly mansions, he prays that we also *(Left page — Folio 108 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...he prays that we also may be delivered from all stumblings caused by the enemy, and from his temptations!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O all-pure Sovereign Lady, more honorable than all creation, turn not thy face away, but accept the prayers of thy servants who run today into thy most honorable temple and bow down before thine all-pure image: stretch forth those all-holy hands wherewith thou didst bear the Creator of all; entreat Him as the Lover of mankind, that He may gird us with His power from on high, wherewith having conquered all those who rise up against us, we may magnify thy good and swift protection and help!
*The Gradual Hymns, the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^511] *Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^512] *Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 43 (Matthew 11:27–30).*[^513]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
*Come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers... (Seek at Vespers on "Lord, I have cried").*
*(Ornamental vignette)*
*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt; for He is glorified![^514]
*Troparia:*
Come, O ye peoples, let us offer praise unto God, Who glorifies His pleased one and showed him forth unconquered in all temptations, and let us sing unto Him a hymn: for He is gloriously glorified!
The all-evil enemy brought death upon thy master, the blessed Prince Boris, through the wretched fratricide, and wished to destroy thee with him; but thou, having been saved from evil men, didst sing: Glory to God for all things; blessed be the name of the Lord![^515]
Saved from death by the hand of God, thou didst come unto Kyiv, unto Predslava, sister of Yaroslav, where seized with grief for thy master, thou didst recount everything concerning him, how much he suffered as a voiceless lamb, not resisting, but giving himself into the hands of those who sought his blood; and thou didst sing: Our God is gloriously glorified![^516]

*(Right page — Folio 109 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
*Glory:*
For a certain time thou didst hide thyself for fear, O venerable one, displaying human weakness in thyself, until the Lord hid thee as a luminary, for an example unto all who endure the narrow way, which thou didst preserve pure through many temptations, singing: O my God, be Thou gloriously glorified in my weak flesh!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Queen who canst do all things through God, speedily stand before those who offer supplication unto thee, and behold how many murderers of souls have risen up against us; destroy them utterly with the arrows of thy power, that we may unceasingly cry unto thee: for thy name is gloriously glorified, great and terrible unto our enemies!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies: for my spirit hath rejoiced in singing: There is none holy as our God, and there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord![^517]
*Troparia:*
Having established the senses of thy soul upon the firm rock of faith, thou didst go into captivity, having hope in Jesus alone, Who endured the most grievous wounds and the Cross, that He would deliver thee from the snare of the fowler; therefore in every place thou didst sing: There is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!
Bound with heavy irons upon hands and feet, and enduring confinement beforehand for five years, thou wast not shattered in mind, but having a tongue full of hope, thou didst give thanks unto God for all that befell thee, singing: Righteously hast Thou brought all these things upon me, for there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord![^518]
A certain young widow among noble women, seeing thee in bonds, handsome in countenance and still more radiant in soul, was wounded with the passion of love for thee, and tempted thee greatly, O holy one; but thou didst answer her: I will by no means submit unto thy will, for God hath so willed: unto Whom I sing: There is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!
The wanton woman, marveling at thy bodily beauty, and even more at thy chaste words, attempted many times to ensnare thee with her flattering words; but thou, O blessed one, having a most steadfast mind, didst speak wisely: "Who hath ever been saved by listening unto a woman?" and didst cry unto God: Save me, O God, from this affliction, for there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!

*(Left page — Folio 109 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...There is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!
*Glory:*
When the defiled woman pressed upon thee with manifold seductions, many a time thou didst answer: "Being bought with the blood of my Christ, I will by no means make myself a slave unto a shameless woman whom I have not known from my birth; and let it not be mine to lose the labor in these bonds, for the sake of which I hope to escape eternal bonds, if I remain thus enduring, and thankfully singing: There is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!"
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Beholding me caught by the enemy with the snares of grievous transgressions, and shut up in the prison of mental darkness, O Sovereign Lady: swiftly release me through thine intercession, and enlighten my mind with the beams of thy tender mercy, and turn me to sing: There is no other help for the sorrowful save thee, O most holy Virgin Mary!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*[^519]
The wanton woman, ever burned with the fire of thy beauty, hastened unto evil counsel: for she reasoned that if she bought thee with a price from him who captured thee, she could easily incline thee unto her will; but thou, O venerable one, having the fear of God in thy heart, didst unceasingly cry out: Save me, O God, from such madness, and unto Thee my Master do I flee!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having received the Divine Fire unconsumed into thy most pure womb, sprinkle me who am burned with the fire of passions with at least a drop of thy grace, O Lady, that unceasingly I may sing: Save me, for unto thee my Sovereign Lady do I flee!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou hast come from the Virgin, neither an ambassador nor an angel, but the Lord Himself incarnate, and Thou hast saved me, whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^520]
*Troparia:*
The woman, burned with passion, having gone unto him who captured thee, O blessed one, gave a price for thee, and taking thee, loosed thy chains, desiring to bind thy soul with sinful bonds; but thou, guarding thyself with the Cross, didst enter into her house, remembering after the manner of the Psalm: Yea, though I walk through the midst of the shadow of death, I will fear no evils, for God is with me; unto Whose power be glory![^521]
Thou wast likened unto thy Master: for even as the Jews, having bought Him, prepared Him for passion, so also the defiled wanton woman prepared thee: if she could not wound thy soul with passions, she would encompass thy body with suffering; but thou didst choose rather to suffer in body than in soul, singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*(Right page — Folio 110 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
That thou mightest not feel the burning of passion in thy body, thou didst turn away from voluptuous food, and didst put off soft garments from thee; instead thereof, thou didst clothe thyself in fasting and prayer, choosing dry bread and water with purity rather than to eat diverse dainties and wine with defilement; and being unconquered by the enemy, thou didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
Behold another Joseph, O brethren: for even as he left his garments in the hand of the wanton woman, fleeing sin, so also this one, stripping himself of the beauty of garments, fled from the hand that dragged him unto defiled mingling, and hereby put to shame the invisible enemy who wished to defile the soul and body of the sufferer; but he accomplished nothing, for the saint sang: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^522]
*Glory:*
Finding another Egyptian, the enemy attempted to ensnare the blessed one: for he could not bear to look upon the purity of his heart nor the beauty of his face, at which the angels also marveled, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Desiring thy beauty, the supreme King, by the counsel of the Father and the overshadowing of the All-Holy Spirit, came down as rain upon the fleece into thy most pure womb, O Virgin; raise up again the fallen beauty of my mind, O Sovereign Lady, that I may unceasingly sing unto thee: Glory to thy power, O Lady Birthgiver of God!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Giver of light and Creator of the ages, guide us in the light of Thy commandments: for beside Thee we know no other God![^523]
*Troparia:*
The senseless woman, unable to deprive thee of the mental light, strove to take away the sensible light, O venerable one: therefore she cast thee into a dark prison, where she tormented thee with hunger and thirst; but thou didst cry out: O my God, forsake me not, for beside Thee I know no other!
He Who once fed Elijah and Paul of Thebes in the wilderness, made His mercy marvelous also upon thee: for He inclined one of that woman's servants unto compassion, and commanded him to feed thee secretly; and thou, accounting it to be a visitation of God, didst cry out: Beside Thee I know no other God![^524]
Many, not knowing the grace of God working in Thee, O holy one, counseled Thee to submit unto the will of thy mistress, that thou mightest become her master and lord over all her possessions; but thou didst despise all these things for the sake of the love of God, saying: Beside Him I will know no other!

*(Left page — Folio 110 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
Thou didst seek not corruptible dominion, but to rule over the passions of the flesh, O our all-blessed Father Moses; therefore God granted unto thee that thou didst not only subdue thine own passions unto the Spirit, but didst also receive authority to assist others in temptations; therefore thou didst cry out: I will know nothing else save His love!
*Glory:*
Thou didst laugh at the madness of the wanton woman, when she commanded her servants to lead thee around in glory through the streets and to magnify thee as their master; but with a loud voice thou didst utter words of chastity: "In vain does he labor who commanded you to do this, for beside God I will know nothing!"
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Put to shame the enemy who rejoices that he hath ensnared me, O Lady, and gladden us who are distressed in the tempest of worldly turmoil, and grant us help to reach the calm haven of thy protection: for beside thee we know no other helper in sorrow!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the fathomless abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^525]
*Troparia:*
Thou didst not sink into the abyss of passions, nor didst thou dip thy wounds into defilements, O venerable one; therefore, guided by God, a monk secretly clothed thee in the angelic habit, and taught thee how to keep it spotless, and neither to fear terror nor to turn thy back unto enemies: for God is He Who delivers His servants from corruption![^526]
The wanton woman, submerged in the abyss of sinful burning, hearing that her hope in thee was lost, laid heavy wounds upon thee, O venerable one: for she commanded that thou be stretched out and beaten severely with rods, so that the ground was filled with thy blood; but thankfully enduring these things, thou didst cry: Lead me up from corruption, O my God!
Wishing to terrify thee, the fearless one, they warned thee how many torments thou must endure, O blessed one, if thou didst not do the will of thy mistress; but thou, being ready for all things, didst boldly answer: Neither fire nor wounds shall be able to separate me from the love of God, in Whom I hope, that He will deliver me from corruption![^527]
Thou didst not submit unto the woman darkened with shamelessness, but rather didst rebuke her, O venerable one, as one who had transgressed the fear of God; and thou didst spit upon her who shamelessly gave herself over unto defiled mingling...

*(Right page — Folio 111 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
...and thou didst spit upon her who shamelessly gave herself over unto defiled mingling, fearing neither her fury nor her wrath: for thou hadst hope in God, O father, that He would deliver thee from her hands!
*Glory:*
The woman, seeing herself put to shame by thee, O father, presented thee unto the duke, from whom she received final judgment to do unto thee whatever she willed, having authority to deliver thee unto death after many torments; but prophetically foreseeing their destruction, thou didst answer: "Ye yourselves shall soon enter into eternal destruction, but Christ my God shall deliver me therefrom!"
*Both now... Theotokion:*
I stand ever before the merciful Duke, and as a captive I hear judgment against myself, that wanton passions may work upon me whatever they will; having received power over me, they strip me in my senses, and by force continually drag me to commit fornication with them in thought; but before the axe of death cut me down in this world, O pure Mother of the peaceful Heavenly King, drive them away, and deliver me from corruption, O Birthgiver of God!
*Kontakion, Tone 3:*[^528]
Seeking on high the one God, thou didst despise all earthly things, and taking no care for thy body, thou didst manfully give thyself over unto torments; hunger, thirst, and bonds, being shut up in prison, thou didst valiantly endure: beatings, the shedding of blood, and the cutting off of thy members for the sake of thy pure virginity thou didst manfully bear;[^529] and standing now with virginal choirs before the All-Holy Trinity, pray that all may be delivered from temptations who cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Moses, our most blessed father!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The ungodly decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread a spiritual dew upon the godly youths, He Who is blessed and supremely praised![^530]
*Troparia:*
Having received the ungodly command from the duke, the wanton woman dragged thee without shame unto defiled mingling, desiring to be satisfied with thy beauty, surpassing in purity, at which the angels also marveled, crying unto Him Who wrought this in thee: Blessed art Thou, O God the Creator!
The enemy loosed all his passionate arrows against thee, O holy one, desiring to wound thee; but being himself wounded, he groaned when he saw thee unconquered, crying all the more: Blessed art Thou, O God!
"In vain dost thou labor, O woman!" thou didst say, O all-blessed one, "for I will in no wise touch thine impurity *(Left page — Folio 111 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
..."for I will in no wise touch thine impurity, foreseeing God always before me at my right hand, nor will my flesh wanton, for the fear of the Lord gives me power; therefore I cry: Blessed art Thou, O God!"[^531]
The wanton woman, wishing to soften the steadfastness of thy mind, commanded that thou be beaten with rods, and that one hundred wounds be laid upon thee every day; but even so thou wast not shaken, for thy flesh was fortified in the love of God, of which thou hast brought forth a hundredfold fruit, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!
*Glory:*
Receiving grievous beatings and the cutting off of thy members, O holy one, thou wast as dead, lying scarcely alive; but thou didst sever the head of him who stood near, showing him dead and powerless, and didst cry out: Blessed art Thou, O my God, my strength!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having fallen into grievous misfortunes, I am in no wise able to lift myself up; stretch forth thy strong hand unto me, O Sovereign Lady, and set me again upon my feet, that I may walk in the straight ways of the Lord, crying unto thee: From generation to generation blessed be thy name!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew: hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him unto all ages![^532]
*Troparia:*
Dragged into the fiery furnace of lustful sin, thou didst call upon the one God, O blessed one, saying after David: O God, give heed unto my help; and having stepped upon the flame, thou didst remain unconsumed, burning rather the head of the multifaceted enemy, who willed not to sing the Lord unto the ages![^533]
The enemy, unable to burn thy purity with the fire of wantonness, stirred up a cruel persecution against the regiments of monastics, and all were expelled from the province of the unmerciful duke; upon whom the wrath of God quickly came according to thy prophecy, O holy one, because he willed not to have in all his dominion those who sing the Lord unto the ages!
The duke having suddenly suffered an evil death, there was tumult and uproar among the people: for the slaves rose up and slew their masters, with whom also the wanton woman who tormented thee perished, and her blood was shed upon her own head; but thou, being set free, didst sing: In all His works I will bless the Lord unto the ages![^534]
Having rendered praise unto God Who freed Thee, Thou didst come into the cave unto Venerable Anthony...[^535]

*(Right page — Folio 112 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
...thou didst come into the cave unto Venerable Anthony, bearing the wounds of martyrdom upon thy body, which all seeing cried out: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him unto the ages!
*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Wishing to conceal thy purity, brighter than the sun, O father, thou didst dwell in the dark cave, wherein thou didst adorn it with fasting and all-night standings; therefore God again made known concerning thee how great grace thou hast to assist all in temptations, that His name may be glorified unto the ages!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thinking to hide the shame of my sin before the all-seeing eye, burned every day with fleshly kindle, I add iniquity unto iniquity; but before my deeds are exposed before the assembly of angels and men, help me, O pure one, to escape such madness, that I may glorify thy name unto the ages!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Unto God the Word, Who came by ineffable wisdom to renew Adam who by eating had fallen grievously into corruption, and was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin for our sake: with one mind let us, the faithful, magnify Him in songs![^536]
*Troparia:*
God the Word, Who came down from God, glorified thee while yet in this life with wonderworking gifts, O holy one: for when a certain brother was warring against wantonness, at the touch of thy staff his passions were deadened, and having received from thee the commandment never to converse with a woman, which he did, he lived the rest of his years in peace, magnifying thee!
From generation to generation thy purity is glorified, for the sake of which having suffered much, thou didst receive the blessedness of the pure in heart; having passed over from this temporary life, thou pure beholdest the All-Holy Trinity; remember also us, O venerable one, who venerate thy memory!
The Lord called thee a dead man, for thou didst deaden fleshly lusts by marvelous endurance, and thou hast power to deaden passions in those who touch thy relics, as thou didst unto the much-suffering John, who having received peace in his bones, magnified God Who glorifies His saints![^537]
Rejoice, O mountain, that hast in the cave the incorrupt relics of the marvelous Moses; sing with praises *(Left page — Folio 112 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...sing with praises, ye assemblies of monastics: for behold, ye have an assistant and an intercessor in sorrows; bow down before the shrine of his relics, touching it with your lips, and ye shall find rest in your members!
*Glory:*
We glorify the All-Holy Trinity, for He gave unto us the luminary of the world, our Father Moses; celebrating whose yearly memory, we the faithful with one mind magnify the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit in one Godhead!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Ever do we glorify thee, O pure Virgin, for thou hast gathered thy faithful servants in this place, where many receive healing, bowing down before thine unhewn icon, and calling upon the prayer of our Caves Fathers![^538]
*Exapostilarion:*[^539]
O tri-hypostatic Light, true Creator and God, enlighten the darkness of our souls: for behold, we bring before Thee in prayer Thine pleased one, and with him we pray: calm the storm of our sins, and deliver us all from sorrow!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Brighter than the sun, O Mary Birthgiver of God: enlighten with grace all who bow down before thee and call upon thy name, and keep this monastery from every evil, and fill those who dwell in it with spiritual joy and gladness: for thou art the mediatrix of our joy!
*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^540]
Gathered together today with voices of rejoicing, O ye lovers of feasts, let us offer hymns of praise unto God, glorifying the all-blessed father, and falling down before the shrine of his relics, let us cry: Standing before the Master, forget not us who honor thy memory with love, but pray that we be delivered from all temptations and grievous encirclements!
Who can hymn thee worthily, O venerable father? For in this temporary life, though in a mortal body, yet thou didst live spiritually, being outside the world and the flesh, having an unceasing desire to be loosed from the flesh and to be with Christ;[^541] reigning now with Him, remember also us who glorify thee!
Rejoice, all-honorable choir of God-bearing Caves Fathers, for thou hast acquired a good luminary...

*(Right page — Folio 113 recto, running header: TO MOSES THE HUNGARIAN)*
...a good luminary, the crown of virgins and the beauty of martyrs, the pure dwelling of the Holy Spirit, Moses the spiritual God-seer; crowning whose memory with praises, we pray you: pray Christ God and the Master that we, your children, may be delivered from misfortunes, and not be deprived of the Heavenly Kingdom!
*Glory, Tone 6:*
As a true follower of Christ and a faithful hearer of His Gospel, we praise thee today, O venerable father: for looking unto eternal recompense, thou didst account all the fair things of this world as refuse, choosing rather to be a captive, that thou mightest keep thy mind free from sin; and having no care for the body, thou didst surrender thyself unto torments, that with streams of blood thou mightest drown the tormenting mental enemy; being thyself now in a place of rest and enjoying the joy of the saints, from on high enrich us also with thy gifts, who lovingly celebrate thy memory!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
*O Birthgiver of God, thou art the true vine...*
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*(Baroque tailpiece: An ornate foliate cartouche) (Left page — Folio 113 verso, full-page engraving of Venerable Pimen the Much-Ailing and service opening)*[^542]
*(Woodcut engraving: Venerable Pimen the Much-Ailing. Top inscription: "Venerable Pimen of the Caves" — the saint stands in monastic habit with outstretched hands against the landscape of the Kyiv Caves Monastery. Bottom compartment: "Angels tonsured the sick Pimen.")*
