# MONTH OF AUGUST

*On the 7th day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Pimen the Much-Ailing of the Kyiv Caves.*[^542]
*At Vespers, Blessed is the man: the 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чино́въ):*[^543]
He Who foreknew thee from thy mother's womb for blessedness, by His providence granted an example of salvation, permitting disease like unto Job's upon thee, that tried thereby as gold in the furnace, thy body might be weakened through much affliction;[^544] and now...

*(Right page — Folio 114 recto, running header: TO PIMEN THE MUCH-AILING)*
...and now being in painless rest, remember us who suffer with passions of the soul, O Pimen, our all-blessed father!
Hating the world, O venerable one, and a life dragging down through fleshly pleasures, thou didst ask of the Lord for thyself that it be exchanged for a disease of many years, in which giving thanks, thou didst cry out thanksgiving unto God, saying after the manner of the Psalmist: My soul hath cleaved after Thee, so let Thy right hand uphold me, O Christ, guiding unto the right way and life eternal![^545]
Unto thee, O Venerable Father Pimen, according to the multitude of painful temptations which thou didst most lovingly offer in body, consolations now gladden thy soul in unapproachable light: for standing with the angelic ranks before the Master's throne, pray there also for us, that from on high we may obtain peace and deliverance from passions!
*Glory, Tone 6:*
Come, ye choirs of monastics, let us praise Pimen the Venerable, who was like unto great Anthony: for even as he received the monastic habit from an angel, so also this one was accounted worthy to be clothed in the monastic state by an angelic hand;[^547] with whom now wearing robes of incorruption, he rejoices with the righteous, keeps festival with the venerable, and with them prays Christ God for our souls!
*Both now... Of the Feast.*[^546]
*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 82 on the reverse).*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 8, Idiomela [Samoglasny]:*
The candle given unto thee by the angels prefigured the brightness of thy soul, O venerable father; therefore thy life was undefiled, and through trial it shone before men; honoring which, we magnify and glorify the Heavenly Father with the Son and the Holy Spirit!
*Verse: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^548]
Beholding the desire of thy heart and thy love toward heavenly things, the supreme King sent His angels unto thee, O father, to clothe thee in the monastic habit, and to regale thee with heavenly singing, that sweetened by endurance of this diseased life, thou mightest crown its bitterness, and by diseases acquire painless rest!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord: he shall delight exceedingly in His commandments.*[^549]
Renunciation of the world, voluntary poverty, undefiled virginity, measureless endurance *(Left page — Folio 114 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...measureless endurance, and perfect love toward God, are truly great virtues, wherewith as upon an animate chariot thou didst ascend unto heavenly things, and didst stand before the Creator of all and God: pray Him also for us, O venerable one, that we may finish our course without offense, and obtain mercy in the day of judgment!
*Glory, Tone 8:*
Multitudes of monastics, we run unto the shrine of thy relics, O father; we bow down before thine incorrupt relics; we draw healing for our diseases; we receive strength against the enemies who war against us, and find victory over passions; we receive remission of sins, and are accounted worthy of the Lord's mercy through thy prayers!
*Both now... Of the Feast.*
*Troparion, Tone 5:*
Emulating the God-bearing Anthony in humble-mindedness, and being likened unto Job in endurance, thou didst desire for thyself not healing from disease, but rather its lengthening, O patient-spirited Pimen, lest having become healthy and being torn away from this place by thy parents, thou shouldst be deprived of the fellowship of the venerable and their order; therefore beyond all hope thou wast tonsured by the angels themselves; with them pray Christ God that our souls may be saved!
*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast.*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast. Glory, to the Venerable One. Both now, of the Feast.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^550]
Great endurance didst thou possess, O father: for lying upon a bed of sickness for many years, beset with corruption from head to foot, thou didst give thanks unto God, choosing in this life to rot entirely, that in the next thou mightest be without corruption, and enduring the smell of stench, that thou mightest be shown forth the sweet fragrance of Christ; therefore by Thy prayers make us also patient!
*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Afflictions like arrows have compassed me about, and I lie upon the bed of my deeds; but heal my infirmities, and raise me up unto virtues, making me whole through thy prayers!
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^551]
Speedily did God give ear unto thee as thou didst pray, O blessed one, and fulfilled the desire of thy heart, sending His angels to clothe thee in the angelic habit; therefore be thou also swift unto our help, and fulfill the desire of our heart, praying the angelic Master to clothe us from on high with His mercy!

*(Right page — Folio 115 recto, running header: TO PIMEN THE MUCH-AILING)*
*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Speedily give ear unto the supplications of thy servants, O Sovereign Lady, and anticipate to overshadow us with the shelter of thy wings, lest we be enslaved unto sin unto the end; but that we may fervently serve thy Son, our Lord, Who grants us great mercy!
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^552]
Thy many diseases, O blessed Pimen, became the cause of health for many: being sick thyself, thou didst heal the sick; ministering thyself in infirmity, thou didst raise up the infirm, by a word driving away every weakness; therefore running now unto thine incorrupt relics, we pray thee: pour forth health unto bodies, and grant healing unto souls, praying Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love!
*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast.*
*The Gradual Hymns, the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^553] *Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song. Let every breath praise the Lord. Common Gospel for a Venerable Monastic.*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron: Come, ye choirs of monastics... (Seek at "Lord, I have cried").*
*(Ornamental headpiece bar)*
*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the whole Pharaohite army; and the Word incarnate hath abolished all-evil sin: supremely glorified is the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified![^554]
*Troparia:*
Held fast by the deep of perplexity, I pray Thee, O Jesus, Deep of Wisdom: elevate my mind, and stir my tongue, to praise the great endurance of Thy pleased one, Pimen!
Having drowned passions in the deep of dispassion, O venerable one, thou didst cross the sea of this world with dry feet, so that not even thy mind touched the sinful mire; from which preserve me also through thy prayers!
*Glory:*
In the deep of thy tears thou didst drown the mental Pharaoh, and now at the heavenly haven thou singest the hymn of victory, where pray also for me, O venerable one!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Unto me who am drowning in the deep of evils, O Sovereign Lady, stretch forth the hand of help *(Left page — Folio 115 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...stretch forth the hand of help, as Thy Son did unto Peter, and guide me unto the haven of salvation!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The wilderness hath blossomed as a lily, O Lord, the barren church of the nations, by Thy coming, wherein my heart hath been established![^555]
*Troparia:*
The Caves Monastery hath blossomed with venerable fathers, even as an orchard with fragrant lilies; in the midst of whom thou also, O venerable one, didst blossom fair by thy stance in life, and after thy falling asleep by incorruption!
Thou didst blossom by thy God-pleasing life as a full flower, O venerable one; and now thy holy relics in the cave emit sweet fragrance as a lily of the valley, and pour forth grace unto those who run with faith!
*Glory:*
A lily among thorns, O Pimen, blossoming in purity through the endurance of diseases, which being transplanted into the gardens of the enemy, now wafts fragrance upon us through his prayers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O sweet-scented Flower, all-pure Virgin: drive away the stench of my sin, and show me forth a sweet fragrance of Christ through thy prayers!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^556]
Radiant was thy prayer unto God, O holy one, when the Caves fathers were praying for thy healing, while thou didst ask the Lord for the increase of diseases for thyself, and the supplication of thee alone prevailed over the prayers of all;[^557] we know therefore that even now thy holy prayers can do much!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
A Mother's prayer can do much toward winning the Master's favor; pray Him therefore for us, O Virgin Mother, and make known our sorrow unto Him, that giving ear unto thee, He may be merciful unto us, and having turned sorrow into joy, grant life eternal!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I hymn Thee, O Lord, for I heard Thy report and was afraid: for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who am gone astray; therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Plenteous in mercy![^558]
*Troparia:*
Grant us by thy prayers, O venerable father, to sing and glorify God worthily, that we may ever call thee blessed!
The fathers who were near heard angelic singings at midnight, when they who entered clothed thee with salvation; account us also worthy to enjoy angelic songs through thine intercession, O blessed one!

*(Right page — Folio 116 recto, running header: TO PIMEN THE MUCH-AILING)*
...account us also worthy to enjoy angelic songs through thine intercession, O blessed one!
*Glory:*
Fervently I sing unto thee, O father; I bless thy singing, I magnify thy guilelessness and meekness, I praise thine endurance, I marvel at thine incorruption, and I bow down before thy healing relics!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save thy hymnographers from eternal weeping, delivering them, O all-hymned Mother: with thy veil wipe away every tear from mine eyes, and account me worthy of eternal joy!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Having become a mediator between God and men, O Christ God: for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto the Father, the Author of Light![^559]
*Troparia:*
Through thine intercession, O holy one, preserve from every assault of the enemy the monastery in which thou didst live in a God-pleasing manner, and be a helper unto monastics, for in thy prayers do we hope!
Be an intercessor for me unto the righteous Judge, O blessed one: for I have been negligent in this, even as that wretched monk whom thou didst show unto the brethren at thy falling asleep, who had the great schema, but no works at all; therefore his incorrupt body is reserved for his exposure and condemnation at the judgment![^560]
*Glory:*
Intercede for salvation for me who am despairing, O blessed one; guide a sinner unto repentance, O venerable one; grant amendment unto the perverse, O father, that I may not be put to shame, trusting in thy prayers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mediatrix before the Creator, all-pure Virgin: cleanse me who am all defiled with sins through thy prayers, for placing all my hope in thee, I shall not be put to shame forever!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the fathomless abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^561]
*Troparia:*
The whole world lies in the abyss of evils; therefore Thou didst renounce it, that in God Thou mightest please God: account me also worthy to please Him, O pleased one of God!
Lying in the abyss of heavy diseases, neglected and forsaken many times by those who served thee, thou hadst God alone visiting thee; forsake not us, O father, through the visitation of thy prayers, as we are ever sick *(Left page — Folio 116 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
*Glory, Tone 2:*
That I may not be condemned unto the abyss of hades by reason of the multitude of my sins, stand thou before the righteous Judge, O father, and entreat Him to deliver me from His righteous judgment!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
As thou hast borne the abyss of mercy, O Mother of compassion, snatch me away from the abyss of mine immeasurable iniquities, who pray unto thee!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^562]
Come, having assembled together, let us praise Pimen the much-enduring, who thankfully endured long-continued disease; for he who was once loathed in his disease on account of the stench is now filled with sweet fragrance for those who approach with faith unto the reliquary of his relics, and drives away the foul stench of passions from those who cry: Come unto us, O Pimen, and the enemy shall in no wise prevail against us!

*Ikos:*
Ye that are held fast by diverse diseases and passions, having assembled unto Pimen the much-ailing, marvel at his patience; for having remained many times hungry and thirsty, he sorrowed not against those that served him, but with joy endured all things, giving thanks unto God; having spent twenty years in his suffering, he was tried as gold in the furnace,[^563] and was found worthy unto God, and received from Him the gift to heal infirmities and to drive away passions from those that run unto him with faith; therefore let us also run unto him and call upon him, crying: Come unto us, O Pimen, and the enemy shall in no wise prevail against us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The contrary commandment of the lawless tormentor raised high the flame; but Christ spread spiritual dew over the pious youths, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.[^564]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst hate the God-opposing world, O father, knowing Him Who says: Whosoever will be a friend of the world is made the enemy of God;[^565] grant therefore unto us also to flee from the world and all its lusts!

The contrary law of mine evil custom draws me by force unto evil deeds, and not what I will, that do I, but what I will not, that I perform;[^566] do thou therefore, O venerable one, free me from that evil by thy prayers!

*Glory:*
The contrary commandment of reigning sin in my members...

*(Right page — Folio 117 recto, running header: TO PIMEN THE MUCH-AILING)*
...in my members forces me often to anger God;[^567] unto thee do I flee, O father, and deliver me from grievous sins!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Virgin who hast borne God, grant us to fulfill the commandments of thy Son, and that in nothing we may be found contrary unto Him through thy help!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The fiery furnace in Babylon once divided its action by divine command, burning the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful as they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^568]

*Troparia:*
That thou mightest extinguish the furnace of passions, O father, thou didst ask an increase of diseases from God, wherein bodily and spiritual purity was refreshment unto thee, O father; grant that we also may preserve it through thy prayers, that with thee we may sing unto all-pure God: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Though the furnace of many years' diseases scorched thy body, yet could it in no wise harm the modesty of thy soul; for in all these things that befell thee thou didst not sin, but abiding in the midst of temptations unconsumed as in the midst of the flame, thou didst sing with the youths: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
As in a furnace in my flesh, I have kindled the fire of lusts, and have increased the flame of uncleanness; O God, do Thou cool the flame, extinguish the fire of evil thought, and bedew the furnace of my passions through the prayers of Thy servant Pimen, that with him I may sing unto Thee, God glorified in Trinity: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The fiery furnace once not burning the youths prefigured thy childbirth, O all-pure one: for having conceived the Divine Fire, thou didst remain unburnt; and I therefore pray thee, O Sovereign Lady: preserve me unburnt from the fire of the adversary, that rejoicing I may sing unto thee: Sing, all ye works of the Lord, unto the Lord, and supremely exalt, glorifying His all-holy name unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the Unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us to enlighten the darkened, to gather the dispersed; therefore do we magnify the all-hymned Theotokos.[^569]

*Troparia:*
O Unoriginate Begetter, we bring unto Thee in prayer Thy servant Pimen, who from the beginning of his monastic life loved Thee with his whole soul *(Left page — Folio 117 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...loved Thee with his whole soul, and with all his strength served Thee: look upon his struggles, and for his sake give ear unto his supplication for us, and grant us a good end!

O Co-unoriginate Son and Holy Spirit, we present unto Thee in prayer Thy good and faithful servant Pimen: look upon his many-yeared and grievous diseases, and for his sake give ear unto his supplication for us, and be merciful unto us!

*Glory:*
O Holy Spirit, co-ever-existing with the Father and the Son, we offer unto Thee the fervent prayers of blessed Pimen; give ear unto him, and for his sake have mercy upon us!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, with blessed Pimen and with all the venerable Caves Fathers who pleased God, entreat the All-Holy and Undivided Trinity to have mercy upon Her servants who glorify thee in hymns!

*Exapostilarion:*[^570]
The bridal chamber of the Savior is opened unto Thee, O Venerable Pimen; for thou hast the wedding garment of the angelic habit placed upon Thee by angels, wherewith having entered into the marriage feast of the Lamb, forget not us who honor Thee!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O undefiled bridal chamber, all-pure Virgin, cover us who have not the wedding garment with thy precious robe, and lead us into the heavenly bridal chamber!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^571]
O most marvelous wonder! O miraculous operation of the Holy Spirit upon thee, which operated in thy frail flesh, O venerable one! For while ailing thyself, thou didst heal others, and by thy patience didst lead them unto amendment; therefore unto those who praise thee, O father, show thy mercy, and entreat of Christ God healing in our infirmities, who with love honor thy holy falling asleep!

O God-wise Pimen, from childhood thou didst surrender thyself unto diseases through prayer, and by bitter sufferings didst wear away thy body, that thou mightest preserve the purity of the flesh and present a spotless soul unto God, before Whom standing in the painless life...

*(Right page — Folio 118 recto, running header: TO PIMEN THE MUCH-AILING)*
...before Whom standing in the painless life, as one powerful to assist by thy prayer, pray ever for us who sing thy holy falling asleep with faith!

O Venerable Pimen, let everyone who suffers in this much-troubled life learn to emulate thy patience, and to send up thanksgivings unto God, from Whom we receive chastening as trial for the correction of spiritual diseases; but be an intercessor for all who run unto the reliquary of thy relics, and ask from Christ God deliverance for spiritual and bodily infirmities!

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Truly mystical myrrh for the healing of the sick does the shrine of thy holy relics pour forth, and it breathes sweet fragrance upon those who approach with faith, granting them rest in necessities; grant therefore unto us also, O all-blessed father, deliverance from spiritual and bodily diseases from the ever-flowing fountain of thy love, as in songful beauties we praise thy precious falling asleep!

*Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Deliver thy servants from misfortunes...

*The Great Doxology and the Dismissal.*
*(Ornamental tailpiece — Baroque basket of flowers) (Left page — Folio 118 verso, woodcut engraving of Venerable Theodore and Basil of the Caves and service opening)*[^572]
*(Woodcut engraving: Venerable Theodore and Basil of the Caves. Above: Icons of "Venerable Basil" and "Venerable Theodore" standing with halos in monastic vestments. Below: Two narrative panels: "Demons war against Basil, who is wronged by Theodore" and "Demons cultivate a garden in the guise of an angel.")*

*Month of August, on the 11th day.*
*Service to our Venerable Fathers Theodore and Basil of the Caves.*[^573]
*At Vespers, Blessed is the man: the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6.*
*To Venerable Theodore, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^574]
Thou didst gather all corruptible treasure, and following the utterance of Christ, O our Father Theodore, thou didst most blessedly distribute it unto the needy, and by their hands didst convey it into an unstealable place;[^575] whence enriched with the light of grace...

*(Right page — Folio 119 recto, running header: TO THEODORE AND BASIL OF THE CAVES)*
...whence enriched with the light of grace, thou didst shine forth in the world as the sun; and now standing with boldness before the Sovereign Lady, enlighten those who sing thy memory with the rays of thy radiant gifts!

Desiring to receive spiritual riches, thou didst neglect sensible wealth; having surrendered it into the hands of the poor, thou didst choose in poverty to follow Christ Who became poor for our sake;[^576] and taking up His cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst walk the narrow path, and after a blessed end didst inherit spacious places, where dwelling with the angels, pray for those who praise thee, O all-blessed Theodore!

Having left all the fair things of this world, thou didst gladly accept the monastic habit, and having struggled well therein, thou didst settle in a dark cave, where by much abstinence and labors thou didst subdue thy flesh unto the spirit, and having wounded the adversary who was warring against thee, thou didst drive him away with the precious weapon; and now living in the unwaning light, remember also us who celebrate thy precious falling asleep!

*Other Stichera, to Venerable Basil, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^577]
Fulfilling the law that is in the Scripture, O Venerable Basil, thou didst acquire love toward God, and didst love thy neighbor as thyself;[^578] and him thou didst establish, O blessed one, not to be shaken by the invisible enemy, against whom Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls, granted thee strength.

Having in thy mouth a word seasoned with the salt of grace, O all-blessed one,[^579] thou didst restore in virtue a heart rotted through money-loving; thou didst discern the wiles of the serpent that was hiding, and didst drive him far away in the name of Christ, before Whom the opposing powers tremble!

Bound by the bond of firm love, O all-blessed fathers, ye were found even in this life as a strong fortress, which the deceitful angels that serve Belial were not able to undermine; and being put to flight, wounded they fell; but ye sang in praise: Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls, is our power and strength!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
O all-blessed fathers, good warriors of Christ! Taking up the shield of unhesitating faith by the utterance of unceasing prayer, and being girded with hope in God, ye conquered the demonic regiments *(Left page — Folio 119 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...regiments, and having cleansed your robes in the blood of your martyrdoms, ye entered into the heavenly bridal chamber;[^580] whence forget not us, but show compassion upon us, asking for us from Christ God peace and great mercy!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*

*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 81 on the reverse).*[^581]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^582]
When the grace of the All-Holy Spirit kindled thy heart with the fire of warm love toward God, O venerable one, then despising all the fair things of this world and following the blessed footsteps, thou didst choose to walk the narrow path unto this life, bearing the cross of Christ upon thy shoulder, and through long-suffering didst enter into spacious places; whence forget not us who call thy precious memory blessed, O Basil!

*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^583]
Fleeing the rumor and turmoil that captivates the human mind in temptation, having cast away all things, O all-blessed one, thou didst depart unto a quiet place, settling in a dark cave, and there didst make it as a certain most beautiful paradise; and having lived therein in many labors, thou didst overcome invisible enemies and wast shown forth a companion of angels; and now standing with them before the Master Christ, pray also for us, O Holy Theodore, that we may be delivered from every rumor and misfortune!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^584]
Beholding a ladder established from the holy Caves Mount unto heaven, most sacred fathers, whereon the Mother of God placed Her dwelling, and angelic choirs with the saints ascended thereby unto heaven,[^585] ye followed their steps as though bodiless, and having ascended through vigilance and deed, ye stand now before the Holy Trinity; where remember also us who bow down before your relics, O all-blessed ones!

*Glory, Tone 4:*
Having shone forth in the world through many struggles, O holy martyrs, marvelous Theodore with valiant-minded Basil! Ye were clothed with an all-embroidered robe, and live now in the ever-existing light; whence looking down upon earthly things...

*(Right page — Folio 120 recto, running header: TO THEODORE AND BASIL OF THE CAVES)*
...looking down upon earthly things, enlighten with the rays of your radiant gifts us who are in distress in the darkness of this life, and ask great mercy from Christ God!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^586]
Bound by the bond of love, O venerable ones, ye trampled down every bond of the wiles of the enemy, and valiantly endured innocent suffering and death from the money-loving prince;[^587] therefore we pray you who lived together and received the crowns of martyrdom: pray unto the Lord for us, that having lived well in abundant love, faith, and hope, we may ever call you blessed, O Theodore and Basil, triumphant in good!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast.*
*Glory: To the Venerable Ones.*
*Both now: Of the Feast.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^588]
Come, ye people, let us honor in hymns the victorious pair of all-blessed fathers: for having been greatly tried, they appeared as pure gold in the world, and now standing before the throne of the Master, they assist those who are in distress in many misfortunes, that they may receive deliverance from all temptations!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O swift helper of all the world and quiet harbor of the storm-tossed! Bring us who are tossed by the waves of voluntary passions into the sorrowless stillness of thy goodness, and deliver from misfortunes those who sing unto thee: Rejoice, the joy and salvation of all!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou wast shown forth" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^589]
Having labored in many sweats, and having received innocent martyrdom, O God-wise fathers, ye passed over into the place of rest, where rejoicing with the angels, pray the all-bountiful Master to grant protection from enemies also unto us who suffer; for we are your children, and the spiritual sheep of your teachings!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Appear unto us at this hour, O all-pure Virgin, and having received the supplications offered unto thee, carry them unto thy Son Whom thou didst bear in maternal arms, asking remission of sins for us; for we are thy servants *(Left page — Folio 120 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...for we are thy servants, having thee as a swift protector and intercessor for our souls!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*[^590]
O locked garden of the Mother of God, sprout forth for us two unfading flowers, the martyrs, through whom monastics are filled with joy, and the marvelous monastery boasts; the people honor them with praises, receiving healing from their relics, and having them as fervent intercessors unto the Lord!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O warm protectress of those in afflictions, and salvation of sinners, all-pure Virgin! Despise not our tears and sighing, but look upon the goodness of thy love of mankind, and save thy servants from all demonic assaults and circumstances!

*Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, composite pericope 38:*
*The Lord said unto His disciples: Every one that shall confess Me before men...*[^591]

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron from the heirs of Vespers, Tone 6: O all-blessed ones...*[^592]

*(Ornamental headpiece band)*
*Canon to the Venerable Ones, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the untrodden, unaccustomed, unwetted path of the sea, chosen Israel cried out: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!*[^593]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to walk the untrodden path of fasting, glorious Theodore, having evangellically despised the deceit of this world and distributed wealth unto the poor, acquired the one pearl, Christ,[^594] unto Whom he also sings: For He is gloriously glorified!

Seeing, O blessed one, that the world lies in wickedness,[^595] thou didst not desire to look upon the corruption thereof; therefore thou didst settle in a cave, remaining many years, attending unto abstinence and labors, looking unto God alone!

Envying thee, the all-crafty enemy through repentance for distributed possessions cast thee into despair, O venerable one, darkening thee for many days; but through confession thou didst again arise, and didst put to shame him that sought to put thee to shame!

*Glory:*
As a perfect monk possessing perfect poverty, thou didst comfort Theodore, O Venerable...

*(Right page — Folio 121 recto, running header: TO THEODORE AND BASIL OF THE CAVES)*
...O Venerable Basil, that he be not corrupted, seeing that the fashion of this world passes away,[^596] but that he look upon heavenly things, seeing that they abide unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Snatch me away, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand that drags me unto corruption, dispose me to look upon incorruptible things, and direct our life, as thou art the swift helper and correction of men!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty is broken by Thy dominion, O Christ, and the weak are girded with strength.*[^597]

*Troparia:*
The bow of Belial stretched against thee was broken, O father; for taking as a sword the sound of the Jesus Prayer, thou didst cut him down,[^598] and he who formerly was sick, being darkened with money-loving, was afterwards girded with divine strength, and became a wound unto the enemy!

Thou didst hear, O venerable one, that he who repented once of distributed alms lost both gold and life; thou didst weep bitterly, and having thereby escaped from the money-loving sickness, thou didst cry aloud: I glorify Thine unsearchable mercy, O God!

As brothers of one womb, Basil and Theodore, ye loved one another, and by striving in the commandments of the Lord ye put Belial to shame, who hates the good; and establish me also in the standing of those commandments, that together with you I may glorify the unsearchable mercy of God!

When thou hadst departed unto obedience, O Venerable Basil, the enemy found time, and having transformed himself into thy likeness, cast again into Theodore the love of distributed possessions, because he remembered not the Jesus Prayer in unprofitable conversation![^599]

*Glory:*
Let him that thinks he stands take heed lest he fall,[^600] let everyone beware, even as it was manifested upon blessed Theodore: for he, thinking he had driven away the adversary, received him again, and his last state would have been worse than the first,[^601] had not the unsearchable goodness of God saved him from these things!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Being insensible, I thought that I was standing in virtues, not knowing that I had fallen; but do thou, O swift helper, raise me up, all-pure Virgin, and teach me diligently to do the commandments of God, that I may glorify thine ineffable mercy, O Mother of God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^602]
Thou wast wounded by the enemy, the author of evil, O father, having conversed superfluously with him concerning unprofitable things, not remembering *(Left page — Folio 121 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...not remembering that evil communications corrupt good manners;[^603] but all this was permitted by God, that Thy trial might appear purer than gold,[^604] and procure for thee a greater crown; which having received at the last, pray Christ God that we also who honor Thy holy memory with love may not be deprived thereof!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
The arrows of the crafty one steeped in the burning of the flesh have wounded my heart, and I lie as dead, despairing of life; apply unto me the plaster of thy healing, O all-pure Virgin, as thou art the healer of the whole Christian race!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, of Thy glorious dispensation, and I have glorified Thine unsearchable power, O Lover of mankind.*[^605]

*Troparia:*
When the all-destroyer heard that in conversation thou didst not remember the name of the Lord Jesus, O Theodore, he took boldness, and advised thee to ask from God that a multitude of gold and silver be given unto thee on account of thy distributed wealth; but thou didst not remember that they that will be rich fall into temptation and an insatiable snare![^606]

Accepting all things advised as from God, O venerable one, Thou didst behold the demon in sleep as an angel in great brightness,[^607] showing thee a treasure in the cave; which having dug a little and found, Thou didst almost lose the pearl of great price, Christ; by Whose grace, having fallen, Thou didst arise again!

Seeking to make the root of money-loving sprout within thee unto eternal perdition, the devil, through secretly suggested thoughts and visibly by appearance in the form of Basil, advised thee to flee unto foreign lands with the found gold and acquire villages for thy need, that having thus departed far from this holy place, O venerable one, thou mightest fall away also from the grace of God![^608]

*Glory:*
Frightening thee with the prolongation of life and the onset of old age, the enemy draws thee unto love of possessions, O Theodore, seeking to take away memory from thee, that thou mightest not remember Him Who said: Take no thought for the morrow, for God takes thought for you![^609]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The crafty one took away memory from me, that I should not remember Him Who said: Take no thought for your soul;[^610] and thus he leads me into a soul-destroying land, that having there become destitute of the grace of God, I may perish; but O pure Virgin, permit me not to fall into this final destruction!

*(Right page — Folio 122 recto, running header: TO THEODORE AND BASIL OF THE CAVES)*
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*The coal shown forth unto Isaiah, the Sun from the virginal womb, hath shone upon those that wandered in darkness, granting the illumination of the knowledge of God.*[^611]

*Troparia:*
Still having a coal burning thy conscience with shame, O blessed one, thou didst make a good promise, saying: I have promised to end my life here in the cave; how then shall I now become a fugitive? But having lived here, I will do all things commanded by thee!

Again the adversary suggests that the discovered treasure will become known, and threatens with the confiscation thereof, whereby thou mightest build up these places; having agreed with these counsels, thou wast preparing thyself for flight, had not the right hand of the God-wise ones, rather than their prayers, restrained thee, O Theodore![^612]

Thou didst recognize blessed Basil as a true obedient disciple and possessor of non-covetousness, O Theodore, when he rebuked thee as one blinded by demonic deception; but he contended not with anger, but rather prayed God to deliver thee from the snares of Satan!

*Glory:*
That thou mightest be justified before many witnesses, O Basil, thou didst come with the brethren unto the deceived one, assuring him that not with thee, but with a demon in thy likeness yesterday and before that, and speaking of crafty snares, he had conversed![^613]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The many-wiled enemy, seeking to ensnare me, transforms himself into many diverse forms, and operates in my thought, that I may depart from thy protection, O all-pure Mother; but do thou, O Sovereign Lady, not permit such temptation upon me!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Having heard the voice of suppliant words from an ailing soul, O Master, deliver me from grievous evils: for Thou alone art the Author of our salvation.*[^614]

*Troparia:*
When the deceiver heard the voice of the words of the Jesus Prayer, he dared not approach thee, O Theodore, but being struck down, fled from thy cave; and thou, being strengthened by the power of that prayer, didst not open the doors unto anyone that came unto thee until he confessed that the Lord Jesus Christ is the Author of our salvation![^615]

Being delivered from so great a pit of destruction, O Theodore, thou didst dig a deep pit, and cast into it the buried treasure, covering over also the memory of love of possessions, saying within thyself: There is nothing so hurtful unto monastics *(Left page — Folio 122 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...saying within thyself: There is nothing so hurtful unto monastics as love of possessions!

Wearing thyself out with many labors, O Theodore, that being idle thou mightest not fall into fearlessness and negligence, thou didst set up millstones, and grinding wheat for the brethren, thou hadst the unceasing Jesus Prayer in thy mouth, and thus wast adorned against the invisible enemy!

*Glory:*
When the adversary sought to frighten thee, O blessed one, he made a sound as of thunder, and ground at the millstones; but thou, understanding his craft, didst forbid him, and he was as a captive, obeying and grinding, until he ground five cartloads of wheat in a single night![^616]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
When I am in negligence, the enemy ever captivates me and prompts me unto unprofitable deeds; but do thou, O Mother of my God, drive him away, and teach me to subdue the flesh unto the spirit, and to serve my God in holiness and righteousness!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^617]
Thou wast shown forth a good counselor unto blessed Theodore, O God-wise Basil, having delivered him by thy counsel from the deception of the devil, and guided him unto the light of the knowledge of God; therefore ye afterwards received a blessed end, having been unjustly shot with an arrow into the belly by the money-loving prince; and now standing before the Lord, pray unceasingly for us all!

*Ikos:*
By the multitude of your virtues, O venerable ones, ye were filled as a fair garden;[^618] and as a shoot ye blossomed forth by fasting and prayers, giving sweet fragrance unto all by the scent of your many labors and struggles, and ascetic sweats, wherein ye passed over favorably unto the sorrowless and eternal life; and ye were adorned with crowns of victory: pray unceasingly for us all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Serving not the golden image on the plain of Dura, the three youths, inspired by godly decree, disregarded the all-wise command; but cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*[^619]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to fast in secret, the blessed fathers revealed their thoughts one to another, and both discerned whether their thoughts were pleasing unto God; emulating them, O brethren, try your thoughts by fraternal discernment, which of them be from God!

Emulating the struggles and labors of Venerable Theodore...

*(Right page — Folio 123 recto, running header: TO THEODORE AND BASIL OF THE CAVES)*
...let us not give our labor unto others, but even as he carried timbers unto his cell himself, so let us also labor for ourselves, if we desire that the enemy shoot not at us through love of idleness!

The demons hating the blessed one did him spite; for as much as he with great labor carried timbers up the mountain, so much did they cast them down again from the mountain; but having wearied the elder with labor, they themselves took up the labors and torment![^620]

A braided cord is unceasing prayer and purity of heart, wherewith blessed Theodore bound the evil spirits; and they were unto him as captives, submitting and carrying timbers for the building of the divine monastery, which had burned down before!

*Glory:*
Tormented and distressed with many labors, the crafty demons cried out against the blessed ones, boasting and saying: Fierce adversaries unto us are Theodore and Basil! With them we will not cease to war, until we deliver them unto death; not knowing that they would become the authors of a most honorable crown unto them!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Beholding me strengthened by thee, O Sovereign Lady, the demons gnash their teeth, and boast unceasingly to war with me until they overcome me; but do thou, my protectress, surrender me not into their hands, but deliver me from their wiles!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*In the golden image disregarding the command, the thrice-blessed youths, beholding the immutable and living Image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all creation sing, and supremely exalt the subsisting Lord unto all ages!*[^621]

*Troparia:*
By the likeness of all their evil craft the demons began to sharpen evil men, that they might destroy thee, O Theodore; therefore having burned with much reproach, the deceivers accused thee unto the judge; but thou didst not heed these things, saying: Let all creation sing the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

In the likeness of blessed Basil, the demon came again unto a certain inhuman boyar, slandering Theodore concerning the discovered treasure, wherewith, he said, he sought to flee unto another country;[^622] but that impious one knew not that the saints possess one treasure, Christ, Whom they supremely exalt with praises unto all ages!

In the likeness of Basil, the impious boyar brought the demon unto the prince, who also lied unto him concerning the treasure against thee, O holy one, and advised him *(Left page — Folio 123 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...and advised him to take you and by many torments extract the gold from thee; but thou, O blessed one, fearing nothing, didst fight valiantly against the adversary who is separated from the Lord unto the ages!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
On the morrow the prince, going himself as unto a hunt or against some mighty warrior, captured thee, O blessed one, and bringing thee into his house, first by flatteries sought to extract the treasure from thee; unto whom thou didst answer thus: I found the treasure, and saw a multitude of golden vessels, which I buried again, because it was unprofitable unto me, and I prayed the Lord that I might forget it unto the ages![^623]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I have buried the talent entrusted unto me as a slothful servant,[^624] and in no wise remember that I must give an answer concerning the gain thereof; therefore I pray thee, O Sovereign Lady, I pray thee, all-spotless Virgin: help me to make the talent acceptable, whereby I may find my Lord merciful unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thou art all desire, Thou art all sweetness, O Word of God, Son of the Virgin, God of gods, holy and all-holy Lord! Therefore do we all magnify Thee with Her that bore Thee.*[^625]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to be loosed from the bonds of the flesh and to be with Christ, thou wast bound innocently by the prince, and being tortured valiantly, thou didst wear out the torturing enemy, O most blessed Theodore![^626]

Streams of blood flowed from thy flesh, and stained the hairshirt, wherein clothed as in a most precious purple robe, Thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal chamber, where with Her that bore God Thou magnifiest the Trinity!

Suspended and suffocated with smoke, and then severely scorched with fire wast thou, O blessed one; but the right hand of God preserved thee whole, so that the fire touched not even thy hairshirt; and standing in the flame as in a cool place, thou didst glorify Christ with Her that bore Him!

Believing the demonic deception, the prince dared to seize thee, O Basil, who didst reign over passions, and seeking to have an accomplice against Theodore, questioned thee concerning the hidden gold; but thou didst unhesitatingly rebuke him and those that stood by, as being deceived by the demon!

*Glory:*
Being heated with wine, and not enduring thy rebuke, the senseless prince wounded thee with an arrow from his bow;[^627] but thou, O blessed one, didst prophesy unto him, saying that he himself should be wounded with that same arrow; and thy word was fulfilled, O blessed one, speedily!

*(Right page — Folio 124 recto, running header: TO THEODORE AND BASIL OF THE CAVES)*
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Our enemies have raised their bow, and compassed us about, shooting arrows of sin against us; but do thou, O Sovereign Lady, being a strong city, an impregnable wall, and a mighty shield, protect and preserve those who magnify thee with thy Son!

*Exapostilarion:*[^628]
Cast into prison, O blessed fathers, after many torments your souls departed from the bonds of the flesh, and He brought you into the ever-existing light, to confess His holy name, where rejoicing with the angelic choirs, remember also us who honor your yearly memory!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Being ensnared by sin-loving nets of the adversary, and cast into the prison of blindness of conscience, I see not the sun of the commandments of my Lord; but unto thee, O pure one, do I cry: Make the sun of repentance shine upon me, and warm my frozen heart with the rays of thy goodness, that I may ever glorify thy swift help!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Idiomela, Tone 4:*[^629]
Who can worthily praise your labors and struggles, O most blessed fathers? For in bodily frames ye lived as though bodiless, and having winged your minds unto God, ye flew up unto the heavens; and standing now before the throne of the Most High, fervently pray to give peace unto the world and save our souls!

Who will not marvel at your patience, O holy ones? For being falsely slandered by the enemy, ye endured many torments from the money-loving prince, and having endured unto the end, ye invisibly conquered him that warred against you, and received the crown of victorious honor from the hand of the Lord: pray that we also may receive this from Him!

Blessed is the earth that drank your blood, O good confessors of Christ; blessed is the cave that preserves your relics as a treasure of great price, not under a bushel, but openly lying in the tomb,[^630] wherein united in body, and standing in soul with the angelic choirs, ye drive away evil spirits upon the earth; from which preserve also us, and pray Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*(Left page — Folio 124 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
*Glory, Tone 3:*[^631]
Come, ye brethren, let us praise our most blessed fathers, Basil with marvelous Theodore! For having first renounced every attachment and striving to attain dispassion, they took no care for this life, and chose to endure death for the sake of their Lord from the money-loving prince; and having appeared as victors, they put to shame the warrior and adversary enemy, and are crowned now with glory and honor from the Heavenly King; Whom also they pray unceasingly to grant peace unto the world and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now... Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O blessed Virgin, the hope of the world, forget not us who sing unto thee, but pray ever for thine unprofitable servants, that we may be delivered from all misfortunes and circumstances!

*The Great Doxology and the Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the common service for venerable monastic martyrs.*[^632]

*(Large tailpiece engraving — Church within rococo foliage cartouche)*

*(Right page — Folio 125 recto, woodcut engraving of Venerable Theodosius of the Caves and service opening)*[^633]

Middle: "Before his death he instructs the brethren" and "His ascetic labor of spinning flax." Below: "Carrying of the body and burial of our Venerable Father Theodosius of the Kyiv Caves.")*

*(Ornamental divider band)*
*Month of August, on the 14th day.*
*Service for the Translation of the Precious Relics of our Venerable Father Theodosius, Abbot of the Kyiv Caves.*[^634]
*At Vespers, we sing Blessed is the man: the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6: of the Forefeast, 3; and of the Venerable One, 3.*
*Of the Venerable One, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^635]
Thou didst take up the struggle of the Lord for Thy salvation, and as an ascetic thou didst depart far from us *(Left page — Folio 125 verso, running header: SERVICE FOR THE TRANSLATION OF RELICS)*
...invisibly, O venerable one, when the persecutor appeared; and having received gold sent unto thee by God,[^636] thou wast a bountiful giver of alms unto the needy; thou didst fill the empty vessels of the granaries, a most ready deliverer for those in want, and a preacher of the faith of Christ our God, Whom pray Thou to save and enlighten our souls!

Beholding the great manifestation of thy precious relics as having an angelic brightness, discerning it from afar, the wonder-worthy Stephen ran swiftly in fear and joy;[^637] and he saw thy limbs whole, and abiding incorrupt, glorifying thee unto Christ our God, Whom pray thou to save and enlighten our souls!

When they came into thy cave, O venerable one, desiring to translate thy body, then having opened the tomb rejoicing, they saw thy relics lying venerable, all the limbs whole and unpartaken of corruption, and the hair of the head adhered unto the head, and the face with brightness, the eyes and the lips joined in godly comeliness, glorifying thee unto Christ our God,[^638] Whom pray thou to save and enlighten our souls!

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^639]
To celebrate today the feast of the translation of thy precious relics, O our Venerable Father Theodosius, thou summonest all to keep festival with faith, saying in thy memory: Rejoice, all-sacred dwelling of the Divine Spirit and pillar of piety! Rejoice, thou who didst multiply the talent given thee by God, boast of the inspired! Rejoice, all-radiant lamp of the world and our consolation! And standing now before Christ's throne, pray that we who faithfully celebrate thy holy memory may be delivered from all wrath and sorrow, and obtain future glory!

*Both now... Of the Forefeast: Thine all-honorable falling asleep... (seek in the Menaion).*[^640]

*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 100 on the reverse).*[^641]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Forefeast (seek in the Menaion).*

*Glory: Of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*[^642]
We honor thee as the instructor of a multitude of monastics, O our Father Theodosius; for by thy path have we truly learned to walk aright; blessed art thou who hast labored for Christ, and hast exposed the power of the enemy, companion of angels, partaker with the venerable and the righteous; with them pray unto the Lord, that our souls may have mercy!

*Both now... Of the Forefeast, Tone 4: Who is partaker of the things on high... (seek in the Menaion).*

*Troparion of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*[^643]
O instructor of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, light of the universe, God-inspired adornment of hierarchs, O all-wise Theodosius! By thy teachings thou hast enlightened all, O spiritual flute: pray Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*(Right page — Folio 126 recto, running header: OF VENERABLE THEODOSIUS)*
*Glory... Both now... Of the Forefeast: O ye people, dance beforehand with your hands... (seek in the Menaion).*
*(Ornamental divider bar)*

*At Matins, Troparion of the Forefeast and of the Saint.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^644]
The tomb where thou wast laid in thy church, O venerable one, fills with blessings those who approach with faith, and thy memory gladdens the hearts of the faithful; therefore standing together, O father, remember us who celebrate thy memory with faith, in this light-bearing and all-sacred solemnity! (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The Light of thy blessings and the Creator, O all-spotless and pure one, thou didst conceive in thy womb by the Divine Spirit; Whom glorifying, we hymn thee, O Virgin, as the palace of the King of all, and the protection of the world!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^645]
The Church of Christ rejoices, beholding the sacred one as upon a lampstand; and having the shrine of thy relics as a light-bearing lamp and a divine treasure, She summons all to glorify the Lord in thy memory: Whom pray thou that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Forefeast, Tone 3, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
*Shown forth more spacious than the heavens... (seek in the Menaion).*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^646]
Today angelic manifestations were seen, O venerable one, in the finding of thy relics: for a cloud as pillars of fire and as radiant rainbows stood over the cave, and thence came upon the top of the holy church, unto the place of thy tomb prepared for the laying of thy holy body, O all-blessed Theodosius, where they greatly glorify thee; therefore do we cry unto thee: Pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
*Multitudes of angels offer doxology... (seek in the Menaion).*

*Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel of the Venerable One.*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*[^647]
O Venerable Father Theodosius, into all the earth has gone out the sound of thy virtuous corrections; therefore in the heavens *(Left page — Folio 126 verso, running header: SERVICE FOR THE TRANSLATION OF RELICS)*
...in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the demonic hosts and didst attain unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate; having boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for our souls![^648]

*Canon of the Forefeast, with the Heirmos on 6 (seek in the Menaion).*

*And of the Venerable Theodosius, on 8, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing Her marvels.*[^649]

*Troparia:*
As I praise the memory of the translation of thy precious relics, O most blessed father, pray, O Theodosius, that illumination from heaven be granted unto me, and forgiveness of soul-corrupting transgressions!

Desiring to follow the Lord, O blessed one, thou didst come unto Venerable Anthony; and having shut thyself up with him in the cave apart from the world, thou didst remain unseparated from God.[^650]

Having emulated the great Theodosius, the founder of the cenobitic life, in character and life, O venerable one, following his struggles and labors, thou dost give thanks for the grace of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Cleanse my defiled heart, O Sovereign Lady, who didst bear the Most Holy Word, thou who alone art holier than all the highest powers, alone all-hymned!

*Katavasia: Adorned with divine glory...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Establish thy hymn-singers, O Theotokos, living and inexhaustible fountain, who have spiritually assembled a choir, and in thy divine glory make them worthy of crowns of glory!*[^651]

*Troparia:*
The unwaning Light has been kindled for thee, even as is righteous, which from thy youth thou didst seek with an upright heart, O Theodosius; therefore dost thou rejoice eternally in the unapproachable Light.

Then was assembled a multitude of monastics with their superior, to translate the relics of Venerable Theodosius; with them we also today spiritually celebrate in memory of his translation.[^652]

Having gone through many years of fasting, as though in an alien body, suffering valiantly, thou didst ascend unto the Lord, to be filled with incorruptible food with all the saints.

*Theotokion:*
By the sprinkling of thy mercy, O Virgin, quench the coals of my passions, O thou who art full of divine joy, and kindle the extinguished lamp of my heart, O golden candlestick, all-immaculate one!

*Sessional Hymn of the Venerable One, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
The memory of those that fear God gladdens the faithful; come, therefore, we also, in memory of the translation of our Venerable Father Theodosius, let us sing unto God Who has glorified him!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Forefeast, Tone 4, Podoben: "At the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
Celebrating the forefeast of the falling asleep of the Mother of God, rejoicing, let us radiantly cry aloud unto Her: Rejoice, O full of grace, who art taken up from earth unto heaven; rejoice, thou who hast revived all ends of the earth by thy dormition; therefore, passing over now, remember the world, O thou full of joy!

*(Right page — Folio 127 recto, running header: OF VENERABLE THEODOSIUS)*
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the transcendent God, came on a light cloud with an incorruptible palm, and saved those that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!*

*Troparia:*
Having put to death carnal mindedness, thou didst dry up the sea of passions through prayer, being strengthened by the sole hope of eternal good things, which thou hast now received abundantly, O venerable one.

It is not fitting for the sheep to be deprived of their pastor, nor likewise for the pastor to leave his sheep; being strangers, they grieved for the flock, O Father Theodosius.

It is not good for our teacher to remain outside his monastery and his flock, which he himself founded, said thy disciples, O venerable father.

*Theotokion:*
My humble heart, O only pure one, and leprous through the impure assaults of passions, heal as a physician, and snatch me away from the eternal rivers!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, granting peace unto all that sing thy praise.*

*Troparia:*
Enlightened today is the most glorious temple of the Mother of God, having received its servant, the Venerable Father Theodosius, who abundantly pours forth rays of healings unto all that come unto him with faith.

Good was your counsel, O God-blessed fathers! Fair was the gathering, the monastic assembly! For ye hastened to translate your pastor and teacher, the radiant lamp, the vigilant mind.

Having dug into the place where thy precious relics were laid, O venerable one, when they found them, immediately they were sweetened by the fragrance of the Holy Spirit, which gladdens the hearts of all with abundant healing.

*Theotokion:*
Hymning thee with hymnody, I glorify thy greatness; therefore deliver me from impure passions, and make me worthy of eternal glory!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, O God-wise ones, come, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of Her!*

*Troparia:*
Come, ye faithful, offering lovely hymns, let us bless Theodosius with love, and worthily let us sing, radiantly celebrating the memory of his translation.

Let us sing to the radiant one, excellent in marvels, great in virtues, good in life, the glorious favorite of Christ *(Left page — Folio 127 verso, running header: SERVICE FOR THE TRANSLATION OF RELICS)*
...the favorite of Christ and of the All-Pure Theotokos, the blessed Theodosius.

Rejoicing now in the heavens with the angelic hosts, O most rich Father Theodosius, pray that grace and salvation be granted unto those who sing to thee with love.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the affliction of my mind, O all-immaculate one, cure the passions of my soul, and destroy the darkness of despondency, that to thy praise I may sing of thee, the ever-blessed and all-hymned Mother of God!

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):*[^653]
Thou wast the successor of the fathers, O venerable one, following their life and teaching, customs and abstinence, prayers and standings before God; with them, having boldness toward the Lord, ask forgiveness of transgressions and salvation for those who cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Father Theodosius!

*Ikos:*[^654]
Having lived angelically upon earth, O venerable one, thou wast accounted worthy of angelic praises in heaven: how then shall I sing of thy most radiant life and thy manifold virtues, signs and marvels, wherewith thou dost enlighten all as the sun? Therefore I cry unto thee such things as these:
Rejoice, thou who from infancy didst follow Christ!
Rejoice, thou who didst count all earthly things as refuse!
Rejoice, thou who didst put to death carnal passions!
Rejoice, thou who didst unmask the principalities of darkness and powers!
Rejoice, thou who didst seek heavenly things rather than earthly!
Rejoice, thou who didst desire the wisdom of God!
Rejoice, thou who wast accounted worthy to behold the Incomprehensible!
Rejoice, preacher of the Most Holy Trinity!
Rejoice, servant of the Mother of God!
Rejoice, glory and boast of monastics!
Rejoice, through whom we are delivered from the harm of the enemy!
Rejoice, in whom we all are saved!
Rejoice, O our Father Theodosius!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The God-wise ones served not creation more than the Creator, but valiantly trampling down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!*

*Troparia:*
Having cast away the care of the world and counted its glory as nothing, thou didst seek future glory, dedicating thyself unto God through stillness upon earth, O favorite; therefore He has glorified thee in the heavens, O venerable one.

Having hated carnal passions from youth, and trampled down fair beauty, thou didst transcend toward God through fasting; and standing now with the angels before the Lord, pray for those who honor thy memory.

Unto him who appeared unto thee, O venerable one, hidden because of blasphemy and repentance, announcing unto him how great glory he had lost, commanding him to be placed upon thy shoulder, thou didst heal him in that very hour.[^655]

*Theotokion:*
Be my protection, O all-pure one, toward Him Who was born of thee, granting me remission of grievous debts, the divine entrance into the kingdom of God, reception of food, and participation in the Light!

*(Right page — Folio 128 recto, running header: OF VENERABLE THEODOSIUS)*
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The pious youths in the furnace were saved by the offspring of the Theotokos: then prefigured, but now fulfilled, it moves the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparia:*
Living in the heights with thy soul, thou dost reveal thy precious relics unto those who seek them in the earth; and having seen them whole and incorrupt, and pouring forth fragrance, they rejoiced in spirit, saying: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

It is fitting for us ever to behold with our eyes the precious shrine of our Father Theodosius, and ever to offer worthy veneration unto it, translating the precious relics, saying: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

The place wherein thou thyself before thy death didst foretell that thou wouldst be laid, having now received thy precious body, grants healing unto those who come with faith.

*Theotokion:*
Shown forth higher than the angels art thou, having given birth to the Lord; therefore pray Him, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, that I may become higher than carnal temptations, and be delivered from the future judgment and eternal torment, O Virgin!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened in spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred solemnity of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!*

*Troparia:*
Thou wast shown forth the conqueror of the ruler of the world, the prince of darkness, having crushed his hostile arrows completely with the weapon of the Cross and with faith; and thou didst seek God, our refuge and strength.

Thou wast a true pastor of Christ's flock; therefore cease not ever to pray, O venerable one, that remission of transgressions, divine protection, and eternal rejoicing be granted unto us.

Foresightful of thy departure from the body and of the future recompense unto thee from God, thou didst pray diligently, O venerable one, for thy flock, and didst comfort these sorrowful ones, saying: I promise you, brethren and fathers, that though I depart from you in body, yet in spirit I will ever be with you.[^656]

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O pure one, who didst bear the all-merciful Savior: have mercy on thy servant, direct me unto the paths of repentance, drive away from our midst the temptations of the crafty one, deliver me from his ensnarement, and snatch me away from eternal fire!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Having adorned the heaven" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Celebrating today the memory of the translation of thy precious relics, we cry aloud: Standing with the angels with great boldness, remember us all, O father, before Christ the Master!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Forefeast:*
*Of thine all-honorable dormition... (seek in the Menaion).*

*At the Praises:*

*(Left page — Folio 128 verso, running header: SERVICE FOR THE TRANSLATION OF RELICS)*
*At the Praises, Stichera of the Forefeast, 3 on the Aposticha (seek in the Menaion).*
*And of the Venerable One, 3, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^657]
O Venerable Father Theodosius, the Lord appointed thee as pastor to His flock; for having edified thyself upon the foundation of virtues, O blessed one, thou wast accounted worthy of acceptance by God, ever standing before the intelligible Light, fittingly glorified by Him; therefore we honor and bless thee!

O Venerable Father Theodosius, thou didst serve God fervently, having lived as a monk in stillness; and walking in abstinence, adorned with humility, and enlightened with all virtues, thou hast now obtained good things from God; therefore we also, even as those who were present, glorify thy precious departure.

O Venerable Father Theodosius, though thou hast departed from us in body, yet in spirit thou art ever with us; for behold, even after death thou dost visit us by appearing, delivering from misfortunes, comforting the sorrowful, revealing what was lost, and healing the sick through thy mediation; therefore we all bless thee with faith!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Today the hierarchs having come with the ranks of the consecrated, and a multitude of monastics, and peoples of the pious, received thy most precious relics from the cave, O Father Theodosius, escorting them honorably with candles and incense, bringing them into the God-built church, and laid them sacredly; of which we celebrate the yearly commemoration, and crying aloud in song, we call out to thee: As thou standest before Christ's throne, O venerable one, cease not to pray that all may be delivered from every harm of the enemy, and that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Of the Forefeast, Tone 3:*
*Come, all ye ends of the earth... (seek in the Menaion).*

*The Great Doxology and the Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service for a venerable monk.*

*(Tailpiece engraving: Flower vase with floral arrangements)*

*(Right page — Folio 129 recto, woodcut engraving of Venerable Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves and service opening)*[^658]
*(Full-page woodcut engraving: Venerable Alypius the Iconographer depicted full-length in his cell painting an icon of the Most Holy Theotokos. Inscription above: "Venerable Alypius the Iconographer." Legend beneath the main composition: "Seeing a dove fly upward from the image of the Mother of God, an angel painted the icon of the Mother of God." Below, two narrative panels: the saint standing before a hierarch and monastics, and the saint upon his sickbed while an angel completes the icon of the Dormition.)*

*Month of August, on the 17th day.*
*Service to our Venerable Father Alypius, Iconographer of the Kyiv Caves.*
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^659]
Having the image of God's glory ever in thy mind, O most blessed Father Alypius, thou didst depict thy soul with virtuous adornments, to show it forth fair, desiring to share in the glory of the Trinity; receiving paints *(Left page — Folio 129 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...receiving paints of spiritual grace, thou wast an excellent wonderworker, and Him Whose countenance thou didst depict in thy heart, with Him dost thou also now reign: pray Him for us, O venerable one, that peace be granted unto us, and the enjoyment of His glory![^660]

Desiring to be a beholder of the face of God, thou didst set in thy mind, O father, to behold the sweetest image of Jesus; having laid thereon the stone of utmost humility, rubbing drops of ceaseless prayers, mingling them with streams of tears, thou didst depict upon fleshy tablets the vision of the invisible glory of the Most Holy Trinity: standing before Which now, pray for us, O holy one, that we may obtain mercy and the sweet vision of the face of God!

Having depicted the countenances of the saints, kindled with desire for the uncircumscribed glory of God, thou didst depict with the colors of good deeds upon the tablets of thy heart the countenance of ceaseless love toward the All-Pure Mother of God; whom having served by thine art, accounted worthy of wonderworking grace, thou didst receive the reward of unending recompense: and now dwelling with the angels, pray for us, O venerable one, that the world may be given peace and our souls be saved!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
The all-crafty one, having once through the deceit of the demonic serpent disfigured the human image, now that thou, O father, hast received grace from God, is filled with shame: for he could not bear to look upon the icons of thine art, which with wonderworking rays disperse darkness, and grant gifts of healing unto all that flee with faith: of which make also us partakers, who celebrate thy memory on this day, O wise Alypius!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast.*
*Glory, Tone 3:*
Having the spiritual portrait of thy soul radiant, O our Venerable Father Alypius, thou wast accounted worthy of radiant and wonderworking grace, needing not compensation for the painting of thine icons, and granting healing in illnesses unto those in need; therefore we pray thee, O all-blessed one: correct the disfigurement of our souls with virtues, and heal our passionate sores with the colors of thy prayers, even as the leper of old, that with praises we may glorify thee, the adornment of the venerable!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*

*(Right page — Folio 130 recto, running header: TO ALYPIUS THE ICONOGRAPHER OF THE CAVES)*
*Troparion, Tone 8:*[^661]
Depicting the countenances of the saints upon wooden panels, through their grace in truth, as a skilled craftsman, O all-praised Alypius, thou didst depict them upon the tablets of thy heart; and for this cause, as an icon godly-adorned, through the grace of the priesthood, as with gold wast thou overlaid by Christ God and the Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Of the Feast.*

*(Ornamental divider band)*
*Canon of the Feast and of the Venerable One, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us send up a hymn, ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who delivered Israel from servitude, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!*

*Troparia:*
Grant help from on high unto Thy servants, O Christ, to send up a hymn, that we may offer praise of warm love, in memory of Thy favorite, unto Thee, the only Master!

As grace multiplied, faith blossomed forth, and the rank of the venerable multiplied on the Caves Mount; desirous of being a companion in life unto the venerable, he came, following on to live unto the only Christ and Master.[^662]

From thy youth thou wast given over by thy parents, O venerable one, to learn the painting of icons; whereunto diligently attending, thou didst inscribe chastity and purity in thy heart, so that in both thou wast a servant unto thy Master.

*Glory:*
Thy marvelous obedience showed thee forth, O father, as a beholder of an astounding wonder: for thou didst behold the icon of the Mother of God depicted not made with hands, and kindled by this vision with greater love for the contemplation of God, thou didst prepare thy heart as a tablet for the inscription of the memory of Christ the Master.[^663]

*Both now:*
O tablet that hast ineffably inscribed by the Holy Spirit the image of the Father's Hypostasis, transform my deformity, O Virgin Mother, into the fair form of good deeds, that therein I may prepare myself to serve Christ, my God and Master!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: There is none righteous above Thee, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
There is none like unto Thee among the gods, O Christ; for by the operation of Thy Most Holy Spirit Thou didst depict *(Left page — Folio 130 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
...Thou didst depict the icon of Thy Mother not made by hands, and a great marvel took place, whereof Thy venerable one was an eyewitness, O Lord!

The Same that worked wonders, flew forth from the mouth of the icon not made by hands in the form of a dove; which having seen flying about within the church, Thy venerable one, O Christ, despised all things, and loved nothing in this world above Thee, O Lord!

Having seen the image of the glory of God, O venerable one, immediately thou didst alter thy worldly guise, becoming a black-robed monk, having well depicted the representation of virtue; which also raise up within us by thy warm prayers!

*Glory:*
From thine iconographic art, O father, thou didst learn to represent spiritual deeds: for having well cleansed the brightness of thy heart with streams of tears, thou didst depict the thought of memory concerning God.

*Both now:*
Rejoice, O scroll of the God-betrothed Virgin, whereon by the finger of God was inscribed the Word of the Father: write upon the scroll of my heart the remembrance of the Dread Judgment, and deliver me from coming torments as I pray against evil things!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Who will not praise thee in hymns with us, O venerable one, as the beholder of God's glory and all-wise worker of icons? For thou didst see in part sensibly the Holy Spirit, and standing now before the throne on high, not through a glass, but face to face dost thou behold the ever-existing glory of God; of which make also us partakers, O Father Alypius, by thy prayers!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O mediatress before the Holy Trinity, stand swiftly before us this day who are assembled in thy most honorable temple, and grant those who glorify thee, O Virgin, to behold the glory of thy venerable ones through thy prayers!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the Prophet foresaw Thee, the One about to take flesh of the only Theotokos, and with fear he glorified Thy power.*[^664]

*Troparia:*
From the mountain Thou didst call Thy venerable one, O Christ, to run after Thee; who, casting away worldly turmoils, adhered unto Thee with his whole soul, and ever depicted Thy power in his mind.

As a radiant star thou didst shine forth, O father, upon the Caves Mount; for virtues stretched out from thee as rays, O blessed one, and today enlighten those who magnify thee; therefore we also glorify thy grace.

*(Right page — Folio 131 recto, running header: TO ALYPIUS THE ICONOGRAPHER OF THE CAVES)*
Not for the sake of wages, but moved by zeal didst thou attend unto thy work; and from this thou didst unsparingly distribute for the beautification of the churches of God, so that all looking upon Thine icons might turn their hearts unto the love of God.[^665]

*Glory:*
A good laborer wast thou shown forth, O blessed one, of Christ's vineyard, so that it is fitting for thee to say with the Apostle: Not only did I eat bread, for my hands ministered unto me and unto others; therefore thou didst receive the denarius from God, and with good servants dost behold the ever-existing glory.[^666]

*Both now:*
From the pure treasury of thy womb, O Mother of God, the Denarius given unto the world, thou didst thereby redeem all from the ancestral debt; unto thee do I flee: deliver me from spiritual creditors, that I may ever glorify thy grace!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Rising early we cry unto Thee: O Lord, save us! For Thou art our God, and we know no other beside Thee.*

*Troparia:*
Raise up from despondency, O father, those who rise early with doxology unto thee, and from dark deeds unto the light of the knowledge of God elevate our minds through thy prayers; for we know thee as a swift helper in assistance.
