From the work of thy hands, O father, in honor of the One God, thou didst separate a threefold division: for the need of icons, for the monastery's work, and for alms unto the poor; and by this didst thou acquire the better, ever-desired dwelling of Paradise!

The day in manual work, and the night in vigilance, prayer, and prostrations didst thou ever spend, O blessed one; and having thus laid thy hand upon the plow, thou wast made fit for the Kingdom of God: remember also us there, by thy mediation unto God!

*Glory:*
The sun shone forth and saw thy labors, O holy one: for humility, non-possessiveness, purity, patience, fasting, and love toward all didst thou blend; and by this ascent thou didst ascend unto the height of divine contemplation, and wast accounted worthy of the heavenly gift.

*Both now:*
Intelligible morning, who didst bring forth into the world the Great Sun, enlighten our mind and heart, and show us to be sons of light through thy prayer, O unwedded Virgin: for thou art our illumination and hope!

*(Left page — Folio 131 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a radiant robe, Thou that deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God!*

*Troparia:*
Having adorned the robe of the angelic image with the colors of monastic labors, thou didst ascend unto the high degree of the priesthood by the counsel of thy superior; and standing thereon as a luminary, thou didst enlighten many by the light of thy deeds.

Unto me who have defiled the garment of baptism with the filth of sin, grant contrition, O holy one, by thy prayers, that washing therein, I may appear clean before the face of God: for behold, I stand before thee, hymning thy praise with clay lips.

Having received the vesture of the priesthood, after the likeness of the Great High Priest, the Son of God, thou didst enter, O father, into the inner veil, to offer sacrifice unto Him Who was sacrificed for the sin of the whole world: by thy prayers now, as by a sacrifice, make the Creator merciful unto us, O holy one!

*Glory:*
By these deeds and the vesture of the priesthood thou wast accounted worthy of greater gifts, so that those clothed with leprosy thou didst heal by thy prayers: heal also us who are mentally diseased with these things, O most blessed intercessor!

*Both now:*
Wound those who have wounded us with the arrow of sin, O all-pure Virgin, and let the demonic ranks be cast down stricken with shame: for thou art a plaster for all incurable sores!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^667]
Having from youth the divine inscription within thee, thou wast given to learn the painting of icons; and when the church of the Mother of God resembling heaven was being painted, thou didst assist therein, and didst behold the shining of the Holy Spirit, from Whom thou didst receive the operation of marvels, so that thine icons worked wonders; therefore we honor and bless thee, O marvelous Alypius!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew children boldly trampled down the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!*

*Troparia:*
Unto him who played actively with doubt in the faith, the righteous punishment of God arrived: for leprosy covered his face; whom thou, O father, didst instruct, and marvelously sang: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^668]

*(Right page — Folio 132 recto, running header: TO ALYPIUS THE ICONOGRAPHER OF THE CAVES)*
Unto him that confessed the sin of his unbelief, thou didst first apply, O blessed one, the plaster of repentance; and when the soul was healed, the face also was healed by the anointing of iconographic paint drops; and he sang concerning thee: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

He who formerly was wounded with the stench of sin, and according to the devil's counsel despaired of salvation, having by thy teaching, O holy one, turned that plow of hope unto God, was healed; and being rid of both sores, he cried: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory:*
Marveling at thy many wonders, O venerable one, we cry out harmoniously: Rejoice, new Elisha, who hast healed the non-covetous one of leprosy like Naaman; and concerning thee we sing the children's song: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^669]

*Both now:*
Who will correct the deformity of my soul, if not thou, O Mother of God, with the colors of thy goodness? Having transformed its countenance into fair form, and receiving godly beauty, I shall sing: Rejoice, iconographer and portraitist of the human form!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Hymn the Lord Who was glorified on the holy mount, and Who revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin in the bush of fire, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparia:*
The Creator of all showed thee forth, O father, upon the Caves Mount as a fair-blossoming tree unto healing: for by the fruits of thy prayers thou didst unsparingly grant healing unto all that came unto thee, and the forms went away supremely exalting the Lord unto the ages.

The innocency of thy heart deprived Thee not, O venerable one, of great gifts: for never didst thou become bitter against those that offended Thee, nor didst thou render evil for evil; therefore Christ has glorified Thee both here and there unto the ages.

Those darkened by the darkness of money-loving accused thee before men, but God brought forth thy righteousness as the light, and thine innocency as the noonday; and those that dug a pit for thee fell into it themselves, and because of their shame all remember them unto the ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
O One God in Trinity, Who workest by seven gifts, Thou didst command seven icons to be depicted not made with hands, and didst reveal the venerable one possessing seven talents of Thy gifts; grant these also unto us, O all-bountiful King, that we may supremely exalt Thee unto the ages![^670]

*(Left page — Folio 132 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONE)*
*Both now:*
Having received a woven robe from all gifts, clearly clothed with golden garments, thou hast stood before the Life-Originating Trinity; stand, therefore, lifting up thy most pure hands, and pray for us who are stripped of well-doing: for thou art the all-immaculate one who coverest our sins!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thee, the unwedded Mother of the Most High God, Thee who above understanding didst give birth by a word unto the true God, higher than the most pure powers, with unceasing doxologies do we magnify!*

*Troparia:*
God has glorified thee who glorifiest Him, O father, so that fire never overcame the icons of thy painting, but they remained whole and unharmed by the fire; seeing which working wonders, we magnify Christ with unceasing doxologies concerning thee![^671]

From all sides the people assembled to see the most glorious wonder, how the fiery flame devoured all other things, but harmed not in any wise the icons of thy painting; hearing which, we magnify God Who works wonders concerning Thee with doxologies!

Thou didst keep innocency and didst behold uprightness, spending thy life in great sanctity; therefore God glorified Thee on the right hand, and delivered Thee from every misfortune: deliver also us from this by thy prayers, O holy one, that we may ever magnify Thee in hymns!

*Glory:*
The city of Rostov rejoices, possessing the icon painted by the nod of God, and as with a costly treasure is filled against all passions; which kissing, it remembers how much God worked through thee, O blessed one; honoring this wonderworking also today, we marvel, and magnify in doxologies![^672]

*Both now:*
Having thee as a tower of strength, O Virgin Theotokos, driven by the face of our enemies, we thy servants flee unto thee, and falling down we pray: preserve us that are in misfortunes, for thou art unto us a tower of strength from the face of the enemy, and an impregnable wall!

*Exapostilarion:*[^673]
He that ever preserves thee, O venerable one, in afflictions, came in thy stead before thy face to paint the icon of the Mother of God, which having depicted radiantly in three hours, He gave it for adornment unto the most pure temple for the feast, and took thee unto the temple on high, to celebrate with the saints unto the ages of ages; where celebrating now with the voice of rejoicing, remember also us, O Alypius, most blessed father!

*(Right page — Folio 133 recto, running header: TO ALYPIUS THE ICONOGRAPHER OF THE CAVES)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Being a temple created by the right hand of the Most High, raise up the temple of my soul that has fallen through sin, O Mother of God, renew it by thy visitation, and grant unto those who bring glory unto thee without slothfulness, ceaselessly to sing unto thee: Rejoice, O temple of the Lord, created as a refuge for Christians!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 7, Podoben: "As noble among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^674]
In Davidic manner today, ye people, let us sing in thy memory, O all-blessed father: thou wast planted in the house of the Lord, and didst flourish in the court of the Mother of God as a sweet-smelling tree, from which receiving fruits of healings, we glorify our God Who has glorified thee, and we pray diligently to obtain mercy through thy mediation, and healing for spiritual passions!

The palm tree, as a palm tree shall flourish: upon thee, O our Father Alypius, was this fulfilled; for at the streams of the living water of the teaching of the God-bearing fathers grew up the blossoms of thy virtuous corrections, looking upon which, as sons, we flee unto the shrine of thy relics, and embracing them with love we pray: grant, O holy one, through thy prayers to obtain peace in Christ, and the correction of life!

Who can worthily praise thy labors and struggles, O venerable one? For thou didst give no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, until thou didst adorn thy soul with the colors of virtues, having depicted it perfectly after the first-beginning fathers; whose zeal of life inscribe also unto our memory, we pray diligently, that we also may not be deprived of heavenly dwellings with them!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Having the image of God's glory ever in thy mind, thou didst depict in thy soul zeal ceaselessly to walk in the way of our first-beginning fathers; following whose life, in a mortal body thou wast likened in holiness unto the bodiless ones: standing now with them before the throne on high, pray, O venerable father, that unity of mind be given unto our monastery, as it honors thy holy memory with love!

*Both now... Of the Feast.*
*The Great Doxology and the Dismissal.*

*(Left page — Folio 133 verso, woodcut engraving of Venerable Hieromartyr Kuksha and Pimen the Faster)*[^675]
*(Full-page woodcut engraving: Venerable Hieromartyr Kuksha and Venerable Pimen the Faster depicted full-length before the Kyiv Caves Lavra. Captions above the saints: "Venerable Kuksha," "Venerable Pimen." Below, two narrative panels: "Holy Kuksha beheaded with his disciple," and "Holy Pimen foresaw the beheading of Kuksha.")*

*Month of August, on the 27th day.*
*Service to the Venerable Hieromartyr Kuksha and Pimen the Faster, of the Caves.*
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^676]
A good concord summons the assemblies of the faithful, piously and prayerfully to praise the memory of the God-bearing fathers, sacred Kuksha and Pimen, emulator of fasters, as warm protectors unto those that flee unto them with faith: for through their mediation they pray unceasingly for those who sing their holy memory with love!

A voice of rejoicing and solemnity, come now, ye feast-loving people, let us establish, crying aloud with sweet-singing virtues, let us bring majesty unto the marvelous fathers, rendering yearly praises, and honoring the day of their journey unto God, before Whom they now stand with rejoicing, and pray for our souls!

Offering honorable sacrifices first unto God, O all-blessed Father Kuksha, afterwards thou didst offer thyself also as a living sacrifice, as an unblemished lamb, with thy revered disciple, being slain with the sword in the preaching of the Gospel teaching; wherein having suffered rejoicing, thou standest before Christ ever praying for our souls![^677]

*(Right page — Folio 134 recto, running header: TO KUKSHA AND PIMEN OF THE CAVES)*
*Glory, Tone 8:*
Elevated unto God by single-minded desire, having equipped the ship of your soul with virtues, O venerable fathers, ye easily traversed the surging sea of the world; and by the assistance of the Holy Spirit ye reached the quiet harbor, wherein also ye subdued carnal passions unto the spirit, and joined yourselves unto Christ, before Whom standing now in heaven, pray diligently for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*At the Aposticha, in the Oktoechos.*
*Glory to the Venerable Ones, Tone 6:*
Teaching the divine mysteries of the faith unto the unbelieving people of the Vyatichi, O most blessed Kuksha with thy disciple, ye exposed the deception of evil belief; and those who obeyed your teaching received holy baptism; but others, blinded by the darkness of blasphemy, ordained that a murderous light depart from you, and severed your heads with the sword: but O most sacred fathers, standing now before the unapproachable Light, pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*[^678]
Honorably adorned with martyr's blood, O sacred Kuksha, in the preaching of the Gospel with thy blessed disciple; and thou, O Pimen, who didst shine radiantly in fasting: in one day and hour ye entered into the unwaning light, where pray unto the Lord that He may grant us great mercy!

*(Left page — Folio 134 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
*Canon to the Venerable Ones, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by the crosswise-stretched hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*[^679]

*Troparia:*
Who can worthily praise the memory of the saints? For precious in Thy sight is their death, as of Thy friends, O God; yet as much as I have received, I offer praise unto them, and I pray Thee, O Master: remove hindrances from me, and strengthen me by Thy power!

O sacred Kuksha with thy disciple, being sacred vessels, ye carried the name of Christ before the unbelievers; whom having amazed with signs and marvels, ye brought unto the knowledge of God; that I also may be a vessel in honor unto my God, help me by your prayers, O fathers!

*Glory:*
Daring today to honor with praises the three of you, Kuksha with his disciple, and clairvoyant Pimen, I pray: stand before the Holy Trinity praying for me, that I may be delivered from the three enemies—the world, the flesh, and the devil—and appear as a good warrior, conqueror over them![^680]

*Both now:*
Thou who by thy most pure birthgiving didst deliver men from the violence of the enemy, deliver me from impure thoughts, I pray, and save me, as the Mother of the God Who loves mankind!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my foundation!*

*Troparia:*
The Great Caves Lavra rejoices, having nurtured you in her bosom, O venerable fathers, and with love cries aloud: For the sake of Kuksha and Pimen, O my Christ, be my refuge and foundation in sorrow!

Unto thy many virtues, O father, many gifts from on high followed: for what is so great as to turn an unbeliever unto the faith? This truly was greater than to raise the dead!

*Glory:*
Having come as an apostle unto an unbelieving people, thou didst amaze them with signs and wonders, and thereby having turned many, thou didst bring them unto God, O Kuksha, from darkness unto light: establish me who am unstable, O venerable one, by thy prayers!

*(Right page — Folio 135 recto, running header: TO KUKSHA AND PIMEN OF THE CAVES)*
*Both now:*
O boast of apostles and beauty of martyrs, deprive me not of a portion in heavenly glory, and make me worthy of that beauty through the mediation of Pimen and Kuksha, O Virgin Mother and Maiden!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^681]
Having received the gifts of marvelous love, the holy ones offered an odorous sacrifice unto Thee, O Christ: Pimen purity and abstinence, as an acceptable olive tree; and Kuksha his blood shed for the sake of the Gospel, as a lovely cluster of grapes: therefore were they worthily accounted worthy of heavenly honor, as good ascetics!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Deeds of love I have not brought unto Thee, O my God, therefore neither do I myself expect to find mercy from Thee; but for the sake of Thy goodness, O my Christ, and by the mediation of Thine all-pure Mother, have mercy on me and save me!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in Her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
Having arisen in warfare against the ruler of the world, ye arrayed yourselves, O all-blessed fathers, and drove the enemies far from men, and by your wonderworking brought many unto Christ, unto Whom we sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Dewed from on high with drops of the grace of God, O Hieromartyr Kuksha, thou didst become like rain-bearing clouds, and poured forth abundant drops upon the earth: dew also us with the drops of thy prayers who sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^682]

*Glory:*
O Pimen, seer of human deeds taking place afar off through the Spirit of God, bring to our remembrance the secret deeds of our hearts that have remained in oblivion; for though thou art now afar off, yet thou canst through the grace of God intercede for those that call upon Thee for help and sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Both now:*
Be a swift visitation unto me in sorrow, O all-pure Virgin: for thou didst give birth above nature unto the Joy that visited us as the Orient from on high; enlightened by Whom, we offer unto thee: Rejoice!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, O my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light, turning from dark ignorance those that hymn Thee with faith.*

*Troparia:*
Thou, O Venerable Kuksha, didst dry up the swamp of evil belief, and *(Left page — Folio 135 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...and the turbid waters dried up, when the sun of right belief shone forth among the Vyatichi: stop also the streams of malice for us through thy prayers, who hymn thee with faith!

Even as thou didst heal the sick with thy word, so visit also us with that same grace given thee by God, and assist in spiritual infirmities those who sing thy memory with love, O Pimen!

*Glory:*
Let us render glory, ye people, unto God Who is in the highest, Who has glorified His saints, and let us pray that through their prayers we also may not be deprived of heavenly glory, joyfully singing their memory!

*Both now:*
Who can declare thy glory, O pure Virgin? For the heavenly powers glorify thee, and we falling down on earth glorify thee, and magnify singing: Rejoice, pure boast of the Orthodox!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of thanksgiving, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.*

*Troparia:*
Those who were about to be devoured by the spiritual whale, the unbelieving people, thou didst snatch away, O father, by holy baptism, and didst establish a church from them, having cleansed them with the blood that mercifully flowed from the Savior's side!

They shall lay hands on the sick, as the Scripture said, and they shall recover; so according to the power given thee from Christ, by thy word thou didst heal the ailing brethren, who departed glorifying Him Who gave thee such good things: therefore visit also us who are diseased in soul, and heal us by thy word, O most blessed Pimen![^683]

*Glory:*
Worthily to praise you, O most blessed fathers, as ye labored much here, who is able? Yet are ye praised in heavenly glory, and ye rejoice upon your beds in the ever-existing Light: pray that we also may be with you, having been cleansed by the blood that flowed from the Savior's all-pure side!

*Both now:*
We praise thee, blessing thee, ye Christian generations, and with love we call: O Sovereign Lady, stand before those that are sinking in the mire of vile deeds; for though we have defiled our souls, yet thou canst wash us who have been redeemed by the precious blood of thy Son!

*(Right page — Folio 136 recto, running header: TO KUKSHA AND PIMEN OF THE CAVES)*
*Kontakion, Tone 4:*[^684]
Coming together, ye people, let us sing with praises of the spiritual luminaries: for they have shone forth in this world: Pimen by many struggles, and Kuksha with his disciple by the preaching of the Word; and these three standing single-mindedly before the Holy Trinity shine as the sun, and enlighten with love those that praise their memory!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
As a single-minded concord, O holy fathers, though standing far apart in body, yet united in spirit, ye ran together unto the Lord, together to sing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Thy slaying, O blessed Kuksha, with thy disciple by the unbelieving Vyatichi, Pimen, standing far off, foresaw, and cried aloud in the midst of the Caves church: Our brother Kuksha has been slain this day at the hour of the offering of the sacrifice! Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^685]

*Glory:*
Having foretold by the Holy Spirit the slaying of Kuksha and his disciple by the unbelievers, he himself also delivered his spirit into the hands of God: for the sake of these three ash-covered ones, receive also our spirits into Thy glory, O Lord!

*Both now:*
O thou who didst receive in thy womb by the descent of the Holy Spirit, in the hour when we must surrender our spirits unto the Lord and stand before His judgment seat, stand before Him through thy mediation and entreat His goodness for us; for nothing is impossible unto thy mediation!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Daniel, stretching forth his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the den; and the children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*[^686]

*Troparia:*
Stretching forth your hands unto God, pray diligently for us who sing your memory, O God-bearing fathers, that through your mediation we may be found worthy to behold the Lord in the ever-existing Light!

Thou, O Hieromartyr Kuksha, with thy revered disciple, having offered thy blood as blended wine, pray that we may be delivered from the assault of enemies that seek our blood, and in peace bless the Lord for the rest of our days!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
O God-wise Pimen, seeing afar off and foretelling things to come *(Left page — Folio 136 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE ONES)*
...proclaiming, as a prophet of God, concerning the evils coming upon us on account of our transgressions: deliver us by thy swift entreaty unto the Master, and make us worthy in the heavenly habitations to bless the Lord unto the ages![^687]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Truly did the venerable one of God foresee thee, that thou dost ever assist the people living in the world: we pray thee, O Sovereign Lady, help us also who are infirm, that being girded with strength through thee, we may be able to bless the Lord unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A stone cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, O Virgin, was Christ the cornerstone, Who joined the disparate natures together; therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos!*[^688]

*Troparia:*
Christ preached by Thee, O sacred Kuksha, became a stone of stumbling unto the unbelieving; for they accounted thy rebuke concerning their evil belief as a snare and an offense; for this cause the foolish pagans, having fallen upon Thee with thy disciple, severed your heads with the sword; therefore, as having shed your blood together for Christ, we magnify you![^689]

Having established thyself upon Christ the Rock with patience, O most blessed Pimen, thou didst pass without stumbling through the deceptive snares of the stumbling enemy, or rather didst tear them asunder like a spider's web, and having departed unto the heights, thou dost enjoy eternal glory; therefore we magnify thee![^690]

*Glory:*
United in the Spirit, dwelling in the heavenly habitations, look down upon us below, O venerable fathers; for we are from one root among children, from which, pray ye, that we may be delivered, as we magnify your memory in hymns!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having received the mystery unknown from the ages, and having given wings unto the world by the incarnation of God the Word, entreat, O Mary, heaven-reaching Mother of God, for my hidden faults, cleanse me, that I be not condemned before angels and men on that day when thy Son shall come to judge the world!

*Exapostilarion:*[^691]
Now an acceptable time has appeared unto us, wherein the holy cave hymns the God-bearing ones, unto which we also, having gathered together, glorify Kuksha with his disciple, hymn Pimen, and worshiping the One God in Trinity, extol Him!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having in time given birth unto Him Who placed times and seasons under His own authority, O unwedded Virgin, well arrange our years that are woven like a spider's web, by the prayers of the venerable ones...

*(Right page — Folio 137 recto, running header: TO KUKSHA AND PIMEN OF THE CAVES)*
...well arrange them, that we may glorify Him Who was born of thee, and fondly magnify thee!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Oktoechos, and to the Venerable Ones, Tone 4:*
*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and the Lord will deliver them out of them all.*[^692]
By a good struggle, O glorious Pimen, and by the martyr's blood, O most blessed Kuksha, with thy disciple who followed thee, ye were enrolled in the army of the heavenly King, and became mighty warriors against the spiritual foes; for by a firm array ye cast them down to the end, and having received crowns as conquerors, ye rejoice in the heavenly habitations; from whence look down upon us who honor your holy memory!

*Glory, Tone 5:*
Ye were shown forth as wise disciples of Christ our God, the all-wise Teacher; for ye fulfilled the word in deed, despised your souls in this world, cared not for the body, cast away every worldly attachment, arrayed yourselves against the ruler of the world, and guided by the Spirit, conquered carnal passions: Kuksha with his disciple severed by the sword, and Pimen having cast down the power of the enemy by fasting labors. But O all-blessed fathers, assist us by your prayers to cast down the adversary who ever wars against us, and entreat great mercy for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, and the Dismissal.*

*(Tailpiece engraving: Large baroque floral flourish with acanthus leaves) (Left page — Folio 137 verso, headpiece engraving: Icon of the Mother of God of the Kyiv Caves enthroned, flanked by Venerable Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves)*
*Month of August, on the 28th day.*
*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves, whose incorrupt relics rest in the Far Cave.*[^693]
*Wherein, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^694]
Theodosius the God-wise, commander of another King, gathered a host of spiritual soldiers of venerable fathers, whose struggles extolling, let us offer unto them hymns of praise, and cry aloud, saying: As ye have boldness before the Master of all and God, ask for our souls great mercy, and peace for the whole world!

A sign is seen marvelously by intelligible eyes: having flame-bearing wings, the spiritual soldiers, the all-blessed fathers, come from heaven and hasten unto our help, arraying themselves against the ruler of the world; unto whom let us fall down, crying: We call upon you, our unslumbering protectors, hasten to deliver us from our enemies that attack us, and to grant us deliverance from afflictions!

Dispel the hostile assaults, O our Venerable Father Theodosius, protecting thy flock with horses and chariots; for they ever counsel to destroy it with evil attacks, but do thou, as a swift helper, grant protection unto those that flee unto thee, and ask for thy deliverance, and withhold not thy visitation in sorrows!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Standing before the throne of the Thrice-Radiant Godhead, and richly...

*(Right page — Folio 138 recto, running header: IN THE FAR CAVE)*
...and richly illuminated with unapproachable brightness, O our all-blessed fathers, deliver from passions and misfortunes those who lovingly stand upon earth and today sing praises to your assembly, as unceasing intercessors before God and pleaders for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion: The King of heaven... (seek in Tone 8).*

*Then the Entrance, the Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings for venerable monks: seek in the beginning of this book, Folio 4 on the reverse.*[^695]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the ranks of heaven" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^696]
O God-chosen laborers, our venerable fathers of the Caves, ye planted upon earth an intelligible vineyard of virtues, wherefrom there grew up for you the worthy fruit of eternal life!

*Verse: The venerable shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^697]
Ye sowed tears in grief, that a plentiful fruit, joy in the courts of the Lord, might blossom forth; wherewith enjoying yourselves unto the ages, pray ye for us unto the Lord!

*Verse: Blessed are all that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways.*[^698]
Desiring to be delivered from worldly turmoils, ye settled in the cave, wherein having lived laboriously, ye entered into eternal rest: pray there for us unto Christ the Master!

*Glory, Tone 1:*
Having fled from the surging turmoils of life, and reached the tranquility of the holy cave, and there having laboriously pleased the Lord, ye are now citizens of the Sion on high, and joint sons of the Kingdom of God: pray, O God-bearing fathers, that those who ever lovingly honor your assembly may obtain a portion in your glory!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Joy of the heavenly ranks, mighty protection of men upon earth, O all-pure Virgin, save us who flee unto thee; for after God, we have placed our hope in thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^699]
Today let us honor the intelligible sun and the radiant moon of the primary fathers of the Caves, with the whole assembly of the venerable; for they illuminated the firmament of the Church, enlightening those who are imperiled in the darkness of passions, and through their prayers they grant from Christ God help in all sorrows, and ask deliverance for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: The mystery hidden from the ages...*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion, and the same Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*[^700]
Through many sorrows ye traversed the path of this life, and having reached the summit of intelligible desire, ye took up your abode in the bridal chamber of divine glory; and now rejoicing *(Left page — Folio 138 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES)*
...and now rejoicing with all the saints, we honor your holy memory, for ye pray for us unto Christ our God!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of transgressions, for all that sing of thee as the pure Mother and Virgin!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Having heard the lamb-like voice in the heavenly bridal chamber, and having responded to the calling that came unto all men, ye left behind all worldly attachments, O God-bearing fathers; and having cleansed your garments with streams of tears, ye entered pure into the banquet of Christ God: but before the doors of that entrance are shut, pray that we also, having entered through repentance, may become partakers with you!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Seeing the bridal chamber ever adorned with eternal glory, we have desire to enter into it, but having no garment, we are ashamed, lest we be bound by the angels and cast out: do thou, who art the garment of boldness for the naked, O Virgin Mother, adorn us and lead us into the Kingdom of Christ!

*Then, "Praise the name of the Lord," and the Megalynarion:*
We magnify you, O our venerable fathers of the Caves, and we honor your holy memory, for ye pray for us unto Christ our God!

*Verse 1: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise is becoming unto the upright.*[^701]
*Verse 2: They that sow in tears shall reap in joy.*[^702]
*Verse 3: The venerable shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^703]
*Verse 4: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous, and glory, all ye that are upright in heart.*[^704]
*Glory... Both now... and "Alleluia," thrice.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^705]
Following the commandment of your Lord, taking the Cross as a staff in your hands, ye feared not the terror that stands in darkness, but steadfastly crossed over unto the harbor free from perils, and having found the recompense of your struggles, ye rejoice in the unapproachable Light; therefore we honor you, O all-blessed fathers, as ever praying unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly celebrate your holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As the only blessed one among women, and the praise and adornment of Adam, an unconquerable fortress in warfare, a confirmation unto the infirm, and a swift visitation unto those in sorrows: having thee, O unwedded Virgin, today in thine all-honorable temple we fall down and offer our supplications unto thee, that thou mayest pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who piously worship thy most holy offspring!

*(Right page — Folio 139 recto, running header: IN THE FAR CAVE)*
*Gradual Hymn, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone.*[^706]

*Prokeimenon:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^707]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*[^708]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Verse: Praise God in His saints.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Section 43 [Matthew 11:27–30]:*
*The Lord said unto His disciples: All things are delivered unto Me of My Father...*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron: Standing before the throne of the Thrice-Radiant Godhead... seek at "Lord, I have cried."*

*(Ornamental divider band)*
*Canon of the Theotokos, with the Heirmos, to 6; and of the Venerable Ones, to 8.*[^709]
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the water as dry land, and fleeing from the malice of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*[^710]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to cross the water of songful words in your memory, O God-bearing fathers, ever direct me in an orderly intelligible state, and restrain me from perilous passions, that in praising you I may magnify the name of my God with song!

Having gone forth into the sweet field of the intelligible paradise of the holy cave, I delight in the sweet-smelling fragrances that your relics pour forth, O venerable fathers, and joyfully with my mind I send up a song unto God the Creator!

A garden enclosed art thou, O holy cave: for in thee the Supreme Planter planted a multitude of trees, the God-bearing fathers, as the cedars of Lebanon, who reached unto the heights of the heavens; marveling at whom, I sing a song unto my God![^711]

O Longinus, who didst keep watch sleeplessly at the gate, having the gift to see into the thoughts of those entering, having obtained the honors of the calling from on high: enter and join with common praises the God-bearing fathers, in whose memory we sing a song unto our God![^712]

O Ignatius, pastor of monastics and healer of the sick, in our infirmities assist those who honor thee, that in thy memory we may offer a song of praise unto our God![^713]

*Glory:*
O Venerable Silvanus, lover of purity and keeper of the gardens, who by prayer didst bind the human thieves and intelligible foes who desired to steal both kinds of fruit: deliver us also from their assaults, we pray thee![^714]

*(Left page — Folio 139 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES)*
...we pray thee.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Surrounded by many thieves, I am ever insensible, and enemies rob me of the fruit of repentance: but by thy vigilant prayer and untold goodness, O Mother of God, deliver me, I pray!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial firmament and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!*

*Troparia:*
The Creator of the celestial and terrestrial sphere created this place, and appointed you, thrice-blessed fathers, as good citizens thereof: number us among your incorrupt cohabitation, and pray that we may reach the desired summit of mercy, we entreat you!

O blessed Agatho, famed for the utterance of goodness, beauty of fasters, truthful speaker, and healer of the ailing: declare unto us who are insensible if there be any way of iniquity in us, and do thou direct us by thy prayers into the way everlasting![^715]

We hymn marvelous Zeno, radiant with fasting, and Macarius, eponymous with blessedness: for the sake of these two fathers, we pray Thee, O Master, deliver us from passions of soul and body, O only Lover of mankind![^716]

We glorify Achilles, the minister of the sacrifice, and as a true faster we bring him unto Thee, O Christ, in prayer; for whose sake deliver us from servitude to carnal passions, and teach us his temperance, O only Lover of mankind![^717]

O Hypatius, minister unto the infirm, as possessing the gift of healing the sick by the laying on of hands, we pray thee: visit us from on high who are held fast by spiritual infirmities, and save us by thy prayers!

As in this life ye were lovingly bound together, and after death were laid in a single tomb, so also in the incorrupt life now dwelling together, O Paisius and Mercurius, through your intercession unto God, confirm us in likemindedness and love, we pray![^718]

*Glory:*
O Laurence, radiant among fasters, and Moses the cross-bearer, enlighten us who exist and walk in the darkness of passions, and direct us by your prayers, that in our cross-bearing we may not dash our feet against the stone of stumbling![^719]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The darkness of passions has encompassed us, and we have turned aside into the impassable ways of pleasures, and see not the light of correction: O instructress, direct us, and cause the light of repentance to shine upon us; for thou art the all-radiant luminary unto all, enlightening our souls!

*(Right page — Folio 140 recto, running header: IN THE FAR CAVE)*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^720]
Who is able to praise the pure life and labors of the venerable Pechersk fathers, who shone forth through fasting and radiant struggles? Yet with love toward them we offer songful gifts; for standing with the angels, they pray unceasingly unto the Lord for all of us!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Being the fountain of tender-heartedness, account us worthy of mercy, O Theotokos; look down upon a people that has sinned, manifest thy power as ever; for putting our trust in thee, we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice! even as once did Gabriel, the supreme commander of the bodiless powers!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead!*[^721]

*Troparia:*
Following the steps of the ancient fathers, ye turned aside not even a little from their labors; therefore, having received the equal denarius of eternal life from Christ, with the angels ye glorify His pre-eternal Godhead![^722]

Marvelous Hilarion, having the rank of the Great Angelic Schema, and according to his rank emulating the life of Venerable Theodosius, eating ever only once in the week, was strong to sing in doxology: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^723]

The fasting patristic army, wherein shone forth the radiant star Dionysius, who now goes unto the glory of the saints, and glorifies the One Godhead![^724]

Arsenius, renowned example of industriousness, eponymous with manly courage, living day and night in labors, received as recompense the gift of wonderworking, wherefrom he gives bountifully unto all that have need![^725]

O Pimen, likeminded with fasters, who didst labor in obedience with bloody sweat continually, carrying firewood, grinding at the millstones, and every night ceaselessly singing unto God and His One Godhead![^726]

O immovable pillar, Titoe the Recluse, being vigilant in seclusion, thou didst not require sensible brightness, since intelligible light illuminated thee: by it pray, O blessed one, that those who honor thee may be enlightened![^727]

*Glory:*
Glory to Thee, O Most Holy Trinity, for Thou hast glorified an earthly place, like unto heaven in its citizens; in whose memory, with the singing offered for us, we sinners glorify Thee, O Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, with slavish veneration, and supremely exalt Thy One Godhead!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Glory to thee, O pure boast of the Orthodox; for the human race glorifies thee together with the angels, and falling down *(Left page — Folio 140 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES)*
...and falling down cries aloud: For thy glory, O Theotokos, has stretched forth unto all for salvation!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant Thy peace unto us, O Lover of mankind!*[^728]

*Troparia:*
Having enlightened the whole Russian land by the grace given unto them, O all-blessed fathers, the darkness of evil belief has now perished utterly; extend those rays upon us for the sake of their entreaties, we pray Thee, O only Lover of mankind![^729]

O marvelous Sisoes, and Gregory who hadst the name from watchfulness, both having withered your passions by fasting, subdue the ferocious lust of our flesh also; for grace has been given unto you to assist us in our passions![^730]

O blessed Paul, lover of temperance, with the two canonarchs, extend supplications for us unto the Master, that by His grace we may restrain ourselves from belly-satiation, and become heirs with you in temperance![^731]

The Word of God that instructs men taught thee not worldly book-learning, O holy Nestor, but the wisdom from on high, wherein thou didst behold angels during thy prayers, and foresawest thine own repose: make us also partakers thereof, we pray thee who honor thy memory![^732]

O most valiant Titus, when thou wast once a soldier, the adversaries struck thy head with a weapon, and the wound became the cause of thy coming unto the Caves Mount, where thou didst weep for thy sins; having received assurance of forgiveness thereof, thou didst depart rejoicing unto the heavenly dwellings on high, O venerable one![^733]

I dare to honor obedient Pamva, and I marvel; for in obedience he was captured by the faithless, and for the sake of the faith endured many bonds, yet being snatched away and carried by angels, he was found in his cell; and upon him was fulfilled that obedience delivers from death![^734]

*Glory:*
I have fallen into the hands of spiritual enemies, and am bound by them with heavy fetters, from which a brother cannot deliver me: do Thou, O Most Holy Trinity, as merciful, have mercy on Thy creation, and by Thy goodness free me from mortal bonds!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
I am bound with many cords of passions, and have no hope of release: but have mercy on me, as the Mother of the Most High God, and loose me from them; for after God, Thou alone art able to loose sinful bonds!

*(Right page — Folio 141 recto, running header: IN THE FAR CAVE)*
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!*[^735]

*Troparia:*
We offer our prayers unto you, O God-bearing fathers, for ye are intercessors and pleaders for us unto God: therefore by your mediation deliver from sinful bonds those who honor your venerable assembly!

Having sleepless spiritual vision toward God, ye were harmless as infants, but ye were an eye against demons, so that they trembled even at your name: we pray you, entreat ye, that the assaults of the enemy against us also may turn back with shame!

O Zechariah, eponymous with the remembrance of the Lord, having acquired a life according to thy name; for thou didst so valiantly arm thyself against enemies with fasting, that in thy whole life thou wast content feeding on herbs; therefore demons feared thy name: delivering us from their wickedness, hasten unto our help, we humbly pray thee![^736]

O blessed Theodore, who didst consign the glory of thy noble birth unto the dust, exchanging thy princely coronet for the monastic habit; wherein having well pleased thy Master, and now living with the angels in heaven: pray for us, that we may become partakers of unfading glory![^737]

O Venerable Sophronius, who didst shut thyself up in a dark place, and didst unceasingly sing unto God in the Psalter; and now in the spacious dwelling on high enjoying angelic melodies: pray that we also may be accounted worthy to hear them![^738]

Having ministered unto the Most High in holiness and righteousness, O marvelous Pancratius, thou wast enriched with gifts of wonderworking, which thou didst bountifully grant unto those in need: grant these therefore also unto us in illnesses, who fervently come unto thee![^739]

*Glory:*
For the glory of Thy name, O Super-essential Trinity, Thou hast glorified Thy righteous ones and this place: by Thy goodness therefore grant unto us also, O Good One, to behold the glory of the saints in Thy glory, and with them to glorify Thee, the One God in three Hypostases!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Whosoever glorifies thee not, O Most Holy Virgin, let him not behold the glory of eternal life; but grant unto us to behold it, for we know thee to be glorified by all *(Left page — Folio 141 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES)*
...by all the heavenly powers, and we offer this unto thee: Rejoice, certain boast of the faithful!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^740]
Chosen from all generations, holy venerable favorites of God of the Caves, having blossomed forth with virtues upon these hills, the earth has not concealed you, but heaven and the paradise habitation have opened unto you! Therefore we offer songs of praise in your commemoration unto God Who has glorified you: do ye, as having boldness, protect by your prayers from all misfortunes those who honor your assembly, as our protectors and intercessors before God!

*Ikos:*
Who is able to praise Thy saints one by one, O Good One? For they are multiplied more than the sand; but do Thou Thyself, O Master Christ, Who numberest the multitude of the stars, and callest them all by their names, receive all their supplications for us, and manifest Thy compassions upon Thy people; for we know that they have great boldness before Thy majesty, and intercede unto Thee as our constant prayerful advocates for our souls!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The youths coming from Judea in Babylon once by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*[^741]

*Troparia:*
Fertile and curdled art thou, O Caves Mount; for in thy citizens the Most High was pleased to place His habitation; therefore we also sing with them: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^742]

Filled with myrrh and like unto the hill of Lebanon art thou, O Caves Mount, dripping with the fragrant myrrh of the God-bearing fathers, for the healing of corrupting diseases; receiving whose sweet fragrance, we sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

O Ammon and Mardarius, a striving pair, who were lovers of poverty, and have now acquired imperishable riches: pray that we also may acquire the same in the heights, where they sing: O God, blessed art Thou![^743]

We hymn Rufus, the mirror of fasters and laborers, with blessed Benjamin, who gave all his substance unto the poor that he might acquire the one pearl, Christ, whose emulators may we be, singing: O God, blessed art Thou![^744]

O Theophilus, adornment of hierarchs, who didst occupy the throne of Novgorod, and didst behold the Lord Himself: entreat His goodness, that we also...

*(Right page — Folio 142 recto, running header: IN THE FAR CAVE)*
...that we also may behold Him with thee, and sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^745]

O Martyrius, luminary among deacons, and example among laborers, lover of purity, who hadst the gift to drive away evil spirits from men and to heal infirmities: drive away from us the spirit of malice and every deceit, that we may purely sing: O God, blessed art Thou![^746]

*Glory:*
Glorify with glory those who glorify Thy saints, O all-good Trinity, which Thou hast prepared from the ages for Thy servants, and make them worthy to sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother and Queen full of joy, make those who bring joyful hymns unto thee worthy of eternal joys, and open the gates of paradise unto the whole Christian race that ever sings unto thee: Blessed art thou, O Theotokos!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn: praise and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*[^747]

*Troparia:*
Habitation of the heavenly Queen art thou, O Caves Mount of a hundred caves, which the multitude of glorious venerable fathers surround; all having sharpened weapons—prayers against the ruler of the world—they sing a song unto God: We sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

East and west, south and north, behold in thine enclosure, O Caves Mount, a spring bubbling with the myrrh of marvels of the God-bearing fathers, pouring forth abundantly unto all, who sing and supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages!

What praise shall we offer unto you, O God-bearing fathers? How shall we declare your labors and struggles with an earthly tongue? We know not: but do thou, O holy Euthymius, teach us silence, that with thee we may sing the Lord unto all ages![^748]

O Cassian and blessed Pior, radiance of fasters and laborers, both shining by your life as heavenly luminaries: illuminate us with the brightness of grace given unto you, that we may be accounted worthy with you to sing the Lord unto all ages![^749]

Remembering the beatitude of those that mourn, O Venerable Paphnutius, thou didst ever weep, and hast now inherited joyful places: pray that we also may take up our abode there in the shelter free from tears, that with thee we may supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages![^750]

With marvelous Joseph, we all honor your holy memory, O saints, who by fasting and labors well pleased God *(Left page — Folio 142 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES)*
...who well pleased God, and we pray that through you we may find protection from all misfortunes, and be accounted worthy to supremely exalt the Lord unto the ages!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
Traversing the enclosure of the holy cave in mind, I behold upon its firmament a multitude of venerable fathers as stars, whom it is impossible to number: rendering praise unto God for all of them, we pray that He may grant us with them to supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Driven by the dragon that rules the world, I have fled unto thine enclosure, O pure one: protect me therefore beneath the shelter of thy mercy, and deliver me from that beast, that being saved, I may supremely exalt thee, the Mother of God, unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Truly do we confess thee as the Theotokos, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, with the bodiless choirs magnifying thee!*[^751]

*Troparia:*
Truly formerly this holy mount was a valley of weeping and a den of thieves, but now it is the house of God, and those dwelling therein are blessed, since it was sanctified by you, O God-bearing fathers; for this cause we all magnify you![^752]

The voice of rejoicing and the assembly of feast-lovers gather together to praise your congregation, O Pechersk fathers: receiving their praises, save them from all sorrows and griefs, that we may ever magnify you!

Having taken up the yoke of the Cross with a joyful soul, and having tilled this mount with the plow of labors, ye planted the spiritual gardens of your sweat, O God-bearing fathers; receiving the fruits whereof, we gladden our souls, and magnify you all!

Walking in the one path of the commandments of the Lord, ye sowed diverse seeds: one purity, another obedience, another fasting, another poverty and tears; yet all alike reap sheaves of joy; grant also of them unto us, that we may ever magnify you all!

The Lord shepherds you now in a place of green pasture, O all-blessed fathers, and what good thing does He withhold from you? For the torrent of sweetness waters you; wherefrom with but one drop sweeten, we pray, the bitterness of our life, that joyfully we may sing unto our God!

Having crossed over from the place of weeping unto that free from tears, we pray you, O all-blessed fathers: deliver from every affliction us who faithfully honor your memory, that we all may magnify you!

*Glory:*

*(Right page — Folio 143 recto, running header: IN THE FAR CAVE)*
*Glory:*
Glory art Thou, O Trinity, unto the choir of venerable fathers, and unfailing rejoicing; for in Thee having conquered the world and the flesh with the invisible enemy, they received crowns, and rejoice with the angels, ever magnifying Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
We glorify thee, O all-glorious city of God, for the Most High Himself founded thee, and gave thee as a refuge unto all that flee from the face of the enemy: be thou also a harbor unto us, that we may ever magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion:*[^753]
Royal priesthood, chosen generation, holy hierarchs, priests, monastics, virgins, wonderworkers, and the whole fasting assembly of the venerable Pechersk fathers: assemble today in prayer, that for your sake the King of glory may save our EMPRESS and all people who piously honor your congregation!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We glorify thee, O pure Virgin, as holier than the holy, and we magnify the Word born of thee: do thou receive from us also this doxology, and grant us to glorify thee where all the saints glorify thee as the Mother of God!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^754]
Carnal wisdom ye took captive through subjection of the mind in obedience unto Christ, O all-blessed fathers, and having sailed across the sea of passions, ye reached the summit of desire; therefore Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls granted unto you the honors of the calling from on high!

Ye quenched the furnace of worldly attachments and wilful tumults by streams of tears and ultimate poverty, O venerable fathers; and as in a radiant bridal chamber ye abode in the dark caves, angelically glorifying Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!

Having cleansed your mind from the depths of tempestuous passions, sorrows, and worldly cares, O God-bearing fathers, by hope of the future life ye despised all corruptible things; and now being in incorrupt life, pray for us unto Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^755]
Desiring the beatitude of the blameless, ye ran in the path of the commandments of the Lord, and seeking the testimonies of the will of God *(Left page — Folio 143 verso, running header: SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE CAVES)*
...and seeking the testimonies of the will of God, ye took up your abode in mountains, dens, and dark caves; ye crucified the world with its passions, and having conquered the demonic hosts, now inherit life and unending glory. Pray for us, we entreat you, O venerable fathers, that we may obtain communion with you in the Jerusalem on high, and salvation for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true vine that hast blossomed forth for us the Fruit of life: we pray thee, O Sovereign Lady, intercede with the holy apostles and with all the saints, that our souls may be saved!

*The Great Doxology and the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*[^756]
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*
*Epistle to the Galatians, Section 213 [Galatians 5:22–6:2].*
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*
*Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he will delight exceedingly in His commandments.*
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Section 43 [Matthew 11:27–30], or according to Luke, Section 24 [Luke 6:17–23].*
*Communion Verse: The righteous man shall be in everlasting remembrance.*

*(Tailpiece engraving: Winged cherub with garland of flowers)*

*(Right page — Folio 144 recto, headpiece engraving: Christ the Savior surrounded by venerable monks and angels)*[^757]

---

# GENERAL MENAION (COMMON SERVICES TO THE SAINTS)

*Common Service to One Venerable Monk, on the Day Whosoever Wills May Sing It.*[^758]
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O martyrs of the Lord" (Мч҃нцы Гдⷭни):*[^759]
A holy and venerable luminary, divine and light-bearing, is never hidden beneath the bushel of worldly grossness; but the Lover of mankind places him upon the summit of high marvels: by his prayers, O Christ, grant great mercy unto Thy people![^760]

Thou didst put the labor of thy hands unto the plow, O all-honored one, ever working divine deeds, and didst not turn back to look behind thee: but wast made fit for the Kingdom of Christ, God incarnate, unto the salvation of our souls![^761]

Borne in the bodily skiff, with gentle breezes of the gentle Spirit, thou didst lightly traverse the deep sea of life: and having sold thy possessions for the pearl of great price, and found it, thou didst retain it within thyself, having become blessed through His divine gifts![^762]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
My thoughts are impure, my lips deceitful, and all my deeds defiled: and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Sovereign Lady Virgin, pray thy Son, Creator, and Lord, that He may receive my spirit in repentance, as the only tender-hearted One!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Bullock voluntarily nailed upon the Tree, the heifer undefiled, lamenting sorrowfully *(Left page — Folio 144 verso, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
...lamenting sorrowfully: Woe is me, my dearest Child! What has the ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O all-beloved!

*If thou celebrate with the Polyeleos, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to 6.*
*Glory, Tone 6:*[^763]
Having preserved that which is according to the image unharmed, and having ascetically appointed the mind as sovereign over the corrupting passions, thou didst ascend as far as possible unto that which is according to the likeness; for having courageously compelled thy nature, thou didst hasten to subdue the worse unto the better, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit; therefore thou wast shown forth as an instructor of monastics, an inhabitant of the wilderness, a teacher of those that run well, a most certain rule of virtue; and now in the heavens, the mirrors being dissolved, O our Father [Name], thou dost purely behold the Holy Trinity: pray unceasingly for those who honor thee with faith and love!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Who will not bless thee, O Most Holy Virgin: seek at the end of this book.*[^764]

*The Entrance.*
*The Prokeimenon of the day, and the readings for a venerable monk: seek in the beginning of this book, Folio 4 on the reverse.*[^765]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the ranks of heaven" (Нбⷭныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^766]
Thy feast has arrived, O God-bearer, brighter than the sun, illuminating those who come unto thee with faith, pouring forth the sweet fragrance of immortality, and welling forth healings unto our souls, O Venerable [Name], pleader for our souls!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^767]
Having restrained sensible bodily passions with the bridle of temperance, O wonderworker [Name], and showed forth on earth the zeal of the bodiless ones, thou didst subject all carnal desires unto the spirit; therefore now dwelling in the heavenly habitations, pray for our souls!

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he will delight exceedingly in His commandments.*[^768]
O blessed [Name], having laid the foundation of virtue, thou didst put off the old man with his lusts, and didst truly put on Christ; therefore also, O venerable one, thou didst expose many enemy arrays, having become an instructor of monastics: pray that our souls may be saved!

*(Right page — Folio 145 recto, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
*Glory, Tone 8:*[^769]
We honor thee, O our Father [Name], as the instructor of multitudes of monastics; for by thy path we have learned truly to walk uprightly: blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst expose the power of the enemy, companion of angels, fellow-partaker with the venerable and righteous; with whom pray unto the Lord that He have mercy upon our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*O unwedded Virgin, who God... seek at the end of this book.*

*If thou celebrate not, say this Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God, pray that we may be saved!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
I cannot bear, O Child, seeing Thee asleep upon the Tree, Who grantest vigilance unto all: that Thou mayest grant divine and saving vigilance unto those who of old fell asleep with the sleep of perdition through the fruit of transgression, the Virgin said weeping, whom we magnify!

*Troparion in the Typikon; but if there be no Typikon, say this:*[^770]
*Troparion, Tone 8:*[^771]
In thee, O father, was preserved with certainty that which is according to the image; for taking up the Cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thy deeds didst teach us to overlook the flesh, for it passes away, and to care rather for the soul, a thing immortal; therefore also with the angels rejoices thy spirit, O Venerable [Name]!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: According to the Tone of the Troparion.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the same Troparion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^772]
Thou didst follow the calling of thy Lord, having cast away the world and all the fair things in the world, O all-blessed [Name]: thou didst endure wilderness affliction fervently, and the hostile legions of demons courageously; therefore we also ever faithfully praise thy memory in hymns! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy divine birthgiving, O pure one, thou didst renew the mortal nature of the earthborn corrupt with passions, and didst raise all from death unto life incorruptible *(Left page — Folio 145 verso, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
...unto life incorruptible; therefore, as is due, we all bless thee, O all-glorious Virgin, even as thou didst foretell!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^773]
Let us honor the faster of the Lord in hymns, as having truly put to death all attacks of passions by temperance and firm patience, and greatly put to shame the adversary foe and all his pride; and now praying unto the Lord that He have mercy on our souls! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The dreadful wonder of thy conceiving and the ineffable manner of thy birthgiving was made known in thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: it confounds my mind and amazes my thought; thy glory, O Theotokos, has stretched forth unto all, for the salvation of our souls!

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*[^774]
We bless thee, O Venerable Father [Name], and we honor thy holy memory, instructor of monastics and companion of angels!

*Selected Psalm: With patience I waited for the Lord.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*[^775]
Thou didst bury all the rising of passions by mortification of the flesh, O sacred father, and after death didst receive endless life; therefore today the Church of Christ celebrates thy marvelous memory, O adornment of the venerable! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Direct us into the path of repentance, who ever turn aside unto evil byways, and provoke the all-good Lord to anger, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of desperate men, habitation of God!

*Gradual Hymn, the 1st Antiphon of the 3rd Tone.*

*Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Section 43 [Matthew 11:27–30].*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*[^776]
O Venerable Father, into all the earth has gone forth the proclamation of thy corrections: therefore in the heavens thou hast found the recompense of thy labors; thou didst destroy the demonic legions, and reached the angelic ranks, whose life thou didst blamelessly emulate: having boldness before Christ God, ask peace for our souls!

*(Right page — Folio 146 recto, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
*If thou celebrate with the Polyeleos: Canon of the Theotokos to 4; and of the Saint to 8.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the water as dry land, and fleeing from the malice of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*[^777]

*Troparia:*
Adorned with good manners, thou didst cleave unto Christ from youth, having put carnal passions to death by temperance, and hast departed unto life, O venerable one!

Having been a fulfiller of the words and laws of God, thou wast filled, O all-wise father, with divine gifts and wonders, and hast abundantly cast forth rays unto all!

Having prevailed through the strength of Christ, thou didst cast down the dominion and power of the enemy, and received the prize of victory, O venerable one, with wonderworking radiances!

*Glory:*
A good conscience thou didst possess, and a heart's eye looking unto God; therefore in reward of Thy prayer, O all-wise one, He united thee with the venerable![^778]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Assuage the passions of my flesh, and the improper movements of my mind, I pray thee, O all-pure Virgin, and correct me who have gone astray in mind!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the confirmation of those that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee!*[^779]

*Troparia:*
Wholly cleaving unto the Almighty, O venerable and all-wise father, thou didst escape all demonic malice!

Adorned with the height of humility, thou didst cast down the boastful one unto the earth by thine excellent deeds!

United with flesh, O all-wise father, thou didst humble the haughty neck of the wicked one by the humility of thy words!

*Glory:*
Having the power of God assisting Thee, O all-wise one, as marvelous thou dost pour forth marvels and drive away infirmities!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Incarnate in thy womb, O all-immaculate one, the Creator took up His abode, for a blessing unto those who faithfully sing of thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^780]
Thou didst subdue the passionate carnal mind, and having enslaved the worse unto the better, O Father [Name], all-glorious one, through fasting thou wast shown forth as a vessel of the operation of life, and didst shine forth in the world as a solar ray, with radiances *(Left page — Folio 146 verso, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
...with radiances of thy virtues; therefore do we hymn thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou art an invincible wall unto us Christians, O Theotokos Virgin; for fleeing unto thee, we abide unharmed, and when again we sin, we have thee as an intercessor; therefore giving thanks, we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The all-immaculate ewe-lamb and Mother of Christ God: a sword passed through thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God voluntarily crucified; Whom, O all-blessed one, cease not to pray, that He may grant us forgiveness of transgressions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^781]
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead!*

*Troparia:*
Having made thy soul a temple unto the Holy Spirit, O all-glorious one, thou wast an heir of the Kingdom on high, with the celestial powers!

Thou ever healest the painful passions of those who hasten unto thee; for thou didst receive grace from God, O venerable one, to work signs and marvels!

*Glory:*
As a most comely sprout in the house of God thou didst blossom forth, O father, adorned with virtues, and abundant with the fruits of marvels!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O spiritual field, as from a furrow thou hast brought forth an ear of corn, Who nourishes all creation, the God of the universe, O Ever-Virgin!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^782]
*Rising early, we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other!*

*Troparia:*
With a cleansed mind, O all-glorious one, thou beholdest the ineffable beauty of Christ, the God of the all-honored!

As Elijah upon a chariot, O father, by thy virtues thou didst ascend unto heaven, made light by the Spirit!

*Glory:*
Having braced thy body by temperance and purity, thou didst reach the breadth of the dwellings on high, O father!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Heal the darkening of my ailing mind, O thou that didst bear Christ the Physician, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^783]
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!*

*Troparia:*
The spiritual serpent was slain by thy sacred prayers, O God-blessed one, and those who asked signs through thee are loosed from his malice; for yea, thou wast known as a most radiant and faithful favorite of God!

*(Right page — Folio 147 recto, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
Having renewed thy soul with the diligent plow, O sacred father, thou didst most wisely cast into heaven the fruitful seeds of virtues, and didst reap the rich ears of manifold designations!

*Glory:*
Having prevailed through the strength of the Spirit, O father, thou didst cast down the dominion and power of the enemy, and didst receive the prize of victory, O venerable one, with wonderworking radiances!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Lord is with thee, O all-pure one, as He was well pleased to be: O Maiden, through thine intercession He has delivered all of us from the dominion of the deceiver; therefore now, as is due, generations of generations bless thee!

*Kontakion in the Typikon; but if there be no Typikon, say this:*[^784]
*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^785]
Having divinely armed thyself with spiritual purity, and holding unceasing prayers firmly in thy hands as a spear, thou didst pierce the demonic arrays, O our Father [Name]: pray unceasingly for all of us!

*Ikos:*
Having loved the divine commandments of Christ, O venerable one, and hated the enjoyment of this world, thou didst come fervently unto Him, and wast a luminary, enlightening the ends of the earth with spiritual radiance; therefore, falling down, I pray thee: Enlighten my spiritual eyes, to hymn thy struggles, fasting, and vigilance, and painful tears, and mortification of the body, for the sake of the blessed future life which thou now enjoyest: pray unceasingly for all of us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^786]
*The youths coming from Judea in Babylon once by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparia:*
Having traversed ascetic struggles with a firm mind, O father, thou didst humble the most haughty mind by divine humility, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

As a most comely sprout, O father, thou didst blossom forth in the house of God *(Left page — Folio 147 verso, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
...adorned with virtues, and didst abound with the fruits of marvels!

*Glory:*
Having enlightened thy heart, thou wast the leader of the sacred assembly, O father, admonishing and teaching, and bringing all unto the will of God, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having born the pre-beginningless Word as a new Child, thou hast renewed us who are grown old through sin, O Virgin, and confirmed us to sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^787]
*The God-proclaiming youths in the furnace, trampling down the flame with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparia:*
O heir of divine dwellings, thou didst live angelically, O father; therefore with the angels rejoices thy spirit!

Having walked without turning back, O all-wise and most marvelous father, in the divine paths leading unto heaven, thou didst completely turn away from those leading unto evil!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
By the grace that took up its abode in thy soul, O father, the unclean demonic spirits that pursue men are greatly driven away!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou art an inexhaustible water, O Virgin, drinking wherefrom we are all filled with grace, sanctified in soul and body!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^788]
*Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God: that the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, from the virginal womb becoming man; therefore the faithful magnify the all-pure Theotokos!*

*Troparia:*
Thy precious shrine pours forth healing abundantly by the Spirit of God, and heals the long-lasting passions of those who come unto thee, O father, and drives away the wicked, ferocious spirits, that we may praise thy radiant struggles!

As a great sun thou hast shone forth upon us by the greatness of thy struggles, and hast illuminated all the ends of the earth, O God-wise one: having died, thou hast passed from the light unto the light that has shone forth; therefore we cry aloud unto thee: Enlighten our thoughts, O Venerable Father [Name]!

Thy patient body, O blessed one, wounded by heavy chains, heals incurable passions by its touch, since God and the Savior greatly glorified thee, O all-wise and most marvelous father...

*(Right page — Folio 148 recto, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
...for by thy good deeds thou didst glorify Him, O venerable one!

*Glory:*
Thou didst blossom forth in ascetic valleys, O [Name], our all-praised father, as a fragrant rose, and as a lily thou didst abound, O sacred initiate, in virtues and wonders; therefore, O venerable one, drive away from us the stench of stinking passions!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Enlighten, O pure Virgin, my heart that is ever blinded by transgressions and many worldly tumults; leave me not to be a rejoicing unto enemies, that I may glorify thee and sing of thee with love, O all-hymned one!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оуслы́шите):*[^789]
Thou didst blossom forth as a palm tree according to David, O father, and wast shown to be a habitation of the All-Holy Spirit, and He showed thee forth glorious unto the universe: pray unceasingly for us who honor thy most precious memory with faith, O Venerable [Name]!

*Theotokion:*
We bless thee in unceasing hymns, O Virgin, for thou didst bear One of the Trinity, O Theotokos, and dost bear upon thy divine arm the pre-wealthy Word, without change and without confusion!

*If thou celebrate his Polyeleos.*
*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 6:*[^790]
O all-blessed God-bearer [Name], thou didst subject all carnal wisdom unto the spirit, having been confirmed by ascetic sufferings, shining as gold tested in the furnace; thou didst show thyself and wast a receptacle of the All-Holy Spirit; having gathered multitudes of monastics, and by thy teachings, as by a certain ladder leading up unto heaven, thou didst lead them up unto the height of virtues: remember us who ever honor thy sacred memory, and pray that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*

Today has shone forth thy most radiant and all-festal memory, O all-glorious [Name], summoning a numerous assembly of fasters and choirs of the venerable; and angels with men truly unite for the praise of Christ our God, Who is worshipped in the Trinity; therefore, hastening unto the sacred shrine of thy relics, we receive bountiful gifts of healing, and we glorify Christ, the Savior of our souls, Who has crowned thee!

O all-blessed God-bearer, Father [Name], thou didst bedew the earth with tears *(Left page — Folio 148 verso, running header: COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE MONK)*
...thou didst bedew the earth with tears, and gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, manifesting heartfelt desire unto Christ Whom thou didst love; therefore thou wast an example unto monastics, and didst fulfill every kind of virtue: therefore do we bless thee, magnifying Him Who has glorified thee!

*Glory, Tone 2:*
O Venerable Father, having diligently trained thyself in virtues from childhood, thou wast an instrument of the Holy Spirit; and receiving from Him the operation of marvels, thou didst admonish men to despise sweet pleasures; and now being most purely illuminated by divine light, enlighten also our thoughts, O our Father [Name]!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
All my hope do I place upon thee, O Mother of God: preserve me beneath thy shelter!

*The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*
*Epistle to the Galatians, Section 213 [Galatians 5:22–6:2].*
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*
*Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he will delight exceedingly in His commandments.*
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Section 43 [Matthew 11:27–30], or according to Luke, Section 24 [Luke 6:17–23].*
*Communion Verse: The righteous man shall be in everlasting remembrance.*

*(Tailpiece engraving: Ornate floral scrollwork with stylized central bloom)*
