[^576]: 2 Corinthians 8:9.

[^577]: *Stichera incipit*: «Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе» (Tone 4, Podoben model).

[^578]: Leviticus 19:18 and Matthew 22:37–39.

[^579]: Colossians 4:6 («сло́во ва́ше да быва́етъ всегда̀ во бл҃года́ти, со́лїю растворе́но»).

[^580]: Revelation 7:14 («и҆ и҆спра́ша ри́зы своя̑, и҆ оу҆бѣли́ша ри́зы своя̑ въ кро́ви А҆́гнчи»).

[^581]: Cross-reference to the three Old Testament composite readings for monastics at Vespers on fol. 81v (Cohort 9, leaf p86).

[^582]: *Aposticha incipit*: «Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва» (Tone 2, Podoben model).

[^583]: Psalm 149:5 (LXX).

[^584]: Psalm 115:6 (LXX).

[^585]: *Ladder from the Caves Mount*: Genesis 28:12, combined with the Lavra's foundation tradition of the Mother of God's special oversight.

[^586]: *Troparion incipit*: «Сою́зомъ любвѐ свя́завшеся» (Tone 4).

[^587]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon recounts that Prince Mstislav Svyatopolkovich of Kyiv, coveting the legendary Varangian treasure hidden by Theodore in the caves, subjected both monks to tortures and fatally wounded St. Basil with an arrow in 1098.

[^588]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: «Гро́бъ тво́й» (Tone 4, Podoben model).

[^589]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: «Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ» (Tone 1, Podoben model).

[^590]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: «Вертогра́дъ заключе́нный» (Tone 1, Podoben: «Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ»). Song of Songs 4:12 («вертогра́дъ заключе́нъ, сестра̀ моя̀ невѣ́ста»).

[^591]: Matthew 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30 (Common Reading for Monastic Martyrs, Conception 38).

[^592]: Sticheron after Psalm 50: «Преблаже́нніи ѻ҆ц҃ы̀» (Tone 6), identical to the Vespers Glory sticheron on fol. 119r–v (leaf p123).

[^593]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Нетре́ную необы́чную» (Ode 1, Tone 2). An allusion to Exodus 14:21–22.

[^594]: Matthew 13:45–46 (the pearl of great price).

[^595]: 1 John 5:19 («мі́ръ ве́сь во ѕлѣ́ лежи́тъ»).

[^596]: 1 Corinthians 7:31 («прехо́дитъ бо ѻ҆́бразъ мі́ра сегѡ̀»).

[^597]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Лꙋ́къ сокрꙋши́ся си́льныхъ» (Ode 3, Tone 2). 1 Samuel (1 Kings LXX) 2:4 (Song of Hannah).

[^598]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon relates that St. Basil instructed St. Theodore never to leave off the unceasing prayer of Jesus («Гдⷭи І҆и҃се Хрⷪтѐ, Сн҃е Бж҃їй, поми́лꙋй мя̀»), which destroys the deceit of demons.

[^599]: The demonic apparition assumed the exact physical appearance, habit, and voice of St. Basil while the real Basil was away on monastic obedience in Ovruch (Paterikon, Discourse 33).

[^600]: 1 Corinthians 10:12 («тѣ́мже мня́йся стоя́ти да блюде́тся, да не паде́тъ»).

[^601]: Matthew 12:45 («и҆ бꙋ́дꙋтъ послѣ̑дняя человѣ́ка тогѡ̀ гѡ́рша пе́рвыхъ»).

[^602]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: «Зло́бы нача́лника врага̀» (Tone 8).

[^603]: 1 Corinthians 15:33 (Menander, *Thais*: «тля́тъ ѡ҆бы́чаи бл҃ги̑ бесѣ́ды ѕлы̑»).

[^604]: Proverbs 17:3 and 1 Peter 1:7 («да и҆скꙋше́нїе ва́шея вѣ́ры многочестнѣ́йше зла́та ги́бнꙋща, ѻ҆гне́мъ же и҆скꙋше́на»).

[^605]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭи сла́вное твоѐ смотре́нїе» (Ode 4, Tone 2). Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX).

[^606]: 1 Timothy 6:9 («а҆ хотя́щїи богати́тися впада́ютъ въ напа̑сти и҆ сѣ́ть»).

[^607]: 2 Corinthians 11:14 («са́мъ бо сатана̀ преѡбразꙋ́ется во а҆́гг҃ла свѣ́тла»).

[^608]: 1 Timothy 6:10 («ко́рень бо всѣ́мъ ѕлы̑мъ є҆́сть сребролю́бїе»).

[^609]: Matthew 6:34 and 1 Peter 5:7 («всю̀ печа́ль ва́шꙋ возве́ргше на́нь, ꙗ҆́кѡ то́й пече́тся ѡ҆ ва́съ»).

[^610]: Matthew 6:25 («не пецы́теся дꙋше́ю ва́шею, что̀ я҆́сте, и҆лѝ что̀ пїе́те»).

[^611]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Оу҆́гль И҆са́їи проявле́йся» (Ode 5, Tone 2). Isaiah 6:6–7.

[^612]: In the Paterikon narrative, Basil returned from Ovruch at that exact hour, entered Theodore's cave, and dispelled the demonic deception.

[^613]: St. Basil brought other venerable Caves elders into Theodore's cave to bear witness that he had been absent in Ovruch for three months and could not have spoken with Theodore on the previous day.

[^614]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Гла́съ глаго́лѡвъ моле́бныхъ» (Ode 6, Tone 2). Jonah 2:2–3.

[^615]: 1 John 4:2–3 («вся́къ дꙋ́хъ, и҆́же и҆сповѣ́дꙋетъ І҆и҃са Хрⷪта̀ во пло́ти прише́дша, ѿ Бг҃а є҆́сть»).

[^616]: Kyiv Caves Paterikon, Discourse 33. The demons, attempting to hinder Theodore from hand-grinding corn for the monastery, were bound by his prayer and compelled to turn the millstones, grinding five cartloads of wheat in one night.

[^617]: *Kontakion incipit*: «До́бръ совѣ́тникъ ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ» (Tone 2). Commemorating St. Basil's holy counsel delivered unto Theodore, and their unjust martyrdom by arrow from Prince Mstislav.

[^618]: Song of Songs 4:12–14.

[^619]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Ѡ҆́бразꙋ злато́мꙋ на по́лѣ деи́рѣ» (Ode 7, Tone 2). Daniel 3:1–18; Song of the Three Holy Children.

[^620]: Paterikon, Discourse 33. St. Theodore carried timber up the Caves cliff to rebuild the cell; demons rolled the logs down, but were subsequently compelled by his prayers to haul all the logs up themselves.

[^621]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Въ подо́бїи зла́тѣ небре́гше» (Ode 8, Tone 2).

[^622]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon relates that the demon disguised as Basil approached a boyar of Prince Mstislav Svyatopolkovich, inciting the prince's greed.

[^623]: In Discourse 33, Theodore explained to Prince Mstislav that he had indeed found the treasure, but reburied it and prayed God to remove the knowledge of its location from his memory, so that he genuinely could not recall where it lay.

[^624]: Matthew 25:14–30 (Parable of the Talents).

[^625]: *Heirmos incipit*: «Ве́сь є҆сѝ жела́нїе» (Ode 9, Tone 2). Song of Songs 5:16 («ве́сь похо́ть и҆ ве́сь жела́нїе»).

[^626]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon recounts that Theodore was suspended over a fire and smoked, but remained unharmed by divine grace.

[^627]: Discourse 33 records that Prince Mstislav shot St. Basil with an arrow in drunken rage. Dying, Basil took out the arrow and prophesied: "With this same arrow thou thyself shalt be wounded." Prince Mstislav was killed by an arrow through an opening in the wooden palisade during the siege of Volodymyr in Volhynia on July 15, 1099.

[^628]: *Exapostilarion*: «Въ темни́цꙋ вве́ржени ѻ҆́ц҃ы блаже́нніи» (Tone 2). Both saints expired from their wounds while cast into the prison of the prince's palace on the night following their torture.

[^629]: *Praises Stichera*: Tone 4 Idiomela (Самогла́сны).

[^630]: Matthew 5:15 («нижѐ вжига́ютъ свѣти́лника и҆ поставлѧ́ютъ є҆го̀ подъ спꙋ́домъ, но на свѣ́щницѣ»).

[^631]: *Praises Glory*: Tone 3 («Прїиди́те восхва́лимъ бра́тїе»).

[^632]: *Common Service for Monastic Martyrs*: Typikon reference to the General Menaion common office for multiple venerable monastic martyrs.

[^633]: *Engraving*: Venerable Theodosius, Abbot of the Kyiv Caves and founder of coenobitic monasticism in Rus' (repose May 3, 1074; translation of relics August 14, 1091). Surrounding scenes depict his early life, monastic obedience, flax-spinning, final instruction to the brethren, and the solemn burial in the Far Caves.

[^634]: *Service incipit*: August 14, Service for the Translation of the Relics of St. Theodosius of the Caves from the cave to the Dormition Cathedral of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra in 1091.

[^635]: *Stichera incipit*: «Зва́нный свы́ше» (Tone 4, Podoben model).

[^636]: Nestor the Chronicler, *Life of St. Theodosius*. A nobleman unexpectedly arrived bringing a pouch of gold at a time of severe monastery shortage.

[^637]: Bishop Stephen of Volodymyr-in-Volhynia, former disciple and abbot of the Caves, while staying at the Klov Monastery across the ravine, saw a brilliant pillar of light above Theodosius's cave on the night of August 13/14, 1091, and hastened with his clergy to join in the excavation.

[^638]: Nestor the Chronicler, who was personally assigned by Abbot John to dig out the relics of St. Theodosius on the night preceding August 14, 1091, describes finding the body completely incorrupt, with all joints intact, hair dry and adhering to the scalp, and the face radiant.

[^639]: *Vespers Glory*: Tone 6 («Понестѝ дне́сь пра́здникъ»).

[^640]: August 14 is the Forefeast of the Dormition of the Theotokos; stichera and theotokia of the Forefeast are taken from the Menaion for August 14.

[^641]: Cross-reference to fol. 100v (Cohort 11, leaf p105) for the composite Old Testament readings for St. Theodosius.

[^642]: *Aposticha Glory*: Tone 8 («Мона́хѡвъ мно́жества наста́вника тя̀ почита́емъ»), canonical idiomelon for venerable monastics.

[^643]: *Troparion incipit*: «Правосла́вїя наста́вниче» (Tone 8).

[^644]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: «Гро́бъ тво́й» (Tone 1, Podoben model).

[^645]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: «Собезнача́льное Сло́во» (Tone 5, Podoben model).

[^646]: *Sessional Hymn incipit*: Tone 8, Podoben model «Премꙋ́дрости». Refers to the luminous signs seen above the cave and church during the 1091 translation.

[^647]: Sticheron after Psalm 50: Tone 6 («Прпб́не ѻ́че Ѳеодо́сїе, во всю̀ зе́млю и҆зы́де вѣща́нїе»), alluding to Psalm 18:4 (LXX) and Romans 10:18.

[^648]: Physical leaf p131 begins at the midpoint of the Sticheron after Psalm 50 for the Translation of the Relics of Venerable Theodosius, Abbot of the Kyiv Caves (August 14), Tone 6: "небесѣ́хъ ѡ҆брѣ́лъ є҆сѝ мздꙋ̀ трꙋдѡ́въ твои́хъ...".

[^649]: The Heirmos of Ode 1 (*Ѡ҆тве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моѧ̑...*) is the widely attested Tone 4 heirmos from the Annunciation Canon by St. John Damascene, traditionally appointed for the Translation of the Relics of St. Theodosius.

[^650]: Historical allusion to Venerable Theodosius coming to Venerable Anthony in the Far Caves hermitage around 1055–1058, receiving monastic tonsure from Abbot Barlaam, and practicing strict solitary hesychia (Kyiv-Pechersk Paterik, Discourse 8).

[^651]: Tone 4 Heirmoi of Odes 3, 4, 5, and 6 follow the classical model pattern of the Theotokos Canon (*Твоѧ̑ пѣсносло́вцы...*, *Сѣдѧ́й во сла́вѣ...*, *Оу҆жасо́шасѧ всѧ́ческаѧ...*, *Бж҃е́ственное сїѐ...*).

[^652]: Historical witness to the solemn translation of the relics of St. Theodosius on August 14, 1091, under Abbot John and Grand Prince Sviatopolk II Iziaslavich, when the incorrupt relics were carried from the cave to the Great Church of the Dormition (Paterik, Discourse 9; Primary Chronicle s.a. 1091).

[^653]: Kontakion model incipit: *Взбра́нной воево́дѣ* ("To thee, the Champion Leader"), Tone 8.

[^654]: The Ikos follows the classical Ruthenian and Byzantine liturgical acrostic chairetismoi pattern (*Ра́дꙋйсѧ...*), magnifying the monastic, ascetic, and pastoral virtues of St. Theodosius.

[^655]: Pechersk Paterik miracle account: St. Theodosius healed a monk afflicted with severe temptation and bodily suffering, commanding him to bow his head and laying his hand upon his shoulder (Discourse 11).

[^656]: The saint's solemn deathbed testament and blessing to the Kyiv Caves brotherhood: "ѡ҆бѣща́ю вамъ бра́тїе и҆ ѻ҆тцы̀, ꙗ҆́кѡ а҆́ще и҆ тѣ́ломъ ѿхождꙋ̀ ѿ ва́съ, но дꙋ́хомъ при́нѡ бꙋ́дꙋ съ ва́ми" (Primary Chronicle s.a. 1074; Paterik, Discourse 8).

[^657]: Melodic model: *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!* ("O marvelous wonder!"), Tone 8.

[^658]: Full-page woodcut engraving opening the Feast of Venerable Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves (August 17). The central illustration depicts the miraculous event recorded in Discourse 34 of the Paterik, wherein a dove descended and an angel assisted the holy iconographer in painting the image of the Dormition of the Theotokos.

[^659]: Melodic model: *Зва́нный свы́ше* ("Being called from on high"), Tone 4, appointed for the opening Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" on August 17.

[^660]: The three Stichera on "Lord, I have cried" for St. Alypius form an extended theological-allegorical hymn on icon painting, where pigments (*ша́ры*), physical boards (*дщи̑цы*), tears, and virtues are inscribed onto the fleshy tablets of the heart (*скрижа́ли се́рдца*).

[^661]: Troparion of St. Alypius, Tone 8: "Зра́ки и҆з̾ѡбража́ѧ ст҃ы́хъ...". Note the historical detail of St. Alypius receiving the grace of the priesthood (*сщ҃енства бл҃года́тїю*).

[^662]: The Kyiv-Pechersk Paterik (Discourse 34) records that Alypius was given by his parents to the Greek iconographers who came from Constantinople to decorate the Dormition Cathedral of the Lavra under Abbot Nikon (c. 1083).

[^663]: Paterik witness: St. Alypius was an eyewitness to the miraculous, unpainted depiction (*нерꙋкотворе́нный ѻ҆́бразъ*) of the Dormition in the altar apse of the Great Church, from which a white dove flew forth.

[^664]: Heirmos of Ode 4: *И҆́з̾ горы̀ приосѣне́нныѧ сло́ве...* (Habakkuk 3:3).

[^665]: Historical detail from Discourse 34 of the Paterik: St. Alypius divided all his earnings from icon painting into three equal portions—one for paints and materials (*і҆ко́нномꙋ требова́нїю*), one for the monastery and brethren (*въ монасты́рское дѣ́ланїе*), and one for alms to the poor (*въ ми́лостыню ни́щымъ*).

[^666]: Classical patristic allusion to 1 Corinthians 4:12 and Acts 20:34 ("these hands have ministered unto my necessities, and to them that were with me").

[^667]: Kontakion of St. Alypius, Tone 2: *Ѿ ю҆́ности бж҃е́ственное въ себѣ̀ и҆мѣ́ѧ начерта́нїе...*.

[^668]: Paterik miracle: St. Alypius healed a man from Kyiv afflicted with severe festering leprosy on his face and body by taking iconographic paints (*ка̑пли і҆ко́нныя*) and anointing his sores after he repented of his doubt and sins.

[^669]: Comparison to the Prophet Elisha healing Naaman the Syrian of his leprosy (4 Kingdoms [2 Kings] 5:1–14).

[^670]: Paterik miracle of the seven icons: When a certain deceitful man stole two boards and defrauded the saint, an angel miraculously painted seven icons in the saint's absence, exposing the slanderer and vindicating the non-possessive righteousness of St. Alypius.

[^671]: Miracle of St. Alypius recorded in the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterik (Discourse 34): In the Great Fire of Podil in Kyiv, the church where the icons of St. Alypius were placed was burned to the ground, yet all of the saint's icons were found completely unharmed by the flames.

[^672]: The miraculous icon of the Mother of God painted by St. Alypius for Grand Prince Vladimir Monomakh, sent to the city of Rostov, where it remained completely preserved from the fire when the Rostov cathedral burned down (Paterik, Discourse 34).

[^673]: Deathbed miracle of St. Alypius: When the saint was terminally ill and unable to paint an appointed icon of the Dormition for the feast, a radiant angel appeared in his cell, painted the icon in three hours, and delivered it to the church, while the saint peacefully gave up his spirit (Paterik, Discourse 34).

[^674]: Stichera at the Praises, Tone 7, Podoben: "As noble among martyrs" (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ*).

[^675]: Full-page woodcut engraving opening the Feast of the Holy Hieromartyr Kuksha and Venerable Pimen the Faster (August 27). The upper scene shows St. Kuksha and St. Pimen standing full-length before the Kyiv Caves Lavra; lower left illustrates the beheading of St. Kuksha with his disciple by the pagan Vyatichi; lower right shows St. Pimen in the church of the Lavra clairvoyantly foreseeing the martyrdom.

[^676]: Opening Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" for St. Kuksha and St. Pimen, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (*Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва*).

[^677]: Historical witness: Hieromonk Kuksha was a monk of the Kyiv Caves Lavra who set out to preach the Gospel to the pagan Vyatichi along the Oka River. He baptized many, performed wonders, and was martyred by beheading together with his disciple around 1114 (Paterik, Discourse 35).

[^678]: Troparion of St. Kuksha and St. Pimen, Tone 3: "Мꙋ́ченическою кро́вїю сщ҃е́нне кꙋ́кшо...".

[^679]: The Heirmos of Ode 1 (*Мо́рѧ чермнꙋ́ю пꙋчи́нꙋ...*) is from the Canon of Moses / the Crossing of the Red Sea, Tone 4.

[^680]: The three foes of the Christian ascetical life: the world (*мі́ръ*), the flesh (*пло́ть*), and the devil (*дїа́волъ*), traditionally overcome by the three holy fathers (Kuksha, his disciple, and Pimen).

[^681]: Sessional Hymn, Tone 2: St. Pimen offered purity and fasting as an acceptable olive tree, while St. Kuksha offered his blood shed for the Gospel as a sweet cluster of grapes.

[^682]: Allusion to St. Kuksha miraculously causing rain to fall during a severe drought among the Vyatichi, causing many pagans to turn to Christ (Paterik, Discourse 35).

[^683]: Allusion to Mark 16:18 ("they shall lay hands on the sick, and they shall recover"), referencing the gifts of bodily and spiritual healing granted through the prayers of St. Pimen the Faster.

[^684]: Kontakion of St. Kuksha and St. Pimen, Tone 4: "Свѣти́ла разꙋ́мнаѧ соше́дшесѧ...".

[^685]: Clairvoyant vision of St. Pimen: On the very day and hour that St. Kuksha was beheaded by the Vyatichi hundreds of versts away, St. Pimen stood in the midst of the Lavra Dormition Church and cried aloud to the brethren: "Our brother Kuksha has been slain this day!" Shortly thereafter, St. Pimen himself reposed in the Lord (Paterik, Discourse 35).

[^686]: Allusion to Daniel 6:16–23 (the Prophet Daniel in the lions' den) and the Three Youths quenching the violence of the fire (Daniel 3; Hebrews 11:33–34).

[^687]: Concluding troparion of Ode 8 and transition to Ode 9 of the Canon to St. Kuksha and St. Pimen (August 27), Folio 136v.

[^688]: Ode 9 Heirmos: *Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный...* from Daniel 2:34–35, 45, standard Tone 4/8 heirmos.

[^689]: Historical martyrological account of the beheading of St. Kuksha and his disciple by the pagan Vyatichi (Kyiv-Pechersk Paterik, Discourse 35).

[^690]: Ascetical victory of St. Pimen the Faster over the demonic snares, comparing temptations to flimsy spiderwebs (*а́ки паꙋчи́нꙋ*).

[^691]: Exapostilarion for August 27, extolling the joint commemoration of Hieromartyr Kuksha, his disciple, and Pimen the Faster in the Kyiv Caves.

[^692]: Psalm 33:20 [LXX 33:19].

[^693]: Opening of the General Synaxis Service of the Venerable Fathers Resting in the Far (Theodosius) Caves of the Kyiv Caves Lavra, celebrated on August 28, following the Dormition of the Theotokos.

[^694]: Opening Stichera at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (*Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ*).

[^695]: Rubrical cross-reference to the three Festal Old Testament readings for monastics at the front of the 1785 print (Folio 4 verso; physical leaf p10).

[^696]: Melodic model: *До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ* ("House of Ephratha / Joy of the ranks of heaven"), Tone 1.

[^697]: Psalm 149:5.

[^698]: Psalm 127:1 [LXX 127:1].

[^699]: Troparion of the Fathers of the Far Caves, Tone 4: "Мы́сленное со́лнце, и҆ свѣти́лꙋ лꙋнꙋ̀...".

[^700]: Sessional Hymn after the 1st Kathisma, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (*Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ*).

[^701]: Psalm 32:1 [LXX].

[^702]: Psalm 125:5 [LXX].

[^703]: Psalm 149:5.

[^704]: Psalm 31:11 [LXX].

[^705]: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom" (*Премꙋ́дрости* / Wisdom and Word).

[^706]: The Gradual Hymn (*Степе́нна*), 1st Antiphon of Tone 4: *Ѿ ю҆́ности моеѧ̀...* ("From my youth many passions war against me...").

[^707]: Psalm 115:6 [LXX 115:6 / MT 116:15].

[^708]: Psalm 115:3 [LXX 115:3 / MT 116:12].

[^709]: The Matins Canon for the Far Caves Fathers, Tone 8, combining the Theotokos Canon to 6 and the Far Caves Canon to 8.

[^710]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 8: *Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ...* (Exodus 14).

[^711]: Ascetical allegorization of the Far Caves as the enclosed garden of the Song of Songs (Song 4:12) and the cedars of Lebanon (Psalm 91:13 [LXX]).

[^712]: Saint Longinus the Gatekeeper of the Far Caves (discourse in the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterik), gifted with clairvoyance to discern the inner thoughts of everyone entering the Lavra.

[^713]: Saint Ignatius, Archimandrite/Pastor of the Caves, known for miraculous healings of sick brethren.

[^714]: Saint Silvanus of the Far Caves, guardian of the monastery orchards, who caught thieves trying to steal fruit by immobilizing them through his prayer for three days until they repented (Paterik).

[^715]: Troparion to Agatho the Wonderworker (Ode 3), invoking Psalm 138:24 [LXX] ("And see if there be any wicked way in me, and lead me in the way everlasting").

[^716]: Commemoration of Venerable Zeno the Faster and Venerable Macarius the Deacon of the Far Caves (Ode 3).

[^717]: Commemoration of Venerable Achilles the Deacon and Venerable Hypatius the Healer, who ministered to the sick brethren (Ode 3).

[^718]: Venerable Paisius and Venerable Mercurius of the Far Caves, who lived in undivided brotherly love and were buried together in one tomb (Paterik).

[^719]: Venerable Laurence the Recluse and Venerable Moses the Wonderworker of the Far Caves (Ode 3).

[^720]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 2, extolling the angelic struggles and unceasing prayers of the Pechersk monastic fathers.

[^721]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 8: *Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭи смотре́нїѧ твоегѡ̀ та́инство...* (Habakkuk 3:2).

[^722]: Allusion to the Parable of the Laborers in the Vineyard and the equal denarius of eternal life (Matthew 20:1–16).

[^723]: Saint Hilarion the Schemamonk of the Caves, who emulated St. Theodosius by eating only once a week (Kyiv-Pechersk Paterik).

[^724]: Saint Dionysius the Recluse of the Caves, known for the Easter miracle in the caves when he greeted the departed fathers with "Christ is risen!" and the holy relics answered from the tombs: "Truly He is risen!" (Paterik, Discourse 37).

[^725]: Saint Arsenius the Labor-lover (Arsenius Trudolyubivyi) of the Far Caves, who labored without ceasing day and night.

[^726]: Saint Pimen the Faster, who fulfilled the lowliest monastery obediences, carrying heavy firewood and grinding grain at the handmill every night (Paterik).

[^727]: Saint Titoe (Tithoes) the Recluse of the Far Caves, who lived in total seclusion illuminated by noetic light.

[^728]: Ode 5 Heirmos, Tone 8: *Просвѣти́ ны повелѣ́нїи твои́ми Гдⷭи...* (Isaiah 26:9).

[^729]: Allusion to the widespread spiritual enlightenment of all Rus' through the ascetic labors and holiness of the Kyiv Caves monastics.

[^730]: Saint Sisoes the Schemamonk and Saint Gregory the Wonderworker / Watchful of the Far Caves (Ode 5).

[^731]: Saint Paul the Obedient and the two holy Canonarchs, Gerontius and Leontius, who practiced rigorous bodily abstinence (Ode 5).

[^732]: Saint Nestor the Unlettered (Nestor Neknizhnyi) of the Far Caves, who possessed divine unlearned wisdom and saw angels while praying.

[^733]: Saint Titus the Soldier of the Far Caves, who was wounded in the head in battle and came to the Lavra to weep for his sins (Ode 5).

[^734]: Saint Pamva the Confessor of the Far Caves, who was captured by infidels, endured imprisonment for the faith, and was miraculously transported by an angel back to his cell (Paterik).

[^735]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 8: *Мл҃твꙋ пролїю̀ ко Гдⷭꙋ...* (Jonah 2:3).

[^736]: Saint Zechariah the Faster of the Far Caves, who ate only herbs and drove away demons by his holy name.

[^737]: Saint Theodore, Prince of Ostrog, who renounced worldly glory and his princely coronet to take the monastic schema in the Kyiv Caves.

[^738]: Saint Sophronius the Recluse of the Far Caves, who constantly recited the Psalter in total darkness.

[^739]: Saint Pancratius the Priest and Wonderworker of the Far Caves, gifted with grace to heal the sick.

[^740]: Kontakion of the Far Caves Fathers, Tone 8: *И҆збра́ннїи ѿ родо́въ оу҆го́дницы Бж҃їи...*.

[^741]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 8: *Ѿ І҆ꙋде́и доше́дше ѻ҆́троцы...* (Daniel 3).

[^742]: Psalm 67:16–17 [LXX]: "The mountain of God is a fertile mountain, a curdled mountain, a rich mountain."

[^743]: Saint Ammon the Recluse and Saint Mardarius the Non-possessor of the Far Caves (Ode 7).

[^744]: Saint Rufus the Faster and Saint Benjamin the Merchant, who gave all his earthly wealth to the poor to buy the single pearl of great price, Christ (Matthew 13:45–46; Paterik).

[^745]: Saint Theophilus, Archbishop of Novgorod, who reposed at the Kyiv Caves Lavra and whose relics were laid in the Far Caves.

[^746]: Saint Martyrius the Deacon of the Far Caves, lover of purity, who cast out demons and healed infirmities.

[^747]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 8: *Цр҃ѧ̀ нбⷭнагѡ...* (Daniel 3:52–90).

[^748]: Saint Euthymius the Schemamonk / Silent of the Far Caves, who took a vow of absolute silence.

[^749]: Saint Cassian the Recluse and Saint Pior the Recluse of the Far Caves (Ode 8).

[^750]: Saint Paphnutius the Recluse of the Far Caves, who wept continually remembering the hour of death and the beatitude of those that mourn (Matthew 5:4).

[^751]: The Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 8: *Вои́стиннꙋ Бцⷣꙋ тѧ̀ и҆сповѣ́дꙋемъ...* from Luke 1:48.

[^752]: Contrast between the ancient pre-Christian hills of Kyiv as a pagan "valley of weeping and den of thieves" and its sanctification as the "house of God" by the Caves fathers.

[^753]: The Exapostilarion (Photogogikon) of the Far Caves Fathers, containing the diplomatic prayer for the reigning Empress Catherine II (*да ва́съ ра́ди Цр҃ь сла́вы спасе́тъ І҆МПЕРАТРИ́ЦƔ и҆ всѧ̑ лю́ди*).

[^754]: Stichera at the Praises, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (*Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе*).

[^755]: Praises Doxasticon, Tone 6: *Бл҃же́нства непоро́чныхъ вожделѣ́вше...*, invoking Psalm 118:1 [LXX] ("Blessed are the blameless in the way").

[^756]: Liturgy appointments for the Far Caves Fathers: Beatitudes from Odes 3 and 6; Prokeimenon in Tone 7 (Psalm 115:6); Epistle Galatians 5:22–6:2; Gospel Matthew 11:27–30 or Luke 6:17–23; Communion Verse Psalm 111:6.

[^757]: Headpiece engraving opening the General Menaion (*Общій Миней*) common services, depicting Christ the Master surrounded by venerable ascetics and angels.

[^758]: Canonical opening of the Common Service to One Venerable Monk (*Слꙋ́жба ѡ҆́бща прпⷣбномꙋ є҆ди́номꙋ*), appointed to be sung on the commemoration day of any holy monk without a dedicated feast in the Menaion.

[^759]: Opening Stichera at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 8, Podoben: "O martyrs of the Lord" (*Мч҃нцы Гдⷭни*).

[^760]: Evangelical allusion to Matthew 5:14–16 ("neither do men light a candle and put it under a bushel, but on a candlestick").

[^761]: Allusion to Luke 9:62 ("No man, having put his hand to the plow, and looking back, is fit for the kingdom of God").

[^762]: Allusion to the Parable of the Pearl of Great Price (Matthew 13:45–46).

[^763]: The classical Great Vespers Doxasticon for a Venerable Monk, Tone 6: *Є҆́же по ѻ҆́бразꙋ соблю́дъ невреди́мѡ...*, synthesizing the Patristic ascetical theology of Genesis 1:26 (the preservation of the image and attainment of the likeness).

[^764]: Rubrical cross-reference to the Dogmatikon Theotokion of Tone 8 at the back of the Anthologion (*Кто̀ тебѐ не оу҆блажи́тъ...*).

[^765]: Cross-reference to the Old Testament Paremias for Monastics at Folio 4 verso (physical leaf p10).

[^766]: Aposticha Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the ranks of heaven" (*Нбⷭныхъ чинѡ́въ*). The placeholder `[и҆́мⷬ҇къ]` indicates where the name of the commemorated saint is inserted.

[^767]: Psalm 115:6 [LXX].

[^768]: Psalm 111:1 [LXX].

[^769]: Aposticha Doxasticon for a Venerable Monk, Tone 8: *Мона́хѡвъ мно́жества, наста́вника тѧ̀ почита́емъ...*.

[^770]: Rubric distinguishing between a saint with a prescribed Troparion in the Typikon and the General Troparion for a Venerable Monk (*Въ тебѣ̀ ѻ҆́ч҃е и҆звѣ́стнѡ спасе́ся...*).

[^771]: General Troparion for a Venerable Monk, Tone 8: *Въ тебѣ̀ ѻ҆́ч҃е и҆звѣ́стнѡ спасе́ся є҆́же по ѻ҆́бразꙋ...*, based on Matthew 16:24–26.

[^772]: Sessional Hymn after the 1st Kathisma, Tone 3, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ*).

[^773]: Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (*Собезнача́льное Сло́во*).

[^774]: Polyeleos Megalynarion (Pripivalo) for a Venerable Monk: *Оу҆блажа́емъ тѧ̀ прпⷣбне ѻ҆́ч҃е [и҆́мⷬ҇къ]...*.

[^775]: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (*Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се*).

[^776]: Matins Sticheron after Psalm 50, Tone 6: *Прпⷣбне ѻ҆́ч҃е, во всю̀ зе́млю и҆зы́де вѣща́нїе...*, citing Psalm 18:5 [LXX].

[^777]: Common Matins Canon to a Venerable Monk, Tone 8, Ode 1 Heirmos: *Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ...*.

[^778]: Troparion to the Holy Trinity / Doxasticon of Ode 1, transcribing the printed text *вна́чѣ моли́твѣ твое́й* ("in reward of thy prayer").

[^779]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 8: *Ты̀ є҆сѝ оу҆твержде́нїе...*.

[^780]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 3, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ*).

[^781]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 8: Biblical Canticle of Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3:2; LXX Hab. 3:2: *Κύριε, εἰσακήκοα τὴν ἀκοήν σου καὶ ἐφοβήθην, κατενόησα τὰ ἔργα σου καὶ ἐξέστην*).

[^782]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 8: Biblical Canticle of Isaiah (Isaiah 26:9, 13; LXX Isa. 26:9: *Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει τὸ πνεῦμά μου πρὸς σέ, ὁ Θεός*).

[^783]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 8: Biblical Canticle of Jonah (Jonah 2:3–8; LXX Jon. 2:3: *Ἐβόησα ἐν θλίψει μου πρὸς Κύριον τὸν Θεόν μου*).

[^784]: Rubric directing the substitution of the Common Kontakion when no specific proper feast is assigned in the Typikon.

[^785]: Melodic model (*podoben*): Tone 2, *Seeking the highest* (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀ / *Τὰ ἄνω ζητῶν*), the classic kontakion automelon of the Sunday of the Forefathers and general ascetics.

[^786]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 8: Song of the Three Holy Youths in Babylon (Daniel 3:26–56; LXX Dan. 3:28).

[^787]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 8: Song of the Three Holy Youths in Babylon (Daniel 3:57–88; LXX Dan. 3:57).

[^788]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 8: The Magnificat and Benedictus (Luke 1:46–55, 68–79).

[^789]: Melodic model (*podoben*): Exapostilarion, *Give ear, ye women* (Жены̀ оуслы́шите / *Γυναῖκες ἀκουτίσθητε*). Biblical allusion to the palm tree of David (LXX Ps. 91:13: *δίκαιος ὡς φοῖνιξ ἀνθήσει*).

[^790]: Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*), Tone 6 automela/prosomoia for a venerable father.

[^791]: Liturgical appendix containing the eight classical Sunday Dogmatika (*Dogmatica Theotokia*) sung at Great Vespers at *Lord, I have cried*, along with the corresponding Sunday Aposticha Theotokia.

[^792]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 1 (*Всемї́рную сла́вꙋ* / *Τὴν παγκόσμιον δόξαν*), attributed to Saint John of Damascus.

[^793]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 1 (*Сѐ и҆спо́лнися И҆са́їино прорече́нїе* / *Ἰδοὺ πεπλήρωται ἡ τοῦ Ἡσαΐου πρόρρησις*); Scriptural reference to the prophecy of the Virgin birth in Isaiah 7:14 (LXX Isa. 7:14: *ἰδοὺ ἡ παρθένος ἐν γαστρὶ ἕξει καὶ τέξεται υἱόν*).

[^794]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 2 (*Пре́йде сѣ́нь зако́ннаѧ* / *Παρῆλθεν ἡ σκιὰ τοῦ νόμου*); typological contrast between the Old Testament burning bush (Exodus 3:2) and pillar of fire (Exodus 13:21) and the Virgin Birth of Christ.

[^795]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 2 (*Ѡ҆ чꙋдесѐ но́вагѡ* / *Ὢ θαύματος καινοῦ*).

[^796]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 3 (*Ка́кѡ не диви́мсѧ* / *Πῶς μὴ θαυμάσωμεν*); dogmatic definition of the Chalcedonian christological union (*without change, confusion, or division*).

[^797]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 3 (*Без̾ сѣ́мене ѿ Бж҃е́ственнагѡ Дх҃а* / *Ἀσπόρως ἐκ θείου Πνεύματος*).

[^798]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 4 (*И҆́же тебѐ ра́ди Бг҃ооте́цъ прро́къ Дв҃дъ* / *Ὁ διὰ σὲ θεοπάτωρ προφήτης Δαυῒδ*); citation of LXX Psalm 44:10 (*παρέστη ἡ βασίλισσα ἐκ δεξιῶν σου*), with the Parable of the Lost Sheep (Luke 15:4–6).

[^799]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 4 (*При́зри на моле́нїа твои́хъ ра̑бъ* / *Ἔπιδε ἐπὶ τὰς δεήσεις τῶν οἰκετῶν σου*).

[^800]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 5 (*Въ чермнѣ́мъ мо́ри* / *Ἐν τῇ Ἐρυθρᾷ θαλάσσῃ*); typological fulfillment of the Red Sea crossing (Exodus 14:21–29) and the virginity of the Theotokos (*post partum*).

[^801]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 5 (*Хра́мъ и҆ две́рь є҆сѝ* / *Ναὸς καὶ πύλη ὑπάρχεις*); Scriptural allusion to the Sun of righteousness (Malachi 4:2; LXX Mal. 3:20).

[^802]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 6 (*Кто̀ тебѐ не оу҆блажи́тъ* / *Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε*); classical exposition of the hypostatic union in two natures without confusion (*ἀσυγχύτως*).

[^803]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 6 (*Творе́цъ и҆ и҆зба́витель мо́й* / *Ὁ ποιητὴς καὶ λυτρωτής μου*).

[^804]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 7 (*Мт҃и оу҆́бѡ позна́ласѧ є҆сѝ* / *Μήτηρ μὲν ἐγνώσθης*); patristic aphorism: *ὅπου γὰρ βούλεται Θεός, νικᾶται φύσεως τάξις* ("where God wills, the order of nature is overcome").

[^805]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 7 (*По́д̾ кро́въ тво́й Влⷣчце* / *Ὑπὸ τὴν σὴν εὐσπλαγχνίαν*).

[^806]: Sunday Dogmatikon, Tone 8 (*Цр҃ь нбⷭный* / *Ὁ βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν*); Scriptural allusion to the Incarnation (Baruch 3:37; LXX Bar. 3:38: *μετὰ τοῦτο ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς ὤφθη καὶ ἐν τοῖς ἀνθρώποις συνανεστράφη*).

[^807]: Sunday Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 8 (*Безневѣ́стнаѧ Дв҃о, ꙗ҆́же Бг҃а неизрече́ннѡ заче́нши пло́тїю* / *Ἀνύμφευτε Παρθένε, ἡ τὸν Θεὸν ἀφράστως συλλαβοῦσα σαρκί*).

[^808]: Collection of the Sunday Dismissal Theotokia (*Theotokia Apolytikia*) chanted throughout the liturgical year following the dismissal troparia of the saint or feast in each of the Eight Tones.

[^809]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 1 (*Гаврїи́лꙋ вѣща́вшꙋ тебѣ̀ Дв҃о* / *Τοῦ Γαβριὴλ φθεγξαμένου σοι Παρθένε*); Scriptural reference to the Annunciation (Luke 1:28) and King David's Ark of the Covenant (LXX Ps. 131:8: *ἀνάστηθι, Κύριε, εἰς τὴν ἀνάπαυσίν σου, σὺ καὶ ἡ κιβωτὸς τοῦ ἁγιάσματός σου*).

[^810]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 2 (*Всѧ̑ па́че смы́сла* / *Πάντα ὑπὲρ ἔννοιαν*).

[^811]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 3 (*Тѧ̀ хода́тайствовавшꙋю спасе́нїе ро́да на́шегѡ* / *Σὲ τὴν μεσιτεύσασαν τὴν σωτηρίαν*).

[^812]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 4 (*Є҆́же ѿ вѣ́ка оу҆тае́ное* / *Τὸ ἀπ᾽ αἰῶνος ἀπόκρυφον*); patristic synthesis of Colossians 1:26 and Ephesians 3:9.

[^813]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 5 (*Ра́дꙋйсѧ, две́ре Гдⷭнѧ непроходи́маѧ* / *Χαῖρε πύλη Κυρίου ἡ ἀδιόδευτος*); Scriptural allusion to the closed eastern gate in Ezekiel 44:1–2.

[^814]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 6 (*Бл҃гослове́ннꙋю нареки́й твою̀ Мт҃рь* / *Ὁ τὴν εὐλογημένην καλέσας σου Μητέρα*); Scriptural reference to the Parable of the Lost Coin (Luke 15:8–10).

[^815]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 7 (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ на́шегѡ воскресе́нїѧ сокро́вище* / *Ὡς τῆς ἡμῶν ἀναστάσεως θησαύρισμα*); affirmation of the perpetual virginity (*semper virgo / ἀειπάρθενος*) of the Theotokos before, during, and after childbirth.

[^816]: Sunday Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 8 (*И҆́же на́съ ра́ди рожде́йсѧ ѿ Дв҃ы* / *Ὁ δι᾽ ἡμᾶς γεννηθεὶς ἐκ Παρθένου*).

[^817]: Traditional concluding colophon formula of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra printing house (*Коне́цъ, и҆ Бг҃ꙋ сла́ва* / *Finis, et Deo gloria*), marking the formal completion of the 1785 print run.
